before that, a new group's frame is judged against the box of its members at a millionth of an inch, so a group with a turned member saves, and a group whose frame is not that box (`LockCalcWH` after a member moved out, a margin, a pin set away from the middle) is written as its members with a note; before that, the history's charge counts a text part at what a part is and the sheet of a page, the sheet of the document, the styles, masters, themes and tables when a step replaces them; before that, a group fits its members and a group with `LockCalcWH` is left, one box rule for a connector's two ends, the group's own points and the connectors glued to it, what is left as it was is said in the answer (an edit that would leave a group the file does not hold is made and the group listed), every allowance is one for the whole edit, and the history is held to its bytes; before that: after an edit of a member a group that was the box of its members is that box again in the same undo step, the glued ends of the page are found once and the cells are worked out in one commit within one budget; before that, a drop makes the page ready before its edit, so that a read the session kept never holds a shape of an edit thrown away, and a new or opened drawing drops the kept reads; before that, the window's previews: the commands that snap find the page the reader made ready, a drop released before the reader answers goes without `snap` at its ghost, an arrow key makes the page ready, a stencil file's master brings its points to the drop preview, the Text tool's drag matches sizes, the angle mark is at the pin, a change under a millionth is sent; before that, the window's previews send what the commands use, so that a drag's preview is its release: a move through `snap_ext` with the turned shape's own frame, Shift's `lock` and the `view`, a turned or flipped shape's resize through `snap_resize_ext` with `keep` (a corner keeps the proportions with no key), the rotation handle through `snap_turn`, a drop with the master's own connection points (`dropped_points`), an arrow key as `shape.move` with `nudge: {tick}`; the window draws the engine's marks by segment with their labels, and a guide along no axis; a page's index of shapes is made ready off the window's thread, and the cap is 10,000 shapes; before that, the engine snaps by the library's three new settings (a place pulls a locked move and stops an arrow key where it lies on the line of travel, a line mark runs from the nearest shape on each side) and by turned shapes' own sides (a turned shape lines up its own sides with the like sides of shapes turned alike and with slanted guides, a turned or flipped shape's resize moves along its own axes), `snap.at` refuses `connect` beside a box, `edges`, `nudge`, `keep` and `rotate`, and `shape.place` and `snap.at` tell `corner_boxes`; before that, `snap.at` takes `edges`, `keep`, `lock`, `nudge`, `view` and `rotate`, a command takes only the words of `snap` it has, and `truncated` says a turned target kept its corners for the page budget; before that, the window's preview of a move works out the box of the shapes that move with `snap::bounds_of`, as `shape.move` does (the box round the paths of a shape turned by other than whole quarter turns), and not with `box_of`, so that a turned shape lands where its preview showed; before that, third part: a turned shape lines up by the box round its paths, and a `shape.move` result says in `snap.corner_boxes` how many moving shapes kept the box round their corners; before that, second part: the new words of `snap` (`lock`, `nudge`, `view`, `keep`, `radius`) and `marks`; before that, `snap` takes `lock` on `shape.move`, and the snapping goes through the shared library; before that, `file.save_as` has an `enabled` that says no, with one reason, for a drawing that came from a flat file (the command list, MCP's `enabled_only`, a refusal and the window share it), the words no longer say a flat file is never written, and the warnings of an open are at most 20 with its own notes first and last; before that, a flat OpenDocument drawing (`.fodg`) is read by every route and saved back as a flat file by Save alone, a Save As of it is refused in words and greyed out in the window, and a Save As of another drawing to `.fodg` is refused; before that, the window asks "Leave the macros out?" when a Save As is refused with `MacrosNotAllowed`, in "Macros are bytes"; before that, the engine opens and saves `.diacraft` files (the new section "`.diacraft` files": the bytes decide the format and a `.diacraft` file is sniffed first, the memory of a read and a save is the process's, the two save paths and their refusals in words, the identifier ledger, the content stamp, templates and stencils, `file.status`'s `format`, `variant` and `kind`, `Session::pin_clock`), and `stencil.open` and `file.new {template}` take the three kinds)

The commands that draw: drop a shape from the built-in stencil, move, resize, turn, flip, order, align, delete, copy, paste, group and label shapes, and join them with
connectors. They are commands like every other (`engine.execute`, the CLI's `run`, MCP's `command_run`), each one undo step, and
the window of maps its mouse to exactly these. The reference app was not inspected, and our output has not been opened in it by the project:
every choice below about how the reference app behaves is DiaCraft's own, marked **ours**, and is listed for checking in
The project's list of assumptions to check.

## The commands

All lengths are **paper inches** on the page (the unit of the renderer's `View`), whatever the page's scale; the command turns them into
the page's drawing units (`DrawingScale` over `PageScale`) at its edge. A new page is drawn one to one, where the two are the same.
Selection stays in the window: a command takes shape `ID`s. `page` is the `ID` of a page; where it is optional, the command takes the first
page that holds every shape named, and `shape.drop` always names it.

| Command | Parameters | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `stencil.list` (query) | none | `{stencils: [{id, name, builtin, category, masters: [{id, name, width, height, connection_points, one_d, part, autoconnect}]}], available: [{id, name, builtin, category, masters_total}]}`, the sizes in inches (a built-in stencil names its `category`; `one_d` is on a connector, `part: true` on a master whose drop is put in a structure and `autoconnect: false` on one that is no connector and that the AutoConnect strip never offers): the built-in stencils that are open, Basic Shapes (`basic`), Basic Flowchart (`flowchart`), Decorative Shapes (`decorative`), Arrow Shapes (`arrows`), Gane-Sarson (`gane-sarson`), the Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes (`cross-functional`) and the Organization Chart Shapes (`orgchart`) in the order the Shapes window lists them (all seven, in that order, until a template changes the subset or `stencil.open {id}` adds one), and `available` the built-in ones that are not; then each stencil opened from a file (`file-1`, ...), whose masters also say `hidden` and `icon`: see **Stencil files** |
| `stencil.open` | `path` (a `.diacraft-stencil`, `.vssx` or `.vssm`), or `id` (a built-in stencil that is not open, from `available`) | `{id, name, already_open, masters, masters_total, file: {variant, bytes, warnings, warning_count, skipped_count}}`; `masters` as `stencil.list` has them; for an `id`, `{id, name, builtin, already_open, masters_total}`. A drawing or a template is refused, and so is a file that cannot be read, in words: **a file that would be read alone but not beside the open drawings and stencils, or while another file is being read and the stencil is past the slice of 192 MiB it is given beside that read (over about 4,500 masters of a typical shape, which main opens: the trade of a read that holds the budget; `stencil.open` does not wait for a read that is running, as `file.open` does, for up to 30 seconds), is `out_of_memory`, and one the memory a read may use (2.6 GB for the process) cannot hold alone is `too_big`** |
| `stencil.close` | `id` | `{id, closed}`. A built-in stencil stays (it is open or in `available` as a template decided); a drawing that took masters from the stencil keeps them |
| `stencil.search` (query) | `text` (at most 256 characters and 16 different words of at most 64), `mode`? (`all`, the default, or `any`) | `{text, mode, words, matches, listed, truncated, searched, total, complete, results: [{id, name, builtin, open, masters}], notes?}`: the masters of every built-in stencil (open or not) and every open stencil file whose name or search words (`ShapeKeywords`) hold the words as whole words, in any case, grouped by stencil, as `stencil.list` has them. At most 100,000 masters and 16 MiB of text are read and 500 masters listed: `complete` and `notes` say where a search stopped. No drawing needed, and nothing changes |
| `stencil.quick` (query) | `stencil`? (an id from `stencil.list`; left out or `quick`: the Quick Shapes stencil) | `{stencils: [{stencil, name, builtin, customized, masters}], offer: {stencil, masters}, readable}`: the Quick Shapes of a stencil, or the group of every open stencil, and the first four the AutoConnect flyout may offer, or where that leaves none the first open stencil's that feeds it, which `offer.stencil` names |
| `stencil.add_quick` | `stencil`, `master` (its id or name), `at`? (from 0) | the group as `stencil.quick` has it, and `changed`: the master is added to the stencil's Quick Shapes (moved to `at` when it is there). **No undo step**: the drawing's data, kept by `.diacraft`, VSDX and `.odg` |
| `stencil.remove_quick` | `stencil`, `master` | the group and `changed` (false when the master was not in it): the master goes below the divider again |
| `stencil.reset` | `stencil` | the group and `changed`: Reset Stencil, the stencil's own Quick Shapes again, and a drawing whose groups are all the stencils' own holds none of the data |
| `stencil.icon` (query) | `stencil`, `master` (its `ID` or its name) | `{format, bytes, data}`: the master's icon as base64 text, and its kind (`ico`, `png`, `bmp`, `gif`, `jpeg`, `emf`, `wmf`, `unknown`). A master with no icon, or one that is not base64 or is over 256 KiB, is refused in words |
| `shape.drop` | `master` (an id such as `right_triangle` or `predefined_process`, or the name, in any case, from any built-in stencil; for a stencil opened from a file its `ID` or its name), `stencil`? (`basic`, `flowchart`, `decorative`, `arrows`, `gane-sarson`, `cross-functional`, `orgchart`, or an opened file's id such as `file-1`; left out, the built-in stencils are searched), `page`, `x`, `y` (where the centre goes), `snap`? | `{id, master, name, page, width, height}` (a position of the Organization Chart Shapes dropped inside another position also `reports_to` and `connector`, and a `notes` entry when it was left unlinked); for a stencil opened from a file also `stencil`, `master_added`, `copied: {styles, colours, faces}` and `notes` (words: formulas that call `USE()`, and references to parts of the stencil's file that the master keeps beside its shapes, which are left out since the drawing does not have those parts) |
| `shape.move` | `ids`, `dx`, `dy`, `page`?, `snap`? | `{page, moved}`: how many of the shapes named moved (see MOVE-1D: a connector with both ends glued has nothing of its own to move, and a move that moves nothing is no undo step) |
| `shape.resize` | `id`, `x`, `y` (where the corner at (0, 0) of the shape's own frame goes: the box's **lower-left corner** for a shape that is not turned or flipped), `width`, `height` (along the shape's own axes), `page`?, `snap`?; or **`ids`** instead of `id`, with `x`, `y`, `width`, `height` the box on the page that the shapes' joint box is made | `{page, id}`; with `ids` `{page, resized, locked?, snap?}`: how many shapes changed, and the shapes a `LockWidth` or `LockHeight` left alone. A group is sized as a shape and its members follow |
| `shape.rotate` | `ids`, `angle` (degrees, to) or `by` (degrees, added; counterclockwise), `about`? (`{x, y}` in inches, with `by`: the point each pin turns about), `page`?, `snap`? | `{page, rotated, locked?}`: how many were turned (**ROTATE-1D-1**) and the shapes a `LockRotate` left alone. An angle a shape has already, and a connector with both ends glued, are not counted; when none is, the command is no undo step |
| `shape.flip` | `ids`, `axis` (`horizontal` or `vertical`), `page`? | `{page, flipped}` |
| `shape.order` | `ids`, `to` (`front`, `back`, `forward`, `backward`), `page`? | `{page, moved}`: how many of the shapes named changed place |
| `shape.align` | `ids` (the first is the one the others move to), `how` (`left`, `center`, `right`, `top`, `middle`, `bottom`), `page`? | `{page, moved}` |
| `shape.delete` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, deleted}`: the number of shapes named, an `ID` named twice counted once |
| `edit.copy` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, copied}`: puts copies of the shapes, and everything inside them, on the session's clipboard. The document is not changed and the call is no undo step |
| `edit.cut` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, cut}`: copy, then the work of `shape.delete`, one undo step |
| `edit.paste` | `page`?, `x`?, `y`? | `{page, ids, created}`, and `notes` (words) when the paste left something out (a master it put back kept references to parts of its file beside its shapes, which its new part does not have): the clipboard's shapes on the page with new `ID`s, on top; `ids` are the shapes pasted, not their members, and `created` counts members too. With no `x` and `y` they land a step right and down of the originals; with them, the middle of the copies lands there (both or neither). Not enabled while the clipboard is empty |
| `edit.find` (query) | `what` (up to 1,024 characters, with `^t`, `^l`, `^-`, `^?`, `^^`), `scope`? (`selection`, `page` or `all`, default `page`), `page`?, `ids`? (the shapes selected, for the scope `selection`), `in`? (any of `text`, `shape_data`, `shape_name`, `user_cell`, default `["text"]`), `match_case`?, `whole_words`?, `after`? (`{page, id, place, row?, offset}`), `wrap`? (default true), `limit`? (1 to 10,000, default 100) | `{matches: [{page, id, place, row?, start, end, shape_name?, context}], truncated, wrapped, shapes}`: Find, in document order, from the place `after` names and going round to the start of the scope; a query, not journaled, no undo step: see **Find and Replace** |
| `edit.replace` | `what`, `with` (empty takes the matches out), `scope`?, `page`?, `ids`?, `in`?, `match_case`?, `whole_words`? as `edit.find`, `at`? (`{page, id, place, row?, start, end}`: only that match) | `{replaced, shapes, pages, skipped: [{page, id, place, row?, start, end, why}], skipped_total, locked, next?}`: Replace All as one undo step (`Replace` for one match, and `next` is where to go on from); a match in a field's result, in a cell a formula works out, under `GUARD` or whose result is not allowed there is left and listed, and so is every match of a shape on a locked layer, counted in `locked` too: see **Find and Replace** |
| `shape.duplicate` | `ids`, `page`?, `dx`?, `dy`? | `{page, ids, created}`, and `notes` as `edit.paste` has them: copy and paste on the same page in one step, `dx` right and `dy` up from the originals (0.25 and -0.25 by default); the clipboard is not used |
| `shape.group` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, id, members}`: a new group shape whose frame is the box that holds the shapes, which keep drawing where they did; an instance among them is given a local copy of its master's geometry and holds its width, height and pin as formulas of the group |
| `shape.ungroup` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, ungrouped, ids}`: each shape named that holds members is taken apart, one level; `ids` are the members, which keep drawing where they did. When none is a group the call is no undo step |
| `shape.change` | `ids`, `master`, `stencil`?, `page`? | `{page, master, name, changed, unchanged, skipped: [{id, why}], glue: [{connector, end, shape, glued, point?}]}`: the shapes are swapped for the master and keep their `ID`s, place in the drawing order, position, size, text, own formatting, shape data and glue (see **Change Shape** below); one undo step, none when nothing changed |
| `shape.union`, `shape.combine`, `shape.fragment`, `shape.intersect`, `shape.subtract` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, ids, deleted, kept}`: the shape operations of the Developer tab's Operations group, each one undo step, on `craft-pathops`: see **Shape operations** below |
| `shape.set_text` | `id`, `text` (empty takes the text away), `start`?, `end`?, `page`? | `{page, id}`. With `start` and `end` only those characters are replaced: see **Text** |
| `connector.add` | `from`, `to`: each `{id, point?}` or `{x, y}`; `page`?, `snap`? | `{page, id, begin, end}`, each of `begin` and `end` `{id, point}` or `null` |
| `connector.reconnect` | `id`, `end` (`begin` or `end`), `target`: `{id, point?}` or `{x, y}`; `page`?, `snap`? | as `connector.add` |
| `connector.autoconnect` | `from`, `direction` (`right`, `left`, `up`, `down`), `master`? and `stencil`? (as `shape.drop` takes them; left out, a copy of the shape), `existing`? (true: join the nearest neighbour in that direction, a copy when there is none), `to`? and `to_point`? (a shape to join, and the connection point of it to glue to), `page`? | `{page, from, direction, created, id, connector, begin, end, name, width, height, x, y}`: with a shape made (`created` true), a new shape beside `from`, and with `existing` or `to` the shape that was there (`created` false, `name` null), and in either case a connector glued from the point of `from` that faces that way to a point of the other shape, one undo step: see **AutoConnect** |
| `connector.neighbour` (query) | `from`, `direction`, `page`? | `{page, from, direction, neighbour}`: the shape a click on that arrow would join (`null` when it would make a copy); writes nothing |
| `autoconnect.settings` (query) | none | `{on, held}`: whether AutoConnect is on for the drawing (it is, for one that holds nothing), and what the drawing holds (`false`, or `null` for nothing) |
| `autoconnect.set` | `on` (boolean) | The settings as `autoconnect.settings` gives them. The drawing is changed (unsaved) and **no undo step is made** and no revision moves. Nothing changes when the box is as asked. Off is written into the drawing's extension data and on takes it out; refused (`failed`, nothing changed) for a drawing whose extension data is of a version this build does not change |
| `connector.style` | `style` (`right_angle`, `straight`, `curved`), `ids`?, `page`? | `{page, style, styled, default_changed}`: the style of the connectors named, which are routed again, or, with no `ids`, the page's default for new connectors: see **Connector styles** |
| `connector.edit_path` | `id`, `leg`, `to` (inches), `page`?, `snap`? (`true` or `{zoom, view}`) | `{page, id, changed}`, and with `snap` also `to` (the line the leg went to, inches) and `snap`: moves one leg of a right-angle route to a new line, by hand, and fixes the route: see **A path edited by hand** and **Snapping a path edited by hand** |
| `connector.reroute` | `ids`, `mode` (`freely`, `as_needed`, `never` or `on_crossover`), `page`? | `{page, mode, changed}`: how many connectors changed. Writes `ConFixedCode` 0, 1, 2 or 3 locally, as one undo step, and routes nothing: see **Reroute** below; a mode that is not one, a shape that is not a connector or one that is not there is refused in words and nothing changes |
| `connector.reset` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, reset}`: Reset Connector, the routes the styles give, now: see **A path edited by hand** |
| `connector.line_jumps` | any of `show`, `code`, `style`, `dir_x`, `dir_y`, `factor_x`, `factor_y`; `ids`?, `page`? | `{page, changed}`: the page's line jump cells (or the own code, style and sides of the connectors named) and the jumps of every connector on the page again: see **Line jumps** |
| `shape.format` | `ids`, `page`?, and any of `fill`, `line` (`#RRGGBB`, `none`, a colour of the page's theme as `theme:accent2` or with a shade `theme:accent2:40`, or `default`), `weight` (points), `pattern` (0 to 23), `begin_arrow`, `end_arrow` (0 to 45), `begin_arrow_size`, `end_arrow_size` (0 to 6), `rounding` (inches), `fill_transparency`, `line_transparency` (percent), `fill_pattern` (1 to 24), `fill_background`, `line_cap`, `compound`: see **Transparency, patterns, gradients, shadows and Quick Styles** | `{page, formatted}`: see **Formatting** |
| `text.format` | `ids`, `page`?, `start`?, `end`?, and any of `font`, `size` (points), `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, `strike`, `color` (`#RRGGBB`, `theme:accent2:40` or `default`), `align` (`left`, `center`, `right`, `justify`, `distributed`), `valign`, `bullet` (0 to 7), `indent` (`increase`, `decrease`), `case` (`normal`, `all_caps`, `initial_caps`, `small_caps`), `direction` (`ltr`, `rtl`), `vertical` | `{page, formatted}`: see **Formatting** |
| `shape.formatting` (query) | `ids`, `page`?, `start`?, `end`? | `{page, shapes: [{id, fill, line, weight, pattern, begin_arrow, end_arrow, begin_arrow_size, end_arrow_size, rounding, font, size, bold, italic, underline, strike, color, align, valign, bullet, ind_left, case, direction, vertical, fill_pattern, fill_transparency, fill_background, line_transparency, line_cap, compound, gradient, line_gradient, shadow}]}` (`font` is the name of the face the text is drawn in and never `null`). With `start` and `end` (one shape, characters as `text.format` counts them) the character properties, `align`, `bullet`, `case` and `direction` are those of the characters in the range, each the one value its runs share or `"mixed"`, and the entry also holds `mixed`, the list of those: see **What a range shows** below |
| `shape.gradient` | `ids`, `page`?, `target`?, `on`?, `kind`? with `origin`?, or `direction`?, `angle`?, `rotate_with_shape`?, `stops`? | `{page, target, formatted}`: a fill's or a line's gradient: see **Transparency, patterns, gradients, shadows and Quick Styles** |
| `shape.shadow` | `ids`, `page`?, `on`?, `preset`?, `color`?, `transparency`?, `size`?, `blur`?, `angle`?, `distance`? | `{page, formatted}`: the shadow: see the same section |
| `shape.quick_style` | `ids`, `page`?, `row` and `column`, or `variant` | `{page, formatted, matrix, colour}`: a Quick Style from the page's theme: see the same section |
| `shape.quick_styles` (query) | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, current, variants, styles}`: the 46 tiles of the gallery for the first shape |
| `text.box` | `page`, `x`, `y` (where the centre goes), `width`? (1), `height`? (0.25), `text`? | `{page, id, width, height}`, the size in inches: a box of text that is a shape with no master; see **Text boxes and the text block** |
| `text.block` (query) | `id`, `page`? | `{page, id, corners, pin, width, height, angle, direction}`: where the shape's text block is on the page, in inches (`corners` in the block's own order, (0, 0) first; `angle` is `TxtAngle` and `direction` the way the text runs, both in degrees) |
| `text.block_move` | `id`, `dx`, `dy` (a distance on the page), `page`? | `{page, id, changed}` |
| `text.block_resize` | `id`, `x`, `y` (where the block's corner at (0, 0) of its own frame goes), `width`, `height` (along the block's own axes), `page`? | `{page, id, changed}` |
| `text.block_rotate` | `id`, `angle` (degrees, the cell `TxtAngle`) or `by` (degrees on the page, counterclockwise), `page`? | `{page, id, changed}` |
| `shape.draw` | `page`, `tool` (`rectangle`, `ellipse`, `line`, `freeform`, `arc`, `pencil`), `points` (`[x, y]` pairs in inches from the page's left and bottom edges, at most 5,000), `shift`? (false), `tolerance`? (0.03125 inch), `smoothing`? (0.4), `continue`? (the `id` of a shape whose open path goes on), `close`? (false) | `{page, id, tool, rows, closed, continued, width, height}`, the size in inches: a shape with real geometry rows, or a path carried on from its end vertex; see **The drawing tools**. One undo step, labelled for the tool (`Draw rectangle`, `Draw with pencil`) |
| `shape.close` | `id`, `page`? | `{page, id, rows, closed, width, height}`: the open path of the shape is closed with a straight segment from its end vertex to its first, and takes the fill. One undo step, `Close shape` |
| `shape.pin` | `ids`, `at` (one of the nine places `top-left` to `bottom-right`, or `{x, y}` in inches in the shape's own frame), `page`? | `{page, pinned}`: how many shapes changed. The shape stays where it is and its `PinX`, `PinY` follow |
| `shape.place` | `ids`, `x`? and `y`? (inches on the page, the place of the pin; at least one), `page`?, `snap`? | `{page, moved, unplaced}`: how many shapes moved, and how many were left for having no place to move from or to. Each shape named gets its pin at the place, as `shape.move` would move it there: a member of a group is placed on the page (and one named with its group ends at the place as the group does), glued connectors follow, and **a line moves only its ends that are not glued, so a line with one end glued has its free end go the distance its pin was to go and its pin goes half as far** (a line from 0 to 2 with its begin glued, placed at x = 10, runs from 0 to 11 and its pin is at 5.5); one with both ends glued does not move |
| `shape.size` | `ids`, `width`? and `height`? (inches along each shape's own axes; at least one), `page`? | `{page, sized}`: how many changed. Each shape keeps its pin; a group is sized as one shape and its members follow |
| `shape.set_end` | `id`, `end` (`begin` or `end`), `x`? and `y`? (inches on the page; at least one), `page`? | `{page, id, changed}`: an end of a line or connector put at a place; an end that is glued is refused |
| `shape.set_length` | `id`, `length` (inches, more than 0), `page`? | `{page, id, changed}`: the end that is not glued moves along the line |
| `shape.placement` (query) | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, units_per_inch, shapes: [{id, name, one_d, group, x, y, width, height, angle, pin: {x, y}, pin_at, flip_x, flip_y, begin?, end?, length?}]}`: what the Size & Position window shows |
| `snap.settings`, `snap.set`, `snap.at`, `guide.add`, `guide.move`, `guide.delete`, `guide.list` | see **Snap, grid and guides** below | the document's Snap and Glue settings and its six snap strengths, where a place or a box would snap to, and guides |
| `window.settings` (query), `window.settings.set` | see **Window settings** below | what the drawing's window shows (the rulers, the grid, the guides, the connection points), held in the drawing and kept by every save |

`point` is a connection point's number counted **from 0** (the row's `IX`, so `ToPart` is 100 plus it); left out, the nearest is taken. An
optional `page` is an addition to the plan's table, which has none on `shape.move`, `shape.resize`, `shape.delete`, `shape.set_text` and
`connector.*`: shape `ID`s repeat across pages, and the engine has no current page. `connector.reconnect`'s `target` is the plan's
"target as above". The ids are dotted words that may hold `_` (`shape.set_text`).

For a window: `diacraft_engine::draw::shape_frame(doc, page, id)` gives a shape's box (in the frame of the shape's parent, which is the page's for a
shape that is not in a group; **as it would be unturned and unflipped**, with its `angle`, `flip_x` and `flip_y`), `draw::shape_corners(doc, page, id)` gives the four corners of the box **on
the page**, in the order of the shape's own frame ((0, 0) first: the corner `shape.resize` takes), and `draw::connection_points(doc, page, id)` gives each
connection point's `index`, its place on the page and the way a connector leaves it, which is what to highlight under the pointer.

For a window: `Session::clipboard()` gives what `edit.copy` or `edit.cut` put aside (`Clipboard`: `len`, `shape_count`, `source_page`, `bounds` and `pastes`), or `None`; `edit.paste` is listed as not enabled
while it is empty, so a menu can grey Paste from `Session::commands()`. The window's own work ("Clipboard, duplicate and group from the window" below) is the system clipboard (text for another program) and the keys:
Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+V, Ctrl+D (ours: the keyboard page names it for the web only), Ctrl+Shift+G, Ctrl+Shift+U, and Ctrl-drag, which sends `shape.duplicate` with the
drag's distance. The commands take a shape's `ID` on the page they name; the page defaults to the first
page that holds all of them, and for `edit.paste` to the page the shapes were copied from.

For a window: `diacraft_engine::draw::connector_path(doc, page, id)` gives a connector's route for handles (`ConnectorPath`: the corners on the page, the style, whether the path was edited by hand
and whether its legs can be moved), `draw::autoconnect_centre(doc, page, from, dir, size)` where an arrow's shape of that size would go (the preview), `draw::line_jump_settings(doc, page)` the page's jump cells, and
`draw::page_style(doc, page)` the page's default connector style. The window sends `connector.autoconnect`, `connector.edit_path`, `connector.reroute`, `connector.reset`, `connector.style` and `connector.line_jumps`.
The connector path work adds `draw::moved_route(points, leg, to)` (the route a leg moved makes, which `connector.edit_path` writes: the window draws it while the leg is dragged), `draw::leg_subject(points, k, leg, to)` (the place and the axis the snap of a leg looks at,
which the window's preview and the command both take), `draw::connector_reroute(doc, page, id)` and `draw::Reroute` (the four settings with their codes and words).

## Text

A shape's text is kept in parts: the characters, the `cp`, `pp` and `tp` markers that start a run, a paragraph and a tab
list, and the `fld` fields with the text they display. The markers and the rows they name must stay in step, so an edit of the text changes the
characters and nothing else: typing over `cd` to give `ce` in `<cp IX="0"/>ab<cp IX="1"/>cd<fld IX="0">3</fld>` saves as
`<cp IX="0"/>ab<cp IX="1"/>ce<fld IX="0">3</fld>`.

* **The editable text** of a shape is its characters, the `Chars` parts end to end. A field is not in it (a field is not typed over; its result is
  the field's) and neither is a marker. A **range** `start` to `end` is counted in **characters** (Unicode scalar values, not bytes) of the editable
  text from 0; `end` is not in the range, and `start == end` is a point where characters are inserted.
* **`shape.set_text {id, text, start, end, page?}`** replaces the characters of the range with `text`. Both `start` and `end` are given, or
  neither; `start` is at most `end`, and `end` at most the number of characters; the result is at most 100,000 characters. A range the text does not
  have is a `BadParams` error and changes nothing. `text` may be empty (a range taken away). A call that changes nothing (a point and no text) is
  no undo step.
* Our rule (no specification says what typing does to a run): a range edit **never removes or moves a marker or a field**, whatever the
  range covers; a range that holds a field leaves it where it is, with the characters of the range gone from before and after it. The new
  characters join **the run of the first character replaced**; at a point they join **the run of the character before it** (so what is typed at the
  end of a run is that run's, and not the next one's), and at the start of the text the run of the first character. A part left with no characters
  is taken out and its markers stay, so the next characters typed after the marker are that run's own. A text with no characters yet takes them
  after its leading markers and ahead of any field; a shape with no text gets one run, as the whole-text form makes.
* **Without `start` and `end`** the command is the whole-text form it was: `text` is the shape's text, as one run, and the markers and fields go
  with the old text. The exception is a `text` that is what the shape shows already (its characters with the results of its fields in place):
  nothing is touched, and it is no undo step, since replacing it would only lose the runs and fields of text that did not change.
  Empty text takes the text away.
* **`text` is checked as XML must have it**: the controls other than a line break and a tab, and U+FFFE and U+FFFF, are `BadParams` (a save would
  otherwise fail naming only the page).
* **After the edit** the cells that read the text (`SHAPETEXT(TheText)`, `TEXTWIDTH(TheText)`, `Sheet.4!TheText`) and what reads those are worked
  out again, and no other cell: for this the text is a cell of the shape called `TheText`, and `calc` follows its readers as it follows those of any cell.
  A text edit is not compared with an earlier document, so a page `calc` cannot compare (two shapes with one `ID` in one list, where
  `changes_between` falls back to the whole page without a word) is not worked out whole for it.

**The window does this ** (`crates/ui-egui/src/edit/text.rs`): the box holds `text_view`'s characters, draws each field under them as a chip that takes no key and no
pointer (the field's result, with "Fields:" before them, and a hover text saying it is not typed over), and sends `changed_range(old, new)` when the edit ends,
with `old` read from the shape at that moment. The rules the window adds are and
Under "Choices that no specification settles (ours)" below.

For a window: `diacraft_engine::draw::text_view(doc, page, id)` gives `TextView { chars, fields }`: the editable text as a `String`, and each
field as `TextField { at, ix, text }`, where `at` is the number of characters before it. The text box shows `chars` and shows the fields as
something that cannot be edited. To commit, take the box's text `new` and the shape's `chars` `old`, find the longest common prefix of `p`
characters and then the longest common suffix of `q` characters of what is left (`p + q` at most the shorter of the two lengths), and send
`shape.set_text {id, text: new[p .. len(new) - q], start: p, end: len(old) - q}`; when nothing differs, send nothing. The same is
`draw::set_text_range(doc, page, id, start, end, text)` inside `Session::edit_if` (→ whether the text changed); the commands go through it.

## Text boxes and the text block

The engine side: the commands the window's Text tool and Text Block tool are made from. The reference app was not inspected: every rule here that a specification does
not give is DiaCraft's own and is in the check list.

* **`text.box {page, x, y, width?, height?, text?}`** adds a text box centred at (`x`, `y`) (paper inches, as `shape.drop` places a shape). Without a size it is **1 inch
  by 0.25 inch**, the size of the text boxes in files; a drag gives a centre and a size. `text` is what `shape.set_text` accepts (the controls other than a line break
  and a tab, and U+FFFE and U+FFFF, are `BadParams`; at most 100,000 characters). The box goes on top, one undo step "Add text box", and its `ID` is above every `ID` the
  page has held. With Auto Size on, a box put past the page's edge grows the page by whole sheets in the same undo step, as `shape.drop` does (`draw::fit_page`). The block commands do not call it: Auto Size is specified for a shape put or moved past the edge, no specification says a text block past the edge grows the page, and they move no shape's box. A size that is not more than 0, a place or size more than a million drawing units from the origin, and a full page are refused and change nothing.
* Our rule (no specification gives a text box's cells). A text box is **a shape with no master**, holding its own cells. The Text tool's grade says it makes "a box
  of text independent of any shape", files hold shapes with no master as a matter of course (126,699 nameless page shapes with no master at any
  depth; every one of the 135,527 page shapes with no master carries its style links), and a master would put a
  master in every drawing that no stencil holds. The cells are those of a shape made from a master (the pose cells, `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` as the formulas written on creation),
  the seven text-block cells with the formulas a public page prints (`Width*1`, `Width*0.5` and the rest), and links to the root style
  So its text is the root's, 12 point black in the middle of the block. It takes **fill, line and effects** as the grade says: one `Geometry` section
  holds a rectangle that fills the box (`NoFill` 0, `NoLine` 0), and the shape holds `FillPattern` 0 and `LinePattern` 0, so that it paints nothing until a fill or a line is
  chosen, and then `shape.format` makes the pattern solid. It has the Rectangle master's four connection points, so a connector can be glued to it. It has no
  name (`NAME()` answers `Sheet.<id>`). It does not grow with its text (no cell of it reads `TEXTHEIGHT` or `TEXTWIDTH`), so text longer than the
  box wraps to the box's width and spills out of it, centred and not clipped, until a person sizes the box (`shape.resize`, or the Text Block tool's handles).
* **`text.block {id, page?}`** (a query) says where the text block of any shape is: `corners`, the four corners of the block on the page in inches in the order of the
  block's own frame ((0, 0), (`width`, 0), (`width`, `height`), (0, `height`)), `pin` (the block's pin on the page, the point it turns about), `width` and `height` (the block's
  own, in inches), `angle` (the cell `TxtAngle`, degrees) and `direction` (the way the text runs on the page, degrees: the net angle, `Angle` plus `TxtAngle`, or minus it
  under one flip). A cell found nowhere has the value, so a shape with no text cells has a block the size of the shape.
* **The block's frame** is the specification's: subtract the local pin, negate x under `FlipX` and y under `FlipY`, turn by `TxtAngle`, add the pin. `draw::text_block_place(doc, page, id)` gives
  the same as the query, in the page's drawing units (`BlockPlace`).
* Our rule (the seven travel as a set, with no partial case in files). A shape with **none** of the seven cells, on itself, its master or a style, is
  given all seven before the first is changed, with the formulas and their values; a shape that has any keeps what it has, and an instance whose master holds them holds
  only the ones it changed.
* Our rule ("moves ... the text area apart from the shape, which then keeps that offset"). `text.block_move` moves the block by a distance on the page. The distance is
  carried into the shape's frame (down through the groups the shape is in, then the shape's turn and flips) and `TxtPinX` and `TxtPinY` change by it; a coordinate that does not
  change is not written and keeps its formula, and a written one is a value of its own (`BOUND` and `SETATREF` keep themselves). So the block moves with the
  shape when the shape moves, and a later resize of the shape leaves a pin that is a value where it was. A cell a `GUARD` protects is a `Failed` error and nothing changes. A distance of
  nothing is no undo step (`changed` false).
* Our rule. `text.block_resize` gives the block a size along its own axes with its corner at (0, 0) of its own frame at a point of the page.
  `TxtWidth` and `TxtHeight` are written; the local pin is worked out again for the new size (a pin at the middle stays at the middle while `TxtLocPinX` is `TxtWidth*0.5`; one that is a
  value stays where it is), and `TxtPinX` and `TxtPinY` are the corner carried by the block's turn and the shape's flips, so the corner lands there whatever the shape's angle, flips and
  `TxtAngle`. The shape, `TxtAngle` and the local pin's cells are not written. The text wraps to the block's new width. A block that has the size and the place already is
  no undo step.
* Our rule (gives the angle, the tool). `text.block_rotate` turns the block **about its pin**, which stays where it is: only
  `TxtAngle` is written. `by` is a turn on the page, counterclockwise: the cell changes by it, or by its negative when the shape's picture on the page is a mirror image (an odd number of
  the shape and its groups flipped once: the net angle is `Angle` − `TxtAngle` under one flip), so the text turns as asked and never mirrors. `angle` sets the cell, kept from just over
  -180 up to 180 degrees (as the rotate rule keeps `Angle`), a quarter turn exact, whole turns no turn. A formula in `TxtAngle` (the `-Angle` that keeps text upright) is replaced by the
  value, as any write replaces a formula; a `GUARD` (files often guard it) is a `Failed` error. The Home tab's Rotate Text 90 degrees button is `text.block_rotate` with `by` 90: no
  desktop page says the direction or the steps it takes, so a quarter turn counterclockwise on the page is ours.
* **Connectors and labels.** The cells of a connector's label are the cells of any shape's text block, and the commands write them. They are recalculated with what reads them and nothing else is
  done: no route, no glue and no label is placed again. A label moved or turned here stays until its connector is next routed (a move of a shape it is glued to, `connector.reconnect`), and then
  The label rule puts it back on the middle leg, upright. The text of a member of a group that is flipped is not mirrored (ours): the renderer and these commands cancel the flips of
  the groups above a shape in its text block, about the block's local pin, as the shape's own flip is cancelled, so the block's corners and `direction` are where the text is drawn.

**For a window** (the Text tool and the Text Block tool): the Text tool sends `text.box` with the centre and size of the click (the default size) or of the drag, and `text` when it has the
characters (a box sent empty and left empty would be one more shape for the person to delete; the box can be opened for typing before it is sent, as the text box of a label is). The Text Block tool
reads `text.block` (or `draw::text_block_place`) for the four corners and the pin to draw its rectangle, handles and rotation handle, and sends `text.block_move` with the drag's distance,
`text.block_resize` with the corner that stays and the size, `text.block_rotate` with `by` (the change of the net angle: `direction` before and after), all on release, one undo step each.

## The drawing tools

The engine side of Home, Tools, Drawing Tools: the Rectangle, Ellipse, Line, Freeform, Arc and Pencil tools.
 The reference app was not inspected: every rule here that a specification does not give is DiaCraft's own and is in the check list.

* **`shape.draw {page, tool, points, shift?, tolerance?, smoothing?, continue?, close?}`** makes a shape from the points of a drag, in paper inches on the page (the page's drawing scale is turned at the edge, as for
  `shape.drop`). The Rectangle, Ellipse, Line and Arc tools read the **first and the last point** (the two ends of a drag: a rectangle's and an ellipse's opposite corners, a line's and an arc's ends); the Pencil and
  the Freeform tool read the **whole stroke**. At most **5,000 points** (counted before any is read); a point that is not a number, one more than a million drawing units from the origin, a stroke that is one spot, a
  rectangle, ellipse, line or arc under a thousandth of an inch, and a `tolerance` or `smoothing` out of range are refused in words and change nothing. The shape goes on top (as `text.box` does), its `ID` is above every `ID`
  the page has held, a page with a theme gives it the theme in the same undo step and a page with Auto Size on grows to take it in (`draw::fit_page`), one undo step labelled for the tool.
* Our rule (no specification gives the cells of a shape a tool draws). A drawn shape is **a shape with no master**, as a text box is (why). Its frame is the box round what it draws: `PinX` and
  `PinY` the middle, `Width` and `Height` its size, `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` the formulas `Width*0.5` and `Height*0.5`, `Angle`, `FlipX`, `FlipY` and `ResizeMode` 0 and the seven cells of the text block
  formulas, so that it holds text and is resized, turned and flipped as any shape is. **Its one `Geometry` section holds the tool's rows, each coordinate a share of the box** (`Width*0.25`, the constant 0 for a share of 0,
  the bare `Width` or `Height` for 1, the constant 0 where the box is flat: the way `shape.combine` and a master write rows, `draw/shapeops/carry.rs`), so a resize takes the path with the box. **`NoFill` is 1 for an open
  path and 0 for a closed one**: "an open path shows the page through it and cannot take a fill; rectangle and ellipse are closed from the start"; the other four flags
  are 0. The shape is **linked to the root style**, so a closed shape takes the style's fill and every shape its line ("a closed shape takes the default fill; an open one has line only"), and
  holds no fill or line cell of its own, so `shape.format` writes them as it does for any shape. The Rectangle and the Ellipse have the **four middles of their sides** as connection points, as the masters of those shapes
  have; an open path has none (a connector is glued to such a shape as a whole). A drawn shape has no name (`NAME()` answers `Sheet.<id>`).
* The rows are the Rectangle master's. A `MoveTo` to the lower left corner and four `LineTo` rows round and back to it, closed. **Shift** makes a square: the side is the longer of the two the pointer
  went, on the side of the press the pointer is on (the tooltip: "hold Shift for a square"). One `Ellipse` row: the centre, the end of the horizontal axis and the end of the vertical one,
  as the shares `Width*0.5`, `Width` and `Height`, closed. **Shift** makes a circle (only the Rectangle's tooltip names Shift, so the circle is the usual reading and ours).
* A `MoveTo` and a `LineTo`, open. A level or upright line has a box of no height or no width: its rows are constants where there is nothing to be a share of, and the shape is a 2-D shape
  whose box is flat (it is not a 1-D shape: a path goes on from its end by the same shape).
* The curve is ours, no specification gives the Arc tool's. A `MoveTo` and one `ArcTo`: **a quarter of a circle** between the two points (`A` is 0.2071 of the chord, `tan(22.5 degrees) / 2`, so
  the sweep `4 atan(2A / chord)` is 90 degrees), **bowed to the left of the way the pointer went** (`A` negative: a positive bow bulges to the right), so a drag from the left to the right arches over. The box is the
  box round the arc as the geometry crate builds it.
* Our rule, but for the Rectangle's tooltip. **Shift holds the Line, the Arc's chord and a Pencil or Freeform stroke to a step of 45 degrees**: the direction of the pointer from the press is turned to the nearest
  step and the length the pointer went along it is kept (so the end is never farther than the pointer), a stroke being the segment from where it began to where it ended. Asks whether Shift
  on a Line is an angle step; the specification does not say.
* The brief: "lines and arcs from the stroke"; the fit is ours. The stroke becomes `LineTo` and `ArcTo` rows, the path a person then edits by its vertices. VectorCraft's pencil and brush fit chains of cubic
  Beziers, which is the Freeform tool's work, so this has a small fit of its own. **It is greedy and longest first**: from the start of a run it finds the farthest point of the stroke a straight line
  fits to within the tolerance and the farthest one an arc fits to (by doubling and halving), and the longer of the two is the next segment, a line when they tie. A line fits when no point of the run is more than the tolerance
  from the segment. An arc fits when the circle through its two ends and the point farthest from the chord has no point of the run more than the tolerance from it, the points go round it one way and do not turn back (a step back
  within the tolerance is noise), and **the sweep is at most 300 degrees**, so a whole circle is two arcs. Its `A` is worked out from the circle it, **positive to the right of the way the pencil went**
  So a stroke counterclockwise is a positive bow. The tolerance is `tolerance` (default **one thirty-second of an inch**; the window sends three screen points). **At most 256 rows**: a scribble of more is
  refused in words and not cut into hundreds. A stroke whose last point is its first (`close`, below) is closed.
* The brief: "a smoothed spline (NURBS)". The stroke is thinned (a point is kept when it is half the tolerance from the last), made a polyline and handed to
  **`craft-pathops`' `simplify_with`** (VectorCraft's pencil and brush use the same library: a least-squares Bezier refit that keeps the corners sharper than a threshold, and with a smoothing takes the jitter out first); what
  comes back is a chain of cubics. **The corner threshold is 60 degrees** (a sharper turn stays a corner: a joint of the composite with no tangent in common) and **`smoothing`** (0 to 1, default 0.4) is the spread of a Gaussian
  along the stroke, **a quarter of an inch at 1**, applied before the refit; the tolerance is how far the curve may stand from the (smoothed) stroke. The chain is written as **one `NURBSTo` row, degree 3, whose knots are those
  of a composite Bezier** (0 four times, then each whole number up to `n - 1` three times, then `n`, the other three copies of the last knot implied, the form `Page.DrawBezier` stores), every weight 1, the
  control points as shares of the box. The geometry crate reads it back as the chain it was made of. The curve begins and ends on the stroke's first and last points to the last digit. **At most 2,000 cubics**.
  Two points are a straight segment, a `MoveTo` and a `LineTo`. Neither the smoothing nor the tolerance is a setting any specification gives (the Draw tab is the ink tab); they are parameters here and, in the window, 3 screen
  points and `window.freeformSmoothing`.
* ("drawing the next segment from that vertex joins them"; "a further drag starting at the last vertex joins a new segment so all act as one shape; tools
  can be switched between segments; undo removes the last one"). **`continue: id`** (the Line, Arc, Freeform and Pencil tools) carries on the **open path of a shape** from its end vertex: the shape must be on the page itself (not in
  a group), have no master, have one `Geometry` section of a single run (a `MoveTo` first) that ends at a vertex and is not closed; a shape that is not is refused in words (`the path of shape 1 is closed`, `an instance of a master`,
  `is in a group`, `has 2 paths`). **The first point of the stroke is taken to be the end vertex**, whatever it was, so the two join to the last digit; the stroke is fitted as a new one is and its rows are added without the
  move. The rows of the shape are carried exactly (a row keeps its kind: `shape.combine`'s `carry`) and the whole path is written again as the shares of its new box, in the shape's own frame (the page's points through the shape's
  pose, an arc's bow turned the other way in a mirrored frame), so **tools are mixed in one shape** and **a shape that is turned or flipped goes on where the person drew**. The box grows to take the new path and the pin moves
  with `shape.subtract`'s rule, so nothing drawn moves on the page. One undo step per segment: Ctrl+Z takes them away in reverse order. A path that has more than 4,096 rows is not carried on. `continue` does not take a
  Rectangle or an Ellipse (a refusal that names the tool), and a closed path has no end vertex (the Rectangle's rows come back to the first corner).
* "dragging the last end over the first vertex closes the shape (it becomes filled)". **`close: true`** puts the last point of the stroke on the first vertex of the path (the stroke's own for a fresh Pencil
  or Freeform stroke, the shape's for `continue`) and the path is closed: it ends where it began and `NoFill` is 0. The window sends it when the pointer is let go within reach of that vertex. **`shape.close {id}`** closes an
  open path with a straight segment from its end vertex to its first (Draw, Create, Close Shape: `InkShapeClose` "closes an ink outline into a shape", a reading); a closed path and an ellipse are refused.
* **What it costs.** The points are counted before any is read; a stroke is thinned and fitted in a number of steps that is at most a few times its points (the Pencil's runs are found by doubling and halving, the Freeform's by the
  path library under its own counted limits); a path carried on reads at most 4,096 rows; refusals are in words, and none of this panics. `crates/engine/tests/it/draw_tools.rs` fits the most points with each tool.
* **Saved and reopened.** The shape is a shape of rows and cells, so it is kept by `.diacraft`, by the VSDX writers and by the `.odg` writers as any shape is (`tests/it/draw_tools.rs`: each tool's shape is saved in all three
  and read again, with its rows and what it draws, and a path goes on from the end of a shape that was read from the file). The `.odg` of a shape holds the rows in DiaCraft's extension; LibreOffice shows what it can of them.

### The window's drawing tools

| Control | Sends |
|---|---|
| Home, Tools, Drawing Tools (Rectangle, Ellipse, Line, Freeform, Arc, Pencil; Ctrl+8, Ctrl+9, Ctrl+6, Ctrl+5, Ctrl+7, Ctrl+4; also Developer, Shape Design, Drawing Tools) | the window's `tool.rectangle`, `tool.ellipse`, `tool.line`, `tool.freeform`, `tool.arc` and `tool.pencil`: the tool is a mode that stays on until the Pointer Tool (Ctrl+1) or Esc. The button shows the tool used last |
| a drag with a drawing tool, released | `shape.draw {page, tool, points, shift, tolerance, smoothing}` and, for a path that goes on, `continue` (and for one that closes, `close`): paper inches, one undo step; the shape drawn is selected |
| Draw, Create, Close Shape | `draw.closeShape`: `shape.close {id, page}` for each shape selected (one `command.batch`, one undo step), quiet with nothing selected |
| `window.freeformSmoothing {percent}` | the window's own Freeform smoothing, 40 at first, not saved |

* **The preview is the engine's.** While the button is down the window asks `diacraft_engine::draw::pen::outline` what the release would make, from the same points and settings it will send, so the outline painted is the
  shape drawn: a rectangle, an ellipse, a line, an arc, the lines and arcs of a pencil stroke, the curve of a freeform one, lightly filled when it closes. A stroke of more than 1,500 points is painted as the pointer made it and
  fitted when the button comes up. Nothing is sent while the button is down, and Esc drops the stroke.
* **What a drag reads.** The Rectangle, Ellipse, Line and Arc read the press and the pointer: the start is on the grid, the guides and the other snap targets as the engine's snap says (`land_place`, as the Text tool's box does),
  and so is the end unless Shift is held (a square, a circle or a segment at a step of 45 degrees, which the grid would undo); Alt turns the snap off. The Pencil and the Freeform tool read every place the pointer went through in
  a frame (egui's `PointerMoved` events), at least two screen points from the one before, at most 4,999. The tolerance sent is **three screen points** in inches at the zoom, and the Freeform's smoothing is the window's.
* **A path goes on** when a tool that goes on (Line, Arc, Freeform, Pencil) is on and **one shape is selected whose path `pen::continuable` says can be carried on**: a press within the reach of a handle of its end vertex is a
  stroke from it (`continue`). The two ends of such a path are marked while one of these tools is on (a filled mark at the end, a ring at the start). A stroke let go within reach of the first vertex of the path it goes on from, or
  a fresh Pencil or Freeform stroke that went out and came back to where it began, is sent with `close`; the ring is lit and `window_state.drawing.closing` is true while it would. A press and release that did not move draw nothing.
* **The cursor** is the crosshair for every drawing tool.
* **Left**: Edit vertices (the Pencil's drag of a vertex, Ctrl+click to add one and Delete to remove one, a P1 row); the Drawing Tools button is a menu whose face shows the last tool and not a
  split button that runs it; a path goes on from its end vertex only, not its first; the Freeform smoothing and tolerance have no Options page (they are the window command and three screen points); the Draw tab's ink controls
  (Select, Touch Drawing, Eraser, Pens, Color, Thickness, Auto-Create Shapes, Ink to Shape, Ink to Text) are not built (their own later work); Lasso Select is built.

### Selecting by type and by region

Selection stays in the window: three queries answer **which shapes a selection would hold**, change nothing and are no undo step (not journaled), and the window selects what they answer. The reference app was not
inspected: the rules come; what they leave open is marked ours.

| Command | Takes | Answers |
|---|---|---|
| `select.by_type` | `types` (shape, group, guide, ole, metafile, bitmap, ink), `roles` (connector, container), `layers` (names, or the `IX` of the page's layers), each a list of at most 64, at least one given; `page` | `{page, ids, count, looked_at, unread?, locked?, read?, overlong?}`: the page's top-level shapes in its order (back to front) |
| `select.by_region` | `polygon` (at most 2,048 `[x, y]` points, paper inches) or `rect` `[x0, y0, x1, y1]`; `partial` (default false); `page` | the same, with `partial`, `work` (the tests made), `steps` (the pieces a curve was cut into) and `coarse?` |
| `select.choices` | `page` | `{page, types, roles, layers, looked_at, read, lookups, unread?, overlong?}`: each type and role as `{word, label, count}`, each layer of the page as `{ix, name, locked, count}` |

* (ours where Q6 leaves it open). A shape's *type* is what the file says: `Type` Shape, Group (a shape with members is one) or Guide, and for `Foreign` the `ForeignType` of its `ForeignData`
  (Bitmap, Metafile or EnhMetaFile, Object is OLE, Ink; with none, the picture's content type decides: EMF and WMF are metafiles, the rest bitmaps). **A connector or a container is a Shape too**: a role is a different
  question. The roles are **Connector** (a one-dimensional shape: its four end cells are there, as the pointer's own reading of a connector has it) and **Container** (`User.msvStructureType` Container or List: a list
  is a container). **The entries of one list are alternatives and the three lists are put together by and**, an empty list asking nothing; a layer is named by its `Name` (then its
  `NameUniv`) or by its `IX`, and one the page does not have is refused in words. A shape deleted from its master is not there.
* Lock "stops shapes on the layer from being selected". A shape on any locked layer is **never taken**, by type or by region, and the answer's `locked` counts the shapes the query
  would have taken but for it. The counts of `select.choices` are of what the page holds, locked shapes included.
  **A shape only on hidden layers is not taken either** (a hidden shape cannot be selected; it is not counted in `locked`), by the same rule as the window's pointer (`layers::standing_of`). The layer table, the names and the caps of a list of layers are the layers' own: `select.*` reads them through `layers::read_layers`, `layers::read_members` and `layers::MemberBudget`.
* The region is a polygon, **the last point joining the first**; repeated points and a closing point equal to the first are dropped, and fewer than three different points are refused.
  By default a shape is taken only when it lies **wholly** inside it, on its edge allowed; with `partial` a shape the polygon **touches, crosses or lies inside** is taken too. A shape's place is **its box** carried
  through its turn (the four corners) **together with the paths it draws** (a section with `NoShow` 1 is not drawn); a **one-dimensional shape is its path alone** (the straight line between its ends when it draws none),
  for the box of one is that line and a bend of its route is outside it. A net is a polygon of four points that is a rectangle, and is tried by one box test. The result is in the page's order, so a net lists the
  shapes as it always did and the primary is the backmost. *Ours:* a shape is first tried by its box (a connector aside): a box the polygon's own box does not touch is out of reach, and a
  path that sticks out of its shape's box is not looked at for it. For **wholly**, the box and the paths must all be inside, so a lasso that cuts a diamond's box corner off does not take the diamond (the frame
  drawn round a selected shape is its box). The page's scale is applied: the polygon is in paper inches.
* Counted, `tests/it/select.rs`. A selection by type reads at most 50,000 top-level shapes and one by region 10,000 (`MAX_SELECT_TYPE_SHAPES`, `MAX_SELECT_REGION_SHAPES`), and the answer's `unread`
  says how many were left. A polygon has at most 2,048 points, checked before any is read. The paths come from the snap's reader of outlines under **one page budget**, so a shape whose paths it cannot pay for is taken
  by its box and counted in `coarse`. **A curve is paid for before it is cut**: the pieces it needs are bounded from its control points (Wang's bound) and charged to the shape's 4,096 pieces, so a curve with control
  points a million inches away is refused as a curve and its shape taken by its box (`steps` counts the pieces made). **Every test of a point or a segment against the polygon's edges is counted** (`work`) and
  charged before it is made: more than 40,000,000 is refused in words ("draw a simpler loop or a smaller one"). **A layer list is read where it lies**: a shape's own `LayerMember` of more than 16 KiB (a list that an instance would take from its master is not read, so a master's long list costs nothing) is read as empty and counted in `overlong`, at most 1,024 pieces of a shorter one are read, every piece counted, the bytes read (`read`) are charged against 8 MiB
  for the whole selection (past it: refused in words), and `select.choices` finds a layer by one table built once (`lookups`). A page with more than 4,096 layers is refused in words.
* **Saved and reopened.** The queries change no file. What they read is the model: types, `Foreign` data, `User` rows, `Layer` rows and `LayerMember`, which every format keeps.

#### The window

| Control | Sends |
|---|---|
| Home, Editing, Select, Area Select | the window's `tool.area_select`: the next drag draws a net, **even from a press on a shape**; one drag uses the mode up (assumed: the page says "the next drag") and the Pointer Tool is back; a click that is no drag leaves it on; Esc puts it down |
| Home, Editing, Select, Lasso Select, and Draw, Write, Lasso Select | the window's `tool.lasso_select`: the next drag draws a loop (the pointer's places at least 3 screen points apart, at most 2,048, simplified to 1.5 screen points on the release); used up by one drag as Area Select is |
| a net from bare page with the Pointer Tool, Area Select's net, a loop, released | one `select.by_region` (a net as a `polygon` of four points) with the option below; the shapes it answers are selected after those Shift or Ctrl kept; **nothing is sent that changes the drawing**. A refusal is a notice and leaves what was kept |
| Home, Editing, Select, Select by Type | the window's `window.selectByType`: the dialog with the seven types, the two roles and the page's layers (`select.choices`); OK is quiet until something is ticked, then sends one `edit.selectByType` with what is ticked; Cancel and Esc select nothing, Enter is OK |
| `edit.selectByType {types?, roles?, layers?}` | runs `select.by_type` for the page on view and selects the answer, with no dialog |
| `edit.selectRegion {polygon? or rect?, partial?, add?}` | runs `select.by_region` for the page on view as a net or a loop does, `add` keeping what is selected |
| File, Options, Advanced, "Select shapes partially within area" (`window.selectPartially {on?}`) | the program-wide option: **off at first, as the page states**, the window's own and not saved; on, a net, Area Select and Lasso Select also select the shapes they touch. OK of the Options dialog sends one `window.selectPartially` when the box was changed |

* **The look**. The net of Area Select is the Pointer Tool's net (the veil, the crosshair from the press). **The loop's trail** is a thin dotted line in the net's outline grey, not
  closed, with no veil and no box and nothing marked on the shapes before the release: the dots are still and no repaint is asked for their sake. *Ours:* R39 says every selection outline is solid; R38
  pictures the lasso's trail dotted, so the trail is dotted (it is the pointer's path, not an outline of the selection), and still. The mode shows the crosshair from the moment it is chosen.
* **State.** `window_state.selectPartially` is the option, `selectByType` is `null` or what is ticked (`types`, `roles`, `layers`), and `tool` is `area_select` or `lasso_select` while a mode is on. The dialog's
  elements are `select_type.type.<word>`, `select_type.role.<word>`, `select_type.layer.<ix>`, `select_type.ok` and `select_type.cancel`; the Options box is `options.partial`.
* **Left**: the Draw tab's Select (`DrawSelectTool`: the ink pointer, with the rest of the ink controls); a lasso on a page of more than 10,000 shapes reads the first 10,000 and says so; a shape on a
  locked layer is not selected by a click either (the window's pointer passes over it, "Layers").

## Size and position

The engine half of the Size & Position window ("Size & Position window", "Pin position", "Rotate by typed angle"): the commands its fields send that
`shape.move`, `shape.rotate` and `connector.reconnect` do not already stand for, and the query for what the fields show. The window half is a task of its own, below. The reference app was not inspected: every
rule below that no specification settles is **ours** and is listed in the report of the task for the check list.

| A field | Is sent as |
|---|---|
| X, Y of a 2-D shape | `shape.place {ids, x?, y?}`, the place typed, for any number of shapes at different places. (The Size & Position pane sends one `shape.place` with the place typed.) |
| Width, Height | `shape.size` (several shapes selected: all of them, `ids`) |
| Angle | `shape.rotate {angle}`; a line takes it too (ROTATE-1D-1) |
| Pin Pos | `shape.pin` |
| Begin X, Begin Y, End X, End Y | `shape.set_end`, one field at a time |
| Length | `shape.set_length` |

* Our rule ("the pin sets the point X and Y refer to and the centre of rotation"). `shape.pin` puts the
  pin of each shape at `at`, **and the shape stays where it is on the page**: `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` change, and `PinX` and `PinY` follow by the change of the local pin carried through the shape's turn
  and flips (`Pose::up_vector`), so every point of the shape lands where it did. `at` is one of nine places, `top-left`, `top-center`, `top-right`, `middle-left`, `center`, `middle-right`, `bottom-left`,
  `bottom-center`, `bottom-right` (in any case, `_` or a space for the hyphen, `centre` for `center`; the names are ours), or `{x, y}`, a point in inches from the corner at (0, 0) of the shape's own frame.
  **A place is written as a formula of the size** (`Width*0.5`, `Height*1`: the form the cell is created with), so the pin keeps its place on the box when the shape is sized and a shape pinned at
  its top left keeps its top left corner under `shape.size`; **a point is written as a value**, which stays where it is. The places are those of the shape's **own frame, before its turn and its flips** (y up, so
  the top is at `Height`): a shape turned a quarter turn has its `top-left` where its own top left went, and the name that `shape.placement` reads back is the one that was sent. A pin that is at the place
  already (to a millionth of an inch) is left as it is, formula and all, and is not counted. A line or a connector is refused (its pin is where its ends put it); a group and the member of a turned or
  flipped group are not, since the shape does not move in its parent's frame and no frame is crossed (and a member named with its group is pinned with it, not left to it as leaves a member to the
  group that turns it: the group's pin and the member's are two cells and neither moves the other, though the group is its members' box again after the edit, so the frame follows the member). A `GUARD` on `LocPinX`, `LocPinY`, `PinX` or `PinY` is a `Failed` error and nothing changes. The shape
  draws where it did, so **no route is made again and Auto Size is not asked**: the cells written and what reads them are worked out (`settle_kept`). One undo step; none when no pin changes.
* Our rule ("typing into a selection of several makes them the same size"). `shape.size` writes `Width` and `Height` of each shape named
  (whichever is given) and nothing else, so **each shape keeps its pin**: `PinX` and `PinY` stay; `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` follow when they are formulas of the size (`LocPinX` 2, `PinX` still 5, the
  left edge from 4 to 3) and stay when they are values (the pin stays on the page and the box grows from it). The sizes are along the shape's own axes, as `shape.resize`'s are, and the shape keeps
  its `Angle`, its flips and its pin; there is no corner to give. **A member of a turned or flipped group is sized** (`shape.resize` refuses it: its corner is a point of the page): `Width` and `Height` are
  lengths, which a turn or a flip of a group does not change. A line and a connector are refused (the words are "it is sized by
  its ends"), **and a group is sized as one shape, its members following** (in the section "Closing the P0 rows"), and one of them among the shapes refuses the whole call and changes nothing. `width` and `height` are more than 0. A shape that has the size already is not written or counted. Connectors glued
  to a shape sized follow it and are routed again, as for `shape.resize`; Auto Size is asked for the shapes named, in the one undo step (`draw::fit_page`); **only the shapes a `Connect` row names are handed to
  the routing**, which looks each shape it is given up from the top of the page (a shape with no connector glued to it can move none). The shapes are found with one walk of the page, whatever their number.
* Our rule. `shape.set_end` puts the begin or the end of a one-dimensional shape at (`x`, `y`) on the page, either coordinate or both, the other
  keeping its place; a place the end is at already is no change. It is `connector.reconnect` to a point (the same code), so the two cells of the end become plain places, `Width` and `Angle` follow by their
  formulas, a route DiaCraft wrote is made again and the label placed, and a hand-edited path is undone. The place is a point of the page, carried down through the groups the
  shape is in to the frame its cells are in. **An end that is glued is refused, in words** ("the begin of shape 7 is glued to a shape and follows it: move the shape, or take the glue off with
  `connector.reconnect` to a place first"): a plain write replaces the end's glue formula and so breaks the glue, and a person who types into the field of a glued end has not asked for that. Auto Size is
  asked, in the one undo step.
* Our rule (the window shows Length for a line). `shape.set_length` sets the distance between the ends **keeping the direction**: the end that is not glued moves along the
  line and the other stays. With no end glued it is the end that moves and the begin stays; with the end glued and the begin free the begin moves (as ROTATE-1D-1 turns the free end about the glued one); with both
  glued the shapes they join set the length and the command is refused. The direction is that of the ends in the parent's frame (a group does not change a length), and for a line whose ends are one place, the cell
  `Angle`. `length` is more than 0 and at most a million drawing units: the direction of a line of no length is not given by its ends. A length the line has already is no change.
* Our rule (X and Y are where the pin is; MOVE-1D). `shape.place` puts the
  pin of each shape named at `x` and/or `y`, **inches on the page**: the place `shape.placement` shows (`x`, `y`) and `shape.pin` chose. It is a move of each shape by the distance from where its pin is to the
  place, so **it does for each shape what `shape.move` does** and the shapes need not share a distance: a shape moves by its pin; a line or a connector moves the ends that are not glued by the same distance (a glued end
  stays where its target holds it, MOVE-1D) and its pin follows by its formulas, and one with both ends glued has nothing of its own to move; the place of a member of a group is a point of the page, carried down
  through the groups it is in to its parent's frame, so `shape.placement` reads back the place asked for; **a member named with its group is placed too and ends at the place as the group
  does**: the group goes first (document order) and the member goes what is left once the group has taken it along, so every pin named is at the place and no member is moved twice (the shift of each group
  named is carried down to the shapes in it); connectors glued to a shape that moved follow it and are routed again, once for the call
  (`settle`); Auto Size is asked, in the one undo step; **snapping is off unless `snap` is given**, with the value `shape.move` takes (`true`, or `{zoom}`; at most 512 shapes), and then the box that holds the shapes
  where they would land is pulled and the pull is added to the place of every shape, on the axes asked for. **At least one of `x` and `y`**: an axis not given does not move, and only the cell asked for is written
  (`x` alone writes `PinX`, and a `PinY` that is a formula stays one; the exception is a member of a turned group, where one axis of the page is both of the group's), and **a shape at the top of the page gets
  the number asked for in the cell itself**, not the sum of its old pin and a distance (a pin of 1.0 placed at 0.3 is 0.3, not 0.30000000000000004); a member's cell is the sum, in its group's frame. Up to 4,096 `ids` (the other commands' bound),
  one undo step. **A shape that would not move is skipped**: its pin is at the place already (to a billionth of a drawing unit), or it is a line with both ends glued; the answer `moved` counts the shapes that moved
  (an `ID` named twice is one shape), and when none did the document is not touched and there is no undo step. **A shape with no place to move from or to is left, and `unplaced` counts it**: its pin, carried through its
  groups, is not a finite number (a cell holds only finite numbers, but their sum through the groups can overflow), or a write would put a cell past the page's reach (a line with an end a million units out, a
  member of a group that far away); the other shapes are placed, so one such shape does not refuse the call. A shape that is not on the page is `BadParams` and nothing changes; `x` and `y` are numbers within
  a million drawing units of the page's origin, as a distance of `shape.move` is (`null`, text and anything past it is `BadParams`; JSON has no NaN).
  **Cost** (counted, the unit tests of `draw/place.rs`): the shapes are found with one walk of the page (no search from the top for any); the frame the groups above a shape give it is composed from the poses of
  the groups, **each group's pose read once** for all the shapes in it (4,096 shapes in the deepest of 150 nested groups read 4,096 + 150 poses, where a pose for each group for each shape would read 614,400); the
  glued ends are found with one pass over the `Connect` rows; and only a shape a connector is glued to, a connector of ours, or a group that holds either is handed to the routing, which looks each up from the top
  of the page (as it does for `shape.move`, which hands it every shape named). **Which groups hold a shape a connector is glued to is found by one more walk of the page** that passes "a connected shape is below" up from
  each list of members to its group (none when no connector is on the page or no group is named), and of a chain of such groups **only the outermost is handed on**, since its subtree is theirs: a chain of 190
  nested groups, all named, over 20,000 shapes with a connector glued to the deepest, walks the page twice and one subtree, where a walk of the subtree of each group was 190 times the page.
* Our rule. `shape.placement` answers for each shape named, once, in the order named, in paper inches (`units_per_inch` is the inches of drawing to a paper inch, `DrawingScale` over `PageScale`: a window multiplies by it to get the length in the inches the cells hold, shows that in the
  page's unit, the unit of `DrawingScale` that `page.setup` gives as `scale.drawing_unit` and the ruler uses, and turns what is typed back the same way into paper inches): `name` (the shape's `Name`, else `NameU`, else `Sheet.<id>` as `NAME()` answers, R36, at most 256
  characters: the window's title), `x`, `y` the **pin on the page** (a member's through its groups), `width`, `height`, `angle` in **degrees from 0 up to 360**
  counterclockwise (the cell `Angle`; for a line the way it points from its begin to its end, and the cell when its ends are one place: `shape.rotate` takes any angle and keeps -180 to 180), `pin` the local pin
  `{x, y}`, `pin_at` the name of the place the local pin is at (to a millionth of an inch) or `"custom"` (`null` for a line, which has no pin of its own), `flip_x`, `flip_y`, `one_d`, `group` (it holds members). A line adds
  `begin` and `end`, each `{x, y, glued}` with the end's place on the page and `glued` either `null` or `{id, point}` (the shape and its connection point counted from 0; `point` is `null` for glue to the whole
  of the shape), and `length`. A window disables Width and Height for a line or a group, Pin Pos for a line, and the fields of a glued end. Numbers are not rounded. The shapes and their glue are found with one
  walk of the page and one pass over its `Connect` rows. A query: nothing is changed or journaled.
* **Cost** (counted, `tests/it/panels_cost.rs` and the unit tests of `draw/placement.rs`, not timed). Every command finds its shapes with one walk of the page, whatever the number named (4,096 shapes on a page of
  20,000 are one visit to each shape and no search from the top of the page for any). Each is one commit of the calculation, which works out the cells written and what reads them: a pin of
  100 shapes on 500 shapes and 300 connectors takes under half of the steps of the whole page; one shape sized takes 45,000 of its 204,000 (44,000 of it the fixed cost of any edit of that page: a line end costs
  that alone); and the steps for 1, 64 and 4,035 shapes sized on a page of 4,100 are a fixed 24,700 and 185 for each shape sized, to a hundredth.

**For a window** (the Size & Position window): read `shape.placement` for the selection once for each revision of the drawing and each selection; one shape shows its fields, several show a field
only where the entries agree (the width and height to a millionth of an inch, the angle as the degrees the answer gives) and blank where they differ; Width and Height typed with several selected are one
`shape.size` with all the `ids`; X, Y, Angle and Pin Pos of several shapes are the window's to decide (the commands take `ids`). Each field is one command, on Enter or on leaving the field, and **a value equal to
the one shown is not sent** (a `shape.move` of a distance of nothing is still a step). Units and arithmetic (`21 in.`, `1 ft. 5 in.`, a comma refused) are the window's, which sends a
number of paper inches.
