The title is `name` of the answer. **Built in ** (`crates/ui-egui/src/panes/size_position.rs`): X and Y send one `shape.place` with the place typed (the answer's `unplaced` is told under the field), Pin Pos sends `shape.pin` and sends nothing for the place the pin is at, the fields of a glued end and the length of a line glued at both ends are quiet, and Width and Height are quiet when a line or a group is among the shapes. **A value typed goes to the shapes the field showed when it took the focus** (the ids and the page are kept with the field), whatever is selected when it lets go; every shape selected is sent, up to the 4,096 one command takes, and with more the fields are quiet; a place or a size over `draw::MAX_COORD` is refused in words before a command is sent.

## Formatting

`shape.format`, `text.format` and `shape.formatting` (the core is `crates/engine/src/formatting/`, the commands `cmd/format.rs`). The reference app was not
inspected: every rule below that no specification settles is **ours** and is listed in the report of the task for the check list.

**What a formatting command writes** is a local cell on the shape (an instance holds what differs), the way a file holds it. A colour
is `F="RGB(r,g,b)"` with `V="#rrggbb"` in lower case; a number is a value with no formula (a weight in
inches on paper, `0.01041666666666667` for 0.75 point; a size the same, `0.1666666666666667` for 12). A cell that was a formula (`THEMEVAL()`,
`THEMEGUARD(...)`) becomes the value, as any write from the drawing window replaces the formula of a cell that is not guarded,
 so **a value set on a themed shape beats the theme's for that cell and the theme is not changed**; the cells nobody formatted stay the theme's. A
`GUARD` cell is a `Failed` error and nothing changes. A cell that holds what would be written already is not written, and a call that changes nothing
is no undo step (`formatted` is 0). A recalculation follows: the cells written and what reads them, not the page, and no connector is routed
again (nothing moves). Connectors take the same cells as any shape.

| Parameter | Cells written |
|---|---|
| `fill` `#RRGGBB` | `FillForegnd`; `FillPattern` 1 unless it is 1 to 40 already; `FillGradientEnabled` 0 unless it is 0 |
| `fill` `none` | `FillPattern` 0; the colours are not touched |
| `line` `#RRGGBB` | `LineColor`; `LinePattern` 1 unless it is 1 to 23 already; `LineGradientEnabled` 0 unless it is 0 |
| `line` `none` | `LinePattern` 0 |
| `weight` (points, more than 0 and at most 1000) | `LineWeight` = points over 72 (on paper, whatever the page's scale) |
| `pattern` (0 to 23) | `LinePattern`; written after a `line` colour in the same call, so it wins |
| `begin_arrow`, `end_arrow` (0 to 45) | `BeginArrow`, `EndArrow` (254 names a master by `USE` and is not taken) |
| `rounding` (paper inches, 0 to 100) | `Rounding` in drawing units, the inches times the page's drawing units per paper inch |
| `font` | `Character` `Font`: the face's `NameU`; see the font rule below |
| `size` (points, more than 0 and at most 1000) | `Size` = points over 72 |
| `bold`, `italic`, `underline` | bits 1, 2 and 4 of `Style` one at a time, the other bits kept; see the style bits rule below |
| `strike` | `Strikethru` 1 or 0 |
| `color` `#RRGGBB` | `Character` `Color` as `RGB(r,g,b)` |
| `align` `left`, `center`, `right`, `justify` | `Paragraph` `HorzAlign` 0, 1, 2, 3 |
| `valign` `top`, `middle`, `bottom` | the shape's `VerticalAlign` 0, 1, 2 |
| `bullet` (0 to 7) | `Paragraph` `Bullet` (0 none, 1 to 7 the marks of the table) |
| `indent` `increase`, `decrease` | `Paragraph` `IndLeft`, to the next multiple of a quarter inch above or below it, from 0 to 100 inches |
| `case` `normal`, `all_caps`, `initial_caps`, `small_caps` | `Character` `Case` 0, 1, 2, 0 and bit 8 of `Style` for small capitals |
| `direction` `ltr`, `rtl` | `Paragraph` `Flags` 0, 1 |
| `vertical` `true`, `false` | the shape's `TextDirection` 1, 0; the text is then laid out down the block. `TEXTWIDTH` and `TEXTHEIGHT` still measure it as it runs across, in the shape's own axes. |

* Our rule, from the form files hold. A bullet put on a paragraph whose
  `IndLeft` and `IndFirst` are both 0 also writes `IndLeft` 0.25 and `IndFirst` -0.25 inch, so that the mark stands at the margin and every line of the text
  at a quarter inch (the default gap for the first line), as the bulleted paragraphs of files are; a bullet taken off a paragraph that has exactly that
  form takes it away again; a paragraph with any other indent keeps it, and a bullet put on one that has a mark already (another style) writes only
  `Bullet`. A bullet and an indent step in one call make 0.5 (the step is taken from the form the bullet gave). The paragraphs are the ones `align` takes,
  and a paragraph typed after a bulleted one joins its row, so Enter carries the bullet on. A bullet of 8 or more is `BadParams` (the table has
  none). `BulletStr`, `BulletFont`, `BulletFontSize` and `TextPosAfterBullet` are not written by any command: they are read and kept, and drawn.
* Our rule: no page or image gives the step of Decrease and Increase Indent. Increase Indent moves `IndLeft` of
  each paragraph to the next multiple of 0.25 inch above it (0 to 0.25, 0.3 to 0.5), Decrease Indent to the next multiple below it and never below 0; an
  indent of 100 inches or more is not increased, one of 0 or less is not decreased, an indent so large that a quarter inch does not move it (past about 2^51
  inches) stays (from past half the largest number a step came to an infinity, which the write took for 0), and a step that changes nothing is no
  undo step. 0.25 inch is the
  `IndLeft` most bulleted paragraphs hold in files. `IndFirst` and `IndRight` are not touched.
* **Change Case is how the letters show, not an edit of the
  text**: Normal, All Caps, Initial Caps and Small Caps are the Case box of the Text dialog, one at a time, so choosing one clears the others: `Case` 0, 1 or 2,
  and small capitals as bit 8 of `Style` with `Case` 0. The stored text is never rewritten (the Replace dialog's Match case compares it as typed, so
  a drawing saved with All Caps holds `abc` and draws `ABC`); the layout already draws `Case` and small capitals (`crates/text` `prepare.rs`). The task's
  brief listed Upper, Lower, Sentence and Title as a text edit, which is what the sibling apps' Change Case does (DeckCraft's `change_case`, which rewrites
  the characters): the specifications say otherwise for this application, so the specifications were followed, and the question is in the report.
* **Text direction**. `direction` is the paragraph's reading direction (`Flags`), which the layout orders
  its characters by with the Unicode algorithm (alignment 0 and 2 keep their sides, so a direction does not turn the alignment); `vertical` is the
  block's `TextDirection`, which runs the text down the block, by the vertical text rule in `crates/text/src/vertical.rs`. Only the drawing turns: `TEXTWIDTH` is still the widest line with the left and right margins, and `TEXTHEIGHT` the stack of lines with the top and bottom ones, as two sample files saved for an empty vertical block. The Rotate Text button (`TextRotate90Degrees`,
  `format.rotateText`) is the Text Block tool's rotation, and is older.
* Our rule (no specification says what picking a colour does to the pattern): a colour that cannot be seen would be a command that
  does nothing, so a colour on a shape whose pattern is none, is the theme's marker, or is a custom pattern makes the pattern solid, and a colour on a
  shape that paints a gradient turns the gradient off (the gradient is painted in place of the colour). A hatch (2 to 40 on a fill)
  stays: the colour is the hatch's foreground. `none` writes the pattern and leaves the colour, so a colour after it brings the shape back.
* Our rule: a group is formatted through its members. Every shape with no members inside the shapes named, to any depth, is formatted
  once, in the order named, and **the group's own cells are left alone**: a group draws nothing of its own. At most 20,000 shapes in one command. An `ID`
  named more than once is walked once; a shape named and also inside a named group is looked at twice, and **every shape looked at, whether kept or
  not, is charged to one budget for the call** (80,000, the groups' nodes included), so that a list of names cannot cost the names times what they hold:
  past it the command is `Failed` and nothing changes. `shape.formatting` answers a group by its first such member, found by going down through the
  first member at each level (it costs the depth, so a group larger than a command formats is answered, and 4,096 names of it cost 4,096 steps).
* **Whole text or a range.** `text.format` without `start` and `end` changes every row the text names (every `cp` for the Character cells, every `pp`
  for the Paragraph cells), or row 0 when it names none; the rows of the master's text when the shape has none of its own; a shape with no text at all
  gets row 0, for the text typed in it next. With `start` and `end` (characters, as `shape.set_text` counts them: not the fields, from 0, `end` not in
  the range) of **one** shape that is not a group, only those characters change. A range past the text, `start` above `end`, a range of several shapes
  and a range of a group are `BadParams`. A point (`start` equal to `end`) formats no character; `align` still takes the paragraph it is in.
* **Formatting a range** (ours; no specification says what formatting part of a run does to the file; the rule for typing is another): the runs and
  paragraphs the range starts or ends in are split at its ends, and every marker and field stays.
  * The part of a run before the range keeps its marker. The part inside gets a new `cp` and a **new row**, a copy of the cells the run's row resolves
    to (formulas as written, values as saved, units kept; the style chain's and the master's cells included, since a row of another `IX` inherits
    nothing by its key) with the change. The part after gets a new `cp` and a new row, a copy unchanged. A run the range covers whole keeps its row,
    which is changed in place, and no marker is added. A text with no `cp` has row 0 from its first character: `cp` 0 is written out when a
    split needs it, first, and `pp` 0 after it for a paragraph split.
  * **A split writes every row the text names as a row of the shape's own** (a reader that takes the first row of a shape for run 0, as LibreOffice
    does, reads a text whose row 0 a style supplies wrongly when the file holds rows 1 and 2 only: a rectangle of the stencil, `Hello world`, `lo` made
    bold showed `Hell` bold). A row that stays where it is, is not in the range and is the shape's own already is left alone; one in the range that
    stays is changed in place; every other row (new, moved, or one a master or a style supplies) is written whole, as a copy of the cells its run's row
    resolves to with the change, and a row that holds a `GUARD` cell the new cells do not hold the same is a refusal (a write of a guarded cell).
  * **Numbering, in two cases** (both write every named row; the unit is a text of `cp` markers, and the same holds for `pp` and `Paragraph`):
    * **Text order** ("a writer numbers them this way"): when the text's first marker names row 0 and **no master or style holds a row of the
      section at `IX` 1 or above** for the shape (the counts: a `cp` finds its row on a master in 935 of about 125,000 runs and in a style in
      344, so about 99 texts in 100), the **rows** are numbered 0, 1, 2 in the order they first appear in the text, and each marker takes the number
      of its row (rows are renumbered, not markers: markers that name one row, which a reader follows, go on naming one, so 320,000 markers
      that name one row write one row and not 320,000); every row takes the cells of the run it is on. A split in any run, formatting `lo` then `H`
      then `w`, keeps `cp` 0, 1, 2, 3 in the order of the text. Rows that were named and are past the last number are taken out of the section, all of
      a section's in one pass (a pass for each would copy the section's rows for each).
      Whether a master or a style holds such a row is answered **from the rows the command has found already, with no copy of the document**: a row of
      the shape's effective rows at `IX` 1 or above with a cell a master or a style would give if the shape held none of its own
      (`Resolver::inherits`), or any row of the root style at `IX` 1 or above, which the copy of a row takes for every shape (below) and which the
      renderer reads in place of a cell a shape with no style link lacks: so the answer agrees with the rows that are copied.
    * **Next free** otherwise: a row a master or a style holds at `IX` 1 or above may be named by a marker, and a row of the shape's own at that `IX`
      is merged with it, so no row is moved; a new row takes **the next `IX` above every row of the section the shape resolves** and every row the text
      names, up to 65,535. That is text order only when the split is in the last run; a reader that follows the `IX` as written reads
      it right whatever the order, and one that reads rows by position (LibreOffice) does not. A text whose first marker is not row 0 is in this case
      too: moving its cells to row 0 would merge them with the style's.
    * **No more rows than a section numbers**: a text that names more than 65,536 rows (the numbers 0 to 65,535, which is all a reader takes) is
      **refused** with the words of a full section, "the text has too many runs or paragraphs (rows are numbered up to 65535)", where it would be
      written: a split needs a new number and there is none above the highest, so neither case can take it (the refusal is the next-free case's; the
      text-order case needs no more numbers than there are rows, so it never goes past), and a format of the whole text writes a row for each row named.
      Nothing is planned before the refusal.
  * A row written whole holds **every cell the command writes** (`Font`, `Size`, `Style`, `Strikethru`, `Color`, `HorzAlign`, as the call names them),
    with what its source row resolves to, or the cell's default when that is nothing (`Font` 0, which says no face; `Style` 0; `Strikethru` 0;     `Color` 0; `Size` 12 points; `HorzAlign` 1).
    A reader that carries a cell from one run to the next that lacks it (LibreOffice does) then shows the
    row as it is, and not as the changed run before it; a row left alone or changed in place is not filled in this way.
  * A row that a run in the range shares with a run outside it (two markers naming one row) is not changed for both: the run in the range is
    pointed at a copy.
  * **Where a marker goes.** A run starts at the first character of the range, after the fields and markers between it and the characters before (a
    field there stays with the run before); a run starts after the range's last character, before the fields and markers after it, unless a marker is
    there already. A paragraph starts right after the line break that ends the one before (so a field after the break is the new paragraph's). A field
    at the edge of a range is in the run it is in, whatever the range says; a field inside it is formatted with it. Neighbouring character parts are
    joined again.
* Our rule: the face is looked up in the document's font table by its name, exactly and then in any case, and **the table's spelling** is what
  `Font` holds; a face that is not there is added to the table with `NameU` alone (the one attribute MS-VSDX requires), at most
  4,096 faces. A name is trimmed, not blank, at most 255 characters, with no control character and no U+FFFE or U+FFFF. `AsianFont` and
  `ComplexScriptFont` are left alone: a letter of those scripts reads its own cell, so a face for them is not set here.
* Our rule: a bit that is as asked already, in a `Style` or `Strikethru` that holds a plain number (no formula, not the theme's marker), is not
  written. A `Style` that is the theme's marker is worked out as the renderer works it out; when the theme cannot answer, it reads as 0 for the bits kept,
  so bold on it writes 1 and the theme's bold, if it had one, is not kept. `underline` is bit 4 only: a `DblUnderline` of 1 stays.
* **`shape.formatting`** reads what the cells resolve to **as the renderer reads them** (inheritance, then the root style for a shape with no style
  link, then `calc`'s answer for a theme marker, then the root's cell when the theme cannot answer), so the controls show what the picture draws: the
  colours as `#rrggbb` or `none` (a pattern of 0); `weight` and `size` in points; `rounding` in paper inches; `font` the face of the first run, named by
  the rule **The face shown** below and never `null`; the first run is the row of the text's first `cp` (row 0 when there is none), the paragraph the row of its first `pp`;
  `align` is `center` when no cell says; 3 and 4 are `justify` and a number past the list is `left`; `valign` 3 and more is `top`. A query:
  nothing is changed or journaled.
* **The face shown** (ours; gap U21). `font` names **the face the renderer draws the run in**, so the window's Font box is never
  blank for a shape with text. **One function reads it for both**: `diacraft_render::FaceReader::named_font` is the renderer's own reading of the run's face (`render/src/face.rs`, which its layout calls too), so the
  query and the picture cannot read different cells. It is the `Font` cell of the run's `Character` row found through the shape's own cells, its master and the style chain; the theme's font where
  the cell holds the marker, which `calc` answers by running the cell's own `THEMEVAL` call (a theme that cannot answer, or no theme, leaves the root style's cell); and the **root style's `Font`** where the
  cell is 0 or empty (the file's default, not the first entry of the font table). **The name is the document's or the theme's, as written**: `Calibri` stays `Calibri` where Carlito draws it
  (keeps the cell and the font table as they are), and a name no face on the machine has is still the name. **When no cell names a face** the renderer asks its font database for the default of an
  unnamed run, `FontDb::resolve("")`: the first of Carlito, Calibri, Liberation Sans, Arimo and Arial the database holds (each open twin before the face it matches), else a face whose name says it is sans, else a bundled face (`fonts/src/standin.rs`
  `class_defaults`), and `font` is the family of that face (`FontDb::default_family`). That is a name of what is drawn, and **depends on the faces the machine has**, since nothing in the document names one.
  `shape.formatting` asks **the database the application shared with the engine** (`measure::set_measure_fonts`, `start_system_fonts`: the one its renderer draws in) **when it is in hand, and never waits for it**
  (`measure::measure_fonts_now`; the window reads this in its frame and the scan of the machine's faces can take seconds): while the scan goes, and when nothing is shared (a library, a test, the control channel
  with no window), it asks the **bundled faces**, the same on every machine. A program that has the renderer's database asks `formatting::look_of_in` and `look_of_range_in` with it. **A view read while the scan was going names the bundled default, and nothing reads it again when the
  scan ends**: the engine keeps no view, and the window's cache (keyed on the drawing generation, the revision, the page and the shapes) does not yet include whether the faces are in hand, so until the selection or the
  drawing changes the Font box can name the bundled default (`Ubuntu`) while the page shows the machine's (Carlito). That is the window's to do (`SharedFonts::is_ready` in the key), and is open as a gap. The default is asked of the database once for all the runs and shapes of a call. A face is compared in any case for **`mixed`** (matches faces
  so), and an unnamed run is compared as the default it is drawn in: a run that names nothing and one that names the default's family draw alike and are one value. `null` is answered only where even the default
  has no name: a database with no face at all.
* **What a range shows** (ours; the engine half of gap U19: the window's Bold, Italic, Underline, Strikethrough and font, size and
  colour boxes show the state of the characters selected, and a toggle that read the first run never flipped for a range elsewhere in the text).
  `shape.formatting` with `start` and `end` answers for **the characters of that range of one shape that is not a group** (`ids` names one shape;
  several, a repeated one and a group are `BadParams`, as for `text.format`). The offsets are `text.format`'s: counted in characters (not bytes, so no
  offset falls inside a multi-byte character), fields not counted, `end` not in the range, and a range past the text or reversed is refused **in the
  same words and by the same check** (`check_range`); a shape with no text of its own has no characters, so only `0` to `0` is within it. The entry is
  the shape's as without a range (id, fill, line, weight, pattern, arrows, rounding, `valign`), with the face, size, bold, italic, underline, strike,
  colour and alignment of the **runs and paragraphs the range touches**, and a list `mixed` (always there with a range, `[]` when nothing is mixed).
  * **Mixed.** A property is `"mixed"` when the runs differ in it, and **one value** when they agree, whatever their rows: two rows that hold the same
    face (compared in any case), the same size (to a millionth of a point, as the reply shows it), the same colour, strike or alignment are one
    value. `Style` is read **a bit at a time**, so runs holding 5 (bold, underline) and 6 (italic, underline) are mixed in bold and italic and agree on
    underline. `mixed` names the mixed ones in the order `font`, `size`, `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, `strike`, `color`, `align`; **a face may be
    called `mixed`, so a program tells a mixed font by the list and not by the word**. The value that `mixed` replaces is not given.
  * **Which runs.** A run is read when it holds a character of the range, by `cp` markers as `text.format`'s split reads them (a text with none is
    one run in row 0; markers that name one row are one row). A run with no character is read only when it holds a **field** and
    stands strictly inside the range: the field shows text in that run's formatting, and `text.format` changes it with the range; a field at the edge of
    the range is in the run it is in, as in the rule for formatting a range, and its run is not read. An empty run with nothing in it shows nothing and is not read.
  * **A point** (`start` equal to `end`) answers **the run before it**: the run of the character the point stands after, and at the start of the text
    the run of the first character. That is the run that typing there joins (characters typed at the end of a run are that run's, and not
    the next one's), so the window's controls show what the next character typed would be. In `ab|cd` with the runs `ab` and `cd`, the point 2 is the
    first run's and the point 3 the second's. A text with no characters yet takes characters after its leading markers and ahead of any field, so a
    point there reads the last `cp` (and the last `pp`) among the markers that open the text, row 0 for a kind with none; a shape with no text reads row
    0, which is what it reads without a range.
  * **The paragraph** is the one `text.format` takes for `align`: the paragraphs from the line break before the range's first character to the line
    break that ends its last (`paragraph_bounds`), and a point is in the paragraph it stands in, which after a line break is the next one (so the point
    just after `one\n` reads the second paragraph's alignment, though the run it reads is `one\n`'s). A point after the final line break of the text,
    where the paragraph has no characters, reads the row of the last `pp`, row 0 with none. The paragraphs are read by their `pp` markers as the runs are
    by `cp`, and alignment is mixed when they differ.
  * **Cost.** One walk of the text's parts that stops at the first marker at or after the end of the range (a part's characters are counted, not
    looked at), and one read of each *distinct* row the range touches (a text of 200,000 markers that name two rows reads two), none after every
    character property is mixed (the rest of the rows cannot change the answer). Counted by the unit tests of `formatting/tests.rs` and timed by
    `format_scale.rs`.

## Formatting and arranging from the window

The Home tab's Font, Paragraph, Shape Styles and Arrange groups and the keys send the commands above through **the window's own commands**, which name the
selection and the page on view and send one engine command each: `format.shape` (`shape.format`), `format.text`, `format.toggle` and `format.size`
(`text.format`), `format.options` (opens the Format Shape pane) and `format.painter`, `arrange.rotate`, `arrange.flip`, `arrange.order` and `arrange.align` (`shape.rotate`, `shape.flip`, `shape.order`, `shape.align`). They
are listed by `engine.commands` with the engine's, and are one undo step each. They cannot run while nothing is selected (`arrange.align` while fewer than
two shapes are: the others move to the first), and the word of the refusal is the tooltip of the control. A control shows the state of the selection from
`shape.formatting` (the engine's `look_of`), read once for each revision of the drawing and each selection. The reference app was not inspected: every rule below is
**ours** and is listed in the report of the task for the check list.

* The Format Painter, `painter.rs`; the behaviour is the specification's, and the rest ours. `format.painter {on?, keep?, to?}` picks up the look of the first shape selected as `shape.formatting` answers it (`fill`, `line`, `weight`, `pattern`, `begin_arrow`, `end_arrow`, `rounding`, the transparency of the fill and the line, a hatch or stipple with its ground, `line_cap` and `compound` for `shape.format`, the gradients for `shape.gradient` and the shadow for `shape.shadow`, a source with no shadow taking the shadow away; `font` where the cells name one, `size`, `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, `strike`, `color`, `align` and `valign` for `text.format`) and turns the painter on, a mode of the drawing area beside the tools: the next press on a shape paints it and the painter turns itself off; `keep: true` (a double click on the button) keeps it on until Escape, the Pointer Tool or another tool; `on: false` puts it down; `to` paints those shapes of the page on view as a press on each would, and needs the painter on. **One paint is one undo step**: `shape.format`, `text.format`, `shape.gradient` and `shape.shadow` run as one `command.batch` (labelled "Format Painter"). With two shapes or more selected the first is the source and the others are painted at once, and no mode is left on (unless `keep`). A shape that is a member of a group is not copied from (refused in words), and a press on a member paints the group it is in, as `shape.format` formats a group through the shapes inside it. A press on the bare page paints nothing and starts no rubber band; Escape before a press puts the painter down and leaves the tool and the selection as they were. It is enabled with a shape selected or while it is on (it can always be put down), its key is Ctrl+Shift+P, and `formatPainter` in the window state is `{keep, from}` while it is on and null otherwise. `format.options {tab?}` opens the Format Shape pane on `Fill & Line` (the default) or `Effects` for the shapes selected, and waits for a selection as `format.shape` does: Fill Options..., Line Options... and More Arrows... are this command, so that the Fill and Line buttons are not woken by them.
* What the engine does not say: `shape.formatting` answers for each shape and has no summary of several: a control that shows a value (the Font
  and Size boxes, the swatch under Fill, Line and Font Color) shows it when every shape selected has it and shows nothing when they differ (a blank box; a
  bar with a slash through it). A toggle (Bold, Italic, Underline, Strikethrough) and an alignment button are pressed when every shape has it, **half pressed**
  (its outline and no fill) when some do, and not pressed when none does. **A press on one that is not pressed in full turns it on for every shape**; a press
  on one that is pressed in full turns it off for every shape. Only the first 1,000 shapes selected are read. A colour that paints nothing (`FillPattern` 0) is
  shown as a bar with a slash and chosen again with **No Fill** or **No Line**.
* No specification gives the steps: Increase Font Size and Decrease Font Size (Ctrl+Shift+> and Ctrl+Shift+<) go to the next size of the list
  6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 48, 60, 72 above (below) the size of **the shape selected first**, and every shape selected gets
  that size. Past the list the step is a point down, to 1, and 12 points up, to 1,000. The Size list offers the same sizes and a box that takes any size from
  more than 0 to 1,000 points.
* "applied to highlighted characters or the whole block when only clicked": with a text box open,
  `format.text`, `format.toggle`, `format.size` and `format.shape` first send what was typed. The characters selected in the box go with the
  command as `start` and `end` (counted as `shape.set_text` counts them), **a caret with nothing selected formats the whole text of the shape**, and the box
  is opened again on the same characters, so that the person goes on typing. **A press on the ribbon's body or on a menu, a palette or a list floating over the
  drawing does not end the edit**; a press on the drawing area, the Shapes window, the status bar or the tabs still does. **What the controls show with a text box open**
  (gap U19 settled): the Font group reads **`shape.formatting` with `start` and `end`** for the characters selected in the box, and for a
  caret with nothing selected, for the run before it (a point; at the start of the text, the first character's run). Bold, Italic, Underline and Strikethrough are
  pressed when the runs of the range all have the style, not pressed when none does, and **half pressed when they differ** (the `mixed` list of the answer, not the word
  `"mixed"`, which a face may be called); the Font and Size boxes show the value, or **nothing when it is mixed**; the Font Color bar shows the colour, or the slash;
  the alignment buttons show the paragraph's, or none when the paragraphs differ. **A press on a mixed toggle turns it on** (no specification says
  otherwise), a press on one that is on turns it off, and the value is worked out after what was typed is sent, from the same range read again. Increase and
  Decrease Font Size step from the size the box shows (the first run's when the sizes differ). **A caret shows the run before it and a press still formats the whole
  text** (the inventory: "the whole block when only clicked"), with the value that run gives: this is the one place the control and the effect differ (gap U19). While
  characters typed have not been sent the drawing has not the characters the offsets count, so the state read last is kept. With no box open, or a selection of shapes,
  the controls show the first run of each shape, as before.
* Gap U9: a shape that is turned or flipped has its eight handles on its own box where its turn and flips carry it (the
  shape's four corners from `draw::shape_corners`), and a handle drag is worked out in **the shape's own frame**: the pointer is carried into the frame, the box
  is resized as for an unturned shape, and the command is sent with the page place of the corner at (0, 0) of the new frame and the width and height along
  its axes (`shape.resize`). The edge opposite the handle stays where it is. A group's handles work as a shape's do (it is sized by its own box and its members follow), the handles round several shapes stretch them as one
  And the member of a turned or flipped group has none.
* (gap U9; for the box and its handles, R37 for the pointers; how the shapes come out is and where a spec is silent the window does what one shape's handles do, scaled to the set). **The handles of the box round
  several shapes work.** The box that is drawn is the joint box the engine stretches from (a connector counting by its two ends) grown by 1.3 handle widths, so what is drawn is what is resized. A press on one of its eight handles (the
  handles of a box small on the screen are the four corners, as for one shape) starts stretching the shapes; **a corner stretches them about the opposite corner in proportion to the joint box with no key, a side stretches in one axis and in proportion with Shift** (as one shape's do), a joint box never
  gets smaller than 0.1 inch on a side, and the engine snaps the joint box as it does a shape's (`snap_resize_set`; Alt turns the snap off). While the drag goes the window paints the outlines of the shapes stretched as the stretch would carry every place of them (a group by its members) and the box dashed; the release sends the one command, `shape.resize` with `ids` and the box
  (and `snap: {zoom, view, keep}` where it snaps), one undo step, and Escape drops the drag. **A press on the rotation handle turns the shapes as one about the middle of their joint box**: the angle is how far the pointer has gone round that point from where the handle was grabbed, in the steps
  (15 degrees under 110 points from it, 5 under 190, 1 under 300 and a tenth of a degree past that), kept from just over -180 up to 180; the engine's `snap_turn` is for the angle of one shape, so no `snap` is sent and the release is `shape.rotate` with `by` and `about` (the middle of the joint box the preview turned them about),
  one undo step. The pointer over a handle is the two-headed arrow along its direction, diagonal at a corner whatever the box's proportions (`ResizeNwSe`, `ResizeNeSw`, `ResizeHorizontal`, `ResizeVertical`), and the grabbing hand over the rotation handle; while a drag is on it is the arrow of that handle, and the hand while turning.
  **A group selected alone has the handles of one shape**: its eight handles and its rotation handle work as a shape's, the preview draws its members stretched or turned. A set with a member in a turned or flipped group, or whose joint box has no width or no height, has handles that start nothing (a press on one keeps the selection).
  The locks of are the engine's: a drag that every shape of the set is locked against is refused in words in the status bar's notice at the release.
* ("turns the shape about its pin, and the further the pointer from the shape, the finer the
  steps"): one 2-D shape or one group selected, not in a turned or flipped group, has a rotation handle 36 points above the middle of its top edge (24 until a later change; the centre of the circular arrow, in the
  direction of its angle, so that a flip does not move it to the bottom). Dragging it turns the shape about its pin, **the pointer going round the pin from where
  it took hold**: the angle is the one the shape had plus that turn, snapped to **15 degrees while the pointer is under 110 points from the pin, 5 under 190,
  1 under 300, and a tenth of a degree past that**, and the one command, `shape.rotate` with `angle`, goes when the button is let go. Escape drops the turn.
* The figures are approximate and each is a theme token (`theme::Tokens` for colours, `theme::select` for sizes in screen points, which do not change with the zoom): **the net** of a drag from bare page is a rectangle from the press to the pointer, filled with a translucent light grey (about #BEBEBE at 40 percent) and outlined by one solid pixel of grey (about #ADADAD), painted over the picture and over every mark, the same whichever way it is dragged; it shows from the first movement and goes in the frame of the release, nothing is marked on a shape under it, and the pointer is a crosshair from the press to the release. A net takes the shapes **wholly inside** it, connectors included, in the page's order (the Advanced option "Select shapes partially within area" is not built). **One shape or one group selected** has a box of 1 point in a grey (about #A6A6A6) on its own box, eight handles (white circles 12 points across with a 1 point ring of that grey; the middle ones of a side only when it can hold three) and the rotation handle: a circular arrow 21 points across with its centre 36 points above the box line, on a 1 point stem from the top middle handle; **a connector selected alone** has a green disc 9 points across with a white halo at each glued end and a square 6 points across at each free one (white with a grey ring at its begin, grey at its end). **Several shapes selected** have a dark blue frame (about #0054A6; a lighter blue in the dark theme) of 2 points on the box of each shape, and of 4 points on the primary, which is the first in the selection (a net lists back to front, so the backmost; Ctrl-click leaves the primary alone, and removing it hands the thick frame to the next); a connector among them is drawn again along its own path in that blue, 2 points wide (4 for the primary); a group is one shape with one frame and no member frame; and one box of 1 point round all of them, 1.3 handle widths (15.6 points) off the outermost boxes, with the same eight handles and the rotation handle. **The handles round several shapes, and a group's, work** (the box is the joint box the engine stretches from, a connector counting by its two ends, grown by the margin). While a drag is on only the frames and the box show, and the Text Block tool shows its own handles. Not drawn: the arrowhead of a connector in blue, the middle mark of a connector, a locked shape's grey handle bars, and the rotation pin of several shapes (open questions of the specification).
* (gap U9; "first click takes the group and second click the member, with Behavior able to
  set group only, group first or members first"): a click selects a group, and **a second click on the selected group selects the member under
  the pointer** (the front-most one level down). **The press of that second click moves the group**: a drag that begins on a member of the selected group drags the
  group, whole, and the selection goes to the member only when the button comes up without a drag. With a member selected, a press on a sibling selects the sibling
  at once, and a selected member is dragged alone. Shift or Ctrl held adds to the selection as before and does not go in. **`SelectMode` is read for each group**
  (by inheritance; **a cell that is absent reads 1**, since the root style holds 1 in every file): **0, group only, offers no members, and neither do
  the groups inside it whatever their own mode** (every ancestor counts); 1 is group first; **2, members first, makes the first click on the group
  select the member under the pointer**, going down through the groups in it that are 2 too. The members are read with the page (at most 5,000 of them, 8 levels
  down), at their places on the page carried through the poses of their groups; a member moves with `shape.move` in its group's frame.
* The keys, each a window command with a parameter. From the specifications: **Ctrl+B, Ctrl+I, Ctrl+U** ("Bold, italic and
  underline"); **Ctrl+Shift+> and Ctrl+Shift+<** ("Font and size"; the keys of the full stop and the comma with Shift, as `>` and `<` are on a US keyboard:
  assumed); **Ctrl+Shift+L, C, R, J** (left, centre, right, justify) and **Ctrl+Shift+T, M, V** (top, middle, bottom) ("Horizontal paragraph alignment",
  "Vertical text alignment"); **Ctrl+L, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+H, Ctrl+J** ("Rotate Left, Rotate Right, Flip Horizontal and
  Flip Vertical, also on Ctrl+L, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+H and Ctrl+J": the letters are matched to the commands in that order: **assumed**) and **Ctrl+Shift+F, Ctrl+Shift+B**
  (bring to front, send to back). **No key is ours** among those wired. **Ctrl+Shift+G and Ctrl+Shift+U** are the inventory's ("Group and ungroup"). **Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X and Ctrl+V** are the platform's keys and
  ours (the inventory names the commands, not the keys), and **Ctrl+D** (duplicate) is named for the web only, so its desktop key is ours. The engine has
  its own key for each of these six commands, and **the window runs its own commands in their place** because they take the selection and the page
  on view: `shortcuts.rs` keeps the table `ENGINE_KEYS_FOR_THE_WINDOW`, and a test names each. On a desktop **egui-winit turns Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X and Ctrl+V into
  copy, cut and paste events and sends no key**, so the window takes those events itself when no text field has the keyboard. With a text box open the first
  group works, on the characters selected; the others, and Ctrl+Z, copy, cut and paste, are the box's (the clipboard keys then copy and paste the box's text).
  A text field that has the keyboard (the Font list's box) has all its keys. **A letter key bound without Shift does not fire with Shift held** (Ctrl+Shift+U in
  an open box is not Underline): egui's logical match lets Shift be extra. **On a Mac, a key the system or the app menu reserves is not used** (ours, `ui-egui/src/shortcuts.rs` `MAC_KEYS`, checked by a test against a list of the Mac's reserved keys from Apple's Human Interface Guidelines, [Keyboards](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/keyboards), Apple's [Mac keyboard shortcuts](https://support.apple.com/en-us/102650), both read 2026-11-23, and winit's app menu): Flip Horizontal is **Shift+Command+H** (Command+H is the app menu's Hide, which takes the key before the window sees it), Flip Vertical **Option+Command+J** (Command+J scrolls to the selection), the Text Block Tool **Option+Command+4** (Shift+Command+4 is the system's screenshot of a part of the screen), the Text dialog **Command+T** (F11 is Show Desktop; Command+T is the Mac's own key to show the fonts), Center **Shift+Command+E** (Shift+Command+C shows the Colors window; beside Shift+Command+L, R and J, the other alignments; moved it from the Mac's own Command+|, which no common Mac layout but Latin American can press in this window), Increase and Decrease Font Size **Option+Command+.** and **Option+Command+,** (Shift+Command+, cannot be pressed on the French, Belgian and Italian layouts, whose comma has no Shift character of its own), All Caps **Option+Command+A** (Shift+Command+A deselects all), Align Text Bottom **Option+Command+B** (Shift+Command+V pastes as), the Format Painter **Option+Command+C** (the Mac's own key to copy a style; Shift+Command+P is Page Setup) and Group **Option+Command+G** (Shift+Command+G finds the previous match; a sibling craft app gives its Group the same key). Every other key is the other systems' with Command for Ctrl. No spec gives the reference app's keys on a Mac. **Every Mac key is one the common Mac layouts can press** (ours; **measured** from the layouts' own tables on a Mac, `UCKeyTranslate` of every key with each modifier, for US, ABC, British, German, Austrian, Swiss, French, ABC-AZERTY, Belgian, Spanish, Latin American, Portuguese, Brazilian, Italian, Swedish, Norwegian, Danish, Finnish, Icelandic and Dutch, 28 layouts on an ISO and an ANSI keyboard, through the path a key takes to the window as read from the pinned winit and egui-winit, not pressed by a person). With Command held the window takes a key's character from the layout's Command table, which ignores Option and, on many layouts (the US and British ones among them), Shift too; a letter of that table wins, and a character egui has no key for falls back to the key's US position. So a key with Command and no Shift is a letter, a digit, `-`, `,` or `.`, a key with Shift is a letter, and Option may be added to either; a test holds the Mac table to these lists (`every_mac_key_is_one_the_common_mac_layouts_can_press`). Command+| had arrived as `\` or a digit on all but Latin American, Shift+Command+, could not be pressed on the French, Belgian and Italian layouts, and Command+= arrives as `0` (Fit to Window) where `=` is Shift+0 (Portuguese, Icelandic, German, the Nordic layouts), so **Command+Plus zooms in too on a Mac**.
* What the ribbon map does not list is **ours**, and its captions are marked as ours (`confirmed` false in `ribbon/spec.rs`): the palette (ten
  greys, five rows of ten hues, made in code, and a box for any `#RRGGBB`); the weights (1/4, 1/2, 3/4, 1, 1 1/2, 2 1/4, 3, 4 1/2 and 6 points); the dashes
  (the solid line and the 22 patterns, each drawn as it is); the line ends (eight of the 45, for the begin and the end);
  Align's six items; Rotate Shapes' four. The Font list holds the machine's families, found **on a thread of its own** the first time it opens (a list with
  "Loading fonts" until it arrives), and takes a name typed in its box that no font has.
* **Bullets, indents, Change Case and text direction from the window.** The Paragraph group's Bullets, Decrease Indent, Increase Indent and the
  three text direction buttons, and the Font group's Change Case menu, send `text.format` through `format.text` and `format.toggle`; each shows the state of what it
  acts on and is one undo step. **Bullets** is a split button: its icon is `format.toggle {what: bullets}` (`bullet` 1, or 0 when every paragraph
  has one: half pressed when they differ), and its arrow opens the marks: None, Round Bullet, Circle, Square, Hollow Square, Diamond, Arrow and Check Mark, the seven
  of the table (**the ids and the captions are ours**: no page describes the glyphs), the row of the mark the paragraphs have being
  marked. **Decrease Indent and Increase Indent** are `format.text {indent}`. **Change Case** is a menu of four rows (Normal Case, All Caps, Initial
  Caps, Small Caps: the ids of the control list), the row of the case the letters show marked, and **Ctrl+Shift+A** and
  **Ctrl+Shift+K** are All Caps and Small Caps on or off: `format.toggle {what: all_caps}` and `{what: small_caps}`, which choose that case or Normal. **Left-to-Right and
  Right-to-Left** are `format.text {direction}` and show the paragraph's direction; **Vertical Text** is `format.toggle {what: vertical}`, the block's `TextDirection`,
  on or off. The specification says these three may be on the ribbon only for complex-script or Asian text: they are always drawn here (ours). **With a text box open** the buttons act as
  the others do: the characters selected get the case, the paragraphs they are in the bullet, indent and direction, a caret and no selection formats the
  whole text, the block gets the vertical text, the text is not rewritten, and the box stays open. The Text dialog has no Bullets tab, no Case box and no Vertical Text box
  yet (the ribbon is the only way in), and a text box being typed in is laid out across whatever `TextDirection` says (the page shows it down).

## Clipboard, duplicate and group from the window

The window's `clipboard.copy`, `clipboard.cut`, `clipboard.paste` (`x`, `y`), `clipboard.duplicate` (`dx`, `dy`), `arrange.group` and `arrange.ungroup` name the
selection and the page on view and send `edit.copy`, `edit.cut`, `edit.paste`, `shape.duplicate`, `shape.group` and `shape.ungroup`. They are listed by
`engine.commands` and are one undo step each (a copy is none). They cannot run while a text box is open (copy, cut, paste: its text has its own keys), while nothing
is selected, while the clipboard is empty (Paste), with fewer than two shapes selected or shapes in different groups (Group), or with no group selected (Ungroup);
the word of the refusal is the tooltip. Paste, Cut and Copy are the Home tab's Clipboard group; Paste is a split button, the face pastes and the arrow lists Paste, Paste Special
and Paste as Hyperlink (quiet) and Paste Duplicate (which duplicates); Group and Ungroup are in the Arrange group's Group menu. A right click on the page selects the shape under it (if it is not selected) and opens a menu
of Cut, Copy, Paste, Duplicate, Group and Ungroup. Add to Group, Remove from Group and Convert to Group stay quiet (gap U20); Format Painter works. The reference app was not
inspected: the rules below are **ours**.

* What is selected afterwards is what the command made: the shapes **pasted** or **duplicated**, the **group** after Group, the **members**
  after Ungroup, nothing after a cut. A paste from the ribbon or a key sends no `x` and `y` (a quarter inch right and down on the source page for
  each paste); **a paste from the context menu sends `x` and `y` of the right click** (the middle of the copies' joint box goes there). A duplicate sends no
  offset. The clipboard is the session's: kept through undo, cleared by a new or an opened drawing.
* Egui carries one family at a time on the system clipboard, text or an image (the last asked for wins). A copy or a cut puts **the text of the
  labels** of the shapes there, one shape to a line, the members of a group after it, in the order of the selection (at most 2,000 shapes and 100,000 characters); shapes with
  no text put nothing there and leave what another program put. **Nothing is read from the system clipboard**: a paste is the session's, and the paste event a
  desktop sends is dropped when no text field wants it. A picture of the shapes would need the page drawn from them alone.
* A cut sends the shapes selected and no more, so a connector glued to one that is cut stays and its end becomes a plain point,
  where the Delete key takes such connectors with the shapes. One undo step puts it all back.
* A drag that **starts with Ctrl (or Command) held on a shape** copies: the originals stay and the release sends `shape.duplicate` with the
  drag's distance (`dx`, `dy`, after the snap, Shift and Alt a move would take), not `shape.move`; the copies are selected. The drag shows the shapes following the
  pointer and the pointer is the copy cursor. **Ctrl is looked at once, at the press**: Ctrl pressed in the middle of a plain drag, or let go in the middle of a
  copy drag, changes nothing. A Ctrl-click with no drag keeps its meaning (the shape joins the selection or leaves it), done when the button comes up. A drag that
  ends where it began copies nothing. Ctrl on the page away from a shape is still a marquee that adds.

## Change Shape

Home, Editing, **Change Shape** (Change Shape; the ShapeSheet index's `LockReplace`, `PageLockReplace`, `DocLockReplace`, `ReplaceLockFormat`,
`ReplaceLockShapeData` and `ReplaceLockText`): the shapes selected are replaced by the master picked and keep what they are. The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked **ours** are DiaCraft's own.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `shape.change` | `ids` (at most 512), `master` (the id or the name of a master of a built-in stencil, or of the stencil `stencil` names: as `shape.drop`), `stencil`?, `page`? | `{page, master, name, changed, unchanged, skipped: [{id, why}], glue: [{connector, end, shape, glued, point?}]}`; for a master of a stencil opened from a file also `stencil`, `master_added`, `copied` and `notes` as `shape.drop` gives them. `master` is the `ID` the master has in the drawing. One undo step, and none when no shape changed. Enabled while the document's `DocLockReplace` is off |
| `arrange.changeShape` (the window's) | `master`, `stencil`? | `shape.change` of the shapes selected, on the page they are on; the shapes keep their `ID`s, so the selection stays, and what was left as it was is said in the status bar. Greyed out with nothing selected |

* Our rule: "Change Shape refuses across kinds". A two-dimensional shape is changed only to a two-dimensional master and a one-dimensional one (a connector: all four of `BeginX`, `BeginY`, `EndX` and `EndY` by inheritance) only to a one-dimensional master. **A shape that cannot be changed is skipped and listed with its reason in `skipped`, and the others are changed**: a mixed selection changes the shapes that fit, as picking a master does. A guide, a picture, a member that stands for a shape of its group's master (`MasterShape`: change the whole shape or ungroup it first), a group that holds shapes of the person's own (a master would replace them: change the members or ungroup it first), a shape with its `LockReplace` on, a shape of the other dimension, a shape whose nesting would pass `MAX_GROUP_DEPTH` with the new master's members, and a shape with more than 1,024 data rows are skipped. **A selection with nothing to change is refused in words**, with the first reason. A shape that is an instance of the master already is `unchanged`.
* The ShapeSheet index. `DocLockReplace` greys the command out and refuses it, `PageLockReplace` refuses it for the page, a master whose own `LockReplace` is on cannot be changed to ("as old or new shape"), and a shape's own `LockReplace` skips the shape. The page's lock alone is enough: a shape whose lock is 0 is refused too.
* Our rule. The shape keeps its `ID` (so the connectors glued to it, the formulas that name it, its layers and its place in the drawing order stay), its `Name` when the person gave it one (an automatic name `Process.5` becomes `Decision.5`), its text and the text sections, its hyperlinks, actions and user cells, and **every cell it holds of its own** (a cell with `F="Inh"` is no cell of its own, so what the old master supplied does not follow the shape and the new master's takes its place). The `Geometry` and `Connection` sections of a two-dimensional shape are the new master's: its outline and its connection points. A connector keeps its own route. A shape with no master gets one.
* Our rule: the brief says the size stays. The frame stays: the shape's `Width`, `Height`, `Angle` and flips are written as cells of its own when the old master supplied them and the new one would give another result, and the middle of its box is put back where it was when the new master's pin is elsewhere in the box (a pin that is a formula is left as it is). A connector keeps its `BeginX`, `BeginY`, `EndX` and `EndY`, and gets the formulas that put its frame between them (`connectors::frame_cells`) for each of `PinX`, `PinY`, `Width`, `Height`, `LocPinX`, `LocPinY` and `Angle` it inherited, so that they do not depend on the master. The members of a group master's new instance name the shape by its `ID` (`tie_to_group`), so they scale with the size the shape had.
* Our rule (leaves open what a swap does to data). The shape data rows the shape had, local or inherited, are given to it as rows of its own, **cell by cell and only where the new master would not give the same**; a row the new master has and the shape lacks is the master's. With `ReplaceLockShapeData` on the new master, a row it has too is the master's and the shape's own values for it are not kept.
* The ShapeSheet index. With `ReplaceLockFormat` on the new master the shape's own line, fill and text cells, its gradients and its style links are not kept (the new master's formatting beats them); with `ReplaceLockText` and a text of its own on the new master the shape's own text is not kept. **`ReplaceCopyCells` is not read**: the index says it names the cells carried over but not how it names them (a question for the check list).
* Our rule. A connector end glued to a connection point the new master has stays glued to it, and the recalculation moves the end. An end glued to a point the new master lacks, or to a member that is gone, is glued to the nearest point of the shape (the answer's `glue`: `glued: "point"` and `point`), and **let go where it is** when the shape has no point at all (`glued: "free"`). An end glued to the whole of the shape stays. More than 4,096 such ends refuse the change.
* **How it is made, and what it costs.** A new instance of the master is dropped as `shape.drop` drops it (so that the members of a group master, the pin and the name come out as a drop's), taken off the page again, and its `master`, members and `Type` put on the old shape, which stays where it is in its list; a one-dimensional shape needs no instance. At most 512 shapes, at most 50,000 new members in all (`MAX_NEW_MEMBERS`), the merged sheet is bounded by the limits of a sheet (`MAX_CELLS_PER_CONTAINER`, `MAX_SECTIONS_PER_SHEET`) before it is copied and its sections are found by a set (one look for each), the recalculation is one commit for the cells the change wrote and one for any pin it put back, then the connectors are glued again and routed once. A page at its cap of shapes refuses a change that needs a new instance in words. The shapes named are found in one walk of the page and each new instance at the end of the page's list where the drop put it, and each drop still walks the page once for the next free `ID`.
* **Files.** The change is cells, the shape's `master` and its members, so it is saved and read back in `.diacraft`, in VSDX (the model writer, and the splice into the file the drawing came from) and in `.odg` (the outline as the format draws it; an `.odg` has no masters, so the shape's master is not named in it).
* **What is left.** The gallery lists the masters of every open stencil that fit, and not "related shapes" (the specification gives no rule for what is related). A group master's new members are the master's: what was edited in the old members is gone. `ReplaceCopyCells` is not read. A shape on a locked layer is refused, in words that name the layer, as every command that changes the drawing refuses one it names (**Layers**).

## Page setup and text from the window

The window sends the commands of "Page setup" and of "Text boxes and the text block" from these controls (`docs/ui-parity.md` says what a person sees; the window's own commands are in `docs/control-protocol.md`):

| Control | Sends |
|---|---|
| Design, Page Setup, Orientation (Portrait, Landscape) | `page.orientation {orientation, page}` with the page on view (a background page is set up by showing it); the whole page is then put in view |
| Design, Page Setup, Size (a named size) | `page.size {preset, page}` with the page on view; the whole page is then put in view. The list is `diacraft_engine::draw::paper::SIZES`, grouped as it is |
| Design, Page Setup, Auto Size | `page.auto_size {on, page}` with the page on view and the state it is not in; the button is pressed while `page.setup`'s `auto_size` is true |
| Design, Layout, Connectors (Right-Angle, Straight Lines, Curved Lines) | `connector.style {style, page}` and, when connectors are among the shapes selected, `ids` (only the connectors: a shape that is no connector is left out); with none, the page's default; the row of the page's default is marked while no connector is selected |
| Design, Layout, Connectors, Show Line Jumps | `connector.line_jumps {show, page}` with the state the page is not in; the box is ticked while the page's `LineJumpCode` is above 0 |
| Design, Themes (a tile, or a row of the gallery) | `theme.apply {theme, page}` for the page on view; a right click on a tile, **Apply to All Pages**, sends `theme.apply {theme, scope: all}`. The tiles are `theme.list`'s `themes`, read once for each revision, page and selection |
| Design, Themes, No Theme | `theme.apply {theme: none, page}`; marked while `theme.list`'s `page.theme` is `none` |
| Design, Variants (a tile) | `theme.variant {variant, page}` (colours and styles together); the tile is pressed while `page.colors` is its place; quiet while the page has no theme (the window refuses with "the page has no theme: apply one first") |
| Design, Variants, Colors | `theme.variant {colors, page}`: the colours' variant alone, the styles left as they are |
| Page Setup dialog, OK | what changed on the tabs, in this order: `page.rename`, `page.type` and `page.background` (Page Properties: Type and Background), `page.size`, `page.orientation` (only when the orientation was chosen in the dialog), `page.scale`, `page.paper`; one command, or one `command.batch` labelled "Page setup" (one undo step). Nothing for what did not change |
| Page tabs, the plus | `page.add {like}` with the page on view |
| Page tab menu: Insert, Delete, Rename, Duplicate, Page Setup, Reorder Pages; drag a tab | `page.add {like}` and then the dialog on the new page's Page Properties; `page.delete {id}` (quiet on the only page that is not a background; the status bar says how many pages let go of a background that was deleted); `page.rename {id, name}`; `page.duplicate {id}` (the copy is shown); `window.pageSetup` on Page Size; `window.reorderPages` (it needs two pages that are not backgrounds); `page.move {id, to}` for a drag; `page.move {id, to, renumber}` for each press of Move Up or Move Down |
| Insert, New Page, Blank Page | `page.add {like}` with the page on view; the page on view stays, as it does for the plus |
| Insert, New Page, Background Page | `page.add {background: true, like}` with the page on view, then `page.select` of the new page |
| Insert, New Page, Duplicate This Page | `page.duplicate {id}` with the page on view; the window shows the copy |
| Text tool, the edit ends with characters in it | `text.box {page, x, y, text}` and, for a drag, `width` and `height` (all in paper inches, the middle of the box); a click on a shape sends `shape.set_text` as the Pointer Tool's edit does |
| Text Block tool, a drag released | `text.block_move {id, dx, dy}`, `text.block_resize {id, x, y, width, height}` (the corner at (0, 0) of the block's frame and its sides along its axes), `text.block_rotate {id, by}`; the block drawn is read from `draw::text_block_place` |
| Home, Paragraph, Rotate Text | `text.block_rotate {id, by: 90}` for every shape selected (one `command.batch`, one undo step, for several) |

* Our rule (the style goes to the connectors selected and, with the page itself selected, to the default for new connectors). The window sends the connectors among the shapes selected (`ShapePlacement.one_d`), and the page's default when it finds none, including with shapes selected that are no connectors. Show Line Jumps is the page's and never a selected connector's. The marks on Right-Angle, Straight Lines and Curved Lines are the page's default and are shown only while no connector is selected: the engine has no reader for a selected connector's own style (`connector_path` answers for a path of lines only, and a curve is not one).
* Our rule (describe the reference application's galleries). The Themes strip shows the first three themes of `theme.list` and the arrow the whole list under a **Built-In** heading; the Variants strip shows the four variants of the page's theme. A theme or variant applies to the page on view and not to the first page. **A tile under the pointer is previewed** on the page (the page is drawn from a copy of the drawing with the theme applied, and nothing is edited); a tile's tooltip is its name. A right click on a tile of the Themes menu shows the two rows under the tiles (a context menu cannot open inside a menu in egui, as the card of a folded group found: `controls.rs`, `fold_card`). The **Colors** menu lists the four colour variants (`theme.variant {colors}`), because the engine cannot give a page the colours of another theme; Effects, Connectors, Embellishments, Create New Theme Colors and Apply Theme to New Shapes have no command behind them and are quiet.
* Our rule (no specification says when the dialog applies). The Page Setup dialog works on a draft and applies on OK; Cancel and Esc send nothing. A size typed as a custom size is in inches, and turning the orientation on the Page Size tab swaps the two typed sizes. A size typed on its own makes the page the way its sides say: the orientation command goes only if the orientation was chosen in the dialog. The dialog works on the page that was on view when it opened (or the one named); a refusal (a text that is not a number, a page taken away) is told in the dialog, which stays open. The Layout and Routing and Alt Text tabs are drawn quiet and cannot be opened (no command), and the Print Setup tab's print zoom, sheets, margins, centring and grid are drawn quiet with the values the page's cells hold (no command writes them).
* Our rule (the tab menu's Insert opens Page Setup on Page Properties). Insert makes the page first (`page.add {like}`), shows it, and opens Page Setup on it, so a Cancel keeps the page (Undo takes it away): the dialog cannot come before a page that does not exist.
* Our rule (describes the Page Properties tab and the Pages group). The tab has the Name box, **Type** (Foreground or Background; Background is quiet, with a tooltip, for the only page that is not a background) and **Background** (None, and the drawing's background pages but the page itself and those that have it for their own background); OK sends `page.type` and `page.background` for what changed, in the one batch. **Background Page** adds a background page like the page on view and shows it, so that what is drawn on it is where the person is; **Duplicate This Page** and the tab menu's Duplicate copy a page and show the copy (a copy is the page on view in the window, whoever sent `page.duplicate`); **Blank Page** keeps the page on view. The tabs of background pages come after the others, set apart by a gap and a line, and are not dragged; the tooltip of one says it is a background page. The **Measurement units** box of the tab has no command and is not drawn.
* Our rule. The window reads the drawing again after any command that may grow a page (`shape.drop`, `shape.move`, `shape.align`, `shape.resize`, `shape.rotate`, `shape.flip`, `edit.paste`, `shape.duplicate`, `text.box`) when the page has Auto Size on. **A view of its own (one that is not Fit) follows a drawing that carried**: the distance a top-level shape the command did not name has moved is added to the middle of the view, so the shapes stay where they were on the screen. A view that follows Fit shows the whole page whatever it grows to. With no such shape (the first shape put on a page), the view stays.
* Our rule (no specification says whether the tool stays on). The Text tool is used up by the box it opens: the Pointer Tool is back while the person types, and the box is sent when the edit ends with characters in it (Esc, Ctrl+Enter or a click away), so a box left empty is never made. A click or a drag places the box on the grid, and Alt turns the grid off. While a new box is open there is no shape for the formatting controls to act on.
* Our rule ("moves, resizes and rotates the text area apart from the shape"). With the tool on, a click selects a shape (a top-level one) and draws its block: the rectangle, the pin it turns about, the eight handles of the block's own frame and a rotation handle above the edge that is the top of the text; the shape's own handles are not drawn. A drag of the block's body is a move (the block's middle goes to the grid), a drag of a handle a resize, and a drag of the rotation handle a turn in the steps of the shape's own rotation handle. Each is drawn from a preview while the button is down and sent once on release. The tool stays on until Esc or the Pointer Tool.

## Find and Replace

`edit.find` and `edit.replace` are Home > Editing > Find > Find... and Replace... (and "Replace text"). The window's two
dialogs run them (`crates/ui-egui/src/find_dialog.rs`, `window.find`); a program calls them straight, and neither opens a dialog. The engine is `crates/engine/src/draw/find/` (`pattern.rs` the pattern
and the search of one string, `walk.rs` the shapes in document order, `source.rs` the text of a shape that can be looked in, `replace.rs` the plan and the writes). The sibling Craft apps were searched
first: DeckCraft has `text.find` and `text.replace` (MIT or Apache-2.0), tied to its slides and paragraph runs, with no budget and a loop that is the pattern's length for each character of the text;
DesignCraft's is a regular-expression engine over its own compose crate; VectorCraft's `edit.findReplace` and WordCraft's `find_pattern.rs` work on their own text models (text objects, paragraphs
and styles). None runs on a ShapeSheet's text parts, rows and names, and none charges its work, so ours is written here and no code was copied (nothing is in `docs/provenance.md`); DeckCraft's whole-word rule (no letter, digit or
`_` beside the match) is the one adopted.

* **What matches.** `what` is literal text of 1 to 1,024 characters, with four special characters, the ones the Special list names: `^t` a tab, `^l` a manual line break (U+2028; a line feed
  ends a paragraph and is an ordinary character here), `^-` an optional hyphen (U+00AD), `^?` any one character, and `^^` is a caret (a caret before any other character is a caret). **The spelling of
  the four is ours** (the specification names the characters and not what is typed for them). A match is a run of characters that the pattern's equal one for one, so its length is the pattern's;
  matches never overlap. **Match case** compares the characters as stored, whatever caps formatting shows; without it a character equals its lower case when that is a single character.
  **Whole words** needs a match not to touch a word character, a letter, a digit or `_`, on either side, so `for` is in `for` and not in `forest`. Match character width and Match Kana type, which
  R19 lists for Japanese text, are **not here** (the window has no Regional option for Kana, which R19 says shows them).
* **Where it looks.** `in` is any of `text`, `shape_data`, `shape_name` and `user_cell` (the Shape text, Shape data, Shape name and User-defined cell), **`["text"]` by default** (R19 gives
  no defaults: ours). *Shape text* is what the shape's text block shows, a field's result in place (a date field is found by its date). *Shape name* is the shape's `Name`, or its `NameU` with no `Name`.
  *Shape data* and *user cell* are the `Value` cell of each row of the `Property` and `User` sections as the shape and its master together have it (the labels are not searched), so a row the
  master gives is found on each instance; a cell is a place when it holds text, and a number is found as its text. A match is told by its **place** (`text`, `shape_data`, `shape_name`, `user_cell`), its
  `row` for the last two, `start` and `end` in characters of the text the place shows, the shape's name and a `context` of twenty characters either side made safe to show. A shape that is a guide or is
  deleted from its master is not searched, and **a section of rows with more than 20,000 rows in a shape is not read** (its rows are charged, and the answer's `unread` counts such sections, so
  that nothing is dropped without being said).
* **Scope, order, Find Next.** `scope` is `selection` (the shapes in `ids` on `page`, and every member of a group among them), `page` (the page named, else the first that holds all of `ids`,
  else the first) or `all` (every page, in the document's order). The order is document order: pages in order, on a page a group before its members, and in a shape its name, its text, its shape data rows
  and its user rows. **`after: {page, id, place, row, offset}`** starts the list at the first match after the characters before `offset` in that place, and **`wrap`** (true by default) goes on from the
  start of the scope up to the place when the end is reached (`wrapped`); Find Next is `after` the end of the last match with a `limit` of 1. A place whose shape is not in the scope is no place: the
  list starts at the start. `limit` is 1 to 10,000 (100 by default) and `truncated` says there are more. `edit.find` is a query: it is not journaled and changes nothing.
* **Replace.** `edit.replace` replaces every match of the scope, or with **`at: {page, id, place, row, start, end}`** only that one, which must still be a match (the error says so in words
  and changes nothing). It is **one undo step** (`Replace All`, `Replace` for one) and none when nothing changed. A text is rewritten in **one pass** over its parts: the characters outside the matches
  stay in their runs, the new text joins the run of the match's first character, a match across runs takes the characters of the later runs out of them, and every marker and field stays.
  The ODF data of the text goes with it, as `shape.set_text`'s range does. A name's `Name` (and `NameU` when it was the same) takes the new name, which must be one a name may be
  (`diacraft_doc::check_text`). A cell is rewritten as it is kept: a quoted-string formula stays one with the new string, a constant stays a constant. **A match is left, and listed with why in
  `skipped` (the first 100, and `skipped_total` is exact), when** it touches what a field shows, when its cell is worked out by a formula, when a `GUARD` protects its cell, when the result is not allowed
  there (an empty name, a control character, a constant cell of text that would read as a number) or when the text would be more than 100,000 characters and longer than it was (a shorter text is always
  allowed; and the text of the whole replacement is within its budget). What reads a changed text or cell (`SHAPETEXT(TheText)`, a reference to a user cell) is worked out again for the page, once. `with` is checked as the text of a shape is (no control
  character but a tab and a line feed), and may be empty. The Replace dialog replaces **shape text** alone, as R20 lists no place; the command takes all four. **A shape on a locked layer is passed over**
  Every match of it is left with the reason that its shape is on a locked layer, and counted in `locked` as well as in `skipped_total`, whatever the scope (a page, the
  drawing, or a selection that holds a group, whose members go each by its own list); one match given by `at` on such a shape is refused in words, and `edit.find` still finds them. The Replace dialog's
  line says them apart (`Replaced 1 match. 2 matches on locked layers left.`).
* **What it costs.** Every search and every replacement is charged to one budget of **2^29 steps** (`MAX_FIND_STEPS`) before it is done, and refused in words past it (`the search is too large to
  finish`, with the way out): a step for each character passed or compared (a text that is plain and matches case goes by `str::find` and is charged a step for each byte), 8 for each shape passed, and
  400 for each row of shape data (counted over the shape, its master and the styles before any is merged), so a pattern and a text made to be slow (a thousand characters of `a` and a `b` against ten
  megabytes of `a`) cost what they are charged and no more. A listing is at most 10,000 matches and a Replace All at most 1,000,000 (every match counts, those that are left as well as those replaced); the contexts of a long text's matches are made in one pass over it. **A replacement writes at most 64 MiB of text in all** (`MAX_REWRITE_BYTES`): each text, name and cell is held to a text's longest, 100,000 characters, which is no cap on the drawing (76,000 shapes by a long `with` is 76,000 times that), and the process's memory budget is judged by `Session::edit_if` after the edit has made its copy, so the plan charges the bytes of every text it will make (an upper bound: the whole text a match is in, and `with` once for each match, and the plan's own) **before it makes it**, and is refused in words past the budget with nothing changed; `with` is at most 100,000 characters (a text's longest). `crates/engine/tests/it/find_cost.rs` runs 76,000 shapes by a 100,000-character `with`, as text and as shape data, and one text of 100,000 one-letter matches, each under `ulimit -v 3000000`;
  a walk goes no deeper than the model allows and visits at most a page's cap of shapes. `crates/engine/tests/it/find_cost.rs` counts it on a drawing of 76,000 shapes, a text of 10 MB, the worst pattern, 50,000
  matches in one text and a group 200 deep, and its ignored test times a search against a walk of the same drawing and against `str::matches`. **Measured** (development profile, a machine at a load of about 35, 2026-11-22): the text of 76,000 shapes in 13 ms (the same walk by hand 2.7 ms), the shape data of 76,000 shapes of three rows each in 0.55 s, a text of 10 MB in 1.1 ms for the plain case (`str::matches` 1.3 ms) and 40 ms folding case, and a refusal at the budget in 2.6 s; a row is charged 400 steps because it measured at about 2.5 microseconds against about 5 nanoseconds for a step.
* **The dialogs.** Home > Editing > Find has **Find...** and **Replace...** (the keys are Ctrl+F for Find, ours: the page names none for Find and gives Alt+H, FD, R for Replace, and Ctrl+H is
  Flip Horizontal, the specification's, so Replace has no key of its own). **Both are non-modal**: a floating window over the drawing area that does not shade the window or take its keys and
  pointer, with a close button, Escape in its box and `window.find {close: true}` to close it. Find what keeps the earlier searches (the button beside it), Special inserts the four characters, the scope is
  Selection (enabled when shapes are selected), Current page (the default) or All pages, the Find dialog has the four places and the options Match case and Find whole words only, a **Found in** line says
  where the match is (the text block, the shape name, the data row or the user-defined cell, with the shape and its page) and what a button did or why it could not, and **Find Next** runs `edit.find`
  from the match the dialog is at, then shows its page (`page.select`), selects its shape (`edit.select`) and moves the view to it when it is out of sight. **Replace** replaces the match the dialog is
  at and goes on to the next (with no match to be at it finds one first), **Replace All** is one `edit.replace`. Enter in Find what is Find Next. `window.find` (`mode`, `what`, `with`, `scope`, `in`,
  `match_case`, `whole_words`, `action`, `close`) opens it, sets its boxes and does what a button does, for a program; its state is `find` in the window state.
* **What is left.** Match character width and Match Kana type; replacing in names, shape data and user-defined cells is the command's and not the Replace dialog's; the data rows' labels and
  prompts are not searched; no regular expressions (the specification lists none); no Find in text being edited (a text box that is open is searched as the drawing holds it).

## Shape ids

* Our rule: a new shape or connector gets an `ID` above every `ID` the session has given on the page, above every `ID` in the page now,
  and above every `ID` in the file the drawing was read from or last saved to (`Session::id_floor`, a **high-water mark** per page). An `ID`
  that has been a shape's is therefore not given again in the session, though the shape was deleted or its drop undone: a formula that named it does not
  come to name another shape, and the writer does not meet a new shape where the file had a picture or a `Data1`, which it cannot write. A new
  drawing (`file.new`, `file.open`) starts the mark again. **A save keeps the mark too** (second): the baseline it replaces held the highest `ID` of the file the drawing was read
  from, and that goes into the mark first, so a shape of the file deleted before the save does not give its `ID` back to the next copy, drop or group (a `Sheet.<id>!` the delete left dangling would name it). The `draw` functions `drop_shape` and `add_connector` keep their earlier rule (the highest `ID`
  on the page plus 1); `drop_shape_above`, `add_connector_above` and `attach_connector_above` take the mark.
* The writer tells a shape by its `ID`, its `Type` and its master together, so a shape that has the `ID` of one taken out but is another thing is one
  removed and one added in the saved file, and not the old element edited.
