## Saving and the file on the disk

* **A file changed on the disk is not replaced without `force`**. The session records the size and modified time of the file it read or
  wrote. `file.save`, and `file.save_as` onto the drawing's own file, compare them with the file as it is before they write. If they differ, something
  else has changed the file since: the command ends with `EngineError::ChangedOnDisk` (its words say so and name `force`, not the path), and
  writes nothing, and the drawing is as it was. `{"force": true}` saves anyway. A program gets the error; a window asks the person and, if the answer is to
  replace the file, runs the command again with `force`. A file that is no longer there is written again. Saving as another file is not compared: the
  check is for the drawing's own file.
* **A link is resolved when the drawing is opened**. A drawing opened through a link, or saved through a link to its own file, writes the
  file the link led to then, and not where the link points at save time. Both `file.save` and `file.save_as` judge the extension of the file that
  would be written (`.vsdx`, `.vsdm`, `.vssx`, `.vssm`, `.vstx` or `.vstm`, in any case): `file.save` of a file opened from a name with another
  extension is an error, and `file.save_as` names the kind of file.
* **The six types**. A file is a drawing, a stencil or a template, with macros allowed or not, by the content type of its document part and not by its extension; `file.save` keeps the type the package has, and `file.save_as` takes the type from the extension. **A template opens as a new drawing made from it** (`file.open` without `edit_template`): the document is the template's content, the session keeps **no file's bytes** (NP10: the model holds every part of the template, and the first save is written from it) and **no path**, so `file.save` is an error that says to use `file.save_as`, `file.status` says `has_file: false`, the drawing is unsaved from its first edit as one from `file.new` is, and the template on the disk is never written. The first file saved from it has times of its own (`created` and `modified`). `file.open {edit_template: true}` opens the template itself. A Save As between the types rewrites the document part's content type and keeps every other part; a drawing or template saved as a stencil keeps its pages and says so in `warnings`; a stencil saved as a drawing or a template gets an empty page part for each page that had none.
* **Macros are bytes**. The VBA project of a `.vsdm`, `.vssm` or `.vstm`, with its signature parts and their relationships, is kept as it is by every save, never run and never read. Saving a file that holds one as `.vsdx`, `.vssx` or `.vstx` ends with `EngineError::MacrosNotAllowed` (its words say what the two choices are) and writes nothing; `file.save_as {drop_macros: true}` leaves the project out of the new file, names its parts in `warnings`, and says how many in `macros_dropped`. The drawing in the session keeps its file, so the macro file is untouched. **The window asks**: the refusal of a Save As is the card "Leave the macros out?" in the engine's words, and Save without runs `file.save_as` again with `drop_macros`; a program that asks the same save gets the error and no card, as a command never asks. `file.save` keeps the type and is never refused for macros, and no command refuses a save for an embedded object, so there is no card for objects.

## `.diacraft` files

`file.open` reads DiaCraft's own format, and `file.save` and `file.save_as` write it, so the window, the CLI and the MCP server reach it through those commands. The format is `docs/diacraft-format.md`, the package
is the `native` crate's (`diacraft_native`: `read`, `write`, `sniff`), and the engine's part is `crates/engine/src/nativefile`: what it keeps of the file, the memory a read and a save may use, the identifier ledger and the
content stamp. The window offers the format: its Save dialog lists the types the engine writes for the drawing, and its Open dialog lists DiaCraft drawings first. Which types a Save As writes is the engine's to say, and a frontend asks it with `Session::save_as_allows(extension)` (the save-path table asked without saving, so it answers what `file.save_as` would accept for a name of that type, and nothing for a `.fodg`, which no drawing is saved as) instead of keeping a table of its own; it is not a command, since it opens no dialog and changes nothing.

* The bytes decide, in this order. `file.open {path}` asks `diacraft_native::sniff` first (the first entry of the package is a stored `mimetype` that names a type of the format), then
  `diacraft_odg::sniff`, then the VSDX reader, **and neither of the others can claim a `.diacraft` file**. The kind the file says it is (a drawing, a stencil or a template) decides, and the extension only gets a warning: a
  `.diacraft` file called `a.vsdx` opens, and `file.warnings` says that it is called by another type's name. The answer is the document as `document.inspect` gives it, and `file: {variant: "diacraft" |
  "diacraft-stencil" | "diacraft-template", kind, format: "1.1", new_from_template, bytes, warnings: [..first 20..], warning_count, skipped_count, diacraft: {state: "absent"}}` (`format` is the version the file says;
  `skipped_count` is how many parts of a role this build does not know were kept as bytes). A file of a later major version is refused in words ("made by a newer DiaCraft (format 2.0)"); a damaged file or one past a
  limit is refused in words with the class and no path or text of the file; **damage confined to pictures, themes and kept parts does not refuse the file**: it opens and `warnings` counts what was lost. A file that cannot
  be opened leaves the open drawing as it was. A theme's colours and fonts are not in the file: they are read from its bytes at the open, as an `.odg`'s are (`odgfile::read_themes`).
* The memory is the process's. A read is given what is left of the process's budget beside `held`, everything the process holds (the open drawing and its file, the open
  stencils, every other session), as a reservation (below), and never more than `nativefile::open::READ_BUDGET`, the smaller of the process's budget (the VSDX reader's `diacraft_vsdx::MAX_MEMORY`, which every read reserves from) and the package's own `diacraft_native::MAX_MEMORY` (the package's is the larger, so the process's is the one a read is held to). A save is given `Limits::for_save(held)` (the figure the process holds in all, the model once, less the codec's own room for the tree of the child in hand, `save_child_items`, the tables of its check and the bytes of a part, out of the package's `diacraft_native::MAX_MEMORY`; the tree of a part is held to the items that fit). **The model is counted once**: the drawing's file holds the model the open read
  and its bytes, the session holds what it has grown by, and a save keeps no second model (the baseline shares what the drawing holds). A refusal for memory beside what is held says so in words that give the numbers
  (`not enough memory beside what is open`, `OpenProblem::OutOfMemory`, with the budget the read is held to named, `READ_BUDGET`: `HeldMemory::words_of`; a save's refusal names the package's own figure, which its room is taken from), and a file too large for the whole budget is told as it is told alone; **a `.diacraft` read holds the same reservation of what is left of the budget as a VSDX read does** (`file.open`, a template path and `stencil.open` take it, the first two waiting for a read that is running and a stencil taking a slice and waiting for none; the read is held to what was reserved, and never to more than `READ_BUDGET`), and its refusal says that a file is being read when one is; **the room is taken before the file is loaded**: the size of the file is looked at first (a path that names nothing, a folder or a pipe is refused at once, without opening it and without waiting for a read that is running), the reservation is taken, a file larger than the room it holds is refused from its size, in words (`out_of_memory` beside what is held, `too_big` when it is more than the whole budget alone) and not a byte of it is read, and the file is then loaded held to that room, so two large opens at the same moment no longer load two files beside a budget one of them holds; the children of `tests/it/native_memory.rs` (a file of 340 MB open alone,
  the same beside a hold of 1.9 GB and a template of 340 MB started from beside it (`file.new` of a `.diacraft-template` path), a save beside 2.3 GB, two sessions, and a drawing of 30,000 shapes read from a VSDX file, edited everywhere and saved, with a file of 150 MB read beside it; and `read_room.rs`: a file of 900 MB beside a read that holds the budget is never loaded by any of the four ways a file is read, and two opens of 900 MB at the same moment load one file at a time) run under `ulimit -v 3000000` and **assert their outcome**: a file beside a hold that leaves it no room is refused in words that say it is for memory (`out_of_memory`, `not enough memory beside what is open`) and opens once the hold is given back, and the others open; none is an abort.
* Two save paths, decided by the drawing's working file; the design's 4.1. `OpenFile::format()` is `diacraft`, `vsdx` or `odg` (`None` for a new drawing and one made from a template), and
  `file.save_as` **switches the working file** to the file it writes (Calls 15 and 16: `file.save` then writes it, in its own format, silently). `save_path` is a pure function of the working file and what the name says:
  a `.diacraft` drawing is saved as `.diacraft` (any of the three kinds) or as a VSDX type, **from the model alone** (the VSDX model writer, with no file to splice into, and the
  thumbnail, `Application` and `modified` kept when the content stamp says the drawing is the one its VSDX file held, and written as an edited save writes them when it is not or when there is no
  stamp), a VSDX drawing as a VSDX type by the splice writer as before or as `.diacraft`, an `.odg` as an `.odg` by the splice writer as before or as `.diacraft` (the model holds what the `.odg` holds, so the file loses none of it, and the package data and the named data of the file as it is now go with the drawing, `with_file_package_data`), and a new drawing as
  `.diacraft` or, from the model alone, a VSDX type. **A `.diacraft` drawing and a new one are saved as an `.odg` (a drawing) or an `.otg` (a template) from the model alone** (which lifted
  Call 18's refusal of them: `diacraft_odg::write_with(doc, None, ..)`, the model writer, makes the package, the kind of document the name says always asked for): the engine's own shapes are handed over flattened to what a shape of an
  `.odg` is, as a save into a file hands over its new ones (a shape that holds ODF data is in the file's form already and is left as it is), the copies taking at most a third of what a save may hold; a drawing started from a built-in
  template holds the VSDX package data of DiaCraft's own skeleton, which is no person's and which no `.odg` says, and is given to the writer without it (`odgfile/skeleton.rs`: only data that is exactly a built-in template's); **the thumbnail
  and the generator follow the content stamp** and never the dirty flag: while the drawing is the content its `.odg` held (a visit through `.diacraft`) the writer writes them as the model holds them and no picture is
  rendered, and otherwise, and for a drawing that was never read from a file, the generator is DiaCraft's and the first page is rendered for the thumbnail (at most 256 pixels). The file written becomes the drawing's own (an `.odg` with the
  index the writer gave), so a later `file.save` splices into it and an unchanged drawing is no edit; the answer is the `.odg` save's, `{bytes, variant: "odg" | "otg", kind: "drawing" | "template", form: "package", edited, macros_dropped: 0,
  dirty: false, warnings, warning_count, notes, carried, checked}` (`carried`; `checked`: the check after a save). **Refused in words, before anything is made or by the writer, and with nothing written:** a **stencil as an `.odg` or an `.otg`** (OpenDocument has no stencil format), and what the `.odg` cannot say that is a drawing's own, a background page (a master page that holds shapes) added to the drawing
  (the words say what is missing, that nothing was written and that a `.diacraft` file or a VSDX type holds the drawing; a VSDX file's package data and the drawing window's settings ride inside the file), and a
  **`.fodg`, which no Save As writes** (the words point to the `.odg` package). `file.save` of a `.diacraft` file writes a `.diacraft` file of the kind it has whatever the file is called, and **never turns a file into another format by its name**: whatever the table says, a name of another format than the file's (a VSDX package called `a.odg`, `a.otg` or `a.diacraft`, an `.odg` called `a.diacraft` or `a.vsdx`) is refused with the words of the file's own format (the name must end in its type, `file.save_as` is the way), so the guard holds once the writers across the formats are there. A name that is no type is refused listing the types. The link, folder,
  changed-on-disk (`ChangedOnDisk`, `force`) and atomic-replacement rules of the other types hold, and every save is made whole in memory and goes through `craft-atomic`.
  The answer is `{bytes, variant, kind, format, macros_dropped: 0, dirty: false, copied, warnings, warning_count, carried, checked}` (`carried`, empty for a `.diacraft` file; `checked`: the check after a save, made when the drawing came from a file of another format or a template file of one): `variant` is the extension written (`diacraft`, `diacraft-stencil` or `diacraft-template`) and
  `copied` how many entries of the package were copied as they were from the file the drawing was read from or last saved as. **A save that changed nothing writes the file it read**, and one that changed a page rewrites that
  page's part and the picture of the first page and copies the rest (**a page of more than 5,000 shapes has no picture, and a stencil has none**: a picture of 256 pixels of thousands of shapes is a blur, and the render of 20,000 shapes was 83 ms of their save; a package that holds none to copy gets one made); the same drawing gives the same bytes after a reopen (`native_reopen.rs`, with the clock pinned).
* Saves across the VSDX and OpenDocument formats; the design's sections 5.1, 5.6 and 6. **`file.save_as` writes the crossings**: a drawing opened from a VSDX file as an `.odg` or an `.otg` (`SavePath::OdgModel`), and a drawing opened from an `.odg` as one of the six VSDX types (`SavePath::VsdxModel`), each **by the model writer of the target**, with no file to splice into; `file.save` never changes a file's format by its name. **What the target cannot say rides in its carrier**, and **the result says what in `carried`: `{classes: [..], document_level, macros, objects, signatures_stale}`**, a structured field and never a warning, which every save answers (empty for a save into a file and for a save as `.diacraft`). The classes are plain words: "line ends", "gradients", "hatches", "glue directions", "layer settings", "style names", "document properties", "settings", "macro project", "embedded objects", "window settings" and "unknown parts", and "dash patterns", "fill images", "page layouts", "package details", "digital signatures", "shape data", "page data", "style data" and "document data" for what the design's list does not name; where the writer carried something that no finer class names, the class is "document data", so nothing is carried unnamed. The note's words are the design's 5.6, exactly, in `diacraft_engine::across::words(target, &carried)` (the window and any program word the field with it). **Options, never requirements:** `drop_macros` leaves the macro project out where the type cannot hold it or the file is of the other format (a macro project of either language goes into a VSDX file of a macro-enabled type only, Call 17: a save to `.vsdx`, `.vssx` or `.vstx` without `drop_macros` is `MacrosNotAllowed`, in words that name the macro-enabled types), and `drop_objects` leaves the embedded objects of the other format out of the file: **what is left of a shape that showed one is its picture** (the spec's question Q3, answered as the design says: its shape shows its replacement picture in both cases), and the drawing keeps them, so a `.diacraft` saved afterwards holds them. **The drawing after a save across to VSDX** adopts the model the file reads as (`Session::adopt_saved`, no undo step), when that holds everything it did (every item of carried data came back, and the options left nothing out): the file is the drawing's own, and a second Save As across in the same session is no longer refused ("a change to the picture of a shape"). **A picture an `.odg` held gets the cells of its box** when it is saved as VSDX (`ImgOffsetX`, `ImgOffsetY`, `ImgWidth` as `Width*1` and `ImgHeight` as `Height*1`: the picture fills the shape), because a `Foreign` shape is drawn by other programs only with them. **The style sheets of a VSDX drawing are carried by the `.odg` model writer** (the concern 1): the root's cells and names, and the links, names and cells of the others, ride in the document file as entries of `style` (`root`, `line-style`, `fill-style`, `text-style`), and what the root's element held rides in an item; a style sheet whose ID is not its place among the style sheets (a VSDX file's numbering has gaps) is carried too (the `id` of its entry), and two style sheets of one ID are refused in words. **What the engine still says it loses**: the links of a VSDX drawing's shapes to its style sheets, which the flattened shape does not carry (the `.odg` holds each shape's own values), in a warning in words; and the two `KINDS` rows (the text formatting a style sheet gives, the link to the Theme style), as before. The warnings no longer carry the note: `SaveNote::Carried` and the signature note are the field's. The VSDX writer says what it carried (`Written::carried`: items, parts, a macro project, objects), and the page's own item no longer goes stale after the trip (its stamp leaves out the `PageContents` and `Rel` entries the writer always makes). A VSDX drawing that holds masters is saved as an `.odg` with each instance written as the shape it shows: the flattened copy holds the text a shape shows through its master (a shape with no `Text` of its own shows its master's), the rows of `Character`, `Paragraph`, `Tabs` and `Field` that text names (the master's, with the shape's own cells laid over them), the cells that place the text block when it is not the whole shape, and the picture of a `Foreign` instance, so another program sees ordinary shapes; the link to the master stays and the master rides in DiaCraft's document file. A core property that an application writes as an empty element (a VSDX file's empty `dc:subject`, `cp:keywords` and the like) no longer refuses the save: the document item carries it. Still refused in words: a style sheet with no `Name`, a background page, a picture the `.odg` reader does not draw. **The save after a Save As, with no edit, is no edit**: a drawing saved as an `.odg` from the model (from a VSDX file, a built-in template or a new drawing) is the drawing's own file, and a `file.save` with no edit between gives the file's own bytes (`edited: false`). The splice is given what the model writer was: the drawing without DiaCraft's own skeleton when the first save left it out, and without what the Save As was asked to leave out (`drop_objects` gives each object's picture, `drop_macros` keeps the macro project out of DiaCraft's document file), on every save into the file; the version of the program a VSDX file names (`AppVersion`) is no field of an `.odg` and is not compared; and the model writer's index holds the drawing's own pages as the pages saved. Tests: `crates/engine/tests/it/odg_resave.rs`, `crates/odg/tests/it/model_resave.rs`. **What a Save As leaves out stays out of every save into the file it wrote, in every pair of formats** ("a Save As option holds for every later save into the file it wrote"). The drawing keeps what was left out (a `.diacraft` saved after it holds the object and the macro project, also after a Save As to an `.odg`, whose package data put on the drawing no longer takes the parts the file lacks). A VSDX file written from the model with `drop_objects` or `drop_macros` (from an `.odg`, a `.diacraft` drawing or a new one) records what it was asked to leave out (`OpenFile::asked`, the union of the Save As that wrote it and every later one into it), and the drawing becomes what the file reads as with what was left out put back on it (`across/dropped.rs`: the shapes that showed an object of an `.odg` and the macro and object parts), when every item the file carries came back and the check after the save found no difference. Each save into the file gives the writer the drawing as the Save As gave it (the objects as their pictures, `across::onto::given`; the macro parts out of a type that holds none) and compares it with a baseline made the same way, not with what the file reads as: a save with no edit is no edit with the same bytes, a move of a shape (the one that showed an object too) is written, and neither the objects nor the macro project come back into the file. A VSDX type that cannot hold macros gets `drop_macros` at each save into it, and a macro-enabled type holds the project whatever was asked, as it did. A Save As from an `.odg` to another `.odg` takes the two options: when the drawing carries a macro project or objects of another format (a VBA project or VSDX objects riding in DiaCraft's folder) the file is written from the model, as a Save As of a VSDX file to an `.odg` is, which leaves them out (a drawing the `.odg` model writer refuses, one that holds the style sheets of a VSDX file, is refused in words, nothing written); with nothing of the other format to leave out it is a splice, as before. **Closed:** an `.odg` that natively holds a VSDX file's object (written as an object frame) saved again with no edit is no edit, and an edit writes the object once: the file records that the model writer took the VSDX embedding out of what rides (`LeftOut::carried_objects`), and each save into it is given the drawing without it (`diacraft_odg::without_carried_objects`). A `.diacraft` drawing that holds an `.odg`'s frames, saved as a VSDX type with nothing left out, becomes what the file reads as, as a drawing read from an `.odg` does (`across::adopts_by_working_file`: the writer was given pictures an `.odg` held; a drawing that holds none and a VSDX drawing never do), so its next Save is no edit and an edit is written. A VSDX file whose page sheet has a `User` section before its `Layer` section, saved as `.odg`, is no edit on the next Save: the sections of a sheet are compared by name. **Closed:** an object of an `.odg` (or of a `.diacraft` drawing) saved as a VSDX type is held once by every save into the file, an edit too: the writer says which parts it wrote as embeddings (`diacraft_vsdx::Written::natively`), the file remembers them (`OpenFile::natively`), and each save into it gives the writer the drawing and its baseline without them (`across::natively::without_parts`), so the object's file does not ride in DiaCraft's part beside the embedding; the drawing keeps it. A drawing made from an `.otg` template that holds `.odg` frames has no working file, and the same gate takes it (the writer was given pictures an `.odg` held), so its next Save after a Save As to a VSDX type is no edit and an edit is written; a blank drawing's saves are unchanged. **Closed by ** (random chains of two to six saves in one session, from every kind of drawing, through every type a Save As writes, with and without its options and with edits between: `crates/engine/tests/it/resave_sweep.rs`): a picture the drawing holds by its bytes (inserted from a file, or read from an earlier file) is the picture its VSDX file names by a row of its own, so the Save after a Save As to a VSDX type is no edit and a move of the picture is written, and an embedded object is the same by its relationship and its picture's bytes; a picture inserted into a drawing whose file is a VSDX type is written by the Save, as a new page's picture is (a row of the page part to a part that holds it), on a blank page too, where it was saved with no `Rel` and lost; a Save with no edit into a file the session wrote keeps the program's name a model writer kept (the unedited visit); a Save As to `.diacraft`, or to another `.odg` from the model, of a drawing that stands on the `.odg` it was read from makes the drawing the model written, and a drawing whose `.odg` the session wrote from its own model takes no package data from it; the baseline of a VSDX save holds the parts the file holds (not a macro project it left out, the embedding and not the object's file it was made from), so a Save As from a `.vssx` to a `.vssm` writes the drawing's macro project and a drawing that becomes its file's model holds its objects' embeddings; a Save As that left something out puts back only what the drawing it was given held; a patch that leaves a part's bytes as they were is no edit; the user-defined fields of an `.odg` in a VSDX file are no change of its custom properties (the vector); and the entries of DiaCraft's folder in an `.odg` are what its document file names. Tests: `crates/engine/tests/it/resave_sweep.rs` (the chains, and one test for each class), `crates/vsdx/tests/it/new_pictures.rs`, `crates/engine/tests/it/drop_resave.rs` (every pair of formats, with a Save As that leaves nothing out; the object held once in every VSDX type after two edits, and by a second file written from the first; a template drawing's saves; a blank drawing's saves), `crates/engine/src/across/dropped.rs`, `crates/engine/src/across/natively.rs`, `crates/odg/tests/it/model_ole.rs`, `crates/vsdx/tests/it/objects.rs`.
* A new drawing is a `.diacraft` drawing; NP10. **A new drawing keeps its model and no file's bytes**, whether `file.new` made it blank or from a built-in template, or `file.new {template: path}` or `file.open` made it from a template file (`.diacraft-template`, `.vstx` or `.vstm`): `Session::source()` is `None`, the session holds the model alone, and the package a template was read from is let go before the drawing is made. The model it starts with is exactly what it was (`native_default::a_new_drawings_model_is_exactly_as_before` pins the content stamp of the blank drawing and of each built-in template, with the property times cleared, and page 1's `ID` is 1), and it holds every part its template's package held, as bytes. The drawing has no file: `file.save` says to use `file.save_as`, `file.status` is `{has_file: false, format: null, variant: null, kind: null}`, and **its first save is `.diacraft`** (the window sets its default extension itself): a Save As to a `.diacraft` name writes the package from the model and copies nothing. **A Save As to a VSDX type writes the file from the model** (`SavePath::VsdxModel`, the VSDX model writer with no file to splice into, as for a `.diacraft` drawing): its `created` and `modified` are the save's (`WriteOptions::new_copy`), the thumbnail is left out and `Application` is DiaCraft (there is no content stamp to say the drawing is as a file held it), and the lists of page and master names in `docProps/app.xml` are made from the model where the template's cannot be told (the one group a built-in template has names its one page: a drawing that has gained pages and masters would otherwise get the lists left as they were, and a warning). A blank drawing, which holds nothing of a package, is written from the writer's skeleton as before. **A drawing opened from a `.vsdx` keeps being saved as a `.vsdx` by the splice writer, silently** (Call 16): no note names `.diacraft`, and a Save As to another VSDX type splices into the file's own parts. A drawing opened from an `.odg` is saved as `.diacraft`. A `.diacraft` drawing or a new one is saved as an `.odg`. A VSDX drawing is saved as an `.odg` and an `.odg` as a VSDX type which lifted the last two refusals that named `.diacraft` as the way out. Tests: `crates/engine/tests/it/native_default.rs`, and `model_writer.rs` of the VSDX crate for the lists.
* A deleted shape's `ID` is not given again, also after a reopen; the design's 4.6. `state.xml` keeps, for each page, the highest shape `ID` ever given on it: the session's mark (`Session::id_floor`,
  which holds the file's own and every `ID` the session gave) is written, and a `.diacraft` open raises the session's marks to the file's (`Session::seed_ledger`). **A ledger raises a mark and never lowers one**: a row below the
  highest `ID` of its page reads as that `ID`, a row for a page the drawing lacks is ignored (a hundred thousand of them seed nothing), and a mark of 4,294,967,295 opens and ends the page's `ID`s in "no shape ids are left on the page".
* The content stamp; the design's 4.4. `Session::content_stamp()` is the fingerprint of the model's canonical encoding **as it was when the drawing was read from a foreign file** (a VSDX one), or as the
  `.diacraft` file says it (`state.xml`). A VSDX file arms it as pending at the open, and **it is made when it is first needed, not at the open and not at a save** (the pass was a third of the open's time, and a save of a VSDX file that nothing asks the stamp of would pay for it; the first save of a drawing of 20,000 shapes was 30 to 50% slower when it did). **The pending stamp keeps the document the drawing was read as** (a clone of the `Arc` taken at the open: no pass and no copy), and the stamp is made from that document, once, by a Save As to `.diacraft` and by `Session::content_unchanged()` of a drawing that is no longer that document; the document is let go of when the stamp is made. **The save of a VSDX file and `mark_saved` make no pass**, and a drawing edited after the open still reads as edited after any number of splice saves, because the stamp does not depend on the baseline a save replaces. **The kept document is memory the session holds beside the drawing**: the session's count includes it as a document that what lets go of a step never frees, the allocations it shares with the drawing and the history counted once, and the process is told of it with the rest of the session's hold; an edit that would be refused with it counted makes the stamp (which lets go of it) and is judged again, so a drawing is not refused an edit because it was read from a foreign file and saved. Whether the pass fits (`stamp_fits`) is asked when it is made, beside what the process holds then. It is one encoding pass in all for a
  foreign file, a part at a time, counted in `Session::stamp_work()`; it is carried by every save and by a Save As to `.diacraft`, and moved by no edit or undo. **A named VSDX file arms it and nothing else does**: a template
  opened as a new drawing and a new drawing saved as VSDX are not read from a foreign file, and a `.diacraft` file keeps its own in `state.xml`. `Session::content_unchanged()` compares it with the fingerprint of the content now (**one pass each time it is asked**, and none while the stamp is pending and the drawing is the very document the stamp keeps, which is `Some(true)`): `Some(true)` when they are equal (an edit that is undone makes them equal again),
  `Some(false)` when they differ, `None` for a drawing that never came from a foreign file (a new one, one made from a template, a `.diacraft` file with none) and for content that could not be fingerprinted
  (the pass is not made when it would not fit beside what the process holds, and the codec's budget refuses a model it cannot encode; **`None` reads as edited**, and the open does not depend on it). **The decision never asks the dirty flag**, which does not
  survive a reopen: a drawing edited after it was read from a VSDX file and saved as `.diacraft` is clean in the session that opens that, and `content_unchanged()` says `Some(false)`. The model writers write
  the bookkeeping the foreign file had while it is `Some(true)`, and an edited save's otherwise.
* Templates and stencils. A `.diacraft-template` **opens as a new, unnamed drawing** (`file.open` without `edit_template`; `file.new {template: path}` takes one too): the model carries everything, the
  session keeps no file's bytes and the template's identifier ledger still stands (spec 2.7), `file.save` says to use `file.save_as`, and the template on the disk is never written; the stencils it names are open, as for a VSDX template. `file.open {edit_template: true}` opens the
  template itself, and a save writes `.diacraft-template` with the built-in stencils open at that moment. A `.diacraft-stencil` opens as a drawing with `file.open`, and `stencil.open {path}` reads it as a
  stencil (a `.diacraft` drawing or template is refused in words by its content, not its name).
* `file.status` answers `{dirty, has_file, format, variant, kind}`: `format` is `diacraft`, `vsdx` or `odg`, `variant` the extension of the type the file was read as or last saved as (`diacraft`,
  `diacraft-stencil`, `diacraft-template`, the six VSDX types or `odg`) and `kind` `drawing`, `stencil` or `template`; the last three are `null` for a drawing with no file of a type (a new one, one made from a template).
* `Session::pin_clock(Some(seconds))` pins the clock every save reads the time from (`Session::now`): the VSDX writer's modified time comes from the caller, so a test that compares two saves byte for byte
  pins it. `None` reads the system's clock again.
* **The command line.** `diacraft-cli export` reads a `.diacraft` file by `diacraft_engine::nativefile::read_native`, the one way a `.diacraft` file is opened; `run --cmd file.open` and `--cmd file.save_as` reach the same commands.

## `.odg` files

`file.open` reads an OpenDocument drawing, `file.save` and `file.save_as` write one, and the window, the CLI and the MCP server reach them through those
commands. The writer is the `odg` crate's splice writer, which changes only what an
edit touched, in the file's own bytes.

* The file's own type decides, as for the six VSDX types. `file.open {path}` sniffs the package (`diacraft_odg::sniff`: the manifest's root row, else
  `mimetype`) and reads an `.odg` with the `odg` crate's reader; any other file goes to the VSDX reader as before. The answer is the document as `document.inspect` gives it, and
  `file: {variant: "odg", kind: "drawing", form: "package" | "flat", new_from_template: false, bytes, warnings: [..first 20..], warning_count, skipped_count, diacraft: {state: "absent"}}` (`form` and the flat file); `skipped_count` is
  what the reader kept in the file's bytes without modelling it. **The first of `warnings` says what the drawing does not hold**, when there is any: the line `What DiaCraft does not hold of this file: a save into the file keeps it, and a copy saved as another file (.diacraft or another format), or a new drawing made from it, is without it`, then a line for each class with its count: shapes of kinds kept only as placeholders (a dimension line, a callout, a form control, a 3-D scene), shapes in hyperlinks that a copy writes over the shapes that were over them, font declarations that repeat a name, style properties no copy writes (a default tab distance), and pieces of the file's markup that no part of the drawing holds. A file that does not open leaves the open document as it was, in words that hold neither the path nor the
  file's text. **A drawing template (`.otg`) opens as a new drawing made from it** (`.otg` is a format to open and save and no built-in template): `file` says `variant: "otg"`, `kind: "template"`, `new_from_template: true`, the drawing keeps no file's bytes (NP10) and has no file until `file.save_as` gives it one, and with `edit_template: true` the template file itself opens (the session keeps its bytes and its index, and a save writes a template). `file.new {template: path}` starts from an `.otg` too, a drawing is refused as "not a template", and the flat form of a template is refused in words. **An `.odg` read from a file has its content stamp pending**, so that a drawing that visits `.diacraft` and comes back unedited keeps the thumbnail and the generator it was read with. The session keeps the file's bytes and the `ShapeIndex` that maps each shape of the
  model to its element (`OpenFile::odg`, behind an `Arc`, so the session's snapshots stay cheap).
* A drawing is saved in its own format. `file.save` writes an `.odg` drawing to its file and `file.save_as` takes the type from the extension, but **an `.odg`
  is saved as an `.odg` and no other type, and a drawing of another type (a VSDX file, a template, a new drawing) is never saved as an `.odg`**: that is refused in words before
  anything is made (superseded: the model writer saves them). The link, extension, folder and changed-on-disk rules of the other types hold
  (`ChangedOnDisk`, `force`, a link refused unless it leads to the drawing's own file). The answer is `{bytes, variant: "odg", kind, form, edited, macros_dropped: 0, dirty: false, warnings:
  [..first 20..], warning_count, notes, carried, checked}` (`notes`; `carried`, empty for a save into the file; `checked`: `null` for a save into the file, which is not checked in 0.2) (there are no `parts`: the writer keeps every part it does not edit, and `edited` says whether it changed any). **The type a drawing is saved as is the type it was read as** (what the package is, `OpenFile::extension`: `odg`, or the VSDX type), not what its file is called, so an `.odg` called `a.vsdx` or `a.dat` is saved as an `.odg` and a VSDX file called `v.odg` as a VSDX one; Save As in the window starts with a name of that type, so the dialog offers it, and gives a name typed with no extension that type. `file.save` of a VSDX drawing whose file is called `.odg` is a failure that says to use `file.save_as`, not a bad parameter.
* **A flat OpenDocument drawing** (`.fodg`: one XML document whose root is the office namespace's `document`) **is read by every route, saved back as a flat file by Save, and saved by Save As as a `.diacraft` file, an `.odg` or `.otg` package or a VSDX type** (never as a `.fodg`). `file.open` tells it by its content (`diacraft_odg::sniff`, so the name decides nothing: a flat file called `a.odg` or `a`, and a package called `a.fodg`, are what they are) and reads it as the package that holds the same elements, so the model is the package twin's: the same pages, shapes in the same order, frames, looks, texts and glue, and the same picture of each page, and every edit the engine has gives the same drawing from either. `file` says `form: "flat"` (`"package"` for an `.odg`) and gives **first, among `warnings`, a note of what Save does with it** (Save writes it back as a flat file, and Save As writes it as a .diacraft file or an .odg package), and `OpenFile::is_flat` says it from the file's own bytes. The Open dialog lists `.fodg`. **`file.save` writes the drawing back as a flat file, in place, whatever the file is called** (a flat drawing is saved as a flat drawing; the file's own form is kept, as for a `.vsdx` and an `.odg`): unedited it is the file's own bytes and `edited: false`; an edit is spliced into the one document (what the edit did not touch is the file's own markup, byte for byte, and the generator says who saved it); the answer is the `.odg` save's with `form: "flat"`; no thumbnail is rendered, since a flat file has none; a second save is no edit; the changed-on-disk check and `force` hold as for any file. **What the flat writer does not write is a refused save in words**: a change of the document's masters (a shape dropped from a stencil brings its master), themes, colours or cells, a page added, taken away, moved or renamed; the words end with the way out of a flat file (`Undo the change (edit.undo) to save, or use Save As to save the drawing as a .diacraft file, which holds it: a drawing opened from a flat file is saved only as a flat file by Save.`), and the file and the drawing are as they were. **`file.save_as` is available for a drawing that came from a flat file** (had its `enabled` say no, with one reason, so that `engine.commands` listed it disabled, MCP's `enabled_only` left it out and the window greyed it out): its `enabled` is that of any drawing (a document is open), and it writes the drawing **from the model** by the name it is given, because a flat file is no package to splice into: a `.diacraft` file (the model reads back equal), an `.odg` or an `.otg` by the model writer (`SavePath::OdgModel`, as for a `.diacraft` drawing), or a VSDX type across the formats. The flat file is left as it was, the file written becomes the drawing's own, and `file.save` writes it from then on. What a flat file holds in its own form comes with the drawing: its `office:meta` and `office:settings` are held by the reader as a package's `meta.xml` and `settings.xml` are, a picture it holds inline is written as an entry of `Pictures/` under the writer's own name, and its content stamp is pending as a package's is, so that a visit through `.diacraft` gives the `.odg` the drawing is saved as directly. A flat file called `a.odg` saved by Save As over its own name is a package from then on. What the flat file holds in the markup of its containers that no field reads (`office:scripts` and the like) is not held, so a Save As does not carry it (`docs/gaps.md`, F23). Save As to a `.fodg` is refused for it as for any drawing: **`file.save_as` of any drawing to a `.fodg` is refused** (`BadParams`) in words that say Save As does not offer it, judged on the file that would be written; a `file.save` of a package that a person called `.fodg` is refused by its name as before (`the file name must end in .odg`). In the window Save As is usable for a drawing from a flat file as for any other (the command's own `enabled`, `command_enabled`): the Save dialog starts from the `.odg` the drawing is saved as and offers what the engine writes, never `.fodg`. A flat template (`-template`) is refused as a template is, and a flat file that is hostile (a DOCTYPE, UTF-16, another kind of document, a tree past the depth cap, a file cut off) is refused in words by the kind of trouble with nothing of the file in them and the open drawing as it was. The window keeps no list of recent files.
* After a save the session's file is the bytes the save made and its index is the one the writer gave, with the model's pages as the save had
  them; the next save compares the drawing with those and the file is not read again to find out. **The same drawing saved again is no edit and writes the same bytes** (`edited:
  false`, and a note is said once, not at every save), for every kind of edit (a move, a resize, a turn, a restyle, a retext, a delete, a reorder, a new shape, a connector, a page
  resized and a page split).
* Memory, `odgfile/hold.rs`. The writer's gate counts the drawing it is given, the file read again beside it, the package it makes and the model's growth since the read. It
  cannot see what the engine holds beside the drawing, which the save counts and takes from the memory it may use: **the undo and redo steps** (earlier versions of the drawing), **the pages the
  last save gave the writer** (the copy with its new shapes flattened), **the shapes the last save flattened** (and the model's shape each was made from) and **the shapes of the model that the
  copy replaced**. They are **measured**, never priced by a share of the model's cost over a count: every allocation is reached by a pointer from a version of the drawing, so what is counted
  is the pages, chunks of shape lists and shapes that the drawing given to the writer and the versions counted before do not hold, each once whoever holds it, **the members of a group
  walked as the shapes of a page are** (a group edited is its members, not one shape), each shape new to the walk priced by what it holds (its cells, rows, sections, text and kept attributes;
  a formula many cells share is priced once, a formula nobody shares at the tree's cost, 25 times its text). The walk stops as soon as the sum passes what a save may hold, so its cost is that
  of the drawing once, the new shapes and one look at each chunk of each version. A refusal for memory names the limit a save has (never what is left of it) and what the engine holds beside
  the drawing when that is much; a drawing whose history holds more than a save may hold is refused before anything is made. **A mass delete is no such case**: the history of a drawing
  of 4,000 rectangles with 3,990 deleted holds 4,000 rectangles, which is a few megabytes. The estimate is of the size of the model's own types and the text it holds, and it stands
  within a factor of three of the reader's own figure for what a model costs (a test holds it there); whole-drawing edits of 350,000 shapes are refused after one or two, by this count.
* `odgfile/flatten.rs`. The writer writes a new shape as a native element, and the engine's new shapes are not that: `shape.drop`
  makes an instance of a master that holds only its pin, a stencil shape links to its style sheet, a shape whose text was formatted holds `Character` and `Paragraph` rows, and
  `connector.add` writes neither route cell the writer reads to tell a connector from a line. The save hands the writer **a copy** of the drawing in which each shape the file does not
  hold is its flattened form; the engine's model is not changed, and `.vsdx` is written as before. A shape an earlier save flattened is **given again** (its copy of that save, at a
  step) when nothing it was made from has changed: the shape, the styles, themes, colours and sheet of the document, its master, whether a connector is glued and how a connector is drawn;
  else it is flattened again. A flattened shape **keeps its links to its master** (`Master` and `MasterShape`: the master is written into DiaCraft's document file, so a file read again has the shape linked to it) and has the three links of the root style; the frame cells, and the fill, line, vertical alignment and margin cells the
  writer has an attribute for, as constants of the values the shape resolves to (found through the master and the styles, a themed colour worked out, a palette index as its RGB);
  its `Geometry` sections, and the `Connection` section of a 2-D shape, merged with the master's, with the formulas the writer reads a basic shape by; for a connector (glued, or with the
  `DiaCraftRoute` marker, or with a route cell) `ShapeRouteStyle` and `ConLineRouteExt` as the route style it is drawn in; its plain text; and no id of the file's (`xml:id` and `draw:id`
  of `raw` are never copied, and a duplicate or a paste drops them from the copy). A group is copied with its frame as constants, its own other cells and sections as they are, and each member flattened where it is in the group. **Every master of the model is the copy's**,
  the file's own and the ones a drop added: the writer writes them into the document file, and a master stays in the model when its instances are gone. **`DrawingSizeType` is left out of the copy of a page of a flat drawing** (no attribute holds it, and a flat file has no document file; a save into a package keeps it, which DiaCraft's document file carries, as the model writer does)
  and **a colour a person chose (`RGB(r,g,b)` of the value the cell shows) is a constant in it**, since the writer refuses a formula in a style cell.
  **Nothing on a new shape is dropped without a word, and almost nothing is dropped at all.** The copy **carries everything the shape holds itself**, as it is: rounded corners, a shadow (a shape that has one from a style sheet is given the cells as constants), a text block that is not the whole shape (a block moved with `text.block_move`), shape data (`Property` and `User` rows beside the route marker), the layer (`LayerMember`), the text's background and direction, the `Character`, `Paragraph` and `Tabs` rows, the markers of runs and the fields of the text, and any other cell or section: the writer carries them in DiaCraft's own data on the shape's style, so the file read again is the same picture. What the master holds is kept by the link. **Two kinds of loss are left, classified and said** (how many new shapes lost it and the first; said again only for a shape that loses something new): **text formatting** that **a style sheet** gives the text (the rows of the shape's own `TextStyle` link, its master's, and each sheet along the chain but the root style, whose rows are the defaults of every shape), and **the link to the Theme style** of a shape whose three links are not that one style. Inert is `ResizeMode`, which the engine writes by itself and the file does not need, and the place of a label on a line or a connector that holds no text, which the copy leaves out (carried, it is written into the style of every connector and made the second save of the drawing an edit). What the writer refuses in a new shape (a link to different style sheets for its three families, a picture that is turned or flipped, **A shape read from an `.odg` is written back as it was read** when the drawing goes through `.diacraft` and is saved as an `.odg` (the model path): a `draw:rect` stays a `draw:rect`, on the style name the file gave it, with its lengths in the units the file wrote them in; a moved one stays a `draw:rect` with a new `svg:x` and `svg:y`, and keeps its style name while the style's own cells are as they were; the layer and the text style name are written only where the file had them. This is no loss of the engine's own copy: what the copy carries is as before.
  a group that is turned or flipped; a dash, a gradient, a hatch and a turn or a flip are written now) stays a refusal, in its words, and says the way out (see "What is refused"). The work is counted (a step for each shape visited, cell resolved and audited, and row and cell of a
  section) and held to 6,000,000 steps, about 74,000 new rectangles in one save, before it is done, in words; and **each copy is charged to the memory a save may hold before it is
  made** (the shape the model keeps beside it, and the copy), by what the shape holds (`hold.rs`, `shape_bytes`).
* A new shape copied from a shape the reader read can hold attributes and elements of the file's own that the model has no field for, shape data
  (`Data1` to `Data3`) and foreign data. The save is not refused (copying a shape is common); a warning says how many attributes and elements and which of the two other kinds the new
  shape held that the file does not, once (`SaveNote::NewShapeUnwritten`). The attributes and the geometry element the writer writes itself are not counted. What the model holds in cells, sections and text that the writer does not write is told.
* An edited save writes a PNG of the drawing's first page, of at most 256 pixels on its long side, on white paper, as the window draws it (a frame's
  own limits hold), as the package's thumbnail; an unedited save keeps the file's own, byte for byte. A drawing that cannot be drawn leaves the file's thumbnail and says so in `warnings`.
  **The first save of a drawing that has no file yet** (a new drawing, or one started from a template: `OpenFile::is_named` is false) also writes the picture when nothing was changed, into a package that has no thumbnail, with its manifest row and no other change (`WriteOptions::first_save`); a package that has one keeps it. A drawing that has a file and is not edited is saved as the file's bytes whatever the session says of changes. **It takes effect once a new drawing can be saved as an `.odg`:** on main no command makes a drawing with no file and an `.odg` package (a new drawing's save as an `.odg` is refused), so a test that makes one is what runs it. A first save whose picture cannot be made writes none and says so in `warnings`; an edited save writes no second manifest row for a thumbnail the manifest lists already.
* An object DiaCraft cannot draw and has no picture of is drawn as its frame's outline, a pale fill, a cross and its name (a table, an embedded object, a 3-D scene, a dimension line, a callout, a form control, fontwork; the name is the cell `User.ODFObject.Value` the reader gives it), the label is left off when the frame is too small to read it in, and the status bar's list says "N objects DiaCraft cannot draw, shown as labelled boxes". The labels have a layout budget of their own, beside the file's text (`Limits::max_label_chars`, `Limits::label_time`). **A copy of one, or one an undo brought back after a save deleted it, is refused in words when the drawing is saved as an `.odg`** (its element and the files that go with it are not copied yet; the way out is to delete it). **A copy of an object that has a picture, of a text box that holds the frame of an object, or of a new group that holds either at any depth, is refused in words the same way** ((B1, owner); odg-shapes).
* A signed drawing keeps its signature parts as they are, and an edited save says it no longer matches (`warnings`); an unedited save says nothing.
* A new line is a line feed: the text commands refuse a carriage return (a control character other than a line break and a tab, `BadParams`). A model that has one by
  another road (code that builds a model) is refused by the save, in the writer's words.
* Milestone-1 class F1. A save keeps to the version the part declares in `office:version`, and never raises it. A part of ODF 1.0 or 1.1 (or one that declares none, which the schemas
  of those versions read) is given **no `xml:id`** (a shape a new connector is glued to gets `draw:id` alone, `id` and the next number, and glue is by `draw:id`) and **no `svg:d` and no `svg:viewBox` on a `draw:connector`** (a new
  or routed again connector is its `svg:x1` to `svg:y2`; an `svg:d` or `svg:viewBox` such a connector already holds goes when it is routed again, since it describes a route the ends no longer say, and its draw:line-skew goes with it, as it does in every version). The save says so: one note for each such connector, naming it and its page (class route_dropped_for_version), also when the file held no path for it, since the route DiaCraft has is not in the file. The saves of every kind of edit
  of the first milestone, in documents of 1.0 and 1.1, were checked against OASIS's RelaxNG schemas of those versions with `xmllint`, and each part is valid. A part of 1.2 or later is written as before.
* Class F2. An edit routes again only the connectors glued to the shapes it changed; every other connector keeps its bytes. The line-jump rebuild (`draw/jumps.rs`) reaches every connector whose
  legs cross the old or the new route of one a command routed, and it rewrote the rows of each, in the form the engine writes them (a route a file made, a point on a leg, other formulas), though the connector needed no jump: the
  model's connector changed and the save wrote its `svg:d` again. A connector no command routed is now rewritten by that rebuild **only when a hop is made or taken away** (its rows or its new path are not a `MoveTo` and `LineTo`
  rows only). A hop is a decoration the `.odg` has no place for: the save keeps the file's own path for such a connector and says it could not write the route. A save compares a connector's route with the route the file holds by its corners on the page, hops taken out, so rows that only spell the route another way, or a hop taken away again, are no new route and the connector keeps its bytes and its draw:line-skew. After a save that kept the file's path, the route the file holds is that path, and a later save writes the model's route once its hop is gone (a change of the page's height keeps `PinY`, so every end moves down the page by the change and the file's path goes with it: a connector no edit touched keeps its own path shifted by the change, its draw:line-skew and `svg:viewBox` as they were, a route with a hop included, and the kept corners are the same whatever the height). A route kept with a word is named once, in the save that kept it, until it changes, and **a change of the page's height does not name it again** (a path with two subpaths is no hop, and is shifted and never said). A path the reader reads that begins with no move has `M 0 <shift>` put in front of it, so that it moves exactly, and a number that moves keeps four places, or the places it had when it had more.
* Class F4. A colour set, or a gradient turned off, on a shape whose edit says nothing of `FillPattern` writes `draw:fill="solid"` when **the model's fill pattern** is 1: the pattern of the
  shape's own sheet, else the one its style sheets give (the first that holds one, ending at the root). The model's gradient (`FillGradientEnabled`) is found the same way, the shape's own cell first and then the style sheets', and
  when it is on nothing is stated, since the colour is not the fill. So a solid colour set on a gradient, a hatch or a bitmap fill leaves no `draw:fill-gradient-name`, `-hatch-name`, `-hatch-solid` or `-image-name`, and one set on a shape
  whose styles state no fill mode states `solid`. A pattern of 0 is never stated by an edit that does not change it (the reader reads `none`, a bitmap and a hatch that is not solid as 0): the colour is written alone, and the fill the
  file states stays, with its image or hatch name. Where the shape's own style already states the same, nothing changes.
* Class F5. A turn or a resize of a shape that is flipped writes the turn of its frame **without the flips the file holds** (they are `draw:mirror-horizontal` and
  `draw:mirror-vertical`, which stay), so a shape flipped both ways reads back at the angle asked, and **beside exactly one mirror the turn written is minus the model's**, as it is for a new shape flipped on one axis: LibreOffice
  26.8.2.2 draws a single mirror after the turn. A change of the flips is not written, on one axis or on both (two flips of a shape turn its frame half a turn, which the mirrors do not follow), and nor is a turn of a shape whose
  `draw:transform` holds a negative scale: each is a refusal, in words. A new shape with `FlipY` is written with `draw:text-rotate-angle="180"` (LibreOffice turns the text of a vertically mirrored shape half a turn more, and the
  text then reads as the session's), a new connector glued to point 1 or 3 of a shape with exactly one flip names LibreOffice's point 1 or 3 on the frame it draws (rows 1 and 3, not 3 and 1), and a new connector whose route has
  line jumps is written as the corners of its route, its rows (the hops) carried in DiaCraft's own data. **A turn of a shape of the file that a connector is glued to at a
  `draw:glue-point`, or at a standard point of a polygon, a polyline or a path, is refused in words**: LibreOffice places those points on the box the shape takes up
  on the page, which a turn moves elsewhere than the model's rows, and the writer has no inverse of that placement yet; a resize is saved, the rows being sums of the shape's size,
  and a turn of a shape glued only at a custom shape's standard points or listed points is saved. A frame's `draw:glue-point` is one of those points (LibreOffice
  places it as a custom shape's), so a turn of a frame glued at one is refused too, and a move or a resize of it carries the end glued to it. A new shape whose connection points are not the four side middles is written
  with them as its geometry's own glue points (`draw:glue-points`, their ways out as `draw:glue-point-leaving-directions`) and `diacraft:glue="own"` on its style, and a new
  connector glued to its row *r* names glue point *r* + 4 (LibreOffice draws such points with the geometry, mirrored and turned with it), as does one glued to a shape of the
  file whose style says so. A move of a shape whose `draw:transform` gives its translation as plain numbers (hundredths of a millimetre, as LibreOffice reads them) writes
  the translation back as plain numbers.
* A page written as an empty element (`<draw:page .../>`, which LibreOffice writes for a page with no shape) is opened and closed round what is put in it; a `content.xml` with no
  `office:automatic-styles` gets the element, empty, before `office:body` and after `office:font-face-decls`, with the new style in it (written only when a style is put in it); and a manifest with the OpenOffice.org document type
  declaration (an external id and no internal subset, which the reader reads and ignores) is scanned by the save as the reader scans it, so deleting a frame that holds an object, or any save that adds a manifest row, is not refused for it.
* `BeginArrow`, `EndArrow`, `BeginArrowSize` and `EndArrowSize` are written as `draw:marker-start` and
  `-end`, with `-width` and `-center`, on a new connector and on an edit of an arrow cell or the weight of a shape of the file, by the restyle rules (a shared style is cloned, an orphan taken out).
  **Every number from 1 to 45 is written, in every size class from 0 to 6.** The marker is DiaCraft's own, a `draw:marker` in `office:styles` of `styles.xml` (added after the last element), named
  `DiaCraft_20_end_20_<n>` for a mark with no band (2, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 11, 13, 39, 42) and `DiaCraft_20_end_20_<n>_20_t<k>` for a mark drawn with a band (a hollow or open part is a filled band one
  line weight thick, so the marker follows the band class k = round(100 w / `S`), 1 to 99), with the display name (`DiaCraft end 16 t23`) always written, since LibreOffice builds a marker's name from it. A marker the
  file holds under a name is used as it is, whatever it draws: it is DiaCraft's by its name alone, and a program that saves our file rewrites its path and rounds its curves; only the markers
  the file lacks are written. **A marker of DiaCraft's name that the save left no style naming is taken out**: of those the styles of `content.xml` named before the save and no longer name,
  not one a common style or a style of `styles.xml` still names, and never another producer's; a marker no style named before is left alone. The width is the spread (the for the numbers it
  does not measure) times `S` of the size class at the line's weight (class 6 is the base, 18 pt), written in points to four places (`6.54pt` for number 13, class 2, 0.75 pt); a disc or a square, solid or hollow, and the slanted stroke through the vertex (9, 10, 11,
  20, 21, 42) are centred on the vertex (`-center="true"`). A size cell that is not there is class 2, a weight that is not there 0.75 pt. A number of 0 writes the empty marker name (the style then draws
  no end whatever its parent does) and takes the width and the centre out. A change of the line's weight writes again the name and the width of the markers of DiaCraft's own that the shape's own style names (a
  marker of another make keeps the width the file gives it). **The art is ours**: each marker is one path of the union of the mark's pieces, drawn in whole thousandths of `S` with holes running against
  their outer contour, so that LibreOffice's even-odd fill and the nonzero rule give the same mark (`marker_art.rs`; every number at every band class is built and checked against its pieces by a test, the work is
  counted: 3,475 markers in all, the largest needing 2,736 steps; **the 1,024-byte bound on a path is on the markers the writer draws only (the longest is 499 bytes): a marker the file holds under our name is used as it
  is, whatever its path, and the part's own limits are the only bound on it**). **The custom end and what has no art** (R32 refused them until tier 2 was written): the custom end (254) is written as **number 4's marker** at the cell's size class (`DiaCraft_20_end_20_4` at 6.54 pt
  for class 2 and 0.75 pt) and **its cell is carried** by DiaCraft's extension on the shape's own style (the formula, `USE("...")`, and the value 254), since the reader rebuilds 4 from the marker, so the model reads back as it
  was; a new picture has no extension to carry it, so a picture with a custom end is refused in words. A number past 45 and a size class outside 0 to 6 have no art: the save writes no marker for them and the extension carries
  the arrow cells (a marker written for a size past 6 would be read back as 2). **A line end on a line whose weight is more than a million points** (13,888 inches: the width of the marker follows the weight and would be `inf` or hundreds of digits) has no marker either: in a new line the end is carried with no marker and its cells in tier 2, as for a size past 6; **in a weight edit of a line that names a marker of ours, and in an end set on a line the file holds, it is refused in words**, and a size cell that is no class is refused in a weight edit too, and not taken for 2. The engine's copy of
  a new connector carries its line ends. **A line end another program changed is told (the third sentence):** the custom end 254 is drawn as number 4's marker and its cell is carried with a fingerprint of the marker as the file has it; when another program sets the end to another marker (`Arrow` in a LibreOffice run) the reader gives the stand-in 4 and no 254, and a rewrite of the marker's width (LibreOffice's 0.231 cm) is no edit and keeps the 254.
* A text is compared with the file's paragraph by paragraph: by the characters,
  the cells of the `Paragraph` row and the cells of the `Character` row of each run, **never by the `IX` of a row** (the engine numbers the rows of a text anew when it formats part of
  one), and a plan keeps the paragraphs that are the same, edits those that differ and inserts or takes out the rest. A kept paragraph keeps its bytes, and **a paragraph written again keeps the `text:span` of each run that is unchanged** (the run lies in the head or the tail the two paragraphs share, in the same place and with the same row, and its span is one element that covers it: ownership R8), with its `xml:id` and whatever else it holds. An edit inside one run of raw text
  of a paragraph whose runs and row are the same is still spliced in place (a span keeps its bytes, a bookmark or a soft page break stays). Any other edit, across a run, at the
  edge of one, a changed row, the whole label replaced, writes the paragraph's content again from the model: a run with a row is a `text:span` whose automatic text style is the span's
  own for the row when the paragraph had one, else a style the part holds with exactly the row's attributes (a style of no parent whose one properties element is of the kind and which
  names nothing else), else a new one named by R27 (the prefix most of the file's text styles use and one more than the largest number of either part: `T13` beside `T9` and `T12`); a
  paragraph's own style changes only when its row does, by the same rule (`P`). A text style whose last span goes is taken out as an orphan. The attributes are inverted
  (`fo:text-align`, `fo:margin-*`, `fo:text-indent`, `fo:line-height`, `style:writing-mode`; `fo:font-size` in points, the bits of `Style` as the weight, style, variant and the two
  complex ones with the underline, the strike-through and the overline from their cells, `fo:text-transform`, `style:text-position`, `fo:color`, `fo:letter-spacing`, `style:text-scale`,
  and a face as the `style:font-name` of the part's own `style:font-face` for it, **declared in the same file's `office:font-face-decls` when the part has none for the family** (the model's table of faces is no field the save compares)), a palette index of a `Color` as its RGB (`#rrggbb`: every colour the writer writes, `fo:color`, `draw:fill-color` and `svg:stroke-color`, is a `#` and six hexadecimal digits, as the schema of the format has it), **and a colour a person chose (`RGB(r,g,b)` in a row of the model) as `fo:color`: the engine's copy makes it a constant, as it does a shape's colours.** An inserted paragraph is a `text:p`; **beside a list item whose row it has it is a new `text:list-item` of that list**, with that paragraph's style; a paragraph taken out takes its list item when it is the item's only child. **Where no neighbour has its row it goes outside any list**: after the outermost list the paragraph before it is in, or before the one after it is in (the reader skips a `text:p` that is a child of a `text:list`, and so does LibreOffice), and between two items of one list it is refused in words (the specification does not say how a list is split). **The paragraphs put in at one place are written in the model's order:** one that has the row of a list item beside it would be a new item of the list, which can be ahead of a paragraph already put in after the list, and is written after that paragraph and outside the list instead; one that would fall between two items of one list (it is after a paragraph that is a new item at the start of a list) is refused in words. **Refused, in
  words:** a link or any element or reference the writer does not map (a change mark, an index mark; a bookmark, a reference mark, a soft page break and a text field are put back); a tab stop marker or a piece of markup of another format the model keeps among a text's parts (**a page number, page count or file name field is kept **: its shown text is among the paragraph's characters and its element stays where the file has it, as any field's); a format that cuts through the characters of a field; a paragraph put between two items of one list where neither has its row; a list item whose paragraph settings change; a text format set where no character is (an empty shape, or an empty paragraph the edit leaves with a row of its own or the characters of a formatted run deleted from the end of a paragraph: the cells of a row that no character uses are not written, and the file would read as having none; a row that no marker holds in force, or that only a stretch of no character amid characters does, is no format a person sees and is saved); a bullet that is more than one character (`BulletStr`) or a bullet's font that is a number other than 0; a list item that holds more than its paragraph, a list header, or another list, next to a paragraph added or taken out; a font that is a number of the document's table other than 0, or a family that cannot be a name; a cell of a row
  that has no attribute here (`Locale`, `Tabs`, a formula); a shape whose file holds no `text:p`. The new shapes' own text keeps its formatting told, not written. The work is counted: each
  paragraph and run of each model is walked once, the plan is linear, **each paragraph of the file is tokenized once however many paragraphs are put beside it** (the bytes are charged
  before they are tokenized, `WriteWork::tokenized_bytes`), a text of more than 2,000,000 paragraphs or 16 MiB is not rewritten, **the lists the retext makes of the paragraphs (about 250 bytes each) are charged to the memory a save may hold before they are made, the names of the faces are indexed once, and the package made is read again through the reader, once, when a paragraph was put in, taken out or made a list's, to count the paragraphs of each shape that changed** (`WriteWork::read_back_bytes`).
* A paragraph whose `Paragraph` row has a list's cells (`Bullet` not 0) and that no neighbour has the row of is the item of a
  `text:list` the save makes, with an automatic `text:list-style` of one level named by R27 (`L` and a number when the file has no list style): `text:list-level-style-bullet` with the character of
  `Bullet` 1 to 7 (U+2022, U+25C6, U+25AA, U+25A1, U+2756, U+27A2, U+2713) or `BulletStr`, the face (`style:font-name`, declared as above; the number 0, the text 0 and nothing name none) and size (`fo:font-size`) of `BulletFont` and `BulletFontSize`, and the label's `fo:margin-left` and `fo:text-indent` of `IndLeft` and `IndFirst`; the other cells of the row are the paragraph's own style. The items that come so in a row at one
  place are the items of one list (two bulleted paragraphs and a plain one are one list and a paragraph), and a plain paragraph of the file that is given such a row is wrapped in a list of its own. A
  `Bullet` of 8 or more with no `BulletStr` is refused in words.
* The review to 3, to 4. **Style sheets in a saved `.odg`.** A style sheet the
  model adds is an empty common `graphic` style at the end of `office:styles`, its sheet is an entry of DiaCraft's document file, and a shape that names it as its three links has it as
  the `style:parent-style-name` of its automatic style. **A style sheet of the file is named by the name its own markup gives it** (`style:name`, as the read kept it), and not by the
  `NameU` the document file may have restored to the model (a `My Theme` the file holds as `My_20_Theme`): a parent written by the model's name named no style, and a second style of
  that name was saved over the first. The characters of the name are the ones XML 1.0 gives an `NCName`; `ª`, `µ`, `²` and `½` are written `_AA_`, `_B5_`, `_B2_` and `_BD_`. **What is
  written is read back**: the style sheets added, taken out or carried, and the links of each shape whose parent was written (a parent the file does not hold is a refused save, never
  a shape read back on the root); a package of more than 16 MiB is not read back, as the checks of the paragraphs and of tier 2 leave one unchecked. **A style sheet the model no longer has** (an undo of a saved `theme.apply`) **is taken out of the file** when it is the last of the file's and its
  markup is an empty common style: the element, its entry of the document file and the parents that named it; any other (a style whose markup holds a property) is refused, and the
  words say that an undo took it back, so that the way out is a redo. **The cells of an empty common style the file holds** (the `Theme` style after another program's save, which keeps
  the empty style and drops the document file) **may be filled in again**, and the document file carries them as it carries a new style's; `theme.apply` fills such a `Theme` style in
  (its answer's `style_added` says so) and the shapes that name it follow the theme. A change of any other style sheet of the file is refused. **An `.odg` is opened one way**
  (`diacraft_engine::odgfile::read_odg`, which `file.open` and `diacraft-cli export` both use): the themes the document file carries are read, and one that cannot be read whole is a
  warning of the report (`theme_unreadable`, `theme_entry_unreadable`, `theme_list_capped`, as a VSDX file's are) with its bytes kept.
* A bookmark, a reference mark (a point, or the two ends of a range), a soft page break and a text
  field the model holds as plain text (a fixed date or time, the title, the author, the file's other fields of the standard that show only their characters; not the page number, the page
  count and the file name, which are `Field` rows: a text that holds one is refused whole, as before) **stay in the file when their paragraph is written again whole**, with their own bytes.
  Where each goes is a rule of the text and not of the bytes: its offset in the model's text (the characters before it, as the reader counts them; a mark between two spaces that collapse
  is after the space) is carried, block by block, across the edit. A block is the run of steps the plan edits (not in place), puts in or takes out between two paragraphs it keeps; a
  paragraph kept, and one edited in place (an edit inside one run of raw text, the runs and the row the same), keep every element where it stands. In a block, the common prefix of the
  old and the new text (length p) is taken first, then the common suffix of what is left (length s): an offset up to p stays, one from the old length less s on moves by the change of
  length, any other goes to p (the suffix first would give another place for V21). A block that puts in no paragraph sends its elements to the start of the next paragraph
  the plan keeps, or to the end of the last. The elements at one offset stay in the order they stood in. In the paragraph written, an element stands at the top level at the
  paragraph's start or end and between two runs of different rows (before the next run's `text:span`), and inside the `text:span` of a run, which is not split, when the characters on both
  sides belong to that run. **A field is kept when its characters lie wholly in the common prefix or the common suffix**, inside the span of the run its characters are in
  (a format that cuts through its characters is refused in words: the specification does not say how one cut in two is kept); a field whose characters changed is written as the new
  characters, with no element, and the save says so once ([`SaveNote::FieldWritten`], V19: `Ann` retyped as `Bob`; **once for each kind of field in a shape, however many of that kind changed**, so a
  shape has at most 42 notes). **Memory:** the tokens of a paragraph (the token of each piece and four bytes for each character room of a text or a field) are held to the memory a save may hold while the paragraph is
  planned, and given back after it; a paragraph whose tokens do not fit is refused as a limit (`write_limit`, "memory for the paragraphs of a retext"). A retext of a paragraph of 1,300,000 bookmarks (the most the reader's cap
  of 4,000,000 items allows), of 600,000 paragraphs that each hold one, and of 50,000 changed fields saves under `ulimit -v 3000000` (peak resident 0.8 GB at the most), tested in child processes. A range
  that is not in the text, or a tag of a paragraph that is not text, is a fault (`write_mismatch`) and never a paragraph with no element in it. **White space** in a paragraph with elements is written in one pass
  over its characters: a space is a plain one unless it would not read back as one (after another space with only elements between, at the start of the paragraph after elements alone,
  after a field, and at the end), where it is `text:s`; no `text:span` of the file is copied as its bytes in such a paragraph. A soft page break between paragraphs
  (a direct child of the shape or of its text box) is **an anchor at the start of the paragraph after it and follows the block mapping (items 1, 3 and 5), as a bookmark does**:
  while the plan keeps that paragraph, or edits it in place, the break stays where it stands, and a paragraph put in before it goes before the break; when the paragraph is written whole or taken out, the
  break is carried over with its block's prefix and suffix and written where it lands: between the paragraphs when it lands on a paragraph's start (at the start of the paragraph's list item when that is
  a list item's: a list holds no soft page break between its items), else inside the paragraph at its offset (inside a run's span when the characters on both sides are the run's), so a break before the last
  paragraph, taken out, goes inside the end of the paragraph before it, and breaks before paragraphs taken out go before the next paragraph kept, in their order. The break's own bytes are copied, the white space before it included
  where it is written between paragraphs.
  **The paragraph written is read again from the bytes made and holds exactly those elements, each at its offset with its bytes** (the check of the bytes made; a paragraph that kept its
  bytes holds the ones put in at its start and its end). **Refused in words:** a link (`text:a`: the placing does not cover one), a change mark or an index mark (R10 places the
  elements it names only), any other element the writer does not map, a format that cuts a field, and a zero-width element that would be put where the namespace prefixes its bytes use are
