  not those in force. The work is counted (`WriteWork::zero_steps`: the elements listed, carried and put in, the bytes of a paragraph taken out looked at for a tag, and the characters of
  the block comparisons; `WriteWork::checked_zero`: the paragraphs read back for the elements put in them), the elements and the spans kept for the second pass are charged to the memory a save may hold before they are listed, and each is linear: n and 4n elements in one paragraph, at one offset, as fields, in n paragraphs written whole, in n paragraphs taken out, and in n spans cost n
  and 4n. **Two faults in the code the elements share were found and fixed**: an edit spliced into one run of raw text read the white space after it another way when it took the text before a
  plain space (the check of the bytes made refused the save), and a `text:span` that began or ended with a space was copied as its bytes where the text before it had changed.
* A new shape and a reorder of the page in one save are written (they were refused as "a new shape on a page whose shapes are put in another
  order in the same save"). Where the order is the document's, one plan puts the shapes that move and the new ones in the model's order: the shapes that stay are a longest run that
  keeps its order, and the rest go after the one that stays before them in the model's order, a moved unit and a new element made in turn. Where the page's shapes carry
  `draw:z-index`, no element moves: a new shape is written with the value of the shape before it in the model's order (the least value for one that is first) and its element is put
  after that shape's, so the reader, which sorts by the value and then by the place, finds it there; a shape of the file with no value, which the reader numbers by its place (a new
  element moves it), is given the value the order needs; and the order the reader will read is worked out and must be the model's, or the page is refused in words (values that repeat).
  A new shape on a page that carries `draw:z-index` is written with the attribute (it was refused). The second and the third save are no edit.
* Design decision 8. The answer of `file.save` and `file.save_as` for an `.odg` has `notes`: an array, empty when the drawing holds nothing a LibreOffice save would drop, else one `{class: "libreoffice_would_drop", words, cross_shape_formulas, shape_data}`. It is judged on the drawing as the file holds it (the copy the writer is given), by shape: a cell holding a formula that names another shape (by `Sheet.<ID>` or by the name of a shape: fix round), a `Property` row (the section the model keeps shape data in) or a `User` row but the `DiaCraftRoute` marker; the ends and triggers of a connector (its glue) are not counted. **A shape that is an instance of a master is no trigger** (2026-11-17: a shape dropped from a stencil is one in every session, and its link and its master are carried by the document file). It is **not a warning**, it is given on every save (an unedited one too), and the window shows its words once in a session, in the banner, which a person dismisses. The count is held to the flattening's step bound.
* A group of new shapes is saved as a `draw:g` holding its members (placed on the page by the groups round them; no `draw:z-index` on a member), read back as the group the session has, and saved again as no edit. A group is written when it is unturned and unflipped and has no text of its own (R45 writes a group's text as one more member, and keeps its angle and flips in the document file with its members written turned: neither is written yet, and **a text on a group, new or of the file, and a group that is turned or flipped are refused in words**, not dropped). **A group's frame is judged against the box of its members at a millionth of an inch** (the cells a save is given are at nine places, which a turned member's box carries to a few parts in a hundred million, so a group of members turned by any angle is the box and is written with no note; the writer held it to 1E-12 before, and refused every group with a turned member), **and a group whose frame is not that box** (a size it was held to by `LockCalcWH` after a member moved out, a margin, a pin set away from the middle) **is written as its members all the same, with one note for each such group naming it and its page** (a `draw:g` has no frame of its own, the file holds the group as its members, and read again it is the box of them; a group in a group is judged by the box of its leaves, and not by the frame of the group inside it; **a group of the file that a save changed something under (a member's pose, a member taken away, its own pose) is judged the same way and told when it is not the box**, as a `LockCalcWH` group is whose member turned after it was saved; **a connector glued to the group itself is named in the note**, since the reopened group has its connection points on the box and the connector's end stays where it was in the file; the precision is a millionth of an inch or a billionth of the group's largest length, whichever is more). **A picture that is a member of a new group is written where the group's map puts it** (it was written at its place in the group's frame, as if that were the page), **and a new picture or a new line or connector that holds members is refused in words** (a picture would be left out of a `draw:g`, and a one-dimensional shape's ends are nobody's there). A group's own cells beyond its frame, its sections, its `ID` (when another shape's formula or glue names it) and its link to a master are carried by DiaCraft's document file as `group` entries, **found by the group's `draw:name`, which is unique among the page's groups**: a group made in DiaCraft with no such name is named `Group_<ID>` (`Group_<ID>_2` when a group of the page has that name) in the copy a save writes, so its element and its entry say so, and the entry has no `nth`; an entry for a group with no name, an empty one or one another group has says its place among the groups of the name in `nth` (always, the first too), keeps no `ID` alone, and is written only when the file holds it already, so that data given to an unnamed group of the file is refused in words (the entry would be found by a place that another program can change) and no program that reorders, adds or takes away groups moves a group's data to another. **A group that holds no connection points says so in its entry**: the reader gives every group the four standard connection rows, so the entry of a group with no `Connection` section (a group of a VSDX file, one made in DiaCraft) says that the section is gone, and the group comes back with none, where it came back with four and the check after the save named them; a line and a connector with none say the same on their own style, and a line's or a connector's outline that holds a flag of its own (`NoFill` 1, as `connector.add` makes it) is carried whole, since the reader gives a segment's outline none; a group with no key of its own (no name, or one another group has) is given no entry for its connection points alone, and comes back with four. **What a group's own markup gives it is no data of DiaCraft's**: a group of the file with no name whose data is still what its markup gave it (its own graphic style, `draw:style-name`, and its own glue points, `draw:glue-point`, which are more than its frame and the four standard connection rows) needs no entry, since the reader builds that data again from the markup, so a save that rewrites the document file for something else (a window setting turned) leaves the entry out and is no refusal, and keeps the item of the group's VSDX data (`docs/diacraft-format.md` 7.3), which is no entry the markup gives; the same group given data in DiaCraft is still refused in words. The reader reads the entries before the pages; **an entry no group of its page takes (the group was renamed or taken away in another program) is warned of, once** (`diacraft_group_unclaimed`), and a page that has none is warned of (`diacraft_group_unknown`). **An entry with no `nth` binds to a group only while exactly one group of the page has that name**: a copy of the group put in the page, or another group given the name, leaves the entry unread, with one warning (`diacraft_group_name_shared`), where it was read for the first group of the name before, and so no group takes data or an `ID` that is not its own. **The `ID` of an entry no group takes is given to no other group of the page**, so a formula that names it reads no shape and never another. **A copy of a group that holds an object is refused in words as a copy of an object on the page is** (the members of a new group that the file does not hold are looked at, with or without a replacement picture; a member the file holds is a shape put into the group, which is refused in its own words). A group dropped from a master of several shapes (an instance of it) carries its links to the master (`master`, `master-shape`) in its entry too, and a file read again has them. A saved group is moved, given data and deleted; a **shape of the file put into a group, a group of the file taken apart, a member added to a group of the file and a group's rename are refused in words**. A Data Flow's label is written as the text of its connector, with its place carried, and the route of a connector that moved is written again.
* Parked items. A connector of the file glued to a shape an undo put back (a delete of both ends, a save, then the undo) is refused with a **redo
  (edit.redo)** or a second delete as the way out, since the undo is the change and undoing again goes round; the redo is the drawing the file holds, and saves with `edited: false`.
  `connector.style` with no `ids` changes the page's own cells, which DiaCraft's document file carries: it is saved and says nothing of the page, and a curved style says nothing either
  (a curved connector is written).
* **What a style's attributes cannot say is carried, not refused**. A dash, a gradient, a hatch, a palette colour, a shadow, a transparency past 1 and a formula in a
  style cell (a theme's, say) are saved: the style keeps the attributes it had, DiaCraft's extension (`diacraft:` attributes on the shape's own automatic style) carries the cell,
  and the file reads back as the model holds it. A program that is not DiaCraft shows the style's own attributes. A user cell, a section the writer does not write and a handle's
  section are carried the same way, **and so is a formula set on a pose cell whose value stays the same** (`shape.pin` puts the pin at another place of the box with formulas on `LocPinX` and `LocPinY`; the shape draws where it did and the file reads back with them), **except on the end of a connector**: its ends are rebuilt from its glue, so a formula or another attribute of its own on an end with the glue as it was is refused in words. A shape that a LibreOffice paste made (its style repeats another shape's `ID`) is read as a copy with none of that shape's data; an edit that adds a cell to it gives it an `ID` and data of its own, and what any edit writes of tier 2 is read back, so a duplicate `ID` is a refused save and never a lost edit. A change to a group's own cells is carried by the document file's `group` entry; a group's rename is refused in words.
* **What is refused, in the writer's words, with nothing written and the session as it was** (`EngineError::Failed`, whose message the window shows as its banner, the CLI prints as
  `step N (file.save): ...` and the MCP server returns as an `isError` result): an arc or a NURBS in an outline; a text edit the
  list in names; a new shape put into a group of the file, a shape of the file put into a group, a group of the file taken apart; a page whose `draw:z-index` values repeat, when a new shape or a reorder cannot be read back in the model's order; a new shape of one of the three new stencils (all thirty of the original masters are saved: ten natively, and twenty whose connection points or outline are not the plain ones by DiaCraft's extension on the shape's
  own style, with a note that a program other than DiaCraft draws a rectangle for the outline; the twenty masters of are written too: see the section below); a connector that is curved (the engine draws it as a quadratic B-spline, which the writer does not write); a page set to a preset
  size (the paper cells have no attribute); a text on a picture or an object frame (no spec says where a graphic frame's text goes in the file, and the reader reads none from it); a flip of a shape, or a turn of one whose transform holds the flip; and a carriage return. **Every refusal says the way out**: an `.odg` is saved only as an `.odg` until a later change, so the words end with "Undo the change (edit.undo) to save", and the undo is followed by a save; **a refusal for memory has its own words and its own way out, and one for a shape an undo put back says to redo**. **A curved connector is written** (the engine's quadratic spline is raised to cubic pieces, which is exact once it is split at the knots, and its own rows are carried), so the answer of `connector.add` or `connector.style` has no `warnings` for it, as for every master of the built-in stencils.

## Additional built-in stencils

The catalogue has seven stencils and fifty-eight masters (the sixth is the Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes, `cross-functional`: `xfunc.swimlane` and `xfunc.separator`, whose drop on a page with lanes inserts a lane or splits a phase; the seventh, the Organization Chart Shapes, has a section of its own: "The Organization Chart"). The original twelve Basic Shapes and eighteen Basic Flowchart masters keep their identities. New stable IDs are:

| Stencil | Master IDs | Count |
|---|---|---:|
| Decorative Shapes (`decorative`) | `decorative.star-5`, `decorative.star-6`, `decorative.star-8`, `decorative.burst`, `decorative.heart`, `decorative.lightning-bolt`, `decorative.cross`, `decorative.ribbon` | 8 |
| Arrow Shapes (`arrows`) | `arrows.right`, `arrows.left`, `arrows.up`, `arrows.down`, `arrows.left-right`, `arrows.up-down`, `arrows.four-way`, `arrows.bent` | 8 |
| Gane-Sarson (`gane-sarson`) | `gane-sarson.interface`, `gane-sarson.process`, `gane-sarson.data-store`, `gane-sarson.data-flow` | 4 |

Use the namespaced ID to distinguish the rounded Gane-Sarson Process from the legacy flowchart `Process`, whose unqualified lookup stays unchanged. `shape.drop` with `gane-sarson.data-flow` creates a real one-dimensional connector, initially horizontal with a 1.5-inch span centered at `x,y`; `connector.reconnect` attaches either end and `shape.set_text` labels the flow. Block arrows are ordinary two-dimensional shapes. Every node has one text block; no separate identifier editor is supplied. Existing templates retain their previous stencil lists.

VSDX save/reopen preserves these masters. An `.odg` save writes all fifty-eight (the two of the Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes, and the six of the Organization Chart Shapes, with a warning for the two fields of a position's text; the gate that refused the twenty of this batch is gone): each master is dropped, saved, read back and compared by what it draws and where it connects, and saved again as no edit, and **the master is carried**: the shape keeps its link to it and DiaCraft's document file holds the master, so a file read again has the shape linked to its master. A catalogue-wide test holds all fifty-eight; two of the twenty (Gane-Sarson's Process and Data Store) are written as a rectangle for other programs, with a note, and carried exactly by DiaCraft's extension.

## Stencil files

`stencil.open` reads a stencil file, the session keeps it with the other **open stencils** (not part of the drawing, its history or its file, and kept when the drawing is replaced), and `shape.drop {stencil: "file-1", master: ...}` puts one of its masters on a page. Real files are not in the tests: every package they open is built in code. Code: `crates/engine/src/stencil/` (`opened.rs` what a session keeps and answers, `bring.rs` the one matching and the copy, `instance.rs` the drop, `icon.rs`) and `cmd/stencils.rs`.

* The path rules; `file.open`'s, gaps A2 and A5. The file is read by `file::read_file_stamped`, the one reading path: a folder, a device and a pipe are refused before a byte is read, a pipe swapped in later is opened without waiting and refused, a file over the cap (what `file.open` takes) is refused from its size and from the bytes that came, and the reader's own limits apply (`diacraft_vsdx::read`, default `Limits`). **A link is read through to the file it leads to**, as `file.open` reads one; there are no granted folders for the control channel, MCP or CLI (A2), so a program names any path the user can read, which a read changes nothing by. A refusal says what is wrong in words and holds neither the path nor text of the file: no such file, not a file, not a drawing file, damaged, too big, and **not a stencil** (a drawing or a template, judged by the package's content type and not its name; the extension disagreeing is a warning). **Macros never run**: a `.vssm`'s project is a part the reader does not take, and nothing here looks at one.
* **Open stencils** are numbered `file-1`, `file-2`, ... for the session; **an id is not given again**, though its stencil was closed. The stencil's name is the file's name without its type. At most **16** are open, and **together they hold at most what the reader limits for one file** (`diacraft_vsdx::Limits`: 512 MiB of XML parsed, 12 million items kept, 512 MiB of pictures): each stencil is charged what the reader reports its model took (`Stats::xml_bytes`, `items`, `media_bytes`), **not the size of its file**, since a file of a few megabytes may inflate to hundreds (a refusal says so and names `stencil.close`; S6 of the review). **The same file opened again** (by the file a link leads to) is the stencil that is open (`already_open`), not read again. A command or batch that fails puts the open stencils back as it found them, so a batch that opened one and then failed leaves it closed.
* What a listing says. `stencil.list` gives the built-in stencils as before, each now with `builtin: true`, and then each opened stencil `{id, name, builtin: false, masters, masters_total}`. A master is `{id, name, width, height, connection_points, one_d, hidden, icon}`: `id` is its `ID` **as text** (as the built-in stencils' are text, so a window sends it back as `master`), `width` and `height` are the master shape's `Width` and `Height` in inches (for a master of several shapes the box that holds the first 8 of them; **at most 10,000 shapes of a stencil are sized**, and a master past that share is listed with 0 and 0: a drop reads the size from the master it drops), `connection_points` the rows of its `Connection` section, `one_d` (and `connection_points`, and the count of top-level shapes) are of the master's shapes that are not deleted (`Del`), as the resolver counts them; `one_d` is whether the shape has `BeginX`, `BeginY`, `EndX` and `EndY` (a line, a connector), `hidden` its `Hidden` flag (a window may leave it out; it is listed all the same), `icon` `null` or `{format, bytes}`. A master with an icon that is not base64 or that is too large has `icon: null` and `icon_refused` saying why. **At most 2,048 masters of a stencil are listed**: `masters_total` says how many there are, and a master past the list drops by its `ID` or name all the same. A master whose `ID` an earlier one of the file holds cannot be told from it and is left out (`masters_ignored` counts them). **A master's icon is answered as bytes by `stencil.icon`** (base64 text, kind and size), or in this process by `OpenedStencil::icon_bytes`; a window draws the master itself when there is none. An icon is looked at by its letters only (an icon over 400,000 characters of text is refused without a pass over it, one over 256 KiB decoded is refused, and a stencil's icons together are read to 32 MiB of text), and the file's `IconSize` is never read.
* Which master `master` means in an opened stencil. A whole number, or text that is one, is a master's `ID`; any other text is a name, in any case, with spaces, hyphens and underscores alike, matched against the master's `Name` and `NameU` (of two masters called the same the first). An `ID` wins over a name.
* Whether a drawing already holds a master; ours; `stencil::find_same_master`, the one place: the built-in stencils' `ensure_master` and a later paste from one drawing into another use it too. `BaseID` names a master across drawings and `UniqueID` names it in one. A master `M` of the stencil is **the same** as a master `D` of the drawing when (1) `M`'s `UniqueID` is on no other master of the stencil and `D` has it, and their `BaseID`s are equal where both have one; or else (2) `M`'s `BaseID` is on no other master of the stencil and `D` has it (the same master under another drawing's `UniqueID`; of several such the one with `M`'s `NameU`, else the first). **A copy keeps its `BaseID` and its `UniqueID`, and takes a `UniqueID` of ours (`{D1AC0003-0000-4000-8000-...}`) when a master of the drawing holds it already**, so a `UniqueID` is never on two masters of a drawing (the answer's `notes` says when the copy's was renewed). An id that two masters of the stencil hold says nothing about either (copies inside a stencil keep their `BaseID`), so a master whose two ids are both shared, or that has none, is matched by what it holds: a master of the drawing that shares an id with it (or has none, as it has none, and is called the same) and is exactly what would be added is the one, else a copy is added. The drawing's master is used as it is: whether it differs from the stencil's is not looked at, since `BaseID` is the identity, so a master edited in the drawing stays what the drawing made it.
* What is done with each thing a master refers to that the drawing may lack; ours. The master is copied under the next `ID` after the drawing's highest (one if there is none), with no relationship of the stencil's (the writer makes the part). A shape that nothing below changes is **shared with the stencil's model, not copied**.

  | What | Done |
  |---|---|
  | **Styles** (`LineStyle`, `FillStyle`, `TextStyle` of every shape and of the master's page sheet, and the links of the styles they reach) | Each style is looked for in the drawing **by name and by definition** (its sheet, with its own links mapped); one that is there is used, one that is not is added under a free `ID` (one above the highest), with the same name. A style with the same `ID` and another definition is not the drawing's own: V11. **Only the copy's links are rewritten, and the text of the old ones is dropped with them**: the model keeps a link, an `ID` or a `Master` that is not the plain text of its number (`LineStyle="03"`, `ID="05"`) in `raw`, which wins on a write, so a copy keeps no `ID` of the stencil's (not the master's, not a style's), no `LineStyle`, `FillStyle` or `TextStyle` text on a sheet whose link was rewritten, and no `Master` or `MasterShape` on a master's shape (a master part names no master). Saved, a copy is under the `ID`s the drawing gave it and its links are the ones it was given. **The drawing's styles of a name are hashed by definition once, and each style of the stencil's chain is compared with the one or two that hash as it does** (and not with every style of the name: 65,536 alike-named styles against a chain of 20,000 would be 1.3 billion comparisons). The root style (`ID` 0) is the drawing's root whatever either holds. A link to a style the stencil lacks is dropped, a cycle of styles is cut where it closes, and a chain of tens of thousands is walked without recursion. Names are not made unique: a drawing can end with two styles of one name |
  | **Colour table** (a colour cell that holds a plain whole number above 23: `FillForegnd`, `FillBkgnd`, `ShdwForegnd`, `ShdwBkgnd`, `LineColor`, `TextBkgnd`, `Color`, `GradientStopColor`, `GlowColor`, `BevelContourColor`, `BevelDepthColor`, in the shapes and in the styles) | The entry is found in the drawing by its colour; if it is not there it is added under the same number when that is free, else under the lowest free number up to 253, and the copy's cells are rewritten to it. A number the stencil's table does not hold is left as it is (it names nothing there either). **Both tables are indexed once** (a table holds up to 4,096 entries, and a master may name a million numbers). A cell with a formula (`THEMEVAL`, `RGB`) is not a table index and is not touched |
  | **Face names** (`Font`, `AsianFont`, `ComplexScriptFont` of a `Character` row) | Named by `NameU`, so no cell changes: a face the drawing lacks is added to its table with the attributes it had |
  | **Theme parts** | Not copied **(left it for the writer to be able to add a theme part, which it can; which part to bring is a later task; gaps F7)**: a master has no theme part of its own (a custom colour scheme rides in `User` rows of its shape, which are copied). Its themed cells read the drawing's theme, or the root style where the drawing has none |
  | **Pictures, objects, embedded packages and any other reference to a part of the stencil's file** (a shape's `ForeignData` with a relationship, and **anything the model keeps as the file had it that names a relationship**: an element or an attribute of a master row, a page sheet, a style, a shape, a section, a row, a cell, a text, a `ForeignData`) | **The master is refused (until the writer can copy related parts; gaps F7)**, in words that count them: a copy would name a relationship the saved drawing cannot write. **A relationship attribute is found by its namespace and not by its prefix**: a prefix bound by the kept text itself (on the element or above it inside the kept text) to the relationships namespace, **in either form of the standard** (`http://schemas.openxmlformats.org/officeDocument/2006/relationships` and the Strict `http://purl.oclc.org/ooxml/officeDocument/relationships`), makes every attribute under it a relationship, whatever the prefix is called (`rel:id`, `q:embed`), and a prefix bound to another namespace makes none. **The declarations of the elements above a kept node, within one sheet, are in scope for it**: the `raw` attributes of the shape (or style, or master) the sheet belongs to, of the sheet, a section, a row and a cell, in nesting order and each taken back when its element is left, so `<Shape xmlns:q="...relationships"><q:x q:custom="rId1"/></Shape>` is found, and so is a name outside the list in a cell's kept text under a prefix bound on its shape or section (a sibling's binding is not seen). A shape's own kept elements and text see its `raw` (and a `ForeignData` its own `attrs` after the shape's). **Those of the shapes above (a group a shape is in) and the part's root are not followed**. **These outer declarations only add**: the writer need not give an outer element's `xmlns:` back at the element, and a kept `r:id` under an `r` rebound there was found, in a saved drawing, to be written as `<Mystery r:id="rId1"/>` under the part's root, where `r` is the relationships namespace, and to dangle. So a binding on an outer element to the relationships namespace makes the attributes under it relationships, and **a binding there to any other namespace is not believed**: the prefix is judged as if unbound (`r` and the names the namespace defines stay relationships). Only a declaration on the kept element itself, or on one above it inside the kept text (both are copied verbatim), lifts the default; an element's own attributes are judged with its own declarations. **A prefix bound out of sight of the kept text** (on the part's root, which the reader does not keep, or on a group above the shape) is **judged closed**: `r`, which every part's root binds to the relationships namespace and which the part a copy is written into binds there too, makes every attribute under it a relationship whatever its local name; any other such prefix makes a relationship of each attribute whose local name is one the namespace defines, the ten of `shared-relationshipReference.xsd` of ECMA-376 Part 1 (`id`, `embed`, `link`, `dm`, `lo`, `qs`, `cs`, `blip`, `pict`, `href`; read 2026-11-07 in the Apache POI copy, <https://sources.debian.org/src/libapache-poi-java/4.0.1-4/ooxml-xsds/shared-relationshipReference.xsd>), so another namespace's attribute of one of those names, bound out of sight, is refused too. **Four more names, `topLeft`, `topRight`, `bottomLeft` and `bottomRight`, are refused the same way, pending a spec check** (the copy of the schema read is the first edition of ECMA-376; the check cites their source and that of the Strict URI, and task puts the list with the format specifications). **The work is bounded by what the kept text holds** (S7): the bindings in scope are one stack for a walk (an element's declarations pushed on entering it and taken back on leaving, so K declarations and N children cost K and N), looked up by a hash on the prefix, and capped at 64 declarations on one element and 256 in scope at once; past either the master is refused in words (`it holds text of the stencil's file that declares more namespace prefixes than a copy reads`), before anything is pushed. **Every node is charged to the meter when it is put on the walk's stack, and an element's children before they are stacked**, so a wide element is refused by the meter and not after its children are stacked. The `Connect` rows of the master and the `Raw` elements of a shape's text are looked at as well, walked where they are. The styles the master reaches are looked at as well as its shapes. The master's other shapes are not affected, and the drawing is as it was |
  | **Other masters named by `USE()`** | Not followed. The answer's `notes` says the master has such formulas, and the drawing's cells that name a master it lacks read as they do without it |
  | **Data connections and recordsets** of the stencil | Not copied; the shape's own data rows (a `Property` section, `Data1` to `Data3`) are cells and text and come along |
  | **The master's icon** | The text, as the file had it |
  | **Macros** | Never read, never copied |

* The instance; ours where no spec says. **Everything about the instance is read from the drawing's master it names** (the stencil's copy, or, when finds the drawing's own, a master that may differ from the stencil's in its pin, its members, or being a box where the stencil has a line): its pose, whether it is a line, its `Type` and its members, so that the shape lands where it is asked and its members name master shapes the master has. The master is brought in first, and a refusal after that (a master of none, a page too full) leaves the drawing as it was. The instance holds **only what differs**: `PinX` and `PinY`, and for a one-dimensional master `BeginX`, `BeginY`, `EndX` and `EndY` instead (a line is dropped with its ends where the master's are, moved so that their middle is the place; its pin and size follow by the master's formulas, and a pin the master holds as a number and not as a formula is set where the ends and `LocPinX` put it: `begin + (LocPinX / Width) * (end - begin)`, which is the middle only when `LocPinX` is half the width, and what reads the pin is worked out again). Its `Type` is the master shape's and its name is the master's with the `ID` (`Process.5`). **The place is the centre of the shape's box**, so the pin goes where the centre lands once the shape's `LocPinX`, `LocPinY`, `Angle`, `FlipX` and `FlipY` are applied; what reads a cell the instance holds is worked out again, so the cached results are right. A drop is **one undo step**, which takes the master and everything copied with it away too, and the id rule holds: the session's mark goes above the instance's `ID` and its last member's.**A master of none is refused**; a master of one shape that is a group is dropped with that shape's members; **a master of several top-level shapes is dropped as a group**.
* The members of a group master; ours. A file's instance of a grouped master lists every descendant of the master's shape, each standing for its own by `MasterShape`, in the master's order, and a reader that is not ours (LibreOffice, in the check that came with this change) draws nothing for a member that is not listed. So the instance lists them: a shape for each descendant, with an `ID` of its own on the page (above the instance's, depth first), its master's `Type`, `MasterShape`, and no cell of its own but a local copy of its master shape's `Geometry` (the instance itself holds none), and no size or pin (but for the members of a master of several shapes, whose pins are in the group's frame). **A master of more than 5,000 descendants (a group's whole tree) is dropped without its members below the top level listed** (MS-VSDX lets an instance omit them, and `Resolver::effective_members` and the renderer take them from the master): listing them would put as many shapes on the page for each drop.

* A master of several top-level shapes; ours. The specifications say what its instance is: **a `Group` with a member for each top-level shape of the master, each standing for it by `MasterShape`** (2% of masters, 293 files from 48 sources, have several); only the group's frame is left to us. **The frame is the joint box of the members on the master's page**, each member's box carried through its own pin, turn and flips (a turned member counts by its corners): the group's `Width` and `Height` are the box's, its `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` the middle of the box, and its `PinX` and `PinY` the place (the box's middle goes there; the group is neither turned nor flipped). A member's pin is in the group's frame, so it is its master shape's pin on the master's page less the box's lower-left corner, held as an override of the member (`PinX` and `PinY`, or the four end cells of a line), and **a member that is not a line holds its `PinX`, `PinY`, `Width` and `Height` as formulas of the group**; every other cell of a member is its master shape's. The members' own descendants are listed under them when the whole tree is within 5,000 shapes, and left to the master otherwise. A master of **more than 5,000 top-level shapes is refused** in words, since each needs a member of its own on the page to carry its override. A member that is a line has its four end cells as overrides, **and its pin checked once what reads the ends is worked out**: a master that holds the pin as numbers and not as a formula of its ends would otherwise leave the pin at the master's page (a line drawn offset by the box's lower-left corner), so a pin held as a number that is not where the member's ends in the group's frame and its `LocPinX` of its `Width` put it (the middle only when `LocPinX` is half the width) is set there, as for the instance of a single line, and a pin that is a formula is left to the recalculation that follows the ends. `connection_points` in the listing is the rows of the `Connection` sections of all the shapes at the master's top level, so a connector glues to a member of the dropped group (tested: glued to a member, the group is moved, the connector's end follows). The group moves, turns and flips as any group does, is sized by `shape.resize` as any group is (its members are formulas of it), and saves and reads back. A master shape that is deleted (`Del`) is not a shape of the master, as the resolver counts them.

**The window** reads `stencil.list` and sends `stencil.open`, `stencil.close` and `shape.drop {stencil}`: see "Stencil files in the window" below.

* The Shapes window and stencil files; ours: no specification says how a window shows them. **More Shapes, Open Stencil** shows the desktop app's native
  file chooser (`.vssx` and `.vssm`, and every file as a second filter) on a thread of its own and sends `stencil.open` with the path it answers; **it is quiet where the
  app has no chooser** (the web), and while one is on the screen. The stencil gets **a row of its own** named for the file and is **the one the Shapes window shows**; the same
  file chosen again is the same stencil. A refusal (`stencil.open`'s words: not a stencil, damaged, past a limit, no such file) is told in the status bar and nothing
  changes. **A close control at the right of the row of each stencil file** (the engine's own have none) sends `stencil.close`; the Shapes window then shows the first stencil
  if it showed this one, and a drawing that took masters from it keeps them. **A tile for each master that is not hidden** (`hidden: true` is left out), at most 2,048
  of a stencil (the engine lists that many), in the file's order. **A tile is drawn from the master's icon when the engine says it has one and it is an ICO, a PNG or a BMP**
  (`icon_image.rs`: sides to 256 pixels, every offset checked, an icon of any other kind or one that does not read is not used), **else from the master's own outline**, read
  in a session of its own that has the file open again by the path the window opened it from (so the person's drawing is never used to draw a tile), **else a box** of the
  master's size. Figures and icons are read for the tiles in view only. A tile is dragged onto the page or clicked as the built-in ones are and sends `shape.drop` with
  `stencil`; **a master the engine refuses** (no shapes, more than 5,000 at the top level, a picture or another part it cannot carry across: gap F7) **is told in the status
  bar in the engine's words and the drawing is as it was**. The window commands are `shapes.openStencil {path?}` (no `path`: the chooser) and `shapes.closeStencil {id}`.
* Our rule. **The file is read on the window's thread**: `stencil.open` is the engine's command on the window's session, and the engine has no way to give it a
  stencil read elsewhere, so a very large stencil holds the window while it is read, up to the most a drawing file may be (gap U22). **A drawing read from a file is a new
  session**, so the thread that reads it opens the stencils the window had open again in it, by the paths the window opened them from (a stencil whose file is gone, has
  changed into something else, or does not fit the memory a read may use beside the drawing just read, is not in the list afterwards, **and is said**: by name in the status bar, and in
  `stencil_notes` of the answer of the open; the ids are numbered again from `file-1`); a stencil that a program opened through the control channel is
  noted by its path as well, since the window runs every command it is sent.

## Search for shapes and Quick Shapes

`stencil.search` finds masters in every stencil by the whole words of their names and search words; `stencil.quick`, `stencil.add_quick`, `stencil.remove_quick` and `stencil.reset` read and change the Quick Shapes of each stencil, the masters above its divider, which feed the AutoConnect flyout. The reference app was not inspected: what the specifications give is (the search box), R4 (Quick Shapes), the right-click menus and Reset Stencil. Code: `crates/engine/src/stencil/search.rs`, `keywords.rs` and `quick.rs`, `cmd/stencils.rs`, and for the window `crates/ui-egui/src/shapes_window.rs` and `stencils.rs`. Every rule below that is **ours** has a row in the check list (the check list, proposed in the task's report).

* (what matches; the words written out, with V1 as the test). A *word* is a run of letters and digits, and everything else parts words (`Pump-House` is two). A master matches a query word when the word is **a whole word, in any case,** of the master's name or of its search words: `Air Cond` finds no `Air Conditioner`, `air` does. With **AND** (`mode: "all"`, the default: Options, Advanced, Shape search, "All of the words") a master needs every word of the query, with **OR** (`"any"`) one of them. The same word twice is one word. The Shape search option that hides the box and the Options page that holds the AND/OR choice are not built: the choice is the window command `shapes.search {mode}` (not saved).
* Our rule. A stencil file's master has its name (`Name`, else `NameU`) and the text of its **`ShapeKeywords`** cell, read on the master's page sheet (files put it there) and on the sheets of its first eight shapes, as a `V` or as the element's text. The built-in masters are made in code and hold no such cell, so their words are a table by id (`stencil/keywords.rs`): plain English words for what the figure is called in other places or used for (a diamond is also a rhombus, a decision is a branch). They are written from the figures and from general knowledge of flowcharts and are not copied from any stencil; they are not written into a drawing. A master that is hidden is not found (the window leaves it out).
* (ours; R2 says "the shapes on the device"). Every **built-in stencil, open or not** (a template that left the flowchart closed does not hide it from a search, and the answer says `open`), and every **open stencil file**; the built-in stencils that are open first, in the window's order, then the others in the catalogue's, then the files. The Online group and Download of R2 are not built (a P2 row).
* The budget; a hostile stencil: 100,000 masters, names and search words of megabytes. The query is refused in words past 256 characters, 16 different words or a word of 64 characters (a word dropped from an AND would find more, not fewer). The search looks at **at most 100,000 masters and reads at most 16 MiB of names and search words**, each master **charged for the text it is read from before it is read**; a search word text is read to **4,096 bytes** and no further; a master stops being read when it is decided. **A search that meets either limit stops and says so**: `complete` is false, `searched` and `total` say how far it got, and a note in words says which limit; a limit that is reached never lets more through. At most **500** masters are listed (`listed`, `truncated`), and `matches` counts them all. The work is counted (`tokens`, `bytes` in the engine's `Outcome`) and tested on stencils of 120,000 masters, names of 1.5 million characters, search words of 1.2 MB and a word of a million letters.
* What the specification gives. Quick Shapes are the masters above the thin divider in a stencil, typically two to six, added by dragging across the divider or with Add to Quick Shapes and taken away the same way; a stencil called Quick Shapes gathers those of every open stencil, each stencil's group under its name when there are several; **the AutoConnect strip shows at most four**, the first four of the stencil that is selected; Reset Stencil gives the stencil its own back.
* Our rule, where are silent. **A stencil with nothing set has its first four masters that are not connectors and not hidden** (what offered before). **The group is the drawing's**, like the AutoConnect box: it is kept in DiaCraft's own extension data (a child `quick_shapes` whose attribute `v` is JSON: `[["b", "basic", ["rectangle", ...]], ["f", "Mine", ["5", ...]]]`, a built-in stencil by its id, a stencil file by the file's name, a master by its id), so **every format carries it** (`.diacraft`, VSDX, `.odg`) and no stencil file is written to; a drawing whose groups are all the stencils' own holds nothing of it. **It is no undo step and makes the drawing unsaved** (a setting: "Unsaved, not undo"), and undo and redo carry it across (`view::carry_settings`). The group is in the order the masters were added and a master added again moves to the place asked (`at`, from 0); the masters below the divider keep the stencil's own order; a group may be **empty** when the drawing set it so, and the strip then offers the stencil's first four masters, so that no stencil is one the arrows cannot use. **The strip offers the first four masters of the group that it may offer (no connector and no part of a structure), fewer when there are fewer** (the strip is not filled from the rest). A group names at most **24** masters, a drawing holds the groups of at most **32** stencils, a key is at most 128 bytes and the data at most 64 KiB; data past them, or that is not what this build writes, is **unreadable**: the stencils' own are shown, `readable` is false, the data is **kept as it is** and a change is refused in words. An entry for a stencil that is not open stays in the data, and one for a master that is gone is passed over when the group is read. Two stencil files of one name share a group. **What is written always reads back the same**. A file's name that a key cannot be as it is, one with a control character or past 128 bytes (64 characters of three bytes are), is kept with each control character as U+FFFD and cut at a character boundary at 128 bytes, so two names that differ only past the cut, or only in their control characters, share a group too. A key the data cannot hold at all (a file with no name) is refused in words, and so is a change whose data would not read back as it is held (past 64 KiB): the reader reads the text before it is written, and nothing is written.
* (replaces; ours where R3 is silent: it says the strip shows the first four shapes of the stencil that is selected and "not all stencils feed it (the Arrow Shapes stencil does not)", and no page says which others, or what the strip shows while such a stencil is selected). **A master feeds the strip** when it is no connector, **no part of a structure** (a Swimlane or a Separator, whose drop is put in a list of lanes or phases, and a stencil file's master whose shape is typed `Container` or `List`), and of a stencil that feeds it: **every built-in stencil but Arrow Shapes, and every stencil file**. Such masters stay Quick Shapes where they are (the group is the person's choice, and the Quick Shapes stencil gathers them), and the strip passes over them. **A stencil that feeds the strip nothing** (Arrow Shapes, the Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes, a file of parts) **gives it what the first open stencil that feeds it offers**, in the window's order (the default R3 names: "the stencil that follows the Quick Shapes stencil"), and `stencil.quick`'s `offer.stencil` says which; with no open stencil that feeds it the arrows still show and a click on one still joins or copies, and no flyout opens. **What the window is told:** `stencil.list` marks such a master `part: true` and `autoconnect: false`, and a master of Arrow Shapes `autoconnect: false`. **A part that reaches the strip from a file** (a stencil file's container or list master, or a drawing's Quick Shapes data that names one) is passed over by the flyout and by the Quick Shapes stencil's strip; **let go on an AutoConnect arrow it is dropped as a drop of it is** (a Swimlane on a page with lanes is inserted in the list, on a page with none it is the shape it is drawn as) and joined to nothing; and **`connector.autoconnect` naming one is refused in words** with nothing changed ("a part of a structure ... drop it with shape.drop").
* **`stencil.quick`** (query, no drawing needed) `{stencil?}` → `{stencils: [{stencil, name, builtin, customized, masters}], offer: {stencil, masters}, readable}`. Without `stencil` (or as `quick`) it is **the Quick Shapes stencil**: the group of every open stencil in the window's order, and `offer` the first four among them that the strip may offer; with a stencil's id it is that stencil's group and the strip it offers. A master of a stencil file carries `stencil`, the id a drop takes.
* **`stencil.add_quick`** `{stencil, master, at?}`, **`stencil.remove_quick`** `{stencil, master}` and **`stencil.reset`** `{stencil}` (Reset Stencil) → the group as `stencil.quick` has it and `changed` (false for a call that changes nothing, which is no edit). The first change of a stencil that had nothing set copies its default group in, so that a change is a change of what is shown. A stencil that is not there, a master that is not in it and an `at` that is not a whole number are refused in words.
* **The window** (`shapes_window.rs`). **The box** at the top has the prompt **Search shapes** and a magnifier at its right: **typing narrows the masters of the stencil on view by name** as before, and **Enter or the magnifier searches every stencil** (`shapes.search {text, mode?}`, a window command: no `text` searches the words of the last search again, blank text clears the search). **The results are a stencil of their own**, `search`, with a row at the head of the stencil menu (a cross on it clears the search) and each stencil's matches under its name; the box keeps the words, taking them out resets the search and shows the stencil that was shown before it, going to another stencil keeps the results, and a refused query is told in the status bar with the results as they were. `window_state` has `shapeSearch` (`text`, `mode`, `matches`, `listed`, `complete`). **A stencil's gallery is its Quick Shapes above a thin divider and the rest below it**; a group the drawing emptied shows the hint **Drop Quick Shapes here**. **The Quick Shapes stencil** (`window.stencil quick`) has the groups of the open stencils under their names. **Right-click on a tile** is **Add to Quick Shapes** or **Remove from Quick Shapes** (the rest of that menu is not built), **a tile dragged and let go inside the window** changes its group (above the divider it becomes a Quick Shape at the tile it was let go on, below it it is one no more) and is not dropped on the page, and **Reset Stencil** is a row of the stencil menu (the rest of that menu is not built; reordering stencils, R6, is not, so the groups gather in the window's order). The flyout of the AutoConnect arrows offers the strip of the stencil on view (`StencilInfo::offer`, which a test holds equal to `stencil.quick`'s `offer` for sets of changes), and a gathering shows the first four it may offer; where the stencil on view feeds the strip nothing, the flyout offers the first stencil's of the window's list that feeds it (`DiacraftApp::autoconnect_offer`), and a part of a structure let go on an arrow is dropped as a drop of it is.
* **Files.** The choice is saved and reopened in `.diacraft`, VSDX (the model writer and a save into the file itself) and `.odg` (tests/it/stencil_quick.rs); a stencil file that is not open when the drawing is reopened has its entry kept. The search writes nothing.
* **Not built** (rows stay as the check list has them): the Online group and Download, the Options page for the AND/OR choice and the box's hide option, the drop-down arrow of the box (Q1 of the pane's spec), reordering stencils, the stencil title's right-click menu but for Reset Stencil, the rest of the master menu, and **the Document Stencil and "Drop shape resizes to the drawing scale"** (the project rule is a ratio of the master page's drawing scale to the target page's, which needs the stencil's master page scale and a recalculation of the instance's size; left for a task of its own).

## Page setup

A page's size, orientation, drawing scale, printer paper and Auto Size, and the page list (delete, reorder). The rows are the checklist's "Page setup and drawing scale"
(presets and custom size, orientation, Auto Size: P0) and "Pages and page tabs"; the cells are the page's (`PageWidth`, `PageHeight`, `PageScale`, `DrawingScale`,
`DrawingSizeType`, `DrawingScaleType`, `DrawingResizeType`, `PaperKind`, `PrintPageOrientation`). Code: `crates/engine/src/draw/page_setup.rs` (what a page says, the writes), `page_fit.rs` (Auto Size),
`pages.rs` (delete, reorder), `paper.rs` (the tables) and `cmd/page_setup.rs`. The reference app was not inspected: every rule below is **ours** and is listed in the report of the task for the check list.
Every length is in **paper inches** (`PageWidth` and `PageHeight` are in drawing units, and the command turns them with the page's drawing scale). `page` is optional on the setup commands and is the first page that is not a background.

| Command | Parameters | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `page.presets` (query) | none; works with no document | `{sizes: [{id, name, group, width, height, paper_kind}], papers: [{kind, name, width, height}], scales: [{id, name, kind, family, paper, paper_unit, drawing, drawing_unit, units_per_inch}]}`: the Size gallery (portrait inches; `paper_kind` null when that size is not a paper whose code is known), the papers a printer paper can be, and the drawing scales (`paper` and `drawing` in the units named) |
| `page.setup` (query) | `page`? | `{page, name, background, back_page, width, height, orientation, size, drawing, paper, scale, auto_size, sheets}`: `width` and `height` the page on paper, `back_page` the `ID` of the background page it names or null, `size` the named size it is in either orientation (`{id, name}`) or null, `drawing` `{width, height, units_per_inch}` in drawing units, `paper` `{kind, name, assumed, width, height, orientation, orientation_set}` (one sheet as held; `assumed` when the size is a stand-in), `scale` `{preset, kind, family, paper, paper_unit, drawing, drawing_unit}`, `sheets` `{across, down}` how many sheets of the paper the page covers. Every command below answers with this |
| `page.size` | `preset`, or `width` and `height`; `page`? | a named size in the orientation the page has, or a custom size |
| `page.orientation` | `orientation` (`portrait`, `landscape`); `page`? | turns the page and the paper |
| `page.scale` | `preset`, or `paper` and `drawing` (`paper_unit`?, `drawing_unit`?: `in`, `ft`, `yd`, `mi`, `mm`, `cm`, `m`, `km`; inches when left out); `page`? | a drawing scale |
| `page.paper` | `preset` or `kind`, and/or `orientation`; `page`? | the printer paper and the way it is held; the page is not touched |
| `page.auto_size` | `on`; `page`? | Auto Size on or off |
| `page.type` | `type` (`foreground`, `background`); `page`? | makes the page a background page or a page that is not one; answers the page's setup and `unassigned`, how many pages named it as their background and name none now |
| `page.background` | `background` (the `ID` of a background page, or `null` or `none`); `page`? | gives the page a background page, or none |
| `page.delete` | `id` | `{id, name, pages, unassigned}`: `unassigned` is how many pages named it as their background and name none now; not enabled in a drawing of one page |
| `page.move` | `id`, `to`, `renumber`? | `{id, from, to, renamed}`; not enabled in a drawing of one page |
| `page.add` | `name`?, `like`?, `background`? | `{id, name}`. `like` is the id of a page whose setup (its whole page sheet: size, orientation, scale, units, shadow offset, ruler and grid) the new page copies; `background: true` makes a background page, named `VBackground-N`, put before the pages that are not backgrounds |
| `page.duplicate` | `id`? (default: the first page that is not a background) | `{id, name, from}`: a copy of the page right after it, with its shapes, connections, layers and page cells, named for it and a number; one undo step |

Each setup command is one undo step, and one that would change nothing (a size, an orientation, a state the page has already) is no undo step and does not make the drawing unsaved. A call that
fails (a page with no size, a number out of range, a cell protected by `GUARD`) changes nothing. What reads the cells written (`ThePage!PageWidth`, the scale cells) is worked out again and no cell else.

* The named sizes are held portrait (width at most height), and choosing one gives the page that size **in the orientation it has**: a landscape page (wider than tall, a page has no orientation cell) takes the size turned. A square page is portrait.
  A custom size is taken as given, from 0.1 to 10,000 inches each way (the files hold pages from 0.775 inch to 3,307). **No shape moves when the page changes size** (the page grows from its lower-left corner).
* A named size that is a paper of the table (`paper::PAPERS`; the Windows codes `PaperKind` holds, from the XlPaperSize page of Microsoft's Excel VBA reference and the DEVMODE page, both read 2026-11-07, because gives no list) also sets `PaperKind` and `PrintPageOrientation`, so that the page is one sheet.
  A custom size, and a size with no paper (A0 to A2, A6, the ARCH sizes), leave the paper alone. The sizes of the gallery are ISO 216 A0 to A6, the North American Letter, Legal, Tabloid, Executive and Statement, ANSI C to E and the ARCH sizes; which are offered, and their ids, are ours.
* A page whose sheet names no paper, or a paper the table has no size for (a user-defined one, `32767`), prints on US Letter, and `paper.assumed` says so. `PrintPageOrientation` 1 and 2 are upright and sideways; absent, 0 (as the printer is set) or any other number holds the sheet the way the page's own size is.
* `page.orientation` swaps `PageWidth` and `PageHeight` if the page is not that way already and sets `PrintPageOrientation`: a landscape page whose paper is held upright (264 pages of the census) is turned on its paper, which is a change, though its size is right. The shapes stay where they are.
* A change of drawing scale keeps the page's size **on paper**, so `PageWidth` and `PageHeight` are worked out again (a Letter page at 1/4 in = 1 ft is 528 by 408 drawing units, not 11 by 8.5), and the shapes' cells are not touched: they print at a new size.
  `PageScale` and `DrawingScale` are written with all their digits (`V` in inches, `U` the unit chosen: `IN`, `FT`, `MM`, ...), since they are divided by each other for every length of the page. A scale of the list is read back as that scale by its ratio (`scale.preset`). The scale lists (architectural, civil engineering, metric, mechanical engineering) are ours.
  A ratio of 1:1 given as a custom scale is `DrawingScaleType` 0. The two lengths are more than 0 and at most a billion inches, the ratio is from 0.001 to 1,000,000 drawing units to a paper inch, and the page may not be more than 1,000,000 drawing units on a side (`MAX_COORD`).
* `DrawingSizeType` (2 a standard size, 5 metric, 6 ANSI engineering, 7 ANSI architectural for a named size; 3 for a custom size at 1:1 and 4 for one on a scaled page) and `DrawingScaleType` (the family of the scale; 3 custom) are written as the labels they are (nothing draws by them).
  `DrawingResizeType` 0 defers to `DrawingSizeType`, so a page that has the 0 is first given the 1 or 2 that its state comes to, and writing the label never turns Auto Size on or off.
* Auto Size is on when `DrawingResizeType` is 1, or is 0 and `DrawingSizeType` is 0 or absent, and off when it is 2 or any other number. **A page that has no `DrawingResizeType` at all, as the pages of a new drawing, has it off**
  (`Page.AutoSize` says off is the default). The same D10 notes that the census holds a 1 on 72 pages in 100; that 1 means "on" is the specification's word, not something this project could check, and `auto_size_on` (`draw/page_setup.rs`) is the one place to change.
  `page.auto_size` writes 1 or 2; turning on a page that is on already (by the 0 and 0 of the rule) is no change.
* Auto Size. With Auto Size on, a shape put or moved past the page's edge makes the page grow by **whole sheets of its printer paper**: one sheet is the paper (`PaperKind`, held the way `PrintPageOrientation` says) on paper, which is `paper.width` by `paper.height` times the page's drawing units to a paper inch;
  on each side that the shape's box passes (its four corners carried through its turn and its groups) the page gains `ceil(overshoot / sheet)` sheets, so the page is then the number of sheets it was plus those. A box that only touches the edge does not make it grow. A page whose `PrintPageOrientation` is not set (as a new drawing's) has its sheet held the way the page is, and **the first growth writes `PrintPageOrientation`**, so that the sheets do not turn when the page grows taller than wide (11 by 8.5 becomes 11 by 17). Holds: Letter paper, a shape dragged past the right edge, the page extends past its old edge and the paper stays Letter.
  Page breaks (the View tab) are the window's: `page.setup`'s `sheets` and `paper` give where they fall. **A page does not grow past 10,000 inches on paper or 1,000,000 drawing units on a side**: a shape that would need more is left off the page, and the command succeeds.
* A page's origin is its lower-left corner, so a growth on the **left or the bottom** moves the origin: every top-level shape is carried by the growth (a group takes its members), the way a paste or a group carries shapes (`draw/lift.rs`), so the drawing is not changed:
  no route is made again, a glued end follows its shape, a cell that reads a pose is worked out again, and the shape that made the page grow ends inside it (a shape put at x -0.2 on an 11-inch page ends at x 10.8 on a page of 22). A page of **more than 20,000 top-level shapes** (`MAX_SHIFT_SHAPES`; the work of a carry is linear in them) is not carried, so it does not grow on the left or the bottom (the shape stays off the page) and still grows on the right and the top.
* The hook looks at the shapes the command named or made, and no others: a shape that was past the edge before is left, and the page does not grow for it. Turning Auto Size on grows nothing.
* **Where it hooks** (`draw::fit_page`, inside the command's own edit, so the growth is part of its one undo step): `shape.drop` (and a drop from a stencil file) after the instance is added, `shape.move` and `shape.align` after the move, `shape.resize` and `shape.size` after the resize, `shape.set_end` and `shape.set_length` after the end moves, `shape.rotate` and `shape.flip` after the turn or flip,
  `edit.paste` and `shape.duplicate` after the copies are placed, and `connector.autoconnect` after the new shape is made, moved beside the other and connected (a growth on the left or the bottom carries the new shape and its connector with everything else, and the answer's `x` and `y` are read after it). `shape.delete`, `edit.cut`, `shape.group`, `shape.ungroup`, `shape.pin` (the shape draws where it did) and the other connector commands do not grow the page. The answer of a command does not say that the page grew: read `page.setup`. **A growth that cannot be made** (the page's size or a shape's pin is protected by `GUARD`, a number is out of range) is dropped and the command goes on without it, since refusing a drop for a shape elsewhere that cannot move would be worse than a shape off the page.
* `page.delete` takes a page out with its shapes. **A drawing keeps one page that is not a background** (a drawing of backgrounds only has no page to open); the last such page is refused. A page that named the deleted one as its background (`BackPage`) names none after it (the answer's `unassigned` says how many, and Undo puts them back with the page). Nothing else is rewritten. The page's `ID` may be given again by a later `page.add` (`Document::add_page` takes the highest `ID` plus one), and the writer takes a page of the same `ID` for the page it replaced.
* `page.move` puts a page at a place among the **pages that are not backgrounds**, counted from 0 as the tabs show them ("foreground tabs stay left of background tabs, and background tabs cannot be moved"); a background is refused, and every background keeps its place in the list. With `renumber` every such page with a default name (`Page-` and a number) is named for the place it has now: `Page-1` for the first, `Page-2` for the second; a name a person gave is left alone, and `NameU` is not touched. A move to the place the page has, with nothing to rename, is no undo step.
* Our rule ("a copy of the page on view, with its shapes, for separate editing"). A copy of a page is the page whole: its page cells (so its layers, its size and its scale), its shapes with their ids, its connects and the background page it names. It has an `ID` of its own, one past the highest, and is named for its source and `-2` (`Page-1-2`), or the first number from 2 that no page has taken: no specification gives the name, and a name too long to take the number is cut short. It stands **right after its source** in the list, and the copy of a background page is a background page. The page's shapes are shared with the source until one of them is written to, so a copy costs what a page costs once it is changed. **What belongs to the place the page had in its package is left behind**: its part, its relationship, the `Rel` its row held, the unknown markup of its OpenDocument page element and every attribute of the relationships namespace among what its slot keeps (counted in `Page::leave_package` and `refs::leave_package_for_copy`, as a master that leaves its package is), and the file the drawing is saved into gives the copy a part of its own. **A picture or an object that a file names by a relationship of its page's part is copied with the page**: the part a save makes for the copy, through either VSDX writer, has a row for every picture, embedded object and other part its shapes name, leading to the part the source's row leads to, so the two pages share one part and nothing is copied; a shape keeps the id it names when the new part has no row of it, and is given another only when two of its shapes name one id for two parts (`vsdx/src/write/page_rows.rs`). So does every relationship id of a shape's kept markup, such as the `ForeignData` of a shape whose `Type` is not `Foreign`, which the reader keeps whole and does not read (`page_rows/kept.rs`). A copy whose source is taken out, in the same save or a later one, keeps them. A picture with no relationship, one that came from another format, is copied as the source holds it, and the save gives it a part of its own made from the model's bytes. (The clipboard still pastes a shape with a picture of a file only on the page it was copied from.) A drawing of 1,000 pages cannot have another, and says so. **A cell that reads the page it is on (`PAGENAME()`, `PAGENUMBER()`) keeps the value it had until the cell is next worked out**, as a renamed page's does.
* Our rule where noted. A page of Type Background is a page with `Background` on, and any page may name one background page of its own, a background page too. `page.add {background: true}` names the page `VBackground-N` for the first number from 1 that no page has taken, copies the setup of the page `like` names, and puts it **before the first page that is not a background**, as a file lists them; the tabs show the backgrounds after the other pages whatever the order of the list. **A page names only a background page, never itself and never a page that names it back** (MS-VSDX forbids a `BackPage` that names a foreground page, and a loop is one the renderer stops at; `page.background` refuses each in words). `page.type` makes a page a background page (it goes where an added one does) or a page that is not (it goes after every other page, and the pages that named it name none after it, counted in `unassigned`); **a drawing keeps one page that is not a background**, so the last is not made one. Each is no undo step when the page has the type or the background already. `Background` and `BackPage` kept in `raw` in a spelling the model does not write back (`Background="true"`, `BackPage="007"`) are taken out of `raw` with the change, because a writer lets a kept spelling win. A foreground page draws over the shapes of its background and the pointer meets only the shapes of the page on view (the renderer paints the farthest background first). **Saving:** VSDX and `.diacraft` hold the type, the assignment and the order (`Background`, `BackPage`; `background`, `back-page`); a drawing opened from an `.odg` takes a copied page, and **a background page added to it is refused at the save in words** (`odg/src/write/structure.rs`: a background is a master page that holds shapes, and a new one is not written into the file), with the way out (Undo, or Save As `.diacraft`).

**For the window (the Design tab's Page Setup group and the dialog's page tabs).** Size: `page.presets` gives the gallery (group by `group`); the current choice is `page.setup`'s `size.id`; More Page Sizes is the dialog's page-size tab (pre-defined: `page.size {preset}`; custom: `page.size {width, height}`).
Orientation: two items for `page.orientation`, the current one `page.setup`'s `orientation` (the page's, which is what the button shows). Auto Size: a toggle, pressed when `auto_size`, sending `page.auto_size {on: !auto_size}`. The dialog's Print Setup tab: `paper` (`page.presets` `papers`, `page.paper {kind}`) and its way round; Drawing Scale: `scales` and the custom form; Page Properties: the name is `page.rename`, the Type `page.type` and the Background `page.background`.
The page tabs: `page.add {like}` for the plus control, `page.duplicate {id}` for Duplicate, `page.add {background: true, like}` for Background Page, `page.delete {id}`, `page.move {id, to, renumber}` for dragging a tab and for the Reorder Pages dialog. A window that draws page breaks reads `paper` and `sheets`. The Rust side is `diacraft_engine::draw::{read_setup, paper::{SIZES, PAPERS, SCALES}}`.
Not here, and not hidden by a control that would send them: Fit to Drawing (P1), resizing the page by dragging its edge (P1), keeping the page and the printer paper apart as a user choice (P1; `page.paper` sets the paper, and the size commands set it when the size is a paper), print margins and centring.

## Themes

Applying a theme to a page or to every page, taking it off, and its variants: the engine side of the Design tab's Themes and Variants galleries. The rows are the checklist's "Themes"
(Apply a theme: P0; Apply a theme to one page or all, Theme variants: P1); the format is the VSDX theme part and the cells are the theme cells (the scheme and variation cells, the Quick Style cells).
Code: `crates/engine/src/theme/` (`catalogue.rs`, the five themes; `kit.rs`, the lists they are made of; `style.rs`, the `Theme` style; `apply.rs`, applying), `cmd/theme.rs`, and the writer's side in
`crates/vsdx` (`theme_write.rs`, a definition made into a theme part; `write/themes.rs`, the part added to, rewritten in and taken out of a package). The reference app was not inspected: every rule below is **ours**
and is listed in the report of the task for the check list. **The themes are originals** (`docs/provenance.md`): names, colours, typefaces and lists are our own.
`page` is optional and is the first page that is not a background; `scope` is `page` (the default) or `all` (every page of the drawing, backgrounds too: "pages in one file can differ").

| Command | Parameters | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `theme.list` (query) | `page`?; works with no document | `{themes: [{id, name, scheme, in_drawing, colours: {dark1, light1, dark2, light2, accents: [6], hyperlink, followed_hyperlink, background}, fonts: {heading, body}, variants: [{index, name, monotone, colours: [7]}]}], variants: [names], page}`: the Themes gallery (`colours` as `#rrggbb`; `variants` are the four of that theme, `monotone` marking the single-hue one), and the theme the page has: `page: {page, theme, colors, styles, selection: [{cell, value}]}` with `theme` an id of the list, `none`, or `custom` (a number of a theme the file brought), `colors` and `styles` the variants picked; null with no document |
| `theme.apply` | `theme` (an id of `theme.list`, or `none`), `variant`? (0 to 3, default 0), `scope`?, `page`? | `{theme, variant, scope, pages, changed_pages, shapes_linked, cells_rewritten, theme_added, style_added, locked}`: the pages worked on and those that changed, how many times a shape began to follow the `Theme` style, how many scheme cells of shapes were given the page's numbers, whether the theme's part and the style were added (the style also when a `Theme` style with no cell, which another program's save of an `.odg` leaves, was filled in), and how many shapes on a locked layer were passed over (no link and no cell is written into them) |
| `theme.variant` | `variant`, or `colors` and/or `styles` (each 0 to 3); `scope`?, `page`? | as `theme.apply`, for the theme the page has (an id or `custom`): `variant` picks the colours and the styles together, `colors` and `styles` one of them. A page with no theme is a failure (with `scope` `all` the pages that have none are left out, and it is a failure only when none has) |

Each is one undo step (all the pages of `scope` all together, the part and the style with it), and one that would change nothing (a theme and variant a page has, with every shape following it) is no undo step and does not make the drawing unsaved.
A call that fails (a page that is not there, a drawing at the cap of themes) changes nothing.

* A theme of the catalogue joins the drawing's themes (`Document::themes`) when no part carries its number (`ThemeIndex` is found by `schemeEnum`, never by a part's place). A part the drawing already holds under that number is used as it is, whoever made it. The part is named
  `theme<n>.xml` for the least `n` no theme of the model has (a neutral name: a later change gave up the folder of a VSDX package's path, which the document file of an `.odg` wrote as it was and so named the product in a file of another format; a VSDX package is the same bytes, the writer naming a part it adds itself); **the writer names it again** when it adds it to a package (the least `n` no part has) and a model that holds the same bytes under another name is the same part (`write/themes.rs`), so a
  save of a drawing that was saved does not take a part out and put it back. A drawing holds at most `MAX_THEMES` (256) parts and `MAX_THEME_BYTES` (4 MiB) of them, as many as the reader keeps: past that the command fails and a save refuses.
* The five components of a theme carry **one number**, 1001 (Tidepool), 1002 (Fernwood), 1003 (Cinderglow), 1004 (Inkstone) and 1005 (Plumline), as the pages of real files hold one number in all five cells. Real parts carry 33 to 60, so a
  drawing that holds both never selects the wrong part. Whether the reference app takes a number that no theme of its own has is not known.
* `ThemeIndex`, `ColorSchemeIndex`, `EffectSchemeIndex`, `ConnectorSchemeIndex` and `FontSchemeIndex` of the **page sheet** take the theme's number, and `VariationColorIndex` and `VariationStyleIndex` the variant's place (0 to 3, zero-based). **No theme** writes 0 to all seven, which sends every themed cell
  back to the root style; the part and the style stay in the drawing. A page that holds none of the cells and is given none is not written to.
* The style a shape follows to take the theme's values is the one named `Theme`, made when the drawing has none (the first free `ID`, based on the root style through all three links, **or, in a drawing opened from an `.odg` whose file has a style named `standard` (LibreOffice's default drawing style, the parent of every shape's own style in the files it writes), based on that style, so that a program that draws the style as an empty common style draws the shapes that follow it with the drawing's face and not its own defaults (a serif, in LibreOffice); a file with no such style, a new drawing's blank package among them, gets the style on the root and an `.odg` that names no parent for it**) and used as it is when it has one. It holds what a file's does: the call `THEMEVAL()` with the marker `Themed` in each line,
  fill, shadow and gradient cell the theme answers, the ten rows of `FillGradient`, `Color` and `Font` of `Character` row 0, 65534 in the scheme cells (which sends a shape to its page) and the Quick Style cells. **The Quick Style numbers are ours**: 100 in the four matrices and in the fill, line and shadow colours
  (the first style of the page's style variant and the first colour of its colour variant, which is what makes a variant one click), 0 in the font colour (`dk1`). A shape of type `Shape` (to any depth of groups; a group, a guide and a picture are left alone) whose line, fill or text family has **no style or the root style**, of its own and from the master shape it stands for (a master that links a style gives its instances that style, and a link of the instance would outrank it), follows `Theme` for that family. A shape with a style of its own keeps it, and
  **a cell a person set stays set**: a fill chosen with `shape.format` is not changed by a theme ("standard colours stay put").
* A shape that holds a scheme cell of its own with a plain whole number (not 65534, not 65535, not a formula) takes the number the page now selects (a new theme rewrites it on the shapes), unless the `Lock...` cell that matches is set on the shape or inherited by it (`LockThemeIndex` for `ThemeIndex`, `LockThemeColors` for `ColorSchemeIndex` and `VariationColorIndex`,
  `LockThemeEffects` for `EffectSchemeIndex` and `VariationStyleIndex`, `LockThemeConnectors` and `LockThemeFonts`: our reading, says only that they keep a new theme from changing the number). A cell the page does not change is not written.
* "Apply theme to new shapes": on by default. A shape put on a page that selects a theme follows the `Theme` style as the shapes of `theme.apply` do, in the same undo step: `shape.drop` from a built-in stencil and from a stencil file (the instance and the members it lists), `text.box` (the box keeps the no fill and no line it is made with, and its text is the theme's colour and face),
  `connector.add`, and the shape and connector of `connector.autoconnect`. Nothing is done when the page selects no theme, or the drawing has no `Theme` style (a page of a file that selects a theme and holds none is left as the file has it). A shape that a paste or a duplicate makes keeps the links its source had; there is no option to turn it off.
  **In an `.odg`** the shape's three links to the `Theme` style are saved: its own automatic style names `Theme` as its parent, and the cells the shape has from that style and from no cell of its own or of its master's are not written as constants, so that the file a session of its own opens holds the shape on the style and `theme.variant` moves it. A program that draws the style as an empty common style draws such a shape from the style's own chain (the file's default graphic style, or its own defaults) and not with the theme's colours, as it draws the shapes of the file that follow the theme; a shape whose three links are not all that style is saved on the root with its look as constants and the save says the link is not written.
* The **whole page** is worked out again after a change (`calc`'s `affected.rs`: what `THEMEVAL` reads of the sheet's theme cells is not followed, and a caller whose edit changes them asks for the page, `Changes::whole_page`). A cell holding the marker is not written by a recalculation (`commit.rs`): the renderer asks the theme each time it draws, so the new theme shows at once.
  A cell that holds a number under a theme formula, as a file from another application has, is written with what the theme gives now; and so is every other formula of the page whose result differs from the number the file saved (that is what a whole-page recalculation is, and why a theme on a foreign page may write more than a move does).
  **An instance is given a cell it inherits and does not hold only where the theme changed what the cell works out to on it** (the Recalculation item): an instance that follows its master's style keeps every cell as its file left it, and one the theme reaches by links of its own holds, `Inh` with the theme's value, the cells its master's theme formulas now give it another value for than its master's, which is how it shows the theme.
