The preview draws the engine's `marks` (`snap.marks`), each by its segment: a guide pulled to is lit across the drawing area (a slanted one along its own direction), another line (grid, ruler, alignment, margin, an own side of a turned shape) is a dashed line from the dragged shape to the nearest shape on it, a place is a ring, an equal gap is a double arrow with its length beside it (a turned row's label runs along the row), and a size and an angle have their labels, placed so that none overlaps another; a guide along no axis is drawn like the others and is not taken hold of.
**The keys (ours).** **Alt** held turns snapping off for the drag: nothing is asked and no `snap` is sent. **Shift** holds a move to one axis and keeps a resize's proportions, which the snap does not know of: where the snap would move the axis Shift holds, the move takes the free axis's pull alone, and where it would change a resize's proportions the resize takes none; either then goes without `snap` (so no hold is written), and the release is still the preview. A Ctrl copy-drag snaps its box as a move does, its originals targets, and sends the distance the snap gave to `shape.duplicate`, which takes no `snap`. The Text tool's box and the Text Block tool's drags snap a place (`text.box` and the text block commands take no `snap`). Past 512 shapes a move goes without `snap` (`shape.move` refuses more with it), and its box is not worked out. The arrow keys step by the grid's pitch. **The grid the window draws is the snap's** (`GridCells::grid`, in drawing inches), so a 1:100 drawing's grid follows its scale (it drew paper inches before).
**Guides.** They are drawn from `SnapPage::guides` (the lines `guide.list` gives): a thin line across the drawing area, the one selected wider in the selection's colour; a file's tilted guide is kept and not drawn. They show while the View tab's **Guides** box (`view.guides`) is on: a setting of the drawing's window, as Grid and Ruler are, held in the drawing and kept by a save (**Window settings**, below): the drawing window's `ShowGuides`, on where the window holds none (ours) and on for a drawing with no windows part. A hidden guide is not drawn and cannot be taken hold of, and still pulls a drag that snaps to guides. A drag out of the top ruler is `guide.add` of a horizontal guide where it is let go, out of the left ruler of a vertical one (the drag shows the guides again if they were hidden); a drag let go back over the ruler, or outside the drawing area, makes nothing. A press on a guide where no shape is selects it alone (and a press on a shape takes the shape first; a guide and shapes are never selected together, whatever selects the shapes), and its drag is `guide.move` at the release, the guide and the shapes held to it (its `Connect` rows) previewed where they go. Delete on a selected guide is `guide.delete`; a guide that shapes are glued to is refused, the status bar says so, and the guide stays selected. A guide is not snapped while it is placed or moved. Each of these is one undo step, as the engine makes it. `Snapped::truncated` says the page has more than the index reads. The Rust side is the module `diacraft_engine::snap` (`snap`, `SnapPage`, `SnapSettings`, `Strength`, `Query`, `Subject`, `Use`, `Edges`, `Rect`, `Snapped`, `Hit`, `Target`, `Anchor`, `Glue`, `Axis`, and the commands' own `box_of`, `own_points`, `moved_edges`, `is_square`, `snap_resize`, `MAX_SNAPPED_SHAPES` and `MAX_OWN_SHAPES`).
**The Snap & Glue dialog, the Visual Aids group and the switches (`crates/ui-egui/src/snap_dialog.rs`).** The Visual Aids group's launcher (`SnapAndGlueDialog`) and **Alt+F9** ("Alt+F9 opens the dialog"; a desktop that keeps Alt+F9 for itself, as some Linux window managers do, never passes it on, and the launcher is then the way) open the **Snap & Glue dialog** (`window.snapGlue {tab}`), the one place the window holds the **Snap and Glue switches** (no feature row puts them elsewhere: the map lists `SnapToggle` and `GlueToggle` among the ids off the ribbon, R200, and names no key or status bar control for them). It has two tabs. **General**: the Snap switch at the head of the column of what a shape snaps to (ruler subdivisions, grid, guides, selection handles, vertices, connection points, shape outlines, the alignment box, shape extensions, intersections: the flags of `SnapSettings`: "`SnapSettings` to the boxes under Snap to"), and the Glue switch at the head of the column of what a connector's end glues to (guides, shape handles, shape vertices, connection points, shape geometry: `GlueSettings`), one column under the other in a narrow window. **Advanced**: the thirteen shape extension lines (`SnapExtensions`), then **Snap strength**: six rows under the headings Weak, Strong and Pixels, in the specification's order, Rulers, Grid, Guides, Points, Geometry and Extensions, each a label, a slider and a number box that change together: the slider runs 0 to 30 pixels (the specification's one image shows about 30 steps) and a number past it puts the thumb at the strong end, the box takes 0 to 999 (the slider is named for assistive technology "Grid snap strength" and so on, the box "Grid snap strength in pixels"; `snapglue.strength.<ruler|grid|guides|points|geometry|extensions>.slider` and `.box` in `ui.elements`, `snapglue.strength.note` the line under them). A box that is no whole number of pixels from 0 to 999 is outlined, and OK is refused in words that name the row ("Grid strength: type a whole number of screen pixels from 0 to 999."): the dialog stays open, sends nothing and keeps what was typed (what the reference app's box does with 1000 is open), and the refusal goes when the box is right again. **Every box starts from `snap.settings`**, read when the dialog opens; the dialog works on a draft, and **OK sends one `snap.set` with the options and the strengths that were changed** (no undo step; nothing is sent when none was), Cancel and Escape send nothing, Enter is OK while no control has the keyboard (one that has it, by Tab, takes Enter as its own press), and a refusal (an option whose word in the file is text) is said in the dialog, which stays open. **Left out, as the engine has no setting for it**: the isometric angles (`SnapAngles`, kept empty; the tab says so). The extension lines and Snap to "Shape extensions" are drawn working: they are settings, saved in the file's words, that nothing draws yet, and the tab says that too; and General says under Glue to which of its boxes are saved and do not act yet: a connector's end pulled to a shape handle, a vertex or an outline is left there unglued (no cell form), and Snap to shape extensions does nothing. The captions are ours, from the words (no source read gives the dialog's own), and each box's accessible name says its list ("Snap to guides", "Glue to guides"). **Visual Aids**: **Dynamic Grid** is the document's `dynamic_grid`, checked from the settings at the drawing's generation and revision (an opened file's revisions count from 1 again), and a press is one `snap.set {dynamic_grid}` the other way; **Connection Points** is a window setting, as Guides is (`view.connectionPoints`): on, the connection points of the shapes in view (of 300 of them at most) are marked whatever the tool; off, only while the Connector tool is on or a connector's end is dragged, as before (ours, says only that the box "shows or hides the points on shapes"); an opened file sets it from its drawing window's `ShowConnectionPoints`, off where it has none (ours), it is held in the drawing and kept by a save, and **a new drawing starts it off, with the grid, the rulers and the guides on**, whatever the file before had (a drawing with no windows part shows these). **What it costs is bounded** (a file can turn the box on): a shape's points are read as the snap reads them, a `Connection` section of more than 256 rows (`MAX_CONNECTION_ROWS`, counted over the shape, its master and the styles before any is merged) not read at all, so that the shape shows none and has none for the Connector tool to glue to (the whole shape still can be), once for each revision; and a frame marks at most 4,096 points (`MAX_POINTS_MARKED`, about 0.3 ms; the point a release would glue to is lit besides them): 300 instances of a master of 10,000 connection rows merge 3,000 rows in all on opening (3 million read whole), and 300 of 200 rows (60,000 points) mark 4,096 a frame; **AutoConnect** was drawn quiet here: no specification names where a drawing keeps its AutoConnect setting ("a View check box sets it for the file"), and the window had no AutoConnect arrows for it to turn off. It is the drawing's box (`autoconnect.set`, and the paragraph "The window's AutoConnect" above). **A visible Snap switch and a Snap to Grid box (`ui-egui/src/ribbon/spec.rs`, `status_bar.rs`; DiaCraft's own, kept beside the dialog, which is as it was).** The desktop reference application has no Snap box on its View tab and none in its status bar: snap is turned on and off in the dialog only, and only its web application's View tab has a Snap to Grid box. The window shows the switch where a person looks for it. The Visual Aids group has a second column, **Snap** (`SnapToggle`) and **Snap to Grid** (`SnapToGrid`; the ids are the map's names of commands that are off its ribbon, R200, and the captions are ours): check boxes that read the document's own `snap` and `grid` options (the checked state is `snap.settings` at the drawing's revision, so a program's `snap.set` and the dialog's OK show in them, each moving the revision, and an undo leaves them as they are set) and whose press is one `snap.set` with the option the other way, as Dynamic Grid's is (no undo step). Snap to Grid is the dialog's Snap to > Grid box and no more: **ruler subdivisions pull as well** (the default has both on), so a drag is free only with both Grid and Ruler subdivisions off. The status bar has a **Snap mark** (a magnet and "Snap on" or "Snap off", struck through when off, `status.snap`) at the right of the page count, which shows the same switch and whose click is the same `snap.set {snap}`; it is left out where the bar has no room for it. All three are quiet with no drawing open. **The snap strength sliders** (the reference's Rulers 4, Grid 5, Guides 10, Points 10, Geometry 8 and Extensions 13 pixels, 0 to 999) are the dialog's Advanced rows, above. **The Glue switch governs the window's own glue too**: the window's pick of a connection point or a whole shape under a connector's end (the Connector tool's drag, the drag of a selected connector's end, and the point lit under the pointer) takes nothing with the Glue switch off or `glue_connection_points` off, and the end is then free and snapped as a free end is (`Use::Connect`, which with the switch off glues to nothing); a connection point is taken only with glue to connection points on, and **the whole of a shape only with the same option on**, since the engine puts an end glued to the whole of a shape at that shape's connection point nearest the other end and `connector.add` refuses a shape that has none (a project rule: no specification says which option governs glue to the whole of a shape; the Microsoft 365 list names `GlueToShapeGeometry` beside "Shape-to-shape glue", and Shape geometry would turn it off in every new drawing).

## Layers

Home, Editing, Layers: a page's layers, the shapes on them, and what the layers do to the picture, the pointer and the paper. Layers are **per page**: the rows of
the `Layer` section of the page's own sheet, keyed by `IX` from 0, and a shape's layers are the `IX` values joined by semicolons in its own `LayerMember` cell. A layer is asked
for by its number or by its name. Every command is one undo step, and one that is refused changes nothing. The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked **ours** are DiaCraft's own.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `layer.list` | `page`? | a query: `{page, layers}`, each layer `{id, name, name_univ, visible, print, active, lock, snap, glue, color, transparency, shapes}`; `color` is `#rrggbb` or `null` for 255, `transparency` the cell's share from 0 to 1, `shapes` how many shapes (the members of groups too) are on it |
| `layer.add` | `name`, `page`?, `visible`?, `print`?, `active`?, `lock`?, `snap`?, `glue`?, `color`?, `transparency`? | `{page, id, name}`: a row with all eleven cells (`Visible` 1, `Print` 1, `Active` 0, `Lock` 0, `Snap` 1, `Glue` 1, `Color` 255, `ColorTrans` 0, `Status` 0, and the name as `Name` and `NameUniv`) at the end of the page's table |
| `layer.set` | `layer`, `page`?, `name`?, `visible`?, `print`?, `active`?, `lock`?, `snap`?, `glue`?, `color`? (`#rrggbb` or `none`), `transparency`? | `{page, id, name, deactivated}`: the cells written; a rename keeps the shapes on the layer; a call that changes nothing is refused |
| `layer.assign` | `ids`, `layers` (numbers or names, empty for none), `mode`? (`replace`, `add`, `remove`), `preserve_members`?, `page`? | `{page, shapes, layers, written}`: each shape's `LayerMember` is written as the set of layers in rising order; a call that changes no list is no undo step |
| `layer.delete` | `layer` or `unreferenced: true`, `shapes`? (`keep`, `delete`), `page`? | `{page, removed, shapes_deleted}`: the layers are taken out and the numbers close up |

* Our rule. A layer's name is its text with the white space at both ends taken off. It is not empty, holds no control character, is at most 255 characters, and is **not another layer's of the page** (no table repeats one). It is **not one of the five names the OpenDocument format reserves** (`layout`, `background`, `backgroundobjects`, `controls` and `measurelines`): a drawing in that format reads those five as no layer, so such a layer would be lost on a save in it. `NameUniv` follows a rename when it was the name.
* Our rule: a locked layer cannot be activated. `layer.set` refuses `active: true` on a locked layer, and **locking an active layer makes it inactive**, which the answer says (`deactivated`). A layer a file holds both locked and active is left as the file has it until it is changed.
* Our rule. A shape added by `shape.drop`, `shape.draw`, `connector.add`, an AutoConnect or `text.box` joins **every active layer that is not locked** (a locked layer takes no new members). With none, the shape gets no `LayerMember` cell. A shape pasted, duplicated or made by a shape operation keeps the layers it has.
* Our rule. A list is a set: the numbers are written in rising order and each once. `replace` makes the list these layers, `add` adds them, `remove` takes them. A layer that is **locked takes no new shape** (`replace` and `add` are refused for it), and **a shape that is on a locked layer is not edited** (it cannot be selected), so a call that names one is refused in words: unlock the layer first. A shape's own `LayerMember` is the one read, never a list its master would give, and a shape that has a master and is put on no layer is given an empty cell, which stops the master's list. Assigning a group changes its own list and **keeps what its members hold**, unless `preserve_members` is false, which writes the list on every member too, at any depth (the Layer dialog's Preserve group member layers box).
* Our rule. A removal **closes the numbers**: the rows after it move up by the removed numbers below them and every `LayerMember` that changes is written again (the shapes of the page and of its groups, one write each, counted against the page's cap). **It costs one walk of the page, one probe of a set and one binary search of the removed numbers for each number of each list**, never a scan of the table for a number (the answer's `lookups`). A shape that was on the layer alone is on no layer, or **is deleted with `shapes: delete`** (the desktop's dialog deletes them; the command keeps them unless asked, and the window asks first). `unreferenced: true` removes every layer no shape is on (Remove unreferenced layers). No `Status` is written; a vacant row a file holds is no layer here and keeps its number until a removal on the page writes the numbers again.
* Our rule. **Every `LayerMember` text is read by one bounded reader, `layers::read_members`, and charged to one budget for the walk** (`layers::MemberBudget`): a text longer than 16 KiB is read as on no layer and counted (`overlong`) before any of it is copied or parsed, a shorter one is split after its bytes are charged against **8 MiB of list text for one call**, at most 1,024 pieces of it, every piece counted, a number kept once (a set, one probe for each piece that is a number) and in rising order. A page whose lists come to more than the 8 MiB is **refused in words** ("the shapes list more layer memberships than the layers are read for"), after a small part of the page; the paths a removal keeps are charged too (at most 16 million steps). A table of more than 4,096 layers is refused when it is read. The window's pointer, the snap, `layer.list`, `layer.assign` and `layer.delete` read through it, and **so do the selections by type, region and layer (`select.by_type`, `select.by_region` and `select.choices`) and the lock test of the shape operations**: one rule for every reader, **a shape is on the layers its own `LayerMember` cell lists, and an instance with no list of its own is on no layer whatever its master lists** (before a change the selections and the shape operations resolved the cell through the master, and counted an instance on its master's layers where `layer.list` did not).
* Our rule. What a table makes of a shape (`diacraft_engine::draw::layers::page_standing`): it is **hidden** when it lists layers and every one is hidden (a shape on a hidden and a shown layer is drawn), **locked** when any layer it lists is locked, **off paper** when no layer it lists prints, and it allows **no snap** or **no glue** when no layer it lists does. A list that names no row of the table is no list. The renderer draws by the first and the print by the third, and the layer's colour goes over a shape on exactly that layer; the window's pointer reads the first two and the glue, and **the snap engine leaves the shapes of a layer whose Snap box is clear out of its targets** (`snap::adapter::build`; a shape on a layer that allows it, and a shape on none, are targets as before).
* Our rule (Lock stops shapes on the layer from being selected, moved or edited). **A command that changes the drawing refuses a shape it names by its `ID` that is on a locked layer**, before it runs and with nothing changed, in words that name the shape and the layer ("shape 3 is on layer 1 ("Frozen"), which is locked: a shape on a locked layer is not selected, moved or edited; unlock the layer first"). The rule is the registry's and not each command's (`cmd/locked.rs`, asked by `Session::check` for every command, also a step of a `command.batch`, which is then refused whole): **every journaled command**, the shapes being its `ids` and its `id`, except the commands whose `id` is a page's or a stencil's (`page.rename`, `page.duplicate`, `page.delete`, `page.move`, `stencil.open`, `stencil.close`); a query (`shape.placement`, `shape.formatting`, `edit.find`, `hyperlink.list`, `text.block` and the rest that are not journaled) still answers about such a shape. `edit.copy` is refused too, as the shape cannot be selected. The page is the one the command names, else the first that holds every shape named. **Each shape goes by its own list**, as the window's pointer does: a group on no locked layer is moved, turned or flipped though a member of it is on one, and the member named on its own is refused; a command that would edit the member through the group is refused (below). A connector's ends (`from`, `to`, `target`) are not edits of the shapes they are glued to, and governs them. A drawing none of whose pages has a locked layer costs the read of each page's table and no walk; else one walk of the page finds the shapes named (`layers::first_locked`). A test walks every registered command (`layers_locked_edits.rs`), so a command added later is held to the rule without a line of its own.
* Our rule (M3 reads `Glue` as whether a connector may glue to a member). **A connector's end is glued anew to no shape whose layers allow no glue** (every layer its own list names has `Glue` 0; a shape on such a layer and one that allows it is glued to, and so is a shape on no layer): `connector.add` and `connector.reconnect` refuse an end that names one, in words that name the layer ("shape 4 is on layer 1 ("Bare"), whose Glue box is off: a connector is not glued to a shape whose layers allow no glue; tick Glue for the layer first"); an end given as a place and snapped (`snap: true`, `snap.at` with `connect`) is pulled to none of its connection points or outlines and glued to none of it, and stays free; **AutoConnect offers no such shape**: `connector.neighbour` and a click on an arrow pass over it to the next neighbour, `connector.autoconnect` from it or `to` it is refused, and so is one that would make a shape onto active layers none of which allows glue (the new shape joins them). **Glue a drawing holds is kept as it is** when a layer's Glue box is turned off: its `Connect` rows stay, the end follows its shape, and a save keeps them.
* Our rule (the specifications say nothing of a group whose member is on a locked layer, so the edit is refused, which loses nothing). **A command that edits a group through its members refuses a group with a member on a locked layer**, in words that name the member, the group and the layer ("shape 2 in group 4 is on layer 1 ("Frozen"), which is locked: a group is not formatted through a member on a locked layer, ..."), with nothing changed: `shape.format` and `text.format` of the group (formats its members), `shape.resize` of it, by `id` or among `ids` (its members follow its size, a member at any depth), and `layer.assign` with `preserve_members: false` (it writes the members' lists). A move, a turn, a flip, the group's own text and an assignment that keeps the members' lists change no member's cells and run. A delete, a cut and an ungroup of the group are refused too (below). A test walks every registered command with the group (`layers_locked_more.rs`).
* Our rule (the specifications say nothing of the route of a connector on a locked layer). **A connector on a locked layer keeps its route**: when a shape it is glued to moves (by itself, in a group or with a container), its glued ends follow by their formulas, as a fixed route's do, and its `Geometry` rows are not made again; nothing routes it, and a rebuild of line jumps neither gives it a jump nor takes one away (the connectors that cross it jump over it where the page's code gives them the jump).
* Our rule (the specifications say nothing of a shape on a locked layer that a container holds or that is a callout tied to a shape). **A shape on a locked layer does not go with the shape it follows**: a move, a place or an align that would carry a member of a container or a callout tied to a shape moved, a delete or a cut of a container that holds one, and a delete or a cut of a shape whose tied callout is on a locked layer are refused in words that name that shape and its layer, with nothing changed.
* Our rule (the specifications say nothing of a group whose member is on a locked layer, so the decision is least surprise, as a container with a locked member refuses). **A group that holds a shape on a locked layer, at any depth, is not deleted, cut or ungrouped**, named or taken with a container that holds it, in words that name the member, the group and the layer ("shape 2 in group 4 is on layer 1 ("Frozen"), which is locked: a group that holds a shape on a locked layer is not deleted, ..."), with nothing changed: a delete and a cut would take the member with the group, and an ungroup would rewrite the cells that tie it to the group (its frame, and its formulas that name the group). **A container that holds a shape on a locked layer is not disbanded** (`container.disband`), which would rewrite that shape's ties to it. A copy or a duplicate of such a group is made, and the copy's member is on the same layer (a pasted shape keeps its layers).
* Our rule (the specifications say nothing of a container on a locked layer, so the automatic steps pass over it and say so). **A container on a locked layer is left as it is**: a shape moved, dropped or pasted over it does not join it (it joins the container under it that is not on a locked layer, if one is), a member moved out of it stays a member, and it is not resized when a member moves, grows or goes, or when a container it holds grows; a container it held that is disbanded does not hand it the members. The command's answer says so in `notes` ("container 5 is on layer 1 ("Frozen"), which is locked, so it was left as it was: no shape joined or left it and it was not resized"): `shape.move`, `shape.drop`, `shape.resize`, `shape.delete`, `edit.paste`, `shape.duplicate`, `container.add`, `container.add_member`, `container.disband` and the fitting commands. A command that would take a member out of it (`container.add` round the member, `container.add_member` and `container.attach_edge` of the member) is refused in words that name the container and its layer, and Add to Underlying Container over it says why none takes the shape. A tie to a shape that is deleted still goes from it, as a connector's glue to a shape deleted does. Fit to Contents and the other commands that name it are refused.
* Our rule (and the specifications say nothing of a theme and a locked layer). **A theme is the drawing's, and writes no cell into a shape on a locked layer**: `theme.apply` and `theme.variant` write the page's cells, so a shape that follows the theme already draws with it, but give a shape on a locked layer (a member of a group by its own list) no link to the `Theme` style and no scheme number, and count the shapes passed over in the answer's `locked`. The page is worked out again as for any theme, which is no edit of a shape.
* **What the window does with them** (`crates/ui-egui/src/layers_dialog.rs`, `edit/scene.rs`). A click, a rubber band, a Select All and the second click into a group **pass over a shape that is on a locked layer or only on hidden layers**; the connector tool's pick of a shape or a connection point to glue to, at either end and for the drag of a connector's end or of an AutoConnect arrow, passes over one whose layers allow no glue (the end is free there), and such a shape shows no AutoConnect arrows. A Delete deletes with the shapes the connectors glued to them that can be picked, and leaves one on a locked layer or only on hidden ones. Home, Editing, **Layers** is a menu of **Assign to Layer** (`window.assignLayer`, with a shape selected) and **Layer Properties** (`window.layerProperties`): each opens a dialog that works on a draft and sends **one `command.batch`** on OK (Layer Properties also on Apply), and nothing on Cancel or Escape. Layer Properties has the columns Name, #, Visible, Print, Active, Lock, Snap, Glue and Color, New..., Remove (which asks first for a layer with shapes: delete them, or keep them), Rename..., a Layer color row of the first sixteen colours of the document's table with None, Transparency, and Remove unreferenced layers; ticking Lock clears Active. Assign to Layer lists the page's layers with a box ticked for a layer every shape selected is on, clear for one none is on, and a dash for one only some are on (a mixed box left alone changes nothing), with All, None, New... and Preserve group member layers.
* **Files.** A page's `Layer` section and each shape's `LayerMember` are saved in a VSDX file as cells, in `.diacraft` as the model, and in an `.odg` as `draw:layer-set` (the model writer declares the layers of the pages' tables after the five of LibreOffice, in the order the pages and rows give, with `draw:display` from `Visible` and `Print` and `draw:protected` from `Lock`; a layer the package declares is not added twice, and one whose flags the row changed has its attribute written; a drawing that has no layer row is given no layer set) and in DiaCraft's document file for what the format cannot say (`Active`, `Snap`, `Glue`, `Color`, `ColorTrans` and the rows themselves). **A drawing read from an `.odg` and saved in place declares a layer added in DiaCraft in the file's own layer set** (a new layer appends a `draw:layer`): a layer of the model's pages that no page of the file reads (not in the document's set, not in a page's own) is written at the end of the document's `draw:layer-set` of `office:master-styles`, with `draw:display` from `Visible` and `Print` and `draw:protected` from `Lock`, under the prefix the file binds to the drawing namespace; a file whose styles hold no layer set is given one holding the new layers, and a `styles.xml` with no `office:master-styles` is refused in words. The rest of the row (`Active`, `Snap`, `Glue`, `Color`, `ColorTrans`) and the page's rows stay in DiaCraft's document file. A shape added to the page is written on the first of its layers when the set declares that layer at the row the model has, a layer the save declares included (on a page that reads the document's set), else on `layout` with its list carried; a shape of the file keeps the `draw:layer` it had, whatever its `LayerMember` says, its list riding on its style, **except that one put on a layer the save itself declares gets its `draw:layer` written** (the first layer of its list, when it is a layer the save adds and its page reads the document's set), its list still riding on its style. **A layer of the file whose `Visible`, `Print` or `Lock` the model changed has its `draw:display` and `draw:protected` written** (regenerated, merged by name): on the first `draw:layer` of that name in the document's set, in place when the layer has the attribute and added when it has not and the flag is not the default; a page whose rows then differ from the set keeps its own in DiaCraft's document file, and an attribute that already says the flags keeps its bytes. **The row that changed decides**, in both writers: a page's row of the layer is changed when it is not what the page had when the drawing was read (DiaCraft's data included, so rows that ride in the document file are no change); of two pages that changed it, the later page's decides; and when no row changed, the set keeps what it says, so the first page that has the layer still decides. A drawing with no `.odg` file has no changed row (its package declares none of its layers), and the model writer gives each layer the first page's flags. **A page with a layer set of its own** (a `draw:layer-set` in its `draw:page`, which LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 reads) has a changed flag written in that set, on the first `draw:layer` of the name, as the document's set has it, and DiaCraft's document file then carries none of its rows. The names the save keeps and the markup it makes of them are charged by their bytes to the memory a save holds before they are kept or made.
* **What is left.** A layer added in DiaCraft to a page with a layer set of its own is declared in the document's set, which that page does not read in another program. A callout that goes with a shape deleted is judged by its own list and not by its members'. A shape pasted or dragged in from another page does not bring its layers or join a layer of the same name (the "Layers come with shapes" row). A master's own layer table is not read when a shape is dropped from it. No right-click menu has Assign to Layer.

## The Insert tab

Insert, Illustrations, **Pictures**; Insert, Links, **Link**; Insert, Text, **Field** and **Symbol**. A picture is a Foreign shape, a link is a row of the shape's `Hyperlink` section, a field is a `fld` of the
shape's text and a row of its `Field` section, and a symbol is characters put into the text. Every command is one undo step, and one that is refused changes nothing. The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked
**ours** are DiaCraft's own, and each is a row in the check list.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `insert.picture` | `path`, `x`?, `y`?, `width`?, `height`?, `page`? | `{page, id, format, width, height, drawn, shared, bytes, note?}`: a Foreign shape (rectangle geometry, `ForeignType` and `ImgWidth`, `ImgHeight`, `ImgOffsetX`, `ImgOffsetY` from the picture) on the active layers, in front, selected |
| `hyperlink.set` | `id`, `links`, `page`? | `{page, id, links}`: the list the shape has now, as `hyperlink.list` gives it; an empty list takes them all away |
| `hyperlink.list` | `id`, `page`? | a query: `{page, id, links}`, each link `{name, description, address, sub_address, extra_info, frame, new_window, default, invisible, kind}`, `kind` where it goes (`page`, `shape`, `web`, `mail`, or what is refused) |
| `hyperlink.follow` | `id`, `link`?, `page`? | a query: `{page, id, link, new_window, kind, ...}` with `target_page` and `target_shape` for a link of this drawing and `url` for a web or mail address; it goes nowhere itself |
| `text.insert_field` | `id`, `category`, `field`?, `formula`?, `format`?, `at`?, `page`? | `{page, id, ix, at, category, field, formula, format, type, shown}`: a `fld` in the text at character `at` and a row in the shape's `Field` section |
| `text.field_choices` | `id`?, `page`? | a query: `{page, categories, formats}`, the dialog's eight categories each with its fields `{id, label, formula, type, format}`, and the date, number and text formats the Data Format list offers |
| `text.field_sample` | `id`, `category`, `field`?, `formula`?, `format`?, `page`? | a query: the answer of `text.insert_field` for the field as it would be made, `shown` being what it would show; nothing changes |
| `text.update_fields` | `id`?, `page`? | `{updated, examined, skipped}`: the text of each field worked out again from its row |
| `text.insert` | `id`, `text`, `at`?, `page`? | `{page, id}`: the characters put in before character `at` (from 0, not counting fields), the runs and fields of the text kept |

* Our rule. The file's bytes are judged, never its name: PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP and TIFF are bitmaps (`ForeignType` `Bitmap`), EMF and WMF are metafiles (`EnhMetaFile`; a WMF is typed `image/x-emf` as the sample drawings hold theirs). **A GIF or a TIFF is held and drawn as a placeholder** (the renderer decodes neither yet) and the answer's `note` says so, which the window puts in the status bar. An SVG and any other bytes are refused in words. The bytes are copied as they are and **never decoded by the engine**.
* Our rule. A picture is as big as its header says at 96 pixels to the inch, a metafile as its frame, and is made to fit within 80 percent of the page when it is bigger; a `width` or a `height` alone keeps the proportions. It goes at the middle of the page unless `x` and `y` are given.
* The readers' budgets: a file of more than **64 MiB** or a picture of more than **64 megapixels** is refused in words before the file is read where its size says so, and a drawing holds at most **512 MiB** of pictures (the VSDX reader's cap). The same bytes are one picture, held once however many shapes show them (one `Arc<Media>`). A picture has no connection points. **It costs one walk of the pages** to sum what the drawing holds, at most 2,000,000 shapes.
* Our rule. A new link is the row `Row_<n>` for the least `n` from 1 that no row has; a set is the shape's whole list (a row it names is kept and the cells it gives change, a row it leaves out is taken away, a link with no name is a new row), at most one link is the default, and a link needs an address or a sub-address. A link with **no address goes to this drawing**: its sub-address is a page's name, or a shape as `<page name>/<shape name>` or `<page name>/Sheet.<id>`. At most 256 links, 4,096 characters of each text, 4,096 rows of a section read and 8 MiB of text for a call; a larger section is refused in words.
* A click follows **web (`http`, `https`), FTP (`ftp`, `ftps`) and mail (`mailto`) addresses and the links of this drawing, and nothing else**: a file, a path, `javascript:` or another program's scheme is refused in words, since a drawing from anywhere must not start a program. What is handed to the browser is **written again from what was checked**: every percent sequence must decode (a lone `%`, or bytes that are no text, refuse the address), a control character or an invisible mark, in the address or in what its sequences stand for, refuses it, the fragment is written as percent sequences, and a mail address passes on its recipients and `subject`, `body`, `cc` and `bcc` only (`attach` and the rest are refused, since some mail clients attach a file by one; a line break in a body is refused). The link followed is the one marked `Default`, else the first that is not invisible, else the first.
* Our rule. A new field is the row of the `IX` one more than the highest of the shape's rows and its `fld`s, from 0, and a shape holds at most **1,024**. The categories are the dialog's (Custom Formula, Date/Time, Document Info, Geometry, Object Info, Page Info, Shape Data, User-defined Cells), each field the function maps it to. A field is made with its kind's default format (a date and time `{<211>}`, a length `esc(7)`, an angle `esc(12)`, a shape data row's own) which the Data Format list changes, and **shows its value through its format**, which the file stores too. The Object Info `Type` field is `TYPEDESC()`. **The clock is the session's and in UTC** (the build reads no time zone); a new drawing shows the time it is made at for its creation and last edit, and a print date not yet set shows nothing. A field **updates by itself** when an edit moves or sizes its shape and when a page is added, renamed, deleted, copied or moved; `text.update_fields` brings the clock and the file name up to date, and is how every field of an opened file is brought up to date (the text a file holds is kept until then). **A field on a background page that reads the page** (`PAGENUMBER`, `PAGENAME`: the page number of an OpenDocument master page among them) shows the page that uses the background, so no refresh works it out to the background page's own answer: it keeps the text the file saved.
* A refresh **counts every field before it works any out**: at most 100,000 for `text.update_fields` and 10,000 for an edit's own refresh, a shape of more than 1,024 fields is **skipped and counted** (`skipped`), the whole call shares one budget of 5 million evaluator steps, and for each shape its rows and the texts shown are indexed once, so the work follows the number of fields and not their square. A custom formula and a format are at most 4,096 characters.
* **SYMBOL** (ours; leaves the contents open). The Symbol dialog has six sets (Common, Currency, Arrows, Math, Greek, Shapes) of characters the bundled faces draw (a unit test holds each to it), and the ribbon's menu fifteen of the most used. `text.insert` puts the characters in; there is no symbol command of its own.
* **What the window does with them** (`crates/ui-egui/src/insert_picture.rs`, `insert_links.rs`, `insert_field.rs`, `insert_symbol.rs`). **Pictures** (`window.insertPicture`) shows the desktop's file chooser on a thread of its own (the picture types as the filter and all files as a second, since the engine tells a picture by its bytes), sends `insert.picture` with the path chosen and selects the shape; with `path` it needs no dialog. **Link** (`window.hyperlinks`, Cmd+K; one shape selected) opens the Hyperlinks dialog: a list of the shape's links, Address, Sub-address (a page of the drawing from a list), Description, New Window, Default, Invisible and New, Delete and Default; OK sends one `hyperlink.set`, Cancel nothing. **A Ctrl+click** on a shape with a link (with nothing else selected) sends `window.followLink`: a link in this drawing shows its page and selects its shape, and a web, FTP or mail address is handed to the system by egui's `OpenUrl` at the next frame. **Field** (`window.field`) opens the Field dialog (a Category list, a Field Name list, a Data Format list with a **sample** of what the field will show, a custom formula for the Custom category), and OK sends one `text.insert_field` at the caret of an open text box or at the end of the one shape selected. **Symbol** (`window.symbols`, `window.symbol {text}`) puts characters at the caret or over the selection of an open text box, or at the end of the text of the shape selected.
* **Files.** A picture, a link, a field and the characters of a symbol are cells and text, so `.diacraft` holds them as the model; **VSDX** (the model writer, for a drawing made here) writes the picture as a part of the package with its relationship, the `Hyperlink` rows as cells and the `Field` rows with their `fld`s; **`.odg`** (the model writer) writes a picture, a link as a `text:a` wrapper, and a page number, page count, file name and date field as the fields of that format, and any other field as the text it shows. **A *new* picture saved over a page of an existing VSDX file is written**, as a new page's picture is: a row of the page part to a part that holds it, one of the file's that holds the picture, else one made from the drawing's bytes; an embedded object new to such a page is still refused in words (save the drawing as a `.diacraft` drawing or a new `.vsdx`). **What is left:** a new field of a kind other than the four above on a drawing saved over an existing `.odg` is written as its text with a save note. **A field of the file whose characters a refresh changed keeps its element** in a save over the `.odg`: the splice writes the element again, its start and end tags as the file had them, around the new characters; a field the model holds only as its text (an author's name, a fixed date) that was retyped is written as the new characters with a save note, as item 4 says.
* **What is left.** The Hyperlinks dialog has no Browse button and no "Use relative path" box (no spec row says what they do); the text box does not show a field in place (U17), so a field is inserted by the dialog, not typed; no field is edited after it is made (change it by taking the text out and inserting it again); no SVG, GIF or TIFF is drawn; a link to a file or a program is never followed by a click; Paste as Hyperlink and Insert Hyperlink from the shortcut menu are not built.

## Shape Data

Data, Show/Hide, **Shape Data Window**; View, Task Panes, **Shape Data**; and a shape's right-click menu, **Data**, **Shape Data...** and **Define Shape Data...**.
 A field is a row of the shape's `Property` section, named by the author (formulas read it as `Prop.<name>`), with the cells `Label`, `Prompt`, `Type`, `Format`, `Value`, `SortKey`, `Invisible`, `Verify` (the cell
the Ask on drop box is: gives `Prop.<name>.Verify`), `LangID` and `Calendar`; a shape takes the rows of its master. A value is a cell write, so a formula that reads `Prop.<name>` is worked out again (`settle_cells`) and
a field of the shape's text that shows it follows. Every command is one undo step, and one that is refused changes nothing. The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked **ours** are DiaCraft's own, and each is a row in
The check list. Shape data **sets** are not built.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `shapedata.list` | `id`, `hidden`?, `page`? | a query: `{page, id, name, fields, hidden}`: the shape's fields in the order the window lists them, each `{row, label, type, type_code, format, value, unit, formula, shown, prompt, sort_key, invisible, ask, lang_id, calendar, calendar_name, items}` (`items` for a list), the hidden ones left out unless `hidden` is true, and `hidden` the count left out. `type` is `unknown` for a `Type` cell that holds no number of the eight, with `type_code` the whole number it holds (`null` for a fraction or a text); `ask` is the `Verify` cell; `lang_id` is the `LangID` cell's text and `calendar_name` the name of the `calendar` number |
| `shapedata.set` | `id`, `row` and `value`, or `values`, `page`? | `{page, id, changed, values, fields, notes}`: the value of `row` entered as the field's type reads it, or the list `values` of `{row, value}`, up to 512, all in one undo step; the answer's `fields` is every field as `shapedata.define` gives them; a value the field already has is no undo step; a text that is no value of the type is refused in words |
| `shapedata.define` | `id`, `fields`, `page`? | `{page, id, changed, values, fields, notes}`: the shape's whole list: a field that names a `row` is kept and the cells it gives change, a field with none is a new row, and a row the list leaves out goes; a field gives `label`, `name` (new fields only), `type`, `format`, `value`, `prompt`, `sort_key`, `invisible`, `ask`, `lang_id` (a language tag such as `en-US`) and `calendar` (0 to 13); `fields` is every field, hidden ones too, in the order listed, and `notes` what a change of type left as it was; a list that changes nothing is no undo step |
| `shapedata.types` | none | a query: `{types, calendars}`, the eight types `{code, id, label, list, formats}` with the pictures the dialog offers for each, and the fourteen calendars `{code, name}` |

* The `Type` cell's numbers are 0 string, 1 fixed list, 2 number, 3 boolean, 4 variable list, 5 date, 6 duration and 7 currency (Learn and MS-VSDX give the table, and the files use no other number); no cell, or an empty one, is 0. **A row whose `Type` holds another whole number, a fraction or a text is of an unknown type** (ours): its value is read and typed as a string's, `shapedata.list` gives `type` `unknown` and the whole number it holds as `type_code` (`null` for a fraction or a text, which is not the type it rounds to), the dialog's Type box says **Unknown (8)**, and the cell is kept as written by a value entered and by a list that names the row, until a type is given to it; `unknown` is not a type a field can be given.
* Our rule, and the Value cell page for the guard. An entered value is read **as its field's type** and stored as that type's value with its `U`: a string and a list item as text (`STR`), a truth value `1` or `0` (`BOOL`; `true`, `false`, `yes`, `no`, `on`, `off`), a date `YYYY-MM-DDThh:mm:ss` (`DATE`; typed `2026-03-04`, `3/4/2026` or with a time, `2:30 pm`), a currency its amount at four places and its code (`CY`; `$1,234.50`, `12.50 EUR`), a duration in days with the unit it was typed in (`ED` for a number alone; `36 hours`, `30 min`, `2 d`, `1 week`) and a number with its own unit (`3 in`, `1/8 in`; a number typed with none takes the unit the cell has). A text that is no value of the type is refused in words, with the forms it takes, and nothing changes; an empty text clears the field. **A date never set is a plain 0 with no unit**, so a new date field, a cleared one and a field changed to a date with no value are written so, and a date never set changed to another type has no value. **The Value cell page says an entered value replaces any formula there, even one under `GUARD`**, so the write is the forced one; the other cells of a row are written as a drag in the window writes them, and a `GUARD` on one is a refusal in words.
* A list's items are its `Format`, apart at the semicolons ("list items sit semicolon-separated in the field's Format cell"), at most 1,024. A fixed list takes only its items and the empty value. On a variable list a new value joins the `Format` **at its end** (ours: the page says only that it joins the list), in the same undo step.
* Our rule. What a field shows is its value through its `Format` picture (`calc::field_text`; the pictures are those of `FORMAT`): the text as it is for a string and a list item, `TRUE` or `FALSE` for a truth value, a date as `M/d/yyyy` (and `h:mm am/pm` when it has a time), a currency as `$1,234.50` (the system's regional setting is not read: ours is the US one), a duration as its unit's short word (`36 h`) and a number in plain digits with its unit. A date never set (a plain 0 with no unit) shows nothing, and the serial 0 with `DATE` shows 12/30/1899 (ours). The type decides the pictures the dialog offers: `shapedata.types`.
* Fields are listed **by `SortKey`, then row order, the unkeyed last** (keys 1, 3, 2 and a fourth with none list as the first, the third, the second, the fourth). The keys are compared as text ignoring case (`str::to_lowercase`, ours: not the locale's collation), and equal keys keep the order of the rows, a master's rows first (the order `Resolver::effective_rows` gives). Rows themselves are never moved.
* Our rule. A new field's row name is `name` (letters, digits and underscores, not starting with a digit, at most 128 characters, a name no other field has, ignoring case), else its label with each run of characters that are no letter, digit or `_` made one `_` and the ends trimmed (a name that starts with a digit gets a `_` first), and the least `_2`, `_3` and so on that no row of the shape has. A field with neither is `Property<n>` for the least `n` from 1 whose name is free, and its label is its name. **A row is never renamed**: formulas read it by its name, so a change of name is a change of label, and a `name` that differs from the `row` it is given for is refused.
* Our rule. `shapedata.define` is the shape's whole list, as `hyperlink.set` is: **a row the list leaves out is taken away, hidden or not**, a cell is written only when it differs from what the row reads now, and a new row has `Value`, `Label`, `Prompt`, `Type`, `Format`, `SortKey`, `Invisible`, `Verify` and `Calendar` (0 unless one is given), and `LangID` when one is given. A change of **type** reads the value again as the new type: it is kept as that type's value when it reads as one and is **kept as it was**, with a note in the answer, when it does not (a change loses nothing). A row the shape takes from its master is marked deleted on the shape when the list leaves it out, and the master is left as it was. At most 512 fields, 4,096 characters of a label, name, prompt or sort key and 32,768 of a value or a format.
* The Ask on drop cell is `Verify` in a file and in a formula (`Prop.<name>.Verify`), kept and listed (`ask`), and only the window calls it Ask on drop; a cell a file spells `Ask` is no cell of the section, asks nothing and is kept as it is. The window asks `shapedata.list` for the shape a master was dropped as, and when a field has the box ticked it opens a card of those fields (below).
* **Hidden fields** (`Invisible`: "a field marked Hidden keeps its value and leaves the pane. The Invisible cell holds the flag and may be a formula"): the flag is read as the cell's value, so a formula that gives 1 hides the field; a hidden field is listed by `shapedata.list` with `hidden: true`, and the dialog lists it.
* **`LangID` is a language tag** such as `en-US`: it is listed as the text the cell holds, given as a tag (letters and digits apart at hyphens, the first part 2 to 8 letters and each other at most 8, at most 64 characters: the shape of a tag and not the registry of its parts, ours) and written as given; anything else is refused in words. **A number a file holds**, such as 1033, is listed as the text it is, kept as written, and changes nothing. A new field is given no language unless one is given. **`Calendar`** is one of the fourteen numbers MS-VSDX lists, 0 to 13, by MS-VSDX's names (which follows MS-VSDX where Learn lists ten): 0 Western, 1 Arabic Hijri, 2 Hebrew Lunar, 3 Chinese National, 4 Japanese Emperor, 5 Thai Buddhist, 6 Korean Danki, 7 Japanese Saka Era, 8 Transliterated English, 9 Transliterated French, 10 Gregorian US English, 11 Gregorian Middle East French, 12 Gregorian Arabic and 13 Um-al-Qura; `calendar_name` gives the name and `shapedata.types` the table. **A new field holds 0**, as every file's does. Only a date reads it, so the dialog offers it for a date only, and the build shows every date in the Western calendar. A number past the table is kept as read and has no name.
* **Hostile input and cost.** A shape that holds more than **4,096** rows of the section is refused before any is merged (the count is of the shape, its master and its styles, `rows_counted`), a shape of more than **512** fields is refused as a whole (a list that left some out would take them away), and the text a call reads is charged to **8 MiB** and each text cut at its cap as it is read. A call reads the section once (one merge for the whole call, `tests/it/shapedata_cost.rs` counts rows merged and lookups built: 512 fields defined build 17, and setting 512 values stays under 64), and the engine's own checks are the budgets: the texts a call is given are checked before anything is built.
* **What the window does with them** (`crates/ui-egui/src/panes/shape_data.rs`, `shape_data.rs`). **The Shape Data pane** (`window.pane {pane: shape_data}`; Data, Show/Hide, Shape Data Window, View, Task Panes, Shape Data, and the shape's menu) is titled **Shape Data - <the shape's name>** (**Shape Data - ThePage** with none selected, since the page's own shape data is not built, and **Shape Data** with several selected: ours, the pane lists one shape's data) and lists the fields as a label and an editor of the type: a text box for a string, number, date, duration and currency (what is typed is sent when Enter is pressed or the box is left, as Size & Position's boxes are, one `shapedata.set` each; a refusal is said under the field and in the status bar), a drop-down for a boolean (TRUE, FALSE), for a fixed list (its items and `(blank)`) and for a variable list (its items, `(blank)` and **New value...**, which turns the row into a text box that takes the keys at once). The prompt is the tip of the editor, and the answer is read again only when the drawing, the page or the shape is not the last answer's (`work::data_queries`). The pane's right-click menu and its button open **Define Shape Data...**; **Shape Data Sets** is not offered. **The dialog** (`window.defineShapeData`, one shape selected) lists every field, hidden ones too, with the boxes Label, Name (read-only for a field the shape has), Type, Format with the presets of the type, Value, Prompt, Sort key, Hidden, Ask on drop, Language (a tag) and, for a date, Calendar (a drop-down of the fourteen by name), and the buttons New, Delete, Move Up and Move Down; OK sends one `shapedata.define` (one undo step) with only the boxes that were changed, so a type the dialog does not know and a language a file holds as a number are kept, and a refusal is said in the dialog; **Move Up and Move Down** swap the two fields and write every field's sort key as its place (`0001`, `0002`, ours: the keys are text, so they are padded). **The card a drop asks** (`window.askShapeData {id}`, which the Shapes window runs after a master is dropped) lists the fields with Ask on drop and an editor of each type; OK sends one `shapedata.set` of the values changed (its own undo step, after the drop's) and Cancel nothing. A paste, a duplicate and a copy do not ask yet (names them).
* **Files.** A field is a row of cells, so `.diacraft` holds it as the model, **VSDX** (the model writer and the splice) writes the `Property` section as it is (the cell `Verify` as `N="Verify"`), and an **`.odg`** holds it in DiaCraft's own data on the shape: LibreOffice has no shape data, so **a save in place of an `.odg` that has fields says so in the save note** ("N shapes with data rows") and the after-save check counts a missing `Property` section in the class "user cells". `tests/it/shapedata_files.rs` saves a drawing with one field of each type in each format, opens it again and compares the fields, and carries a drawing between each pair of formats.
* **What is left.** Shape data sets (a named set of fields, Apply to shapes) are the row Shape data sets and stay missing; the page's own shape data (`Shape Data - ThePage`) is not shown or edited; a paste, a duplicate or a copy of a shape with a field that asks does not ask; a date is shown in the Western calendar whatever its `Calendar` says, and a field's language changes nothing it shows; the Format of a currency does not follow the system's regional setting.

## Callouts

Insert, Diagram Parts, **Callout**: a gallery of fourteen tiles that adds a callout, which is tied to the shape selected and moves, is copied and is deleted with it.
 A callout is a **shape with no master** whose figure, its leader and the end of its leader are formulas, and whose tie is the `Relationships` cell of both shapes, as files
hold it. Every command is one undo step, and one that is refused changes nothing. The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked **ours** are DiaCraft's own, and each is a row in the check list.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `callout.styles` (query) | none | `{styles: [{id, name}], width, height}`: the fourteen tiles in the order the gallery shows them, and the size a callout is made at in inches (1.5 by 0.5) |
| `callout.insert` | `style`?, `ids`?, `x`?, `y`?, `text`?, `page`? | `{page, id, ids, targets, style}`: one callout for each shape of `ids` (a shape named twice gets one), tied to it, up and to the right of it, or **one tied to nothing** at (`x`, `y`) in inches, in the middle of the page when neither is given. The first tile when `style` is left out, the word `Text` when `text` is. On the active layers, in front, with the page's theme |
| `callout.attach` | `id`, `target`, `page`? | `{page, id, target, changed}`: ties the callout to the shape (the yellow handle dropped on it). A tie to another shape is taken away first; a callout tied to that shape already is no step |
| `callout.detach` | `id`, `x`?, `y`?, `page`? | `{page, id, changed}`: ties the callout to nothing, and with `x` and `y` (both) puts the end of its leader there (the yellow handle dropped on bare page); with none the end stays where it is, and a callout tied to nothing that is given no place is no step |
| `callout.list` (query) | `id`, `page`? | `{page, id, is_callout, callouts, target, style, tip}`: the callouts tied to the shape, the shape this one points at, the tile it was made from and where the end of its leader is on the page in inches (the last two for a callout DiaCraft made) |

* Our rule (gives the gallery in words and no cell). The fourteen tiles are drawn in code from standard figures: text with a line, underlined text, a box with a line, a rounded box with a line, an oval
  with a line, a left bracket, a right bracket, both brackets, a speech box, a rounded speech box, a speech oval, a thought cloud, a box with a header line and a double box. A callout has **no master** (as a text box has none),
  is linked to the root style so that its fill, line and text come from the page's theme ("colour comes from the drawing's theme"; gives it the page's theme), has `User.msvStructureType`
  `Callout` and `User.CalloutStyle` (the tile's id), the text block of the whole box, and the four middles of the sides as connection points. The **figure** is one `Geometry` section for each path, every coordinate a
  formula of `Width`, `Height` and the end of the leader, so that it follows the box and the end; `Scratch` row 2 holds what several rows share (the side a speech box's wedge leaves by, the corner radius).
* Our rule. `Scratch` row 1 holds the end of the leader in the callout's own frame: `X` and `Y`, and `A`, `B` and `C` for the working. **Tied to nothing**, all five are numbers and the end stays where it was put
  as the callout moves. **Tied to a shape**, they are formulas: `A` and `B` are the distance from the callout's pin to the target's (`CALLOUTTARGETREF()!PinX` and `PinY`), `C` the share of it that lies inside the target's box,
  and `X` and `Y` carry the rest of the way into the callout's frame through its turn and flips, so that **the leader ends at the edge of the target nearest the callout** (a callout inside the target's box ends at its own
  middle). A move of the target moves the end, a move of the callout alone makes the leader longer or shorter, and a recalculation does both, since `calc` reads `CALLOUTTARGETREF` through the shape's `Relationships`.
* The reading is, the upkeep is ours. The tie is held **on both sides**: the callout's `DEPENDSON(6, Sheet.<target>!SheetRef())` and the target's `DEPENDSON(3, Sheet.<callout>!SheetRef(), ...)`.
  A call this code does not own (codes 1, 2, 4, 5, 7 to 11) is kept as it is, and a target it cannot read as `Sheet.<id>` is kept as the text it was. Tying, untying, a copy and a delete change both cells, so stays true,
  and an emptied call stays as `DEPENDSON(3)` (files hold such calls). A shape has at most **256** callouts (`MAX_CALLOUTS_PER_SHAPE`).
* Our rule. A callout is tied to a shape of the page's **top level**, and is itself on it: the end is worked out from the target's `PinX` and `PinY`, which are in its parent's frame, and the callout's own pin is in
  another if the two are not in one list. So a shape inside a group is no target (tie the group: the words say so), a callout inside a group cannot be tied, a guide is no target, a shape is no target of itself, and a tie that would
  make a circle of callouts (each reading the pin of the other, a loop in the recalculation) is refused.
* Our rule. A callout for a shape goes up and to the right of it, 0.3 inch off its box (to its left for the right bracket, whose leader leaves by its right side), and below or to the other side when that
  would leave the page; the next callout for the same shape goes one callout's height higher. A callout tied to nothing has its end below it and off its left side (off its right for the right bracket).
* "it moves with the shape". A shape that moves takes the callouts tied to it, and theirs, along by the same distance on the page, **in the same undo step**: `shape.move` (a drag and a
  nudge), `shape.align`, `shape.place` (the pin set in the Size & Position window) and the autoconnect copy all reach it. They are not counted among the shapes moved. A callout named with its target moves once; a shape inside a
  shape moved that is moved itself is carried by both and its callouts go by the sum. **It costs:** the shapes moved are looked at once each, whatever the nesting; the callouts are found with **one walk of the page** for all
  of them and never by a search from the top for each (`tests` count it against the same move with no callout); a shape that names more than 256 callouts, or a move that would take more than **20,000**, is refused in words, and
  so is a `Relationships` formula that names more than **20,000** shapes (`MAX_TIE_MARKS`; the parser holds a formula to 131,072 nodes), which is read that far and never rewritten.
* "copying S copies the callout". A copy of a shape (`edit.copy`, `edit.cut`, `shape.duplicate`) takes the callouts tied to it that are on the page's top level, and theirs, and a paste ties each
  copy to the copy of its target; the original keeps its own. **A callout copied alone is a callout tied to nothing** (the specifications do not say; the choice loses nothing): the copy holds its end where it was in its own
  frame. A shape copied whose callout is not copied is tied to nothing.
* "deleting S deletes it". Deleting a shape (`shape.delete`, `edit.cut`, the shapes of a layer) deletes the callouts tied to it, and theirs. Deleting a callout takes its name out of its target's
  `Relationships`, in one pass over the marks whatever their number, so that no cell names a shape that is not there (a later drawing may give the `ID` again). **A shape operation is not a delete of this kind**
  Union, Combine, Fragment, Intersect and Subtract leave the callouts of their originals, tied to nothing.
* Our rule. A tile's picture is the callout the command makes, **drawn by the renderer** on white paper from a drawing of its own (`diacraft_engine::draw::callout_preview`, 120 by 70 pixels at 50 to the inch),
  so a tile cannot show a figure the command does not make. Pointing at a tile names it and shows no preview on the page (a preview would be an edit and an undo step).
* **What the window does** (`crates/ui-egui/src/ribbon/callouts.rs`, `insert_callout.rs`, `edit/callout.rs`). The Callout control opens the gallery (fourteen tiles in rows of four, four, four and two, each a picture); a press on a
  tile sends the window command `window.callout {style?}`, which sends `callout.insert` with the shapes selected on the page on view as `ids`, or with the middle of the drawing area as `x` and `y` when none is, and
  selects the callouts made. **A callout that is the one shape selected shows a yellow handle at the end of its leader** (the theme token `callout_handle`; "a yellow handle"): a drag of it onto a shape (a connection point
  or a shape within reach, the callout itself never) is `callout.attach`, onto bare page `callout.detach` with the place, one undo step each, Escape drops the drag and a press with no drag sends nothing. The drag is drawn as the
  line from the callout to the pointer, green over a shape it would tie to.
* **Files.** The callout is cells, so **`.diacraft`** holds it as the model, **VSDX** writes its sections and both `Relationships` cells (and a VSDX file's own callouts are read by the same `DEPENDSON` reader, tied or moved
  by the commands; a callout of a file has no `User.CalloutStyle`, so `callout.attach` ties it and leaves its figure to its own formulas), and **`.odg`** (the model writer) carries the shape and its formulas as DiaCraft's own
  data on it. A callout's tie, the end of its leader, its figure's formulas and its text come back from a new drawing saved in each format, and a target moved after the reopen takes the callout along with the leader
  (`crates/engine/tests/it/callout_files.rs`). **What is left in `.odg`:** a callout tied to a shape that an **opened `.odg`** held, saved over the file, is refused in words at the save (the splice keeps no `ID` of a file's
  shape for a formula to name: "a formula of a new shape that names a shape of the file"), and `edit.undo` makes it saveable; a callout tied to a shape made in the session saves.
* **What is left.** The tile's hover preview on the page; callout shapes of stencils (they connect and are not tied); a callout tied to a shape inside a group (tie the group); the end of the leader is at the
  box of the target, not at its outline, and does not follow a target turned past its box; the callout's text is not opened for typing at once; the colour follows the theme and has no control of its own, as R9 says.

## Comments

Review, Comments: what a reviewer says about a shape or a page. A comment names its page and its shape by `ID`, so **it stays with the
shape when the shape moves** and nothing is written to it by a move; a comment with no shape is the page's. A reply is a comment with a parent: a thread is one level deep. Every edit is one undo step, and a call that
is refused changes nothing, in words. The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked **ours** are DiaCraft's own.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `comment.add` | `text`, `id`? (the shape), `page`? | `{comment}`: a new comment by the author `comment.author` says, dated now in UTC; with no `id` it is the page's. It turns the markers on |
| `comment.reply` | `comment`, `text` | `{comment}`: a reply that sits where its comment sits; a reply to a reply joins the thread of the comment it answers |
| `comment.edit` | `comment`, `text` | `{comment}`: the new text and an edit date; the same text is no step |
| `comment.delete` | `comment` | `{deleted}`: the comment and the replies to it (a reply alone goes alone) |
| `comment.list` | `page`?, `id`?, `details`?, `after`?, `limit`? | a query: `{comments, markers, total, more}`, each comment `{id, parent, page, shape, author, initials, author_id, text, date, edit_date, done}`; `details` adds `anchor` (`{x, y}` in paper inches), `shape_text` and `shape_gone`; at most `limit` (1 to 10000, default 1000) |
| `comment.markers` | `show`? | `{markers}`: Show Markup, a setting of the drawing that is **no undo step** (the View settings' rule); without `show` it toggles |
| `comment.next`, `comment.previous` | `from`? | queries: `{comment, wrapped}`, the next or the previous thread's first comment |
| `comment.author` | `name`?, `initials`? | the session's setting: `{name, initials}`; not journaled, and not the drawing's |

* Our rule (New Comment adds a comment to the selected shape, or to the page when nothing is selected). The text is not blank, holds no control character but a line break or a tab, and is at most
  65,536 bytes; a drawing holds at most 100,000 comments, 10,000 authors and 32 MiB of comment text (the caps of `diacraft_doc::Comments`). The shape must be on the page. The date is the session's clock in UTC
  (`2031-03-07T20:05:09Z`), as fields are.
* Our rule (replies can be written in the pane). A reply's parent is a comment that has none, and a reply to a reply is a reply to the first comment of that thread. A file that holds a
  chain of replies is read with the chain made one level deep, in one pass (a parent that is not there, a comment its own parent and a loop leave the comment a thread's first).
* (ours; E4 and the Delete icon). A comment taken away takes its replies. **A shape taken out of the drawing (`shape.delete`, a cut, a layer's shapes) takes its comments, and a page taken out takes
  its comments** (a `CommentEntry` naming a removed page or shape is removed with it); undo brings them back. A shape that goes some other way (an ungroup) leaves its comments, which then
  show as the page's (`shape_gone`). A copy of a page does not copy its comments.
* Our rule (comments show only while markup display is on, which adding one turns on). Show Markup is `ShowCommentTags` of the comments part, and absent means shown.
* Our rule (no page says how Next and Previous order comments). The stops are the threads' first comments, by the pages' order in the drawing and then by number; the answer wraps from the last to the
  first and says so; a reply as the place stands for its thread; with no place, next is the first and previous the last.
* Our rule ("the author name comes from a setting, there is no account"). The author is the session's, `Author` with the initial `A` until `comment.author` sets a name (not blank, at most 255
  characters) and initials (at most 8; the first letters of the name's words, at most three, when none are given). The same name and initials are one author of the file.
* Our rule. The list is in the order the comments were made, paged by `after` (the number of the last comment of the page before) and `limit`; the details are one walk of each page the comments are on.
* Our rule (no source gives the place). A comment on a shape has its marker at the top right corner of the box round the shape's corners on the page; a comment on the page, or on a shape that is not
  there (a file may hold one), at the page's top right corner, 0.3 in inside it.
* A card is headed by the text of the shape it belongs to. The first 80 characters of the shape's text, its line breaks as blanks.
* **Files.** A VSDX file holds comments in its comments part (`comments.xml`) (`AuthorList`, `CommentList`, `ShowCommentTags`; the splice writer rewrites the part the read took as the comments part and no other, the model writer and a
  new drawing write it, and a part the model cannot hold exactly, or one over the size of a small part, stays the bytes it is, with a warning); **VSDX's part has no replies, so a reply is written as a comment of the same page and shape after its comment, and DiaCraft's own part (the solution part that carries other formats' data)
  holds an item of the kind `comments`: a `link` for each reply with its number and the number of the comment it answers, stamped with what the comments part says**. A DiaCraft read gives each
  thread back; a read of a file whose comments part another program changed leaves the links out (the stamp no longer matches), and another program always reads flat comments. **The part is written as
  it was read** (GATE.ea42): no attribute the file lacked (an author's `Initials` or `Name`, the root's `xml:space`), an empty `Initials` or `Name` only where the file had one, and `xml:space="preserve"` on a
  part made new only where a text needs it (a line break, a tab, a run of blanks or a blank at an end); a part read from a file that had none is written with none.
  `.diacraft` holds the whole model (`comment-list`, 3.18 of `docs/diacraft-format.md`), replies included. **An `.odg` holds a page's comments as `office:annotation` elements** (the
  model writer and the splice): the ODF 1.4 schema puts them at the end of a `draw:page`, and each is `dc:creator`, `dc:date` and `meta:creator-initials`, a `text:p` for each line of the text,
  `svg:x` and `svg:y` (the place) and DiaCraft's attributes `diacraft:version`, `id`, `shape`, `parent`, `done` and `edited`. A comment the model made is written at its marker,
  one read from a file keeps the place the file gave. LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 shows them: it reads `office:annotation` and its own `officeooo:annotation` (which the reader takes the same), and writes
  `officeooo:annotation` with a place, an author, initials, a date and the lines, and nothing of DiaCraft's attributes. **The splice** takes out only the annotations the reader took as comments (the
  index says which) and writes the page's comments anew at the end of the page when they differ from what the file read as; a page whose comments are as the file has them keeps their bytes, and an
  annotation the model cannot hold stays where it is. **A shape a comment is on is named by its number**: a new shape keeps its `ID` in the file (as a shape another shape names does), a shape of
  the file is tied by the number the reader will give it, and a save that renumbers a page's shapes writes the comment of a shape that has no `ID` of its own as a comment of the page, which the
  next save that leaves the numbers alone ties again, so a tie is never to another shape.
* **What the window does with them** (`crates/ui-egui/src/comments_ui.rs`). Review, Comments, New Comment (`window.newComment`) opens the Comments pane with a box for the comment on the shape selected, or on the page;
  Post sends `comment.add`. The pane lists the threads in cards with the author, the date, a delete X, the text, Edit and Reply, under Filter by (All, This page), a Show Markup box and the New Comment, Previous and Next
  buttons; clicking a card's head, or a marker on the page, opens the comment (`window.showComment`: its page, its shape selected, its card in view). The Comments menu of the Review tab has the pane, Previous
  Comment, Next Comment (`window.stepComment`) and Show Markup, and the Comments button at the right of the title bar opens and closes the pane. **The markers are painted by the window over the picture and are no part
  of what print, export or the pictures draw: comments are not printed.** Escape cancels the box a comment, a reply or an edit is typed in (what the Cancel button does) and leaves the drawing's selection as it was.
* **What is left.** In an `.odg`: an annotation the model cannot hold exactly (formatting, a list, a style, a second author, a date string, text of its own, or text past the cap of a comment),
  one past the caps of a drawing, one inside a shape or a paragraph and one of a master page stay in the markup, are counted (`annotation_kept`) and are no comments; a save by LibreOffice drops
  DiaCraft's attributes, so a file it saved holds flat comments on the page; a comment's place is the marker's when it was written and does not follow the shape later; and the setting that hides the
  markers, a VSDX comment's kind and an author's address are not written (the save says so). A comment is not on the shape's right-click menu; the `Done` mark has no control (the model holds it);
  Track Markup and the Reviewing pane are not built; and a VSDX file's modern comments (`moderncomments.xml`) are kept as bytes.

## The browser version's own commands

Three window commands (`crates/ui-egui/src/commands.rs`, `page_host.rs`) that need the page of a browser. They are on the desktop's list too, where each is disabled and says why ("only the browser
version has this: ..."): the window's `PageHost` is the page's side of them, and a window with none (the desktop) has no page to ask. They are the window's and not the engine's: they change what the
window shows or does, and never the document.

| Command | Where | Params | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app.ownWindow` | File view, Home, "In a window of its own" (a button: **Open in its own window**) | none | Asks the browser to open the app in a window of its own: its install prompt (`beforeinstallprompt`, kept by the loader because the browser sends it before the program has started). It runs only where the browser gave a prompt that has not been used; otherwise it is disabled, and its reason is the steps in words (Safari: File, then Add to Dock; Firefox: no windows of their own for web apps on Linux or macOS; any other browser: the install button of the address bar or the browser's menu), or "DiaCraft is in a window of its own already". |
| `window.fullScreen` | View tab, Views, **Full Screen** (a box, checked while the page is in full screen) | `on` (optional true or false) | Goes to full screen with the keyboard locked (`requestFullscreen` and `navigator.keyboard.lock()`: it needs the click the press is), or leaves it; without `on` it turns the other way from how the page is. The status bar says "Full screen. Hold Esc to leave it." (and, where the browser gave full screen and no keyboard lock, "... this browser kept its keys ..."). Leaving, by the command or by Esc held, lets the keyboard go. |
| `app.closeWindow` | **Ctrl+W** (Command+W on a Mac), the browser version only | none | Asks about unsaved changes as New and Open do ("DiaCraft is closing."), then asks the page to close the window (`window.close()`: a window the browser opened for an installed app closes; a tab that was not opened by a script may not, and the status bar says the browser keeps it). It works from the File view and from a text box, as the other keys on files do. The browser's own question about leaving the page (below) is taken away first, so that the person answers DiaCraft's question once and the browser does not ask a second time when the window closes. |

**Ours** (no specification gives any of these; the reference application's `FileClose` closes a drawing and leaves the app open, which this does not, and its `FileExit` closes the app): the names,
the places, and Ctrl+W as the key. Presentation Mode (`ViewFullScreenView`, F5), the reference application's full-screen view, is another thing and is not built.

**The keys the page takes.** In the browser version the page calls `preventDefault()` on a key before the browser acts on it for **every chord of the command key (Ctrl, or Command on a Mac) and
every function key that a command of the window has, except Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X and Ctrl+V with nothing else held** (the browser's copy, cut and paste events, which the window gets from the web runner).
`shortcuts::keys_to_take(os)` lists them, from the same table the window runs its keys from, so a new key of a command is taken with no second list. Ctrl+N and Ctrl+W are among them; in a window
of its own and in full screen with the keyboard locked the browser passes them to the page, and in a tab it does not ("Measured, ", `docs/website.md`). The desktop window's keys are what they were.
