**Why more than the keys a browser keeps in a tab:** a key a tab does pass to the page is acted on by the browser too, unless the page prevents it, and eframe's web runner prevents only Ctrl+O,
Ctrl+P and Ctrl+S, the arrow keys, Tab, Backspace and Space. Before this, in a Chrome tab, **Ctrl+R (Rotate Right) reloaded the page and lost the drawing**, Ctrl+U (Underline) opened the page's
source in a new tab, Ctrl+H (Flip Horizontal) the history, Ctrl+J (Flip Vertical) the downloads, Ctrl+F (Find) the browser's find bar, Ctrl+D (Duplicate) the bookmark box, and Ctrl+- zoomed the
browser's page as well as the drawing.

**Asking before the page is left.** A tab that is closed or reloaded loses a drawing that has unsaved changes, and the web runner's own handler for it never runs (it listens for `onbeforeunload`, an event no browser fires). So while the drawing has unsaved changes (the engine's `Session::is_dirty`: an edit and its undo leave it clean) the window tells its `PageHost` (`PageHost::guard_leaving`, when the answer changes: the frame that made the edit, the save or the new drawing says it, not the one after), and the page puts a handler for `beforeunload` on the window, which cancels the event and sets `returnValue`; **the browser then shows its own "Leave site?" box** when the person closes the tab, reloads the page or goes elsewhere (the words are the browser's: a page cannot give them). A saved drawing, a new one and a page with nothing in it ask nothing, and have no handler, so the back/forward cache is not blocked. **Close (Ctrl+W)** asks DiaCraft's question first and takes the handler off before it asks the browser to close the window (`close_in_browser`), and the page keeps it off until it knows whether the browser kept the window; a tab the browser will not close (a script did not open it) keeps the drawing, and the handler comes back. The desktop window has no page and asks nothing more than it did.

**What a tooltip shows.** The window shows no key in its menus and tooltips, except where it is the browser's to say (`DiacraftApp::tip_with_key`): the tooltip of the Blank drawing tile on the Home page
names the key of New (`Ctrl+N`, or the command key of a Mac) where it reaches the window, and in a tab says "Ctrl+N belongs to the browser in a tab; open DiaCraft in its own window, or in full screen, to
use it". A tooltip never shows a key that opens a browser tab. `shortcuts::BROWSER_KEPT` lists the keys a browser keeps in a tab and what it does with each.

## Window settings

What the drawing's window shows: the rulers, the grid, the guides and the connection points (the View tab's Ruler, Grid and Guides, and Visual Aids' Connection Points).
**They are the drawing's**: the model holds them as a VSDX file's windows part holds them, the children `ShowRulers`, `ShowGrid`, `ShowGuides` and `ShowConnectionPoints` of
the drawing `Window` (`Document::vsdx`, `VsdxHeld::windows`), so every save writes them. Code: `crates/doc/src/window_flags.rs` (the flags of a part,
and a part with them set, changing nothing else), `crates/engine/src/view.rs` (what a drawing shows, and the edit) and `cmd/window.rs`; the writers are `crates/vsdx/src/write/mod.rs` (`sync_windows`),
`skeleton.rs` and `held.rs`, and `crates/odg/src/ext/docfile.rs` (`windows`). The window's boxes write through `window.settings.set` (`crates/ui-egui/src/window_settings.rs`).

| Command | Parameters | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `window.settings` (query) | none | `{rulers, grid, guides, connection_points, held}`: what the drawing shows, each a boolean, and `held`, the flags its windows part holds (each a boolean or `null` where it holds no value), or `null` when the drawing holds no windows part |
| `window.settings.set` | any of `rulers`, `grid`, `guides`, `connection_points`, booleans | The settings as `window.settings` gives them. The drawing is changed (unsaved), **no undo step is made** and no revision moves. Nothing changes at all when every value given is what the drawing shows already |

* A drawing of any format that holds **no windows part** (a new drawing, an `.odg` that DiaCraft's document file gives none, a
  `.diacraft` file that never had one, a VSDX file without one) shows what a new drawing shows: the rulers, the grid and the guides on, the connection points off. A drawing that holds one shows
  the flags of its drawing window (the first `Window` marked `WindowType="Drawing"`, or `WindowType="1"`, which is `visDrawing` among the window types, the window opens a page from; a flag as a child or an attribute, `1` or `true` on, `0` or `false` off) and, where it
  holds none, the rulers and the grid off (a project rule), the guides on and the connection points off (ours). **A part that holds no drawing window holds no settings**:
  an empty `Windows`, or one whose windows are a stencil's alone (which hold no flag), shows what a new drawing shows, as no part does, and no other
  window stands in for the drawing window (read the first window as the drawing's, so such a file opened with the grid and the rulers off).
* Our rule (and the save specs do not say whether a view change makes a drawing unsaved; the owner's ruling, 2026-11-22: "Unsaved, not undo"). **Turning a box marks the drawing
  changed and is no undo step.** The drawing is unsaved, so a save keeps the setting, the title shows the mark, `file.status` says `dirty` and closing asks before the setting is lost. No step is
  recorded, the redo steps stay and no revision moves: the page's picture and the snap's reads are kept for a revision, and no setting is part of them (`Session::edit_view`), so nothing is calculated or drawn
  again for a box turned. Ctrl+Z goes on undoing the drawing's edits, and **undo and redo leave the settings alone**: the document they go back to or on to is given the windows part the drawing holds now
  (`view::carry_windows_part`), so a grid turned off stays off through an undo of a move made before or after it and past the start of the history, and an undo back to the saved drawing is clean only when its
  settings are the saved ones too (a box turned back is still a change until the drawing is saved). The command is journaled as any command that ran. It is the choice that never loses a setting that
  was turned. A value the drawing shows already writes nothing (the drawing stays clean). A drawing with no windows part is given a new drawing's (`diacraft_vsdx::new_windows_part`: one
  drawing window for the first page, with `ShowRulers` and `ShowGrid` on, which shows what no part shows) before the flag is set in it. A drawing whose file holds a windows part DiaCraft does not
  read (not XML, or another root: it is kept as the bytes it is) refuses in words; the window still shows the box turned and says why it is not kept.
* Our rule (which extends the owner's ruling of 2026-11-22, "Unsaved, not undo", to the settings of View > Visual Aids that are the document's). **The AutoConnect box (`autoconnect.set`) and the Snap & Glue options (`snap.set`: the Snap and Glue switches, the options of `SnapSettings`, `GlueSettings` and `SnapExtensions`, Dynamic Grid and the six snap strengths) mark the drawing unsaved and are no undo step**, as the window's own boxes are. The drawing is changed, so a save keeps them and closing asks; no step is recorded and the redo steps stay; Ctrl+Z goes on undoing the drawing's edits. **Undo and redo leave them alone**: the document they go back to or on to is given what the drawing holds now (`view::carry_settings`): the windows part, the `autoconnect` and `snap` children of DiaCraft's data and the words of `DocumentSettings`. So a setting turned stays through the undo of an edit made before or after it and past the start of the history, and an undo back to the saved drawing is clean only when its settings are the saved ones too (a setting turned back is still a change until the drawing is saved). **The revision:** `autoconnect.set` moves none (nothing a frontend keeps for a revision is made of it: `Session::edit_view`), and `snap.set` moves it once (`Session::edit_snap_setting`), because a Snap & Glue option decides which targets the snap reads and the window keeps its settings, its reads of the page and its snapped previews for a revision: with no move, a box turned would show the old value until the next edit. **What stays an undo step:** a shape's own edit, a guide (`guide.add`, `guide.move` and `guide.delete`: a guide is a page shape), the line jumps (cells of a page or a connector) and the Reroute settings of a connector (its own cells). A batch of settings alone records no step, and a batch that fails takes them back with the rest. The Snap & Glue dialog's OK sends the same `snap.set`, so it is no undo step either (the ruling names the Visual Aids group and the launcher beside it; keep the dialog's own OK as a question for the owner).
* And (3). A flag is set where the window holds it (a child's text, or an attribute), a flag it lacks goes in as a child at its place in the order, and
  nothing else of the part changes; a part with no drawing window gains the drawing window of a new drawing's part (`diacraft_doc::with_drawing_window`: the window for the first page, with
  `ShowRulers` and `ShowGrid` on, after the part's own windows, with an `ID` of its own and in the part's prefix), and the flag is set in it, so that only the flag set changes what the drawing
  shows; a save into the file puts that window in the file's part, and an unedited save writes the part as it read it. **VSDX**: a save into the file sets the
  model's flags in the file's windows part when they differ from those it was read with, or when a page or master taken out took a window with it, and keeps every other byte of the part's
  content; a file with no windows part is given the model's, with the document's relationship and the content type; a drawing whose settings did not change writes the part as it read it, so an
  unedited save keeps the bytes and a second save is no edit. A drawing of no file is written as a drawing made new is, with its own windows part (a windows part alone is no VSDX package,
  `VsdxHeld::holds_windows_alone`), and a drawing window that a page taken out takes with it leaves its flags to a drawing window of their own. **`.diacraft`**: the model holds the part, and the file holds the model. **`.odg`**: no item of `settings.xml` is read for them yet (leaves
  `GridIsVisible` to a later task), so DiaCraft's document file holds the windows part whole (its child `windows`, beside `data`); LibreOffice drops that file, and its drawing then opens as a new
  drawing shows. **A flat drawing (`.fodg`) has no such file**: a save of one whose settings were set says that they are not kept.

## Containers

Insert, Diagram Parts, **Container**; the Container Format tab (Size, Container Styles, Membership); right-click, **Container**, Add to Underlying Container. A container is a frame that holds shapes, with a
heading. **The model is the ShapeSheet and nothing else**: a container is a shape typed `User.msvStructureType` `Container`, who it holds is its `Relationships` cell
(`SUM(DEPENDSON(1, Sheet.<id>!SheetRef(), ...))`), and each member names it back under 4; a shape on a container's edge holds 7 to 10 instead of 4 (left, right, top, bottom) and the container holds it under 1. A list or
a callout's call in the same cell (2, 3, 5, 6, 11) is kept as it is, in its place. `CONTAINERMEMBERCOUNT`, `CONTAINERCOUNT` and `CONTAINERSHEETREF` of the formula engine read the same cells. Every command is one
undo step, and one that is refused changes nothing. Lengths are paper inches. **Search first (the owner's rule):** the carve plan's `craft-container` is the bounded zip and XML layer (`crates/container`), not a
diagram container, and no sibling Craft app has one (FigCraft's frames are a tree of nodes, the opposite model: a member is a child of its parent, where the reference app's container holds shapes of the page by a list and by
where they lie), so the engine is ours (`crates/engine/src/draw/container/`). The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked **ours** are DiaCraft's own.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `container.styles` | none | a query: `{styles, margins, resize, headings, strip_inches}`, the 14 styles of the gallery (`id`, `name`, `frame`, `tint`, `strip`, `centred`), the margin presets (`none` 0, `narrow` 0.1, `medium` 0.25, `wide` 0.5 inch), the three resizing words and the four sides |
| `container.info` | `ids`, `page`? | a query: `{page, containers, held_by}`: each container among `ids` as `{id, style, heading, resize, margin, locked, members, inside}`, and for each shape among them that is no container the containers that hold it |
| `container.add` | `style`?, `heading`?, `ids`?, `x`?, `y`?, `width`?, `height`?, `page`? | `{page, id, members, style, inside, fitted}`: with `ids`, the container is the box that holds them with a margin and the heading's strip, **at the back of the page**, and holds them (a shape that was in another container leaves it; one in a locked container refuses the command); without, an empty container is dropped at `x`, `y` (the page's middle) and **holds nothing, even over shapes** |
| `container.style` | `ids`, `style` | `{page, styled}`: the frame, tint, strip and heading alignment of the style replace the container's look; its size, heading text, members and settings stay |
| `container.heading` | `ids`, `side`? or `rotate`? | `{page, resized, skipped, moved}`: the heading's side (`top`, `right`, `bottom`, `left`), or one side on clockwise (Rotate Heading); the container makes room for the strip by its resizing setting. A container a file holds has no heading of ours and is refused in words until a style is chosen for it |
| `container.margin` | `ids`, `margin` (inches or a preset word) | `{page, margin, resized, skipped, moved}`: the room kept round the members; the container is brought to it by its setting |
| `container.fit` | `ids` | `{page, resized, skipped, moved}`: Fit to Contents: each container becomes the box that just holds its members; one with no member, a turned one, a group and one that holds itself are left alone (the last three are said in `skipped`); a fit that changes nothing is no undo step |
| `container.auto_resize` | `ids`, `mode` (`none`, `expand`, `fit`) | `{page, mode, resized, ...}`: None keeps the size, Expand as Needed grows it to hold a member and never shrinks it, Always Fit to Contents is always the size that just holds the members |
| `container.lock` | `ids`, `locked`? | `{page, locked}`: toggles from the first container when `locked` is left out |
| `container.disband` | `ids` | `{page, disbanded}`, and `notes` when a container on a locked layer held a frame: each frame is deleted and its members stay, as members of the container that held the frame (not one on a locked layer, which is passed over); a locked container is refused, and so is one that holds a shape on a locked layer |
| `container.select_contents` | `ids` | a query: `{page, ids}`, the members (the window selects them) |
| `container.add_member` | `ids`, `container`? | `{page, joined, resized}`: Add to Underlying Container: each shape joins the container beneath it (or the one named, wherever the shape is); one that is already its member is left out, and none changed is no undo step |
| `container.attach_edge` | `id`, `container`, `edge` (`left`, `right`, `top`, `bottom`) | `{page, moved}`: the shape becomes a member whose middle is on that edge, and stays there as the container is moved or resized |

The window has two commands, `window.containerAdd {style?}` and `window.containerDo {op, value?}` (`ops`: `style`, `heading`, `margin`, `fit`, `auto_resize`, `lock`, `disband`, `select_contents`, `add_member`),
which add what only the window knows (the shapes selected, the page on view, the middle of the view) and call the engine's commands above. A container command that names a shape, or a container (`container`), on a **locked layer** is refused in words with nothing changed, as every command that changes the drawing is (the registry asks it of every command, and `cmd/locked.rs` reads the `container` of a `container.*` command as a shape named). A container on a locked layer that a command does not name is left as it is, and the answer's `notes` says so.

* Our rule: the specifications give no cell of a container's look. A container is a **shape with no master** with its own cells, so a drawing needs no stencil to have one: one `Geometry` section
  whose rows are in the forms of the basic shapes (`0`, `Width`, `Width*k`, `Height`, `Height*k`) with no flag set (`NoFill`, `NoLine`, `NoShow` 0), because those are what an `.odg` saved in place can hold:
  the frame is a ring, the strip's divider is a two-point path and a corner bracket goes out and back along its arms, which enclose no area. The heading is the shape's text in a text block that is a strip along one
  side (`Txt*` cells as formulas, a quarter turn on the left and the right), bold, aligned by `Paragraph` `HorzAlign` (the style says left or centred). The 14 styles are four frames (plain, heavy, dashed,
  corner brackets, none), a tint or none, a strip with a divider or none, and the heading's alignment; **the look is ours, drawn in code**, and the tiles of the galleries are drawn from the same table in the theme's
  colours. A command that changes the container's size makes its outline again (the divider is a share worked out for a size); a drag of its box by hand leaves the divider where its share puts it.
* (ours; **question for the specifications:** which cells a file holds the margin, the resizing, the lock and the heading side in, gives no name and says the default is
  `unknown`). They are `User` rows of ours on the container: `DiaCraftContainerStyle` (the style id), `DiaCraftContainerHeading` (0 top, 1 right, 2 bottom, 3 left), `DiaCraftContainerMargin` (a length),
  `DiaCraftContainerResize` (0 none, 1 expand, 2 fit) and `DiaCraftContainerLock` (0 or 1). A container with none (one a file holds) is **Expand as Needed with a quarter inch of margin, unlocked, with no strip of ours**
  (the strip is 0.3 inch, `BAND_INCHES`). The default of a container made here is Expand as Needed: it loses nothing and surprises least.
* Our rule: the specifications say a shape dropped inside joins, one moved a little inside joins, a locked container takes and loses none, and give no rule for "inside". A shape is **inside** a
  container when the middle of its box is in the container's box. It joins the **innermost** container (the smallest box) it is inside, is a direct member of one, and leaves it when its middle is outside the box. A
  shape moved into an inner container leaves the outer one, which holds the inner container. **A container joins another only when its whole box is inside** (a copy a quarter inch off its original has its
  middle in the original, and is not nested in it); once in, it stays while its middle is. A shape never joins a container it holds, itself or through its members; a list takes no member by a drop. **Locked:** a
  locked container takes no member (a drop over it does not join, `container.add_member` and `container.attach_edge` are refused), a member of it stays in it, **cannot be moved out of it** (the move is refused
  in words: "a warning bubble says that ... container ... properties stop the command from finishing") and **cannot be deleted**, and the container cannot be disbanded (the 2020 image
  of the tab shows Disband dimmed: our reading); deleting the container itself still deletes all it holds.
* Moving a container moves what it holds, by the same distance (`draw::move_each`: nested containers too, a member named as well moves once). **The walk that finds what goes with a
  shape is one, shared with the callouts** (`draw::callouts::followers`): a shape that moves takes its callouts and, when it is a container, its members, and what is tied to those (a callout of a
  member, a container inside a container), each shape looked at once, with the ties of each shape read once, in one walk of the page for the first round and one index of the page's shapes for the rest (so a
  thousand levels of nesting cost two walks of the page, not a thousand; the answer `moved` of `shape.move` counts the shapes named and not the members that go with them, as it does not count the callouts). All that follow one edit are at most 20,000 (`MAX_FOLLOWING`), and a drawing that has more is refused in words with nothing moved. A shape on a locked layer among them refuses the move, as it refuses a delete or a cut that would take it. **The
  `Relationships` cell has one reader and one writer** (`draw::ties::Ties`), used by the callouts, the containers and the window. Copying (`edit.copy`, `edit.cut`,
  `shape.duplicate`) copies what it holds, and the copies' ties are renumbered to the copies (`repair_copies`; a tie to a shape that was not copied is taken out), and a copy of a member alone joins the container it is
  put inside; deleting it (`shape.delete`) deletes what it holds. A shape dropped (`shape.drop`), moved (`shape.move`), resized (`shape.resize`), pasted, cut or deleted brings the containers it touches to their setting
* Our rule: no spec says what a move does with a member a `GUARD` holds, and the gate's check of the sample files found two container drawings whose first move was refused whole because a member's end is guarded. **`shape.move` of a container moves it and every member that can move, and leaves a member whose move a `GUARD` refuses where it was** (`draw::move_shapes_leaving`): a follower (a member, or a callout tied to a shape moved) is moved by writing its pin, or the ends of a one-dimensional shape that are not glued (MOVE-1D), and one of those cells under `GUARD`, here or through its master, leaves the whole follower where it is, with what follows only it, still a member; the answer names it under `left` and in `notes` ("shape 3 was left where it was: a GUARD protects its cell `BeginX`, which the move would write (it follows the shapes moved, as a member of a container or a callout)"; five named and a count past them), and the window's status bar shows it. The move is one undo step. A shape named whose own cell is guarded still refuses the move in words, and so does a follower on a locked layer (unchanged). The other commands that carry followers (`shape.align`, `shape.place`, the fit of a container, Re-Layout Page) still refuse such a move in words.
* Our rule: only "Fit to Contents resizes the container to just enclose its shapes". **The box a container needs** is the box that holds its members' boxes,
  grown by its margin on every side and by the heading's strip on the heading's side (a member never lies under the strip). Fit to Contents makes the container exactly that; Always Fit does it after every change;
  Expand as Needed makes it the box that holds what it is and what it needs. A member on an edge is on the border and asks for no room (or a fit would grow the box to hold it and move it out). **A fit is planned
  in memory in one pass and written in one:** the containers it reaches (the ones named and every one above them) are put in order, each after the ones it holds, each is sized in one write
  (`draw::shapes::resize_cells`) and the page is recalculated once; **a command fits at most 4,096 containers** (`MAX_FITTED`) and a plan that reaches more is refused in words before anything is written.
* A shape deleted by `shape.delete` or `edit.cut` leaves every tie it was named in, so that a new shape given its `ID` after a save and a reopening is not taken for a member. (A shape
  operation's produced shape and `layer.delete` leave a tie naming a shape that is gone, as SHAPEOPS-CONTAINERS rules: a lost target is no target.)
* A `Relationships` formula that is not a `SUM` of `DEPENDSON` calls is never rewritten. An automatic step (a move's membership, a fit) leaves that shape and its containers as they are, and
  says nothing; a command that names it is refused in words (`... is not a list of ties`). A formula that names more than 20,000 shapes (`MAX_TIE_MARKS`, below what the parser's 131,072 nodes hold) is read that far and not rewritten, and a container that would
  name more is refused in words, with nothing written.
* Our rule: the specifications say what a container holds and that a member names it back, and nothing of a loop. **Membership is a tree**: a container is never its own member, directly or through the shapes it holds, by its members (code 1) or by the containers its shapes name (code 4 and the edges). Every tie a command makes is asked first (`Graph::join`), so `container.add` round a container, a move or a drop of containers (two of one box moved together), `container.add_member`, `container.attach_edge`, a paste, a duplicate, an undo and a redo leave no loop: the container a shape goes into is looked for with the ties the command has made so far counted (a container put round another held nothing yet in the page's read of its ties, so the other, under its middle, took it and each held the other), and a tie that would close a loop is refused in words (`... a container is not put inside what it holds`). **A loop a file holds is broken when the file is read** (`.diacraft`, VSDX and `.odg`; `draw::container::cycles`): the page's shapes are walked depth first in drawing order, and the tie that leads back to a shape the walk is still inside is taken out on both sides, so of two containers that hold each other the one drawn first keeps the other, a loop of any length loses one tie, and a tie of a shape to itself goes. Every other call stays in its place, the open says so first among its warnings (class `container_loop`), and the open is no step of the history. A loop among the copies of a paste is broken the same way, and the answer's `notes` say so. A file of a loop of ten thousand containers opens as a chain, within a small multiple of the time of the same containers in a chain (a step for each tie and one walk; a page past `MAX_WORK` is left as it was, and the note says it was not looked at). The walks that follow a move and the fit visit each shape once and leave a container in a loop alone, so a loop that a library caller writes by hand stays bounded too. The concern 1: a VSDX file saved in place after such an open, with no edit, gets the repaired ties: the file as it is read is the drawing's baseline (`Session::set_read_baseline`), so the splice writes the page the open changed, as it writes an edit, and no other part of the drawing; the drawing stays clean, and the next open finds no loop.
* The security scan's three findings of 2026-11-22. **A command's reads of containers are one budget** (`MAX_WORK`, 8,000,000 steps, the edit's: `Session::edit_if` takes it, so a move's
  followers, its membership step and its fit add up); a shape visited, a tie read, a box measured and a container looked at (one step in eight for each shape it is tried against) are steps, charged before the work is
  done, and **a budget that is spent is an error in words wherever it is met, never "no box" or "no container"**. Every listing that holds each shape once (`ids`, `contents`, `select_contents`, `holding`,
  `containers_of`) asks a set and not a search of its list; the ties keep an index, and taking out N shapes is N steps and one pass. Hostile drawings are tested: containers nested a thousand deep fit to the
  outermost, ten thousand containers that hold the same shapes are refused in words, a container of 20,000 members is read in one pass, and 4,000 members moved among 2,000 containers keep their places.
* **Files.** A container is cells, so a VSDX file holds it in the `User` section and the `Relationships` cell of its shape and its members (Shape Layout), `.diacraft` holds the model, and an `.odg` holds it in
  DiaCraft's markup beside the shapes (OpenDocument has no container); the `.odg` save note says the ties are cross-shape formulas a LibreOffice save would drop (`libreoffice_would_drop`). Tested in each
  format from a new drawing (the model writers) and from a drawing read from a file and saved in place (the splice). **In a drawing read from an `.odg`, a shape that joins a container cannot be saved in place yet**:
  the file keeps no number for a shape of its own, so the new shape's formula cannot name the container (the save says "a formula of a new shape that names a shape of the file"); Save As keeps everything.
* **What is left.** A member of a group is not a member of a container (only the shapes of a page's own list are, a group is one shape). The Size & Position panel writes a pin directly, so a container it moves does not take its members; Align moves
  members with their container but, like every caller of `move_each` but `shape.move`, does not settle membership afterwards. A connector is a member when its middle is inside, and no rule asks for both its ends (the specifications
  count a connector with an end outside as not held). A list takes no member by a drop: lists are built for the swimlanes (the section Swimlanes below), and a list a file holds is laid out when it is a list of lanes or phases and left as it is otherwise. A shape does not snap to a container's margins or centre lines
  (F27). The Heading Style's alignment commands are the ordinary paragraph ones. Wireframe containers that fill from their items need the wireframe stencils.

## Swimlanes

File > New, **Cross-Functional Flowchart**; the Cross-Functional Flowchart tab (Insert: Swimlane, Separator; Arrange: Orientation, Move Earlier, Move Later; Design: Rotate Lane Label); the Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes
stencil (Swimlane, Separator); right-click on a lane or a phase, **Swimlane**. A cross-functional flowchart is a **frame that holds a list of lanes and a list of phases**: the lanes are containers laid out in a sequence, the
phases are bands across them, and a shape dropped in a lane is its member and moves with it. **The model is the ShapeSheet and nothing else** (B1):
 the frame holds the two lists under code 1 and each list names it under 4; **a list holds its members under code 2, in the order the formula names them** (a writer must not sort it), and each member names the list under
5; a lane holds the shapes dropped in it under 1 and each names the lane under 4. `LISTMEMBERCOUNT`, `LISTORDER` and `CONTAINERMEMBERCOUNT` of the formula engine read the same cells (a test evaluates them). Every command is
one undo step, and one that is refused changes nothing. Lengths are paper inches. **Search first (the owner's rule):** the carve plan's `craft-container` is the bounded zip and XML layer, not a diagram container, and no sibling
Craft app has a list of lanes, so the engine is ours (`crates/engine/src/draw/container/lists.rs`, `swimlane.rs`, `stencil/swimlane.rs`, `cmd/swimlane.rs`); it **builds on ** and writes no second container
engine: a lane is a container of the gallery's `strip-centre` look, membership, the followers walk, the fit, the locks and the loop rules are the containers'. The reference app was not inspected: the rules marked **ours** are DiaCraft's own.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `swimlane.info` (query) | `ids`?, `page`? | `{page, structures, selected}`: each structure of the page as `{frame, lane_list, phase_list, orientation, length, rect, lanes: [{id, thickness, heading, text, rect}], phases: [{id, width, heading, text, rect}]}` (at most 512 lanes and phases are told, and of each label its first 256 characters, read from at most 1,024 parts of its text, a step of the command's budget for each part walked), and for each shape among `ids` that is a part of one `{id, role, lane_list}` (`frame`, `lanes`, `phases`, `lane`, `phase`) |
| `swimlane.create` | `orientation`? (`horizontal`, `vertical`), `lanes`? (1 to 256, default 3), `phases`? (0 to 256, default 0), `x`?, `y`? (the first lane's top left corner), `length`?, `thickness`?, `label`?, `phase_label`?, `page`? | `{page, frame, lane_list, lanes, phase_list, phases, orientation}`: the frame, the list of lanes and the lanes, and, for `phases`, the list of phases and the phases, at the back of the page; it holds no shape that lies under it |
| `swimlane.insert` | `lane`?, `where`? (`before`, `after`), `label`?, `page`? | `{page, id, list, index}`: a lane as thick as the one it is put next to, before or after `lane` (**at the end** when `lane` names another part of the structure or nothing: the page's first structure); the lanes after it, with what they hold, make room |
| `swimlane.insert_phase` | `phase`?, `where`?, `at`?, `label`?, `page`? | `{page, id, list, index}` (and `phase_list`, `phases` when the structure had none): a **separator**: the phase (the last by default) is split in two, at `at` (a coordinate of the page along the lanes) or in the middle; `where` says whether the new phase is the second part or the first; **the length of the lanes is not changed**. A structure with no phases is given a list of two |
| `swimlane.move` | `lane`, `to`, `page`? | `{page, id, index}`: a lane or a phase goes to the place `to` of its list (from 0), taking what it holds; the others make room |
| `swimlane.resize` | `id`, `size`?, `length`?, `page`? | `{page, id, changed, least}`: `size` is a lane's thickness or a phase's width (at least 0.4 inch; **a phase made wider takes the room from the next one**, and the last from the one before it, so the length of the lanes does not change); `length` is how long every lane is (the last phase takes the difference) |
| `swimlane.orientation` | `orientation`, `id`?, `page`? | `{page, orientation, changed}`: the structure is turned over its diagonal; the way it already lies is no change and no undo step |
| `swimlane.rotate_label` | `ids`, `page`? | `{page, rotated}`: Rotate Lane Label: each lane named (a frame or a list names all its lanes) has its label's strip moved from its left to its top, or back; the text stays; a list of more than 512 lanes is refused in words |

The window has one command, `window.swimlaneDo {op, value?}` (`ops`: `insert` with `before` or `after`, `separator`, `orientation` with `horizontal` or `vertical`, `rotate_label`, `earlier`, `later`), which adds what only the
window knows (the lane, the phase or the structure selected) and calls the engine's commands above; the lane or phase made is selected. The tab shows while the page on view has a structure (the window asks `swimlane.info` once for each
revision, page and selection), after Help and before Container Format. A command that names a lane or a phase (`lane`, `phase`, `id`) on a **locked layer** is refused in words with nothing changed (`cmd/locked.rs` reads
the `lane` and `phase` of a `swimlane.*` command as shapes named), and **so is a layout that would move or resize a shape on a locked layer**, in the words of ("laid out").

* Our rule: the specifications name the parts and what each is made of, and no cell of a look. A lane is a container of the gallery's `strip-centre` look with its label on the left (a horizontal lane) or the
  top (a vertical one); a phase is a container of a look that is not in the gallery (`phase`: a dashed line at its beginning and a divider under its label); the frame is a plain outline with no heading; a list is typed `List`
  (`User.msvStructureType`) and drawn with nothing. Each is a **shape with no master** with its own cells, in the forms an `.odg` saved in place can hold. **What a shape is in the structure is a `User` row of ours**,
  `DiaCraftSwimlane` (`frame`, `lanes`, `phases`, `lane` or `phase`), and a list says it is one DiaCraft lays out by `DiaCraftListDirection` (0 the members lie one under the next, 1 side by side): **a list another program wrote, which has none of these rows,
  is read from its own cells** (below). **Question for the specifications:** which cells a file holds a list's direction in (R5 names only `msvStructureType`, `msvSDListRequiredCategories` and `msvSDContainerRequiredCategories`).
* (ours where the specifications are silent: they give the cells of a list and of a container, the structure, B1 and R5, and the masters' names, C2, and **no cell that says which way a list runs**). **A cross-functional flowchart another program wrote is laid out as DiaCraft's own**, read from the file's own cells, and **no row of DiaCraft's is written into its shapes** (`draw/container/lists.rs`, `FileParts`). A shape with no `DiaCraftSwimlane` row is a **list of lanes or of phases** when it is typed `List` by `User.msvStructureType` (its own or its master's) and holds 1 to 512 members under code 2, each typed `Container` (B1: a lane is a container inside a list); a list of anything else (a legend's, say) is not one, and is left as it is. **Which it is** is the name of its master (`Swimlane List`, `Phase List`), else the names of its members' masters (`Swimlane`, `Separator` and their `(vertical)` forms). **The frame** is the first container typed `Container` the list names under code 4: of the lists it holds, the one named for lanes is the lanes and the one named for phases the phases, and one no name tells is what the frame still lacks; **a frame whose lists the names do not tell apart (two that none names, two of one kind, or more than two) is left as it is**, since which is the lanes would be a guess. A list in no frame is a list of lanes unless its name says phases. A lane or a phase is a container in such a list. The lanes lie one under the next when they share their left and right edges and not their top and bottom ones, side by side the other way round, and otherwise (a lane dragged out of line, a list of one) by what most of them are, wider than tall or taller than wide (a thousandth of an inch is one edge); a list of phases runs across its lanes. **The layout is **, with two differences for the file's own shapes: **a phase of a file keeps how far it stands out before the lanes and how far it reaches across them**, against the box the lanes' list had, where DiaCraft's phases take the 0.35 inch band; and **a frame of a file keeps the room it had round its lists** (a title, a margin) on each side, where DiaCraft's frame is their box, and resized by hand it is fitted as a container is and the lanes keep their length. **A lane or a phase added to a file's structure is DiaCraft's own** (its look, its rows, its label on the side a new lane's is on), and the file's shapes beside it keep their cells. **Orientation of a structure that holds a part another program drew is refused in words** (its label and sides are drawn by its own cells, which a turn would leave across it), and so is Rotate Lane Label of such a lane (its heading is part of its own drawing, the container gallery's refusal). **Costs and the security scan's finding:** what each shape is, and which way a list runs, is found once for a page read and kept for it, each read charged to the edit's one budget; a frame's lists are read to the third and no further; **a container's list is followed only to a shape typed `List`, by a lookup that asks no other container's role**, so a chain of 100,000 containers each naming the next as its list, two that name each other and a list that names itself are read in a few steps each and never walked down (they overflowed the stack before the fix). Tested (`tests/it/swimlane_cells.rs`) on models built in code with those cells alone, written by DiaCraft's VSDX writer and opened: a list of three lanes, lanes side by side, the samples' frame of a lane list and a phase list named by their masters, an insert, a resize by command and by hand, a drag, a move, a delete, a save in place, and the cases left as they are.
* Our rule: the specifications say a list arranges its members in sequence and that a lane's width changes and the list reflows, and give no rule of the arrangement. A list of lanes is laid out **in one
  pass, in memory**: the lanes lie one after the other in the order of the list, the first at the list's top left corner, **each as thick as it is** (never less than 0.4 inch, `MIN_INCHES`) and **all as long as the longest**
  (or as the command says); a lane that moves to a new place takes what it holds with it, by the containers' walk. The list is the box of its lanes, and the frame is the box of its lists. The phases lie in a band 0.35 inch thick
  (`PHASE_BAND_INCHES`) along the first side of the lanes (above horizontal lanes, to the left of vertical ones), each as wide as it is, **the last taking what the lanes' length leaves**, and each as high as the band and the lanes
  together (a phase has no member, ours: a shape in a lane is not also in a phase, which B1 reads as both; where a shape is in phases is where it lies, and a shape dropped over a phase goes to the lane
  under it). **What runs the layout** is the steps that keep containers in order: a lane or a phase **moved** is put in its place by where its middle lies, so that a drag of its header reorders it at the place it is let go (the sort is stable, and a lane
  let go on its own place, or sideways, is put back); a lane **resized** (the Size & Position window, a drag of its edge, Fit to Contents) makes the lanes after it make room, **the frame or the list of lanes resized sets the length of every lane**, and a
  phase resized takes from the next one; a lane or a phase **deleted or cut** closes the gap. The layout begins at the list's own top left corner (at the box the lanes make now when a lane was resized, so that a lane dragged out at its top grows up).
  **Cost:** a list of more than 512 members (`MAX_LIST_MEMBERS`) is refused in words and not laid out; one pass reads each lane's box once and writes only the boxes that differ, each charged to the edit's one budget
  (`page::Work`, below).
* Our rule. A structure is made with its first lane's top left corner where it is asked (0.5 inch in from the page's top left corner by default), lanes of one thickness (1.25 inches by default), the length the
  page leaves (the page's width less an inch), a phase band above the lanes, and a placeholder label, `Function` for a lane and `Phase` for a phase, that a person types over (a double click or typing on the lane edits its text as for any
  shape). It holds no shape that lies under it: a shape joins a lane when it is dropped or moved inside one. At most 256 lanes and 256 phases a command makes, in words past it.
* ours: the specifications say a lane is added before or after one, from the header's menu, a corner arrow, the tab's Swimlane button or by dragging from the stencil, and at the end when none is selected.
  The new lane is as thick as the lane next to it (the one before it, for a new first lane), carries the label of its own kind and the heading side its neighbour has, and the lanes after it make room. **The corner arrow on a lane's
  header is not built** (the other three ways are: the tab's button and its menu, the right-click row, the stencil's Swimlane). A Separator splits a phase in two (a phase at least 0.4 inch wide in each part).
* (ours: E1 says the tab can change the orientation and no page says what happens to existing lanes). The whole structure is turned over its diagonal about its top left corner: **the lane that was the k-th is the
  k-th**, a row becomes a column, the phase band goes from the top to the left, each label's strip goes with it (left and top change places, right and bottom), and **what a lane holds keeps its place in it** (each shape's middle is
  carried over the same diagonal and it is not resized); turned back, everything is where it was.
* Our rule: "Rotate Lane Label" turns the label's direction. The label runs up a horizontal lane's left side and along a vertical lane's top; rotating **moves the strip to the other of the two**, so that the text
  turns a quarter, and the text a person typed stays. The strip is 0.3 inch (`BAND_INCHES`) either way: a label that does not fit it wraps and shows in the lane as a container heading does.
* Our rule: "or by dragging from the stencil". **The Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes stencil** (`cross-functional`, 2 masters of ours, drawn in code, `stencil/xfunc.rs`) has a Swimlane and a Separator. On a page that
  has a structure, `shape.drop` of either **inserts** it: a Swimlane before the lane it is dropped on when the point is in the lane's first half and after it in its second (at the end when no lane is under it), a Separator across the phase it is dropped
  on, split at the point; no master is added to the drawing. **On a page with no lanes either is dropped as the shape it is drawn as** (a lane with its strip, a thin box), like any master.
* Our rule: "the drawing opens with swimlanes already placed and the Basic Flowchart stencil beside the swimlane stencil". **The third template** is `cross_functional_flowchart`, Letter, landscape, no theme, opening
  `cross-functional`, `flowchart` and `basic` in that order, and **its page holds a structure of three horizontal lanes and three phases** (`TemplateSpec.seed`, the lanes' commands run before the template is written): half an inch in from the corner,
  10 inches long, 2 inches thick. A drawing started from it is read from the template's bytes like any other.
* What the lists add to the containers' machinery. **What a list holds is walked like what a container holds**: a frame that moves takes its lists and a list takes its lanes (`draw::callouts::follows` reads code 2 as
  well as code 1, each shape once, at most 20,000 followers), a copy and a delete of a frame take everything below it (`Page::parts_of`), the fit goes up from a lane to the list that holds it, a tie that would put a list inside what it holds is
  refused in words, and **a loop a file holds through a list is broken at the open** as one through containers is (`cycles.rs` reads code 2 as holding and code 5 as held). A lane is not made a member of the frame that holds its list by being
  moved: it is in its list.
* A layout reads the page once (a step for each shape) and each lane or phase once (a few steps for its box), and writes **only the boxes that differ**, each charged `16 + shapes / 64` steps; all of it is the
  edit's one budget (`MAX_WORK`, 8,000,000), and a spent budget is an error in words. Hostile drawings are tested: a list of 10,000 lanes moves with its frame and is refused in words when a lane is moved in it or one is added; lanes nested
  a thousand deep move with the outermost, each once; a list that holds a lane that holds the list is moved each shape once; a list of 10,000 phases is refused in words when a separator is added; the labels of a list of 600 lanes are not turned at once; a label of a million field parts is read for a step each part, to 1,024 parts; the most a command makes (255 lanes
  and 255 phases) is made and laid out again within the budget.
* **Files.** A structure is cells, so a VSDX file holds it in the `User` section and the `Relationships` cell of its shapes, `.diacraft` holds the model, and an `.odg` holds it in DiaCraft's markup beside the shapes (OpenDocument has
  no list); the `.odg` save note says the ties are cross-shape formulas a LibreOffice save would drop. Tested in each format from a new drawing and from a template (the model writers) and from a drawing read from a file and edited in
  place (the splice: a lane made thicker, a phase dragged, the lanes moved about and turned, a member deleted). **In a drawing read from an `.odg`, a lane or a phase added cannot be saved in place yet** (the same refusal as a shape
  that joins a container, gap U39: "a formula of a new shape that names a shape of the file"); Save As keeps everything, and an undo makes the drawing saveable.
* **What is left.** The corner arrow of a lane's header, and the indicator that shows between the lanes where a lane dragged by its header will land, are not drawn (the lane goes to its place when it is let go). A lane does not grow
  to hold a member dropped near its edge (its resizing is None: the layout sizes it; Fit to Contents does and the layout follows). A shape is not a member of a phase. The tab's Arrange group is ours (the third group of the tab has no
  English source). A list from a file is laid out only where tells its parts apart, and it is not turned. The Subprocess page that keeps the lanes and the Create Diagram from Data wizard's lanes are not built.

## Re-Layout Page, Auto Space and page breaks

Design, Layout, **Re-Layout Page** and More Layout Options; Home, Arrange, Position, **Auto Space**, **Spacing Options**, **Avoid Page Breaks** and **Move off Page Breaks**; View, Show, **Page Breaks**.
 **The reference application publishes no placement algorithm**
("Microsoft gives the names and tells programmers to observe the dialog, which this project can't do"), so every rule of the placement is **ours** and each is a row in the check list.
Every command is one undo step but the Page Breaks box, and one that is refused changes nothing. Lengths are paper inches, as in the other editing commands; the page's cells hold drawing units.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `page.relayout` | `style`?, `direction`?, `alignment`?, `spacing`?, `spacing_x`?, `spacing_y`?, `block_x`?, `block_y`?, `depth`?, `flip`?, `connector_style`?, `appearance`?, `apply_routing`?, `enlarge_page`?, `ids`?, `page`? | `{page, style, direction, alignment, place_style, spacing, block, depth, flip, enlarge_page, avoid_page_breaks, connector_style, appearance, nodes, joins, moved, routed, not_reglued, locked, inside_groups, followers, cells, enlarged}`: lays out the shapes `ids` names, or those of the page, in the style (flowchart, hierarchy, compact_tree, radial, circular; the page's own where the call leaves it out), **writes the choice into the page's cells** and routes the connectors again, as one undo step. A layout that changes nothing is no step. Enabled when a drawing has a shape |
| `page.layout` (query) | `page`? | The same settings as the answer above, read from the page's cells: what the Configure Layout dialog opens with |
| `shape.autospace` | `ids`?, `horizontal`?, `vertical`?, `page`? | `{page, shapes, moved, columns, rows, spacing: {x, y}, locked, inside_groups}` |
| `page.spacing` | `horizontal`?, `vertical`?, `page`? | `{page, horizontal, vertical}`: Spacing Options. Writes `AvenueSizeX` and `AvenueSizeY` (more than 0). A value the page holds is no step |
| `page.avoid_page_breaks` | `on`, `page`? | `{page, on}`: writes the page's `AvoidPageBreaks` |
| `shape.move_off_page_breaks` | `ids`?, `page`? | `{page, shapes, moved, too_large, locked, inside_groups}` |
| `page.breaks` (query) | `page`? | `{page, units_per_inch, x, y}`: the places along x and along y, in paper inches from the page's lower left corner, where the page passes the printer paper |
| `page_breaks.settings` (query) | none | `{on, held}`: whether the drawing shows its page breaks, and what its data holds (`null` for nothing) |
| `page_breaks.set` | `on` | The settings as `page_breaks.settings` gives them. **No undo step** |

* The style is written the way the reference application writes it, in the page's own cells, so it rides in VSDX and in `.diacraft`: `PlaceStyle`
  (the table: 1, 2, 4 and 5 a flowchart top to bottom, left to right, bottom to top and right to left, 3 radial, 6 circular, 7 to 14 the eight compact trees, 16 to 27 the hierarchies by direction and alignment), `AvenueSizeX`
  and `AvenueSizeY` (the spacing box's "space between shapes": the dialog's one Spacing field sets both, **ours**), `ResizePage`, and what the dialog's other fields hold (`BlockSizeX`, `BlockSizeY`, `PlaceDepth`, `PlaceFlip`, and the
  page's `RouteStyle` and `LineRouteExt`). A cell that holds what it is given is not written. `PlaceDepth`, `PlaceFlip` and `BlockSize` are **kept and not read**: the placement reads the spaces and the style.
* Our rule. The placement is `diacraft-layout`'s (`crates/layout`): **a flowchart** is layers along the flow (the longest path from a shape nothing points at, a cycle broken by turning the connectors that point back, a connector
  across layers a chain of stand-in points counted before any is made), ordered by sweeps that keep the order with the fewest crossings and set across by a weighted least-squares pass so that connectors run straight where they can;
  **a hierarchy** is a spanning forest (each shape reached once, by the first connector that came to it) with a parent over its first, middle or last child (start, center, end: left, middle, right for a flow down or up, top, middle,
  bottom for one across) and each subtree as wide as its own children need; **a compact tree** is an outline (`run` then `level`: `down_right` is a list read down the page with each level indented to the right; the first word is the
  way the shapes of a parent run, the second the way each level is set off); **radial** puts a root at the middle, its children on a ring and theirs on the next, each in the arc its subtree needs; **circular** puts every shape on one
  circle in the order a walk down the forest meets them. Shapes keep their sizes, **no two boxes overlap**, the space between shapes is at least `AvenueSizeX` and `AvenueSizeY` (along the flow and across it as the direction says: for a
  flow across the page the space across is `AvenueSizeY`), separate parts of the diagram are laid out each by itself and set in rows, in the order of their places now, and **the top-left corner of the diagram stays where it was**.
  The graph is the shapes and the connectors **glued at both ends** to two of them (a connector glued to a shape inside a group is glued to the group); a flow follows the way the connectors point, from the begin to the end. The order the shapes
  have now is the order the layout starts from, so a diagram laid out twice comes out the same and one that is laid out changes little.
* Our rule ("the selected part of the diagram, or the whole page when nothing is selected"). A shape is a shape at the top of the page's list that is not a guide and not one-dimensional (a group is one
  shape); a shape named that is inside a group is the group's and is counted in `inside_groups`. **Left where they are:** a shape on a locked layer (the rule for the shapes a layout does not name: the call lists them in
  `locked`, and one named in `ids` is refused by the registry's lock rule), and a shape that **moves with another** of the scope: a member of a container and a callout tied to a shape go with it (`callouts::followers`
  walk; `followers` counts them). A connector between shapes that move with other shapes is not part of the graph and is routed again.
* **One edit.** The shapes move, the connectors of the scope are routed again once (a route made by hand, `ConFixedCode` 3, is made again, as a route made for places that are no more cannot be kept), the cells are written,
  and a page that grows (Auto Size, or `ResizePage`: "Enlarge page to fit drawing", by whole sheets of its paper as Auto Size grows it) does. `apply_routing` writes `ShapeRouteStyle` and
  `ConLineRouteExt` on every connector of the scope, as the Connectors menu does, and the page's `RouteStyle` and `LineRouteExt` (right angle 1 or straight 2; straight 1 or curved 2). `connector_style` and `appearance` alone
  write the page's cells and leave the connectors as they are.
* Our rule. A connector glued at both ends to two shapes of the scope is **glued again at the pair of connection points that face each other** (as a new connector between the two shapes is
  glued: `connectors::reglue_all`), so that a flow down the page leaves a shape by its bottom and comes to the next by its top. **What it costs** (counted, `draw/relayout/glue_cost.rs`): one walk of the page, one pass over its
  glue, **the connection points of each shape read once for the call** within 65,536 rows for all of them (as the routing reads them), the held points indexed by shape and by connector, one rebuild of the glue rows and one batch
  of cell writes; a shape with ten thousand points and ten thousand connectors glued to it is a few reads of its ten thousand rows, not a hundred million. A connector whose shape has no connection point, whose layers allow no glue,
  or whose points are past the rows a call reads is left as it is and **counted in the answer's `not_reglued`**. The reference application's connectors choose their sides again when a page is laid out again; this is the nearest the glue of ours comes to it.
* Hostile pages. A page with more than **20000 shapes to lay out**, **10000 connectors to route** (the routing of one edit), **60000 shapes at its top** or **100000 glued ends** is **refused in
  words** that name the limit and say to select part of the diagram, and the counts are checked as each shape is found, before a list of them is any longer than the limit. A flowchart whose connectors would cross more than 400000
  layers between them is refused too (a hierarchy or a radial layout of the same drawing is made). A layout that would put a shape past `MAX_COORD` (a million drawing units) from the page's origin is refused. The layout's own work is
  charged before each pass (`diacraft_layout::Work`, at most 200 million steps), a walk of a chain or a ring of a hundred thousand shapes uses no stack, and a page of 2,000 shapes and 1,999 connectors is laid out with a handful of walks of
  the page and no search for a shape from its top (`draw/relayout/hostile.rs`, counted, not timed). A session's routing past 10,000 connectors in one edit names the connectors it left, as for any other edit.
* Our rule where the specifications are silent. The shapes are taken in **columns** (a column is the span of a chain of shapes whose boxes overlap along x) and **rows** (the same
  along y). The leftmost column and the topmost row stay; each next one is moved so that the gap to the one before is the horizontal or the vertical space (`horizontal` and `vertical`, else the page's `AvenueSizeX` and `AvenueSizeY`),
  and the shapes of a column or a row keep their places in it, which is "keeps their alignment as it is". A shape moves by its own column's change along x and its own row's along y. With the page's `AvoidPageBreaks` on a shape that
  would land across a page break is moved off it. Connectors glued to the shapes follow and are routed again; callouts and a container's members go with their shapes; a shape on a locked layer is left. A call that
  moves nothing is no step.
* The breaks are the borders between the sheets a print of the page takes: the tiles `diacraft_render::print::plan_page` works out from the page's paper, its way, its margins, its print
  scale and its centring, so what the window marks is what Print Preview and Print give. A page that is one sheet has none; a page that cannot be printed as it is (no printable area, too many sheets) has none drawn and `page.breaks` says why.
  The reading is ours. A shape that a break runs across goes, along that axis, to the side of the break it is nearer, until the break is 0.05 inch from it, provided the shape then lies on
  one sheet (not across another break, not past the sheets' outer edges); a shape too large for one sheet is left and counted in `too_large`. A shape whose edge only touches a break is not across it.
* (ours; "Page Breaks is not among the four" flags of the windows part, and Q4 leaves the default open). The Page Breaks box is the drawing's own setting, **off for a drawing that holds nothing**:
  `page_breaks.set {on: true}` writes `<page_breaks on="1"/>` into the drawing's extension data (`Document::diacraft`, as the AutoConnect box does: VSDX's solution part, `.odg`'s DiaCraft document file, the model of a `.diacraft`
  file; a flat `.fodg` keeps it too) and off takes it out. It is **no undo step**: the drawing is unsaved, no revision moves, and undo and redo carry the child across
  (`view::carry_settings`). **The box is a fifth flag of the drawing window, `ShowPageBreaks`** (`diacraft_doc::WindowFlags::page_breaks`), read and written as the grid and
  the rulers are: the window's value is read first, then the extension data's. A drawing from a VSDX file, and one whose drawing window holds the flag already, keep the box there: `page_breaks.set`
  sets it where the window holds it or puts it in at its place in the order (after `ShowGrid`, before `ShowGuides`), makes the windows part or its drawing window as a turned grid does, and
  takes the extension data's child out, so the box has one place and a VSDX file's DiaCraft data holds nothing the format can say; a file whose box was not turned keeps its windows part byte for byte. Every other
  drawing (a new one, an `.odg`, a flat `.fodg`, a `.diacraft` of no VSDX file) keeps it in the extension data as before, since a flat drawing keeps that data and not a windows part, and a new drawing saved as VSDX
  also writes `ShowPageBreaks` 1 in the window of its new windows part when the box is on (`vsdx` `skeleton::drawing_window`). A file saved by an earlier build reads its box from the extension data, and the
  first turn moves it into the window.
* **What the window does** (`crates/ui-egui/src/relayout_ui.rs`). Design, Layout, **Re-Layout Page** opens a gallery of 14 tiles under the headings Flowchart, Hierarchy, Compact Tree, Radial and Circular, each a picture drawn from the engine's
  own layout of a small sample diagram (so a tile cannot show a placement the command does not make), and **More Layout Options...**, the **Configure Layout** dialog: Style, Direction (greyed for radial and circular), Alignment (greyed unless a
  hierarchy), Spacing in inches, the connectors' Style (Right Angle, Straight) and Appearance (Straight, Curved), the boxes **Apply routing style to connectors** and **Enlarge page to fit drawing**, **Apply settings to** Selection (greyed
  with nothing selected) or Current page, a Preview picture, and OK, Cancel and Apply (it works on a draft; nothing is sent until OK or Apply). Home, Arrange, **Position** holds **Auto Space**, **Spacing Options...** (a dialog of the two
  spaces), **Move off Page Breaks**, **Avoid Page Breaks** (a check box that shows the page's cell) and the rotations. View, Show, **Page Breaks** is a check box; with it on a dashed line in the theme's `page_break` colour crosses the page at each break.
  The tiles and the items send the window's commands (`window.relayout {style?, direction?, ...}`, `window.autoSpace`, `window.spacingOptions`, `arrange.moveOffPageBreaks`, `arrange.avoidPageBreaks`, `view.pageBreaks`), which send the
  engine's for the shapes selected on the page on view, or for the whole page when none is. **There is no live preview** while the pointer is over a tile (a preview on the page would be an edit and an undo step, as the Themes gallery's).
* **Files.** The layout is cells and moved pins, so **`.diacraft`**, **VSDX** (the model writer, and a save into the file) and **`.odg`** (the model writer, and a save into a package) hold it and read it back
  (`crates/engine/tests/it/relayout_files.rs`); a save into a flat `.fodg` refuses a change of the page's cells in words ("saving does not yet write this change ... use Save As"), which Save As `.diacraft` gets round.
* **What is left.** The tile's hover preview on the page; Auto Align, Auto Align & Space ("the shapes must be linked by connector lines") and the Distribute commands; the Configure Layout dialog offers Right Angle and
  Straight and the straight and curved appearances, and not the other styles lists (organization chart, flowchart, tree, network, centre to centre, simple), which a file keeps; `PlaceFlip` and `PlaceDepth`, `BlockSize` and the shapes'
  own layout cells (`ShapePlaceStyle`, `ShapePlaceFlip`, `ShapeFixedCode`) are kept and not read; the four of the eight compact trees the gallery does not show are in the dialog; the connectors of shapes that move with a container or a callout are
  routed again and do not shape the layout.

## The Organization Chart

The Organization Chart template, the Organization Chart Shapes, dropping a position on its manager, the layouts of a whole chart, Arrange Subordinates, Re-Layout and the picture on a position. **The specifications give this feature in sentences and not in cells**: they name the controls of the Org Chart tab and say in a line what each does, so the stencil, the cells, the layouts and the arrangement choices below are **ours**, each is a row for the check list, and nothing here has been compared with the reference application. Every command is one undo step, and one that is refused changes nothing. Lengths are paper inches.

| Command | Parameters | Answer and effect |
|---|---|---|
| `orgchart.info` (query) | `page`?, `ids`?, `quick`? | `{page, positions, links, in_loops, roots, layout, layouts, arrangements, spacing, pages, document_positions, complete, selected}`: how many positions the page has, how many connectors link two of them, how many positions are in a reporting loop (or report to one), the first positions nothing reports to, the layout the page holds (one of the six below, or `null`), the spaces, how many positions each page and the drawing hold, and for each shape named (the selection) `{id, position, kind, manager, reports, arrangement, picture: {has, hidden}}`. `quick` stops at the first page that holds a position: it is what the window asks to know whether the Org Chart tab shows |
| `orgchart.layout` | `layout` (`top_to_bottom`, `bottom_to_top`, `left_to_right`, `right_to_left`, `hanging_right`, `hanging_left`), `spacing`?, `page`? | `{page, positions, layout, spacing, moved, joins, routed, not_reglued, locked, followers, enlarged}`: the layout of the **whole chart**; it clears every manager's own arrangement, moves the positions (their sizes are kept), glues the connectors at the points that face each other, routes them again and writes the choice into the page's `PlaceStyle`. Enabled when the drawing has a position |
| `orgchart.relayout` | `spacing`?, `page`? | The same answer. Lays the chart out again **keeping the layout options already applied**: the page's layout and each manager's own arrangement |
| `orgchart.arrange` | `id`, `arrangement` (`horizontal`, `vertical_right`, `vertical_left`, `inherit`), `page`? | The same answer, with `id` and `arrangement`. Writes the manager's `ShapePlaceStyle` and lays the chart out again |
| `orgchart.link` | `id`, `manager`, `page`? | The same answer, with `id`, `manager`, `connector` and `replaced`. Makes `manager` the manager of `id`: a connector from the one to the other, the connector that linked `id` to another manager taken away, and the chart laid out again. Linking again to the same manager is no step. A manager that reports to `id`, directly or through others, and a position that is its own manager are refused in words |
| `orgchart.picture_set` | `id`, `path`, `page`? | `{page, id, format, drawn, shared, replaced, bytes, note?}`: Insert Picture and Change Picture. The budgets and the formats are those of `insert.picture` (PNG, JPEG, BMP, EMF and WMF are drawn; a GIF and a TIFF are kept and drawn as a placeholder, and `note` says so) |
| `orgchart.picture_delete` | `id`, `page`? | `{page, id, deleted}`: takes the picture away. A position with none is no step |
| `orgchart.picture_hide` | `id`, `hidden`? (default true), `page`? | `{page, id, hidden}`: hides the picture and keeps it, or shows it again. A position with no picture is refused in words |
| `shape.drop` | (as before) | A position dropped with its centre inside another position becomes that position's report: the answer adds `reports_to` and `connector`, and the shape, the connector and the layout are one undo step |

* Our rule ("the template opens a stencil of position shapes, such as executive, manager, position, assistant, consultant and vacancy ... Each shape has name and title text"; the default Department, Telephone, Name, Title and E-mail are the fields of a block). **The Organization Chart Shapes** (`orgchart`, category `Business`) are six masters, **Executive** (`orgchart.executive`), **Manager**, **Position**, **Assistant**, **Consultant** and **Vacancy**, plain rectangles told apart by size, tint and the weight of the line (every line is solid: a dashed outline can leave a gap where a connector meets the shape). Each has the four middles of its sides as connection points, **five Shape Data fields** (Name, Title, Department, Telephone and E-mail, in that order, each a string; the row of E-mail is `E_mail`), **a text of two lines, the name over the title, each a field** that shows its Shape Data field (`Prop.Name`, `Prop.Title`: a name typed in the Shape Data window or by `shapedata.set` shows in the text), and the marker below. Name and Title start as placeholders (`Name`, and the kind as the title; `Vacant` for a vacancy). A shape dropped from a master has a text of its own to keep what the fields show, and a copy of the marker.
* Our rule. A **position** is a shape at the top of a page that holds the user cell `User.DiaCraftOrgPosition`, whose value is the kind of position, of its own or through its master. The shape dropped from the stencil holds it on the shape as well as on the master, so that a format that keeps no masters (`.odg`) still knows the shape. A position inside a group is not one.
* Our rule ("dropping a subordinate shape onto a superior connects the two in the hierarchy, with no connector to draw"). **The hierarchy is the glue**: a connector whose begin is glued to one position and whose end is glued to another makes the first the *manager* of the second. `orgchart.link` keeps a position to one manager (the old connector goes) and refuses a loop in words; a drawing from a file may hold more than one manager or a loop, and the layout takes each position once, by the first connector that reaches it (`orgchart.info` says how many positions are in a loop).
* Our rule. **A position dropped inside the box of another position becomes its report**: the shape, the connector and the layout are one undo step, and the new report goes **last** among its manager's reports (it is set past the others in the layout's frame before the layout reads the order). A drop on bare paper, a drop of another master and a drop inside a shape that is no position link nothing. Dragging a position that is on the page onto another is not a gesture of the window yet: `orgchart.link` does it.
* Our rule (the reference application publishes no placement algorithm). The chart is laid out by `diacraft_layout::org_chart` (`crates/layout/src/org.rs`) as a tree of **blocks**: each position owns a rectangle that holds its own box and, along the flow below it, the blocks of its reports, so **no two boxes overlap by construction**. A manager's **spread** says how its reports sit: **across** (side by side under it, the manager over the middle of the row, the shapes of a row on one line and the row after them starting below the thickest) or **hanging** (in a column under it, one under the other, their left edges, or their right edges, a space from the manager's middle line). Reports keep the order they have on the page (across the flow for a row, along it for a column); roots stand in a row; separate charts are laid out apart; a reporting loop and a second manager are harmless (each position is taken once); the top-left corner of the chart stays where it was; the walks are loops over lists, so a chain of a hundred thousand uses no stack, and each pass is charged to a work counter before it runs. **The choices are cells**: the page's `PlaceStyle` holds the layout of the whole chart (17, 20, 23 and 26 for the four directions, a hierarchy with the parent over the middle of its children, 7 and 14 for the two hanging layouts, the compact trees down and to the right or to the left), a manager's own `ShapePlaceStyle` holds its arrangement (0 or 15: the page's; 1 to 6 and 16 to 27: across; 7 to 14: hanging, to the right for levels that go right or down, else to the left), so Re-Layout keeps "layout options already applied, such as a vertical layout", and they ride in VSDX and `.diacraft`. **The six layouts** of the gallery are ours (the set is not listed on a page we read): top to bottom, bottom to top, left to right and right to left (each manager's reports side by side) and hanging, to the right and to the left (each manager's reports in a column); **the four arrangements** of Arrange Subordinates are horizontal, vertical to the right, vertical to the left and the page's own (for a chart that flows across the page, right is below the manager's line and left above it). A layout for the whole chart clears every manager's own arrangement. The scope is the positions of the page and the connectors glued to them; a position on a locked layer is left (listed in `locked`), the callouts and the members of a container go with their shape, and a group is one shape. The connectors are glued again at the points that face each other and routed again once, and a page that grows (Auto Size, `ResizePage`) does.
* Our rule (the specifications say nothing of where a chart's connectors are glued). After a layout of a chart **each connector is glued out of the manager along the flow and into the report from the side it is set off**: for a flow down the page a manager's bottom to a report's top (the Rectangle's points are 0 top, 1 left, 2 bottom, 3 right), up the page top to bottom, to the right right side to left side, to the left left side to right side; for a manager that hangs its reports, its bottom (for a flow across the page its bottom or top) to the left side of each report in a column to its right and to the right side of one to its left. A shape with no point that faces so is glued at the nearest pair. The other layouts keep the nearest pair. `connectors::reglue_all` takes the two sides as part of its plan.
* Our rule ("tab commands insert, change, delete or hide a picture in the selected position shape"). **The picture is the position's own**: the shape takes the picture as its `ForeignData` and becomes a shape of `Type` `Foreign` (a VSDX reader takes `ForeignData` of that type only), keeping its geometry, its text, its data and its glue, so it moves, resizes, copies and is laid out as one shape. The picture goes in **a square at the left** of the shape, a twelfth of the height in from the edges and 84 hundredths of the height across, **its proportions kept** inside it (the longer side fills the square), in the cells `ImgOffsetX`, `ImgOffsetY`, `ImgWidth` and `ImgHeight`; the text block moves to the right of the square (`TxtWidth` is `Width-Height`, never less than a quarter of the width, and `TxtPinX` its middle). All are formulas of `Width` and `Height`, so they follow a resize. **Hide** is the user cell `User.DiaCraftOrgPictureHidden` (1 hides), which the box's size and the text block read: the picture is kept, its box is 0 and the text has the whole shape. **Delete** takes the `ForeignData`, those cells and the user cell away and the shape is a shape again. The same bytes are one picture however many positions show them, and the budgets are `insert.picture`'s.
* Our rule (the specifications give the template's stencil and its tab, and the window layout's specification says the tab shows "given an org chart in the document"). **The Organization Chart template** (`organization_chart`, category `Business`) is Letter, landscape, no theme, with the Organization Chart Shapes and then the Basic Shapes open, and **an Executive already at the top of the page** in the middle (a `Seed` of the template: the drawing has a chart, so the tab shows, and a manager to drop positions on). A template may seed its page; the position is dropped by the same function `shape.drop` uses.
* Hostile drawings. A page is read once: **at most 60000 shapes at its top** and **100000 glued ends** (refused in words before any is read), a chart of **more than 20000 positions** or **10000 connectors to route** is refused in words by the layout's scope, and every lookup is by a map. A chart of 100,000 positions, a manager with 3,000 reports (counted: the page is walked a handful of times and no shape is found by a search from its top), a chain 9,000 deep (no recursion) and a ring of positions each managing the next are each laid out or refused in words (`draw/relayout/org_hostile.rs`; `crates/layout/tests/it/org.rs` for the layout itself, a chain of 100,000 and a manager with 10,000 reports). **The window asks `orgchart.info` once for each revision of the drawing, page and selection.**
* **What the window does** (`crates/ui-egui/src/org_ui.rs`). The **Org Chart tab** shows after View (and Developer) and before Help while the drawing holds a position, and goes, with the ribbon back on its first tab, when it does not. **Layout** is a menu of the six layouts (the one the page holds is marked); **Re-Layout** lays the chart out again; **Arrange Subordinates** is a menu of the four arrangements for the position selected (its own is marked); **Insert Picture, Picture...** shows the file chooser Insert, Pictures uses and puts the file on the position selected; **Change Picture** does the same; **Delete Picture** and **Hide Picture** (a check box that shows the state) work on it. They send `window.orgChartDo {op, value?, path?}`, which adds the page on view and the position selected (`select a position of the org chart first` when none is, `select one position` when several are) and sends the engine's command. A position dropped from the Shapes window onto its manager is `shape.drop` at the pointer, and the chart reflows in the drop's step. **The tab's other controls are not built** (below).
* **Files.** The positions, the data, the marker, the links (the glue), the page's `PlaceStyle` and a manager's `ShapePlaceStyle` are cells and rows, and the picture is `ForeignData` of the shape, so **`.diacraft`** and **VSDX** (the model writer, and a save into the file) hold them and read them back, and the check after a save names no class (`crates/engine/tests/it/orgchart_files.rs`). **An `.odg`** holds the positions, their data (in DiaCraft's own data), their text (the two fields are written as the text they show, and a save into a file says so in a warning), the layout cells and the links, from the model writer and by Save As from a `.diacraft` file; the check names only what any connector between two shapes loses in an `.odg` (its formulas, its route and the formatting of its label, a class for each connector, the same for plain rectangles). **Two things an `.odg` does not hold, said in words and never dropped silently:** a position **with a picture** is not written (an `.odg` has no place for the text of a picture frame: "saving does not yet write this change: a text on a picture or an object frame ... Nothing was written ... save it as .diacraft, or as a VSDX type"; delete the picture, or save as `.diacraft` or VSDX), and a save **into** an opened `.odg` after a layout (which glues the connectors again) is refused with "a connector glued or glued anew (a connection is written by a later task)", which Save As `.diacraft` gets round.
* **What is left.** From the tab: Fit to Page, Synchronize and its copies, Move Further and Closer, Move, Move Left and Right, Show/Hide Subordinates, Change Style, the height and width of the shapes, Position Type, the data display options and Organization Data (Import, Export, Compare) are named controls and do nothing (P2 rows, except the wizard). **The Organization Chart Wizard from a data file** (a P1 row) is not built.
