It would take: a dialog that asks for a delimited text file (comma or tab) or an Excel workbook, a reader for them, a choice of the columns that hold Name, a unique ID and Reports To (and the others to show), a pass that makes a position for each row with its Shape Data, a link for each Reports To (a blank one is the top, `orgchart.link` for the rest), one `orgchart.layout`, and the sample file; the engine has the rest (the whole table would be one undo step, bounded as the layout is: 20,000 positions). Team frames and dotted-line reports (a Team Frame is a container), pictures from a folder, a chart over several pages and the fields shown on the shapes are not built. A position cannot be dragged onto a manager from the page (`orgchart.link` does it), the gallery has no pictures of the layouts (a menu of six rows), the right-click menu has no Arrange Subordinates row, and the placement is DiaCraft's own.

## Closing the P0 rows

The rules that closed fourteen of the seventeen P0 rows still scored partial (`parity-checklist.md`): what the engine and the window gained, and what the rows said that was stale. Each is ours, and each is on the list of what to compare if the reference application is ever available (the check list).

* Our rule ("a group acts as one shape for resize, rotate and align"). `shape.size` takes a group: `Width` and `Height` of the group are written and **its members follow by the stretch of its frame** (`draw/scale.rs`, `scale_members_of`), the same stretch `shape.resize` gives it, so each member stays at the same place of the group's box; the group keeps its pin (`LocPinX` and `LocPinY` follow when they are formulas of the size) and the whole is one undo step. A member on a locked layer refuses it in words ("resized"), and a shape named together with a group it is in is refused too ("shape N is in group M, which is sized in the same command"), since the group's size would carry it a second time. The members one command carries are bounded (`MAX_SCALED_MEMBERS`, 20,000), as for a resize. The Size & Position pane's Width and Height are no longer quiet for a group; they are quiet only for a line. Before this the command said "a group cannot be resized yet".
* Our rule ("left, centre, right, justify and distributed"; `HorzAlign` 3 stretches every line but the last and 4 the last too). `text.format {align: "distributed"}` writes `HorzAlign` 4, and `shape.formatting` reads 4 back as `distributed` (before, it read 3 and 4 both as `justify`, so a distributed paragraph could not be told from a justified one, and a selection holding both showed one value). The text layout already stretched the last line for 4. **No ribbon button and no key**, as in the reference application (`ParagraphDistributed` is off the ribbon): the Text dialog's Paragraph tab offers it. An `.odg` carries 4 as `text-align: justify` with `text-align-last: justify`.
* Our rule (the Line section's Begin Arrow size and End Arrow size, Medium by default). `shape.format {begin_arrow_size, end_arrow_size}` writes `BeginArrowSize` and `EndArrowSize`, whole numbers 0 to 6 (very small, small, medium, large, extra large, jumbo, colossal) and refuses others in words; `shape.formatting` answers both (2 where a shape holds none, the root style's value). The renderer already draws the class. The Format Shape pane's Line section has the two lists beside the arrow lists, the Format Painter carries the sizes, and the cells are saved and read back in `.diacraft`, VSDX and `.odg`. A size is a class: the length of an arrowhead is not typed.
* Our rule (More Shapes "opens a menu of stencil categories similar to the template categories"). `stencil.list` names the `category` of each built-in stencil, open or in `available`: Basic Shapes, Decorative Shapes and Arrow Shapes are `General`, Basic Flowchart is `Flowchart` (the words of the template categories) and Gane-Sarson is `Software and Database`; a stencil opened from a file has none. **More Shapes** (the chooser at the head of the Shapes window) files every stencil under its category (the open ones first within it), puts a check mark on each that is open and shows the one on view highlighted; a click on one that is not open runs `stencil.open {id}` and then `window.stencil`, so a drawing made from a template, which opens only the stencils its kind needs, can open the rest. The stencils opened from files stand under their own heading, and a list with no categories (a stencil list the app was given) is plain. The menu is one popup of headed groups, not a flyout per category (R3 gives no layout).
* Our rule ("a blank page with basic geometric shapes and arrows"). **Basic Diagram opens Basic Shapes and Arrow Shapes** (Basic Shapes holds ten geometric shapes and no arrows); `template.list`, `file.new {template}`, a template saved from such a drawing and the model a new Basic Diagram starts with say so, and Decorative Shapes, Basic Flowchart and Gane-Sarson are one choice away in More Shapes. Basic Flowchart is unchanged: its stencil holds the shapes the row lists (Terminator, Process, Decision, Predefined Process, Document, Data, On-Page Reference and Off-Page Reference).
* Our rule ("picking a tile opens a dialog with its name, a description, preview thumbnails, a close button, Metric Units and US Units radio buttons, and a large Create button"). A press on a template's tile on File > New opens its detail in place of the gallery: the name, the category, the description, a larger picture of its page (drawn from `template.list`, as the tile's is), **Create** and **Close**. Create asks about unsaved changes as Ctrl+N does and then runs `file.new {template}`; a Cancel there leaves the detail as it was; Close and Escape go back to the gallery and make nothing, and a second Escape leaves the File view. **There is no Metric Units or US Units choice** (the engine has no unit choice at the start of a drawing: the P1 row "Choose metric or US units when starting"), and one picture and not a row of thumbnails. The Blank drawing tile makes its drawing at once, as before. The window command `window.templateDetail {template?, close?}` opens and closes it (`control-protocol.md`).
* The window's keys for zoom are **Alt+F6** (`view.zoomIn`), **Alt+Shift+F6** (`view.zoomOut`) and **Ctrl+Shift+W** (`view.fitPage`, Fit to Window), beside the window's own Ctrl+= and Ctrl+- (Command in place of Ctrl on a Mac, where `Cmd` is the platform's key). **On a Mac, Fit to Window is Command+0** (ours; `ui-egui/src/shortcuts.rs` `MAC_KEYS`): Apple's Human Interface Guidelines give Shift-Command-W to "Close a file and its associated windows" in their table of standard keyboard shortcuts and ask an app not to repurpose one ([Keyboards](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/keyboards), read 2026-11-22), so Command+Shift+W is left to the system there. Command+0 is in no row of that table, takes Command alone, sits beside Command+= and Command+-, and is the key the sibling craft apps give their own Fit in Window. Nothing in the window took Command+Shift+W before (winit's app menu binds Command+Q, Command+H and Option+Command+H, and egui binds no W): the clash was with the system's convention. No spec gives the reference app's keys on a Mac. **On a Mac, Command+Plus zooms in too** (ours; `MAC_ALSO`): with Command held the window takes the character from the layout's Command table, so the `=` that the Portuguese, Icelandic, German and Nordic layouts type with Shift+0 arrives as `0` (Fit to Window), while `+` is a key of its own there; the US and British layouts give `=` for Shift+Command+= as well. Each measured layout reaches one of the two keys, the Swiss ones only by the key at the US position of `=`. Before, Command+Plus was egui's own key for the size of the whole window, which it scaled instead of zooming the drawing; elsewhere Ctrl+Plus is still egui's (the shortcut list gives Ctrl+Plus to the web app only). The Ctrl+Shift click magnifier of the zoom page is not built (Ctrl with the wheel zooms).
* Our rule ("Shift with arrow keys moves it a pixel at a time"; Shift with an arrow key moves a pixel at a time). With one connector selected whose ends are both glued (a nudge of the shape would move nothing) and that has handles, **Shift with an arrow key moves the leg that key moves across** (Left and Right an upright leg, Up and Down a level one) **by one screen point**, with no snap, as one `connector.edit_path` and one undo step. **Assumed** (the specifications say "it" and not which leg): of the legs with a handle that the key moves, the one nearest the middle of the route (the first of two the same distance away). A connector with a free end is nudged as a shape, as before, and a plain arrow key leaves a connector with both ends glued alone.

**What the rows said that was stale** (re-scored with evidence, no code): Auto Size extends the page (the page breaks are drawn: `auto_size_grows_the_page_by_whole_sheets_and_the_page_breaks_show_their_borders`), Snap on or off (the drawing tools obey the switch: `the_snap_switch_decides_whether_a_drawing_tool_lands_on_the_grid`), Right-angle connectors (the grade is "Design's Connectors menu or the connector's own menu": the Design tab's menu does it), Basic Flowchart template (the stencil holds the shapes the grade lists) and Ribbon with tabs and groups (the grade is the structure of named tabs and groups, one set at a time, which the order tests hold; a control the commands do not stand behind is drawn disabled and belongs to its own row).

## The colour palette and the theme preview

What the Fill, Line and Font Color menus offer and write, the Colors dialog, and the page showing a theme while its tile is under the pointer. The rows are the checklist's Fill colour and Apply a theme (P0), and Custom colours by RGB or hex and Standard colours stay put (P1).
 Code:
`crates/engine/src/theme/palette.rs` (the palette and what a pick is), `theme/preview.rs` (the copy a preview shows), `formatting/shape_cells.rs` and `text.rs` (what a pick writes), `cmd/theme.rs`; the window:
`crates/ui-egui/src/ribbon/palette.rs` (the menu), `colors_dialog.rs` (the dialog) and `theme_preview.rs` (the preview). The reference app was not inspected: every rule below is **ours**, on the list of what to compare if the
reference application is ever available.

| Command | Parameters | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `theme.colors` (query) | `page`? | `{page, themed, theme_colors: [{column, slot, label, swatches: [6]}] (ten columns, `column` 1 to 10, the colour then its five shades; the third and fourth repeat the first two), variant_colors: [7], standard_colors: [10 {label, hex}]}`; a swatch is `{label, tooltip, hex, pick, tint, formula}`. With no theme on the page `themed` is false and the colours are the table `THEMEVAL` gives with no theme. Nothing is changed |
| `theme.preview` (query) | `theme`? (an id of `theme.list`, or `none`), `variant`?, `colors`?, `styles`?, `scope`?, `page`?, `ids`? | as `theme.apply` answers (`pages`, `changed_pages`, `shapes_linked`, `cells_rewritten`, ...) with `preview: true`, **worked out on a copy of the drawing: the session is not touched**, and for `ids` `shapes: [{id, fill, line, color}]` as the copy shows them. A page of more than `MAX_PREVIEW_SHAPES` (5,000) shapes, or one that takes more than a tenth of the steps of a recalculation, is refused in words |
| `shape.format {fill, line}` | `#RRGGBB`, `none`, `default`, `theme:<colour>[:<shade>]` | |
| `text.format {color}` | `#RRGGBB`, `default`, `theme:<colour>[:<shade>]` | the same for the text's `Color`, for the shapes or the characters of `start` to `end` |
| `window.colorsDialog {target, close?}` | `target`: `fill`, `line`, `color`, `stop_color`, `line_stop_color`, `fill_background` or `shadow_color` | the Colors dialog (`docs/control-protocol.md`) |

A `<colour>` is `light`, `dark`, `background`, `accent1` to `accent6` or `variant1` to `variant7` (the names `THEMEVAL` answers also work: `AccentColor2`), and a `<shade>` a whole number from -100 (darker) to 100 (lighter): the
`delta` of `MSOTINT`. A word that is neither is refused in words that name what is taken, and the answer to a refused word is short however long the word was.

* Our rule ("no source gives what applying a theme does to a cell under THEMEGUARD", and the specifications do not say what a palette pick writes). **A theme colour is a formula, so
  that it follows the theme.** The cell holds `THEMEGUARD(THEMEVAL("AccentColor2"))`, or with a shade `THEMEGUARD(MSOTINT(THEMEVAL("AccentColor2"),40))`: `THEMEVAL` answers the colour of the page's theme (and
  `VariantColor1` to `VariantColor7` those of its colour variant), `MSOTINT` the shade (above 0 the lightness rises by that share of the room to the top, below 0 it falls by that share of itself) and
  `THEMEGUARD` marks the cell as following the theme (it evaluates to its argument, and a later format replaces the formula, as it does a `THEMEVAL()`). The cell's value is the colour it works out to on the page; a
  theme applied afterwards (`theme.apply`, `theme.variant`) works the page out again and the value follows. **A colour from Standard Colors, Recent Colors or the Colors dialog is `RGB(r,g,b)`** and no theme changes it
  (`a_theme_colour_follows_the_theme_when_it_changes_and_a_standard_colour_does_not`). A pick on a shape that reads no theme (a shape with its own style, a pasted one) makes it read the page's: it
  follows the page's `Theme` style for the families that have no style of their own, and the scheme cells that send a shape to its page are written 65534 where it does not read them so (as a Quick Style does); a page with no
  theme needs neither, the formula answers the no-theme table (`#9DBB61` for the second accent) and a theme applied later links the shape and works the formula out again. A fill or a line colour is as say
  (a solid pattern, a gradient turned off). **Bounded:** a colour of the theme (or `default`) is put on at most `MAX_PICK_SHAPES` (10,000) shapes in one command, groups opened, refused in words before any work is done (a plain colour is held to the 20,000 shapes any format takes); the shapes named are made to follow the theme **in one pass over their places** (`theme::follow_page_theme_refs`: one planner, one resolver, no walk of the page to find a shape by its `ID`), and the passes are counted (`theme::follow_work`, tested in `theme_color_cost.rs` on a page of 100,000 shapes). **The shapes named are themselves found in one walk of the page**: `formatting::targets` asks `draw::find_named` for all of them at once, and the walk stops at the last one found, where a search for each by `Document::find_shape` walked the page from the top for each (4,096 names on a page of 30,000 shapes were 58 million visits, and the page a command works on was found the same way, so 117 million for one fill); `page_for` is `draw::page_holding`, which walks each page once for all the names. A shape not on the page is refused in the same words as before ("page 1 has no shape 999"), in the order the shapes are named. The count is tested in `formatting/tests.rs` (the visits of one walk, and none by `find_shape`) and in `theme_color_cost.rs` (a fill, a theme colour, a text colour and a Quick Style on 4,096 ids spread over a page of 30,000 shapes).
* "a top row plus five shade rows", the tooltip "Turquoise, Accent 6, Darker 25%"; the tints are those the sample files saved by the reference app hold in a page shape's own cells, `inferred-from-sample`. Each column is the colour and five shades: an accent and a variant colour are lightened
  by 80, 60 and 40 and darkened by 25 and 50 percent, the light colour (the first and third columns) darkened by 5, 15, 25, 35 and 50, and the dark colour (the second and fourth) lightened by 50, 35, 25, 15 and 5. A swatch's tooltip is the colour's name
  (`colour_name`, ours: a hue word, `Light` or `Dark` in front, the greys by lightness), its theme name and its shade.
* **The palette has ten columns**: **the first four are white, black, white and black in every theme
  state and the last six are Accent 1 to 6** (drew nine, with Background third, because no function names a tenth). **The third and fourth columns repeat Light and Dark** until a source settles what they are
  (no public file shows a pick of a third or a fourth column as anything but a `Light` or a `Dark` form): a pick of one writes the same formula as the same swatch of the first two (`THEMEGUARD(THEMEVAL("Light"))`,
  `THEMEGUARD(MSOTINT(THEMEVAL("Dark"),25))` and the rest), and `theme.colors` answers each column with its `column` (1 to 10), since two columns name the same colour. Background is no column of the palette, and `theme:background`
  is still a colour a command can name. The window names a repeated column's swatches `palette.fill.theme:light:-25@3`, the place after an `@`, so that no two elements of the menu share a name; its Use Default row shows the swatch of
  the column that picks Accent 1 (a fill) or Dark (a line and text), whichever place it is in.
* Our rule ("Standard Colors (ten swatches)"). The ten are our own (Dark red, Red, Orange, Yellow, Light green, Green, Light blue, Blue, Dark blue and Purple: `theme::STANDARD_COLOURS`), listed by `theme.colors`.
* Our rule ("Use Default's behaviour is not described"). `default` hands the cells to the theme: for a fill `FillForegnd`, `FillPattern` and `FillGradientEnabled`, for a line `LineColor` and `LinePattern`, for text
  `Color`, each as the call `THEMEVAL()` with the marker `Themed` (what the `Theme` style holds), so the shape has the colour its theme gives it and follows a theme applied later.
* Our rule ("Recent Colors (ten swatches)"). A colour chosen in the Colors dialog, or picked from the recent row, is the newest of ten that the three controls and the pane share; the ten Standard Colors are always on
  the menu and are not added, and a theme colour is not a colour to remember (it follows the theme). **They are kept while the window is open and not between runs**: the application stores no settings yet.
* Our rule ("Color model (combo; the image shows RGB)", "a palette of colour cells"). The Colors dialog's Custom tab has a colour field (hue across, saturation down), a lightness bar, a Color
  model of two, **RGB** (Red, Green, Blue) and **HSL** (Hue, Saturation, Lightness), a Hex box that takes `#RRGGBB` and is refused in words when it is no colour (and takes the keyboard with its text selected), and the New and
  Current swatches; the Standard tab is a grid of twelve hues in six lightnesses and a row of greys. OK sends one `format.shape`, `format.text`, `format.gradient` or `format.shadow` with the colour as `#rrggbb`; for the text colour, with a text box
  that was open, **the characters that were selected in it** (the box closes while the dialog is up, what was typed is sent, and the box opens again over the same characters when it ends). Cancel and Escape change nothing.
* Our rule ("pointing at a tile previews it on the page"). **A preview is made on a copy of the drawing** by the code `theme.apply` and `theme.variant` run (`theme::preview`), so the session is not touched: no
  command, no undo step, no revision, no unsaved mark, and the redo stack is kept. **Budget:** the pages of the copy hold at most 5,000 shapes (counted before anything is copied, stopping at the cap) and their recalculation at most a
  tenth of the steps of a document's (a rectangle is about 180 steps); a page past either is refused in words ("apply the theme to see it") and the window says so once for the tile. The window asks for the copy when a tile is under the
  pointer (`ask_theme_preview`), makes it once for the drawing, revision, page and tile, draws it on a render worker of its own in place of the page's picture, and **lets it go in the first frame with no tile asked for**; moving to another
  tile stops the picture being drawn and the picture of the one before stays until the next arrives. A tile that is the page's theme and variant already is not previewed. It is made on the window's own thread (about a fifth of a second
  for a page at the cap). Not previewed: Quick Styles, the layouts, Effects, Connectors and Embellishments (no engine setting behind them).
* Our rule (the `.odg` model writer, `odgfile/flatten.rs`). A drawing from a VSDX file keeps the shape's own formulas of the cells the writer has an attribute for; **any other drawing now keeps the
  theme formulas** (`THEMEGUARD(...)` and `THEMEVAL(...)`) beside the colour value, so that a colour picked from the theme is a value in the file's attribute with its formula carried in DiaCraft's data, and follows the theme when the file is
  read again; a fixed colour's `RGB(r,g,b)` is lost as a formula as before (the save note's `formulas` says so). **A text colour that is a formula is a run's cell with a formula, which a save in place into a drawing opened from an `.odg` does not write yet**
  (tier 2): Save refuses it in words and Save As keeps it.

## Shape operations

Union, Combine, Fragment, Intersect and Subtract build new shapes out of the geometry of the shapes named, or, for Subtract, change the first one. The rules are
a draft, and the choices it leaves to the project are SHAPEOPS-PROPS, -FRAME, -ROWS,
-NEEDS, -GLUE, -UNDO, -AREA, -COMBINE, -FRAGMENT, -INTERSECT, -SUBTRACT, -PRECISION, -CAPS and -PRIMARY in the check list. **The reference app was not
inspected, and the output of these commands has not been opened in it by the project**: every behaviour below that the specification marks `unknown` is ours and
unverified. Join, Trim, Offset and Fit Curve are a later task's; the Operations menu of the window is **The window**, below.

| Command | Parameters | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `shape.union` | `ids` (two or more), `page`? | `{page, ids, deleted, kept}`: one new shape with the outline of all, one section for each boundary of the union (each outer boundary and each hole), in front, with the first shape's text and formatting; the originals are deleted |
| `shape.combine` | `ids` (two or more), `page`? | one new shape with a section for each shown section of each original, in selection order and then section order, so that where they overlap there is a hole (the sections share one fill); its rows are the originals' own, carried exactly |
| `shape.fragment` | `ids` (one or more), `page`? | one new shape for each region the shapes make together, in the order of R23, with the first shape's formatting and **no text**; open paths and `NoFill` 1 paths cut the regions and leave nothing of their own |
| `shape.intersect` | `ids` (two or more), `page`? | one new shape for the area **every** operand covers, with one section for each boundary; refused when no area is common to all |
| `shape.subtract` | `ids` (two or more), `page`? | the area of every shape after the first is taken from the first, which **stays the same shape** (id, name, master link, text, formatting, Shape Data, layers, glue, place in the drawing) and has only its geometry and frame changed; the others are deleted; refused when nothing of the first is left |

`ids` lists the shapes with the **first shape first** (SHAPEOPS-PRIMARY): the first id is the shape whose text and formatting a result takes, and Subtract's
first shape. The shapes must share one container: all on the page, or all members of one group. `page` is the page's `ID` (default: the first page that
holds all the shapes). `ids` in the answer names the shapes made, the last in front (SHAPEOPS-ORDER); `deleted` the shapes deleted; `kept` is Subtract's first shape and
`null` for the others. Each command is **one undo step**; a command that is refused changes nothing and adds no step (SHAPEOPS-UNDO). The made shapes get ids above
every one on the page and above the session's high-water mark. The commands are enabled whenever a document is open, as the other shape commands are: the selection
is the window's, so what each needs of its shapes is checked when it runs, in words (`draw::check_shape_ops` asks the same questions without running one).

**The path work is `craft-pathops`** (the Craft apps' shared library of path operations on `kurbo::BezPath`, here from the one-bit fork at revision
`533839399341547b29ababfb8ef030298b133914` (the head of its pull request, storytold/craft-libs#677, with upstream's main of 2026-11-23 merged in) until the pull request merges), and its `Limits` are its defaults, never raised. The calls are the
`try_` forms, so that a refusal reaches the person: Union is `try_unite_all_with`, Intersect `try_pathfinder_with(Intersect)`, Subtract `try_pathfinder_with(MinusFront)`
with the first shape at the back, Fragment `try_pathfinder_with(Divide)` over the fills and the cutting lines, and `try_area` tells whether a shape has area. A shape's
fill goes in as the rings of its sections that take the fill, filled by the odd-crossings rule. What the library refuses is said in words: `TooComplex`
as "the shapes have too many path elements, or their edges cross or meet in too many places, to be worked on in bounded time (the limits of the path library)",
`Degenerate` as "the shapes are too degenerate to combine (coordinates too large for the sweep, or a shape it cannot take)", `NonFinite` as "a shape's geometry holds a
number that is not finite"; each is a refusal that changes nothing (`Failed`, not `BadParams`). The library is not changed here.

**What is read of an operand** (`draw/shapeops/operand.rs`):

* The rows are the shape's effective ones (its master's and its own), read by the renderer's own reader of a row's cells and built by
  `diacraft_geom::build_paths_bounded`, so what is worked on is the curve that is drawn. Arcs, elliptical arcs, an ellipse, NURBS, splines and Beziers stay curves.
* The paths are carried **through the shape's own pose** into its container's coordinates: the page's, for a shape on the page, and the group's own frame, for a member
  The groups round a member play no part in it, so a result inside a turned group is as turned as the members beside it, and a turned or flipped operand takes
  part with its real outline.
* A section with `NoShow` 1 is not read. A section with `NoFill` 0 gives its fill ring, which closes an open path by a straight edge, whatever the
  shape's `FillPattern` says (SHAPEOPS-AREA). A section with `NoFill` 1, or one whose path is open, is a cutting line for Fragment (ours:
  R23 says "open paths and `NoFill` 1 paths"; a ring that is a cutting line is cut in two before it goes to the library, which takes a ring for a closed shape).
* **A guide, a group, a picture or an embedded object, a connector** (a one-dimensional shape with a glued end, or one of ours that keeps a route), **a container or a
  list, a shape with no shown geometry and a shape on a locked layer are refused** (SHAPEOPS-NEEDS). A container or list is a shape with `User.msvStructureType`
  `Container` or `List`, or whose `Relationships` formula holds `DEPENDSON(1, ...)` or `DEPENDSON(2, ...)`. A shape is on a locked layer when **any** layer its own
  `LayerMember` names, in the page's `Layer` section, has `Lock` set (ours: the specification does not say which of several layers
  decides; a page with more than 4,096 layers is refused in words). Union, Intersect and Subtract need a fill area in every shape, Fragment needs one shape with one,
  Combine and Fragment take lines for the rest. Plain unglued lines are accepted. **A section has a fill area** when it takes the fill (`NoFill` 0) and its fill
  ring is thicker than 1E-5 in some direction: a point, a line there and back (an open straight path with `NoFill` 0) and a run along one line have none. **That is judged from
  the ring, and by no sweep of the library**: the sweep of one operand costs what its crossings do, which no count here bounds, and it would be paid for each operand, again
  by a window asking about a selection. So a shape whose rings pass and cover nothing under the odd-crossings rule (two equal squares one over the other, as two sections) is
  let through by the check, and what a command makes of it is what the rule gives: where nothing would be made the command is refused by its own result ("no area to unite",
  "no area in common", "nothing of shape N is left", "no region"), in words and with nothing changed, and a Union of it with a shape that fills is that shape's outline (the
  one that fills nothing adds nothing, and goes with the rest).
* **The locks** (SHAPEOPS-LOCKS): a shape that would be deleted and has `LockDelete` refuses the command (Subtract's first shape is not deleted, so its own
  `LockDelete` does not refuse it); `LockVtxEdit` on Subtract's first shape refuses Subtract, and on any other shape refuses nothing (no vertex of it is edited: it is
  deleted); `LockFormat` refuses nothing.
* **The work is counted before it is done** (SHAPEOPS-CAPS): a command takes at most 4,096 shapes (the command's id limit), a shape of more than 1,024 `Geometry`
  sections (counted over the shape, its master and its styles) is not an operand, and all the operands together may hold at most 8,192 sections, 200,000 rows, 1 MiB
  of constructor text (a `PolylineTo`'s `A` and a `NURBSTo`'s `E`), as many path elements as the library takes in one operation (524,288) and 50 million
  multiplications of spline work. Each is charged from the sheet's own counts (`Resolver::rows_held`) **before any section is merged or built**, so a shape that
  inherits a huge master costs a count and not a merge, and every section looked at is charged, a hidden or an empty one too. What each shape made is given of the first shape's formatting (its cells and the rows and cells of its sections) and of its frame, times the shapes made, is charged as well (below). A command makes at most 10,000 shapes (Fragment, which stops building pieces when it meets the cap and says "more than 10000", not how many there were) and a shape holds at most 8,192 sections.
  **What is not counted is the library's own sweep.** One call of the path library (`craft-pathops`, its default limits, never raised here) costs what the crossings of its
  edges do, and its limits do not bound that: a shape of 2,001 vertices each joined to the one 1,000 steps on, which passes every limit, takes a sweep 15 to 20 seconds and
  several gigabytes. The engine runs that sweep **once for a command** (the checks before it are by no sweep) and neither lowers the library's limits nor counts crossings of its
  own: the bound is the library's, which `craft-libs` has as a pull request (#677) and DiaCraft takes when its pin moves to it. Until then such a shape makes a command slow,
  and a test of it is ignored (`shapeops_hostile.rs`, with the reason).

**What is written** (`draw/shapeops/rows.rs`, SHAPEOPS-FRAME and -ROWS):

* The library's contours are read as loops: vertices closer than 1E-5 merge, the vertices a straight run passes through are taken out (the library keeps its inputs'
  own anchors, and the union of four bars in a ring would otherwise list a vertex in the middle of each side), a region of less than 1E-8 of area is dropped, and the
  loops are turned so that **outer boundaries run anticlockwise and holes clockwise**. The unit of these tolerances is the cells' own, which is the inch on a page of
  the default scale and the drawing unit on a scaled page, the unit `geom` closes a path in. Curves stay within 1E-5 of the library's exact result, a tenth of
  the 1E-4 of R46; the library's refit precision is that.
* A loop is one section: `MoveTo`, a `LineTo` for each straight edge and one clamped cubic `NURBSTo` for each curved one (degree 3, knots 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, weights 1,
  `NURBS(1,3,0,0,x1,y1,0,1,x2,y2,0,1)` with the control points as shares of the box), each coordinate `Width*k` or `Height*k` with the share to
  **twelve places** (`Width*0.666666666667`, V2: a cell holds nine, which would be 1E-9 out), the constant 0 for 0, plain `Width` for 1, the constant 0 where the box is
  flat. A closed loop ends on the formulas its `MoveTo` starts with. The flags are `NoFill` 0 (every loop written is closed), `NoLine`, `NoShow`, `NoSnap` and
  `NoQuickDrag` 0. Rows are numbered from `IX` 1, as a file's are.
* **The frame of a made shape**: unturned and unflipped (`Angle` 0, `FlipX` and `FlipY` 0), a two-dimensional shape; `Width` and `Height` the box with sides along the
  container's axes that holds the geometry, **a curve counting by its true extent**; `PinX` and `PinY` the middle of the box in the container's coordinates, `LocPinX` the
  formula `Width*0.5` and `LocPinY` `Height*0.5`; the text block the whole shape (the seven text transform cells, as a text box's). A made shape has no master, no
  name and no `UniqueID`.
* **The work is done near the origin.** The container's coordinates are moved so that the middle of everything worked on is the origin, and the results are moved
  back for the pin, so that a small shape far out is as easy for the library's sweep as one near the origin (two squares at 1E8 or 1E12 unite to the shape V2 gives, with the
  pin as far out). A shape that really is that large is refused by the library (`Degenerate`) in words.
* **What a made shape takes from the first shape** (SHAPEOPS-PROPS, `draw/shapeops/look.rs`): its three style links as it reads them (its own or its master
  shape's); every direct cell of the three families the catalogue lists (Line Format, Fill Format with the shadow, gradient and effect cells, the theme and Quick Style cells,
  the Text Block Format cells) that the shape or its master holds, as formula and value; the `FillGradient` and `LineGradient`
  rows as the shape reads them; `LayerMember` (SHAPEOPS-LAYER); and, where the command gives text (Union, Combine, Intersect: the first shape's, or its master's), the text with its runs and
  the `Character`, `Paragraph` and `Tabs` rows its markers name. **Fragment gives no text and none of those rows** (a first shape may hold a thousand rows of each, and a Fragment may make 10,000
  shapes). **The sections are shared:** the shapes a command makes hold the same `Arc` of each section, not a copy for each, and what the sections hold (a row and each of its cells, a section
  and each of its cells), **with the cells the first shape holds itself (up to the 97 of the families) and the frame's cells each shape is given**, times the shapes made, is charged before
  any shape is built and refused in words past 500,000 in all (about a kilobyte is held for each cell by the recalculation that follows, which a million cells fill), for a file written from the drawing writes the sections for each shape. A
  section of more than 1,024 rows, or more than 8 sections of one name, is **refused in words** and not dropped whole. A value a style supplies is not copied, the link is. **Not carried:** Shape Data,
  User-defined cells, Hyperlinks, Actions, Action Tags, Controls, Connection Points, Scratch, Events, Protection, Miscellaneous, Glue Info and Shape Layout. A field
  becomes the text it shows, and a formula that names anything but a direct cell the made shape has (another shape, `Prop.Cost`, `TheText`) becomes its value.

**Fragment's regions** (SHAPEOPS-FRAGMENT): the fills of the operands and the cutting lines go to the library's Divide, which gives one face for each region any fill
covers, a face with holes as one path of several contours; a cutting line leaves nothing of itself. The faces are ordered by the top of each face's box, highest first, then
by its left side, leftmost first, the sides compared to a hundred-millionth as whole numbers so that the order is total; the last is frontmost.

**Combine's rows are carried, not computed** (SHAPEOPS-COMBINE, `draw/shapeops/carry.rs`): a pose is a move, a turn and flips, never a scale, so every row type
is carried by moving its points through the pose and writing its numbers again in the new frame. A point is moved; an `ArcTo`'s bow is a distance, which a turn leaves and a
flip of one axis **changes the sign of**; an elliptical arc's axis angle `C` is turned and reflected with the points and its ratio `D` stays; knots, weights and a
spline's degree stay; `RelMoveTo`, `RelLineTo` and `RelEllipticalArcTo` are written as `MoveTo`, `LineTo` and `EllipticalArcTo` with their shares as formulas, and
`RelCubBezTo` and `RelQuadBezTo` keep their kind with their shares worked out for the new box (ours); a `POLYLINE` or `NURBS` constructor is moved
point by point and written in the relative type. A constructor that cannot be read is carried as what it draws (a line to the end, a move), and an
`InfiniteLine` is left out. Each carried section keeps its own `NoFill`, `NoLine`, `NoSnap` and `NoQuickDrag`, and a section with `NoShow` 1 is dropped.

**Subtract changes the first shape in place** (SHAPEOPS-SUBTRACT): the work is done in the first shape's own frame, so its geometry is what it was. Its shown sections
that have a fill area (they take the fill, `NoFill` 0, and cover an area: not an open straight line with `NoFill` 0) are replaced by the boundary of its fill less the union of the others' fills; its
`NoFill` 1 sections, its open sections that cover no area and its hidden ones are kept as they were, and a first shape that keeps one keeps its frame (below).
On a master instance a replaced section it inherits is marked deleted on the instance (`Del`) and the new ones are local, numbered from the lowest `IX` that neither the
shape nor its master holds. The other operands are deleted whether or not they touch it. **The frame** is refitted to the box of the new geometry in the shape's own axes,
`Angle`, `FlipX`, `FlipY` and the formulas of `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` kept, and `PinX` and `PinY` moved by what the box's corner and the local pin moved, through the shape's
turn and flips, so that nothing moves on the page; the local pin follows its formula, so a centred pin stays centred. **A first shape that holds `LockCalcWH`, or keeps
any section of its own, keeps its frame exactly** and the geometry is written in it (R28 for `LockCalcWH`; ours, for the rest: a refit moves the origin
of the shape's own frame, and what is kept, written in that frame, would move on the page; the specification says kept sections stay "as they were" and does not say what
happens to the frame then). A first shape with no width or no height in that case is refused. Cells that follow `Width` and `Height`, such as connection points written as
shares, move with the box, and a connector glued to them follows; formulas that name a row of a replaced section are not kept.

**Glue, guides, containers and undo**: the originals go by the engine's own delete at the places one walk of the page found them: a
connector glued to one keeps that end where it is, unglued, its `Connect` row goes, and it is neither glued to the made shape nor deleted; a shape held to one by alignment cells
is released; jumps are settled. A made shape has no connection points. **A command that makes one shape puts it where the first shape was in containers and lists**
(SHAPEOPS-CONTAINERS, ours: only the first shape's ties of kinds 4 and 5, `DEPENDSON(4, ...)` and `DEPENDSON(5, ...)`, go to the made shape, and each
`Relationships` formula that holds `Sheet.<first>!` as a member, in a `DEPENDSON(1, ...)` or `DEPENDSON(2, ...)` call, holds the made shape instead); callouts' ties are not
carried and a tie to a shape that is gone names nothing, as a delete leaves it; a command that makes many shapes puts none in a container. The made shapes are appended to the end of
the container's list (the page's, or the group's members), so they are frontmost in it, and what was between the originals is behind them.

**What these cost**, measured by `crates/engine/tests/it/shapeops_hostile.rs` in the development profile (the times move with the load of the machine; each test holds the
whole call to 60 s, far above what it takes and far below a hang): 120 thin bars crossing in 3,600 places unite in 0.3 s to a shape of 3,482 sections; 300 bars turned about one centre
in 0.6 s; a pie of 256 slices (512 ends at one point, the library's limit) in 1.1 s, and one of 300 is refused in 2 ms; 2,000 and 3,000 squares nested unite in 0.1 s, a Subtract of
1,500 in 0.09 s, and an Intersect or a Fragment of 3,000 nested is refused in 0.1 s (the library's limit on the entries of its windings); 4,096 disjoint squares unite in 0.6 s;
a Fragment into 10,000 regions (99 lines each way across a rectangle, 199 operands) is made in 1.9 s and one into 10,201 is refused in 0.3 s; shapes 85 groups deep take 1 ms; a
union of 9,000 sections is refused by its count in 0.05 s and a section of 200,001 rows in 0.1 s. Two squares at 1E8 or 1E12 unite in 0.5 ms.

**What is not here:** Join, Trim, Offset and Fit Curve, which the window's Operations menu draws quiet; a container for the many shapes of a
Fragment; the refit of a Subtract frame that keeps sections; a drawing scale's inches (the tolerances are the cells' units).

**The window (`crates/ui-egui/src/operations.rs`, `options_dialog.rs`).** The Developer tab's Shape Design group has the **Operations** menu (`OperationsMenu`) with nine rows. **Union, Combine, Fragment, Intersect and Subtract** run the window's `operations.union`, `operations.combine`, `operations.fragment`, `operations.intersect` and `operations.subtract` (no parameters; `engine.commands` lists them with the engine's), each of which names the shapes selected and the page they were selected on (refused in words when that page is not the one shown) and sends the engine's `shape.*` command, and **clears the selection when it succeeds** (nothing stays selected, and a Subtract's first shape stays but is not selected). **Join, Trim, Offset and Update Alignment Box** are drawn and quiet, with the tooltip "Join (not available yet)", the words a quiet button of the ribbon uses (a quiet row of any menu now says why on hover: the reason its command gives, or "(not available yet)"). No row is in the canvas menu (lists none).

**The order of `ids` is the order the window keeps its selection in** (SHAPEOPS-PRIMARY), and the first shape is the one a result takes its text and formatting from: a click selects one shape alone and a Shift-click or a Ctrl-click adds one at the end, so a click selection is in click order and the shape clicked first is first; a rubber band lists the shapes it takes in the page's drawing order, back to front, so the backmost is first, and a Shift-rubber-band adds them after what was selected; Select All is the page's order too; `edit.select {ids}` keeps the order it is given.

**The shapes go to the page they were selected on, and a selection that is not of the page on view is refused**: a shape's `ID` is the page's own, so `edit.select {ids: [1, 2]}` on page 1, `page.select {id: 2}` and `operations.union`, served in one frame by the control channel, would have united page 2's shapes 1 and 2. `selection_target`, which every window command that acts on the selection takes its page and shapes from (the five here, `format.*`, `format.rotateText`, `arrange.*` and `clipboard.copy`, `.cut` and `.duplicate`), now returns the page the selection was made on and refuses with "the shapes selected are on another page than the one shown" when that is not the page on view, changing nothing; an entry of the Operations menu is quiet with the same words. It refuses and does not drop the selection, since a command must not change what the window holds; a person cannot reach it, as the next frame drops a selection that is not of the page on view. **The panes follow the same rule**: Size & Position, Format Shape and the formatting the Home tab's controls show take the shapes selected only when the selection is of the page on view (`selection_on_view` and `selection_here` in `format.rs`, one place with `selection_target`); with a selection of another page they show nothing of it: their boxes are quiet and blank, Size & Position's title has no shape's name, and no formatting is read. A pane box that cannot be changed takes no keys even while it still holds the focus from the frame before (egui's `add_enabled` greys a text box and leaves its keys on), so a value typed in the frame the page changes reaches neither page.

**An entry is enabled only when `draw::check_shape_ops` says its operation can run on the selection** (two shapes for four of them and one for Fragment; no group, guide, connector, container, list, picture or shape without geometry; no `LockDelete` on a shape the command would delete, no `LockVtxEdit` on Subtract's first shape, no shape on a locked layer), and the Operations button is enabled when any entry is (with every entry quiet the menu does not open and the button's tooltip says why, as the other menus do). A quiet entry's tooltip is the engine's own words ("Union needs two shapes at least (a shape named twice counts once)"), and a command run by name while its entry is quiet is refused with them. **The engine is asked when the selection or the drawing changes, never for a frame:** the answer for each operation is kept for the drawing's generation, its revision, the page and the shapes selected in order (a file that is opened starts its revision at 1 again, so the revision alone would find the old drawing's answers in the new one), and an operation is asked about only when something draws or asks whether it can run: the button asks for the first entry that works, the open menu for the five (a check is one reading of the shapes' geometry, 13 ms on the worst input the engine's tests make; `work::Work::shape_op_checks` counts them). **A run that the check let through and its result refuses** (Intersect of squares that do not meet: "no area in common") puts the engine's words in the status bar and leaves the drawing, the undo history and the selection as they were. Each operation is one undo step: Ctrl+Z brings the originals back with their ids and cells, and Ctrl+Y makes it again.

**The Developer tab is off in a new window** and is turned on in **File, Options, Customize Ribbon** (`window.options {tab?, close?}`; `window.developer {on?}` is the command it sends). The dialog lists the ten sections, of which only Customize Ribbon works (the others are quiet, with the tooltip "General (not available yet)"), and its Main Tabs list shows every tab ticked and quiet except the Developer box; **OK sends one `window.developer` if the box was changed**, Cancel and Escape send nothing, and the File view closes behind the dialog. Where the window is too narrow for the list of sections beside the page, the page shows alone. The setting is the window's and is not saved (the dialog's note says it is for this window only, and the next window starts with the tab off); the dialog's layout, its captions and the quiet tabs are ours (R32 says only that the Options dialog's Main Tabs list orders the tabs as the row does). **The reference app was not inspected, and the window has not been opened in it by the project.**

## What each one does to the document

* **Masters.** `shape.drop` copies the master into the document's masters if the document does not hold it (by the master match rule above: its `UniqueID`, and its `BaseID` where that says it is the same master; for a master of an opened stencil see **Stencil files**) (every master of the built-in stencils has a
  fixed GUID, **ours**: `{D1AC0001-…}` for Basic Shapes, `{D1AC0002-…}` for Basic Flowchart, `{D1AC0004-…}` to `{D1AC0006-…}` for Decorative Shapes, Arrow Shapes and Gane-Sarson, `{D1AC0007-…}` for the Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes and `{D1AC0008-…}` for the Organization Chart Shapes, numbered from 1 in stencil order: each library has a series of its own, and `{D1AC0003-…}` is the GUID a master copied into a drawing is given when its own is taken there), then adds an instance that holds only `PinX` and `PinY`. The rest is inherited; a resize makes the
  instance hold `Width` and `Height` and `Inh` cells with the results that now differ from the master's. Masters stay in the
  document whether or not an instance is left.
* **Basic Shapes** (`crates/engine/src/stencil.rs`): rectangle, square, ellipse, circle, triangle, right triangle, diamond, pentagon,
  hexagon, octagon, parallelogram, trapezoid. Plain mathematics; names, sizes and connection points are **ours**. Each has its geometry as
  formulas of `Width` and `Height`, at least four connection points, and the seven text block cells with their creation formulas.
* **Basic Flowchart** (`crates/engine/src/stencil/flowchart.rs`): eighteen symbols, in the order of the Shapes window. Names are the generic
  ones of the public notation (thirteen from the symbol tables of [Flowchart (Wikipedia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flowchart), read
  2026-11-01, which names ISO 5807; the standard itself was not read; delay, display, internal storage, summing junction and or are the
  other symbols general descriptions of ISO 5807 flowcharts list, and the article does not name them). The drawings are plain geometry,
  **ours**; nothing is traced from another application's stencil. The article's annotation symbol is not a master: it has one place to join,
  and a master here has at least four connection points.

  | Id | Name | Size (in) | Drawn as |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `process` | Process | 1.5 × 0.8 | rectangle |
  | `decision` | Decision | 1.5 × 1.0 | diamond; the text block is the inside of it |
  | `terminator` | Terminator | 1.5 × 0.6 | stadium: half circles of radius half the height at the two ends |
  | `data` | Data | 1.5 × 0.8 | parallelogram leaning right, the slant a quarter of the height |
  | `document` | Document | 1.5 × 0.8 | rectangle with a wavy bottom (two circular arcs) |
  | `multiple_documents` | Multiple Documents | 1.5 × 1.0 | three stepped sheets: a document in front, and the two strips that show above and beside it |
  | `predefined_process` | Predefined Process | 1.5 × 0.8 | rectangle with a vertical line a fifth of the height in from each end |
  | `manual_input` | Manual Input | 1.5 × 0.8 | rectangle whose top slopes up to the right from four fifths of the height |
  | `manual_operation` | Manual Operation | 1.5 × 0.8 | trapezoid, wide at the top, slanting in by a quarter of the height |
  | `preparation` | Preparation | 1.5 × 0.8 | elongated hexagon, points at the two ends |
  | `on_page_reference` | On-Page Reference | 0.5 × 0.5 | circle |
  | `off_page_reference` | Off-Page Reference | 0.6 × 0.8 | box ending in a point at the bottom |
  | `database` | Database | 1.0 × 1.2 | cylinder: half-ellipse rims, the front of the top rim a stroke across the fill |
  | `internal_storage` | Internal Storage | 1.0 × 1.0 | rectangle with a vertical and a horizontal line, 0.15 in from the left and the top |
  | `delay` | Delay | 1.2 × 0.8 | rectangle with a half circle for its right end |
  | `display` | Display | 1.5 × 0.8 | a point at the left, a half circle at the right |
  | `summing_junction` | Summing Junction | 0.5 × 0.5 | circle crossed by its two diagonals |
  | `or` | Or | 0.5 × 0.5 | circle crossed by its horizontal and vertical diameters |

  Arcs are `EllipticalArcTo` rows through three points (a circle, or a half-ellipse whose ratio `D` is a formula of the box), so no sign
  convention for a bow is relied on. A slant or a round end is a share of the height, so widening a symbol keeps its angles and its round
  ends (`Width-Height*0.25`); narrowing a terminator, delay or display below its height turns its round ends past each other. A symbol with
  strokes inside its fill (the lines of a predefined process, the front rim of a database, the cross of a summing junction) holds them as
  `Geometry` sections of their own with `NoFill` 1, so that they do not punch the fill (a project rule).
  Connection points are the middle of each side, moved onto the outline where a side is slanted or curved; each lies on the drawn stroke.
* **Glue**: an end glued to connection point `IX` `n - 1` of shape `S` holds `PAR(PNT(Sheet.S!Connections.Xn,
  Sheet.S!Connections.Yn))` in both its cells, and a `Connect` row (`FromPart` 9 for the begin and 12 for the end, `ToPart` 100 plus `IX`). A
  recalculation moves the end with the shape. An end glued to the whole of a shape (`ToPart` 3) in a file holds `_WALKGLUE(...)`, which `calc`
  cannot work out (behaviour `unknown`); the engine puts such an end where says, as the cached value of its
  cells, and the formula stays as the file had it (see Choices).
* **A connector** is a shape with no master, one-dimensional: `Width`, `Angle`, `PinX`, `PinY` and
  `LocPinX` hold the creation formulas, `Height` is 0, and the begin is at (0, `LocPinY`) of its own frame. `EndArrow` is 4. A `User` row
  `DiaCraftRoute` marks a connector as one DiaCraft made; a connector read from a VSDX file has none, and is routed again only (the `.odg` reader gives every
  connector it reads the marker, as one DiaCraft may route again).
  Its label (`TxtPinX`, `TxtPinY`, `TxtAngle`, `TextBkgnd`) follows.
* **The route** is the right-angle rule, named once in `crates/engine/src/draw/route.rs`: Z for ends that face each other,
  L for a corner ahead of both, C for ends that leave the same way, a detour by a standoff of 0.25 otherwise, (rule 6) a bridge in
  place of any of those that would double back on itself, and (rule 7) a way round the boxes of the shapes the ends are glued to, in place of any of those that goes through one; a connector's style
  may make the route one straight leg, or a curve drawn through this one). It is written as `Geometry` rows of constants (`MoveTo`, then `LineTo`) in the
  connector's own frame, **by the command**, after the page is recalculated, and not by a recalculation hook (`crates/engine/src/draw/mod.rs`
  says why). A route has no repeated vertex and no zero-length leg, and no leg that goes out along a line and comes straight back along it
  (which a viewer draws as one line with a second arrowhead on it); the one exception is a connector whose two ends are the same point, which
  is two equal points, since no leg is left to point an arrowhead along. The first row is the begin, at (0, `LocPinY`) of the connector's own
  frame, and the last is the end, at (`Width`, `LocPinY`); `EndArrow` is 4 and there is no `BeginArrow`.
* **Recalculation**. Each command that changes a cell ends by working out **what it changed and what reads it**
  (`calc::changes_between` finds the cells that differ from the document the command began with, and `calc::commit_changed` works out those and
  every cell that reads one of them, again and again), and writes the results under the `Evaluation` origin, so the saved `V` values are right
  for the renderer and the writer. A cell is read by another when its formula names it (`Width`, `Sheet.4!PinX`, `User.Row`), when it calls a
  function that carries a point between frames (`PAR`, `LOC` and the others) and the cell is a pose cell (`PinX`, `PinY`, `LocPinX`,
  `LocPinY`, `Angle`, `FlipX`, `FlipY`) of a shape whose frame it carries between (this is how
  `PAR(PNT(Sheet.4!Connections.X1, ...))` follows shape 4), when a `Connect` row glues its end to a shape, and when it inherits a formula from
  a master that reads it. **A cell that nothing the command changed is read by keeps the value the file saved, byte for byte, whatever this
  evaluator would give for it**, and so does a cell that is worked out and comes out as it was. The route is rewritten from the ends that came
  out. See `crates/calc/src/affected.rs` for what is and is not followed, and `crates/calc/src/index.rs` for how what reads what through other sheets is kept from one command to the next. **A cell passes the change on only if it changed**: the cells that
  read a cell the command wrote are worked out, and the cells that read one of those only if working it out planned a write. A reader that comes out
  as it was, or that `calc` cannot work out (a connector end glued by `_WALKGLUE`), ends the chain: the width and the pin that read such an end keep the
  values the file saved, and are not moved a rounding away from them by a move that did not change the end. **An edit outside the page** (a
  style, the theme, a master's formulas), which `calc` cannot follow cell by cell, works out every formula cell of the page, **measured from the
  document the command began with** (`Changes::since`): the cells of the masters the page's instances use that the edit changed
  are worked out, so an instance that leaves a cell to its master reads the new value through it, and an instance is given a cell it inherits
  and does not hold only where the edit changed what the cell works out to on it and the result is not its master's:
  an instance at its master's size keeps no copy of a master's cell, and one of another size keeps leaving to its master the rows its file
  left there, except a cell the edit changed to another value than its master's.

## Choices that no specification settles (ours)

* A text box is a shape with no master that paints nothing until it is given a fill or a line, and the text block
  of a shape is moved, sized and turned by writing its seven cells as values, the pin carried by the block's turn and flips and the text turned about the pin; see **Text boxes and the text block** above.
* The window: the text box holds the characters only. The fields are shown under them, in order, and not at the places they
  stand in the text: the box is an egui text edit, whose cursor counts the characters it holds, and a field in the middle of them would have to be
  characters it could be typed into. What the box sends has the characters that a file cannot hold (the controls other than a line break and a tab,
  U+FFFE and U+FFFF) taken out first, so a paste that carries them does not make the engine refuse the label and lose it. The box takes at most
  `MAX_TEXT_CHARS` characters. A box that is open on another shape when a new one opens is sent first.
* The window: a text box that is open is sent before the window runs any command that changes the document or leaves the drawing
  area: every engine command that is not a query, and the window's `page.select`, `edit.select`, `edit.selectAll`, `window.backstage` and `window.tab`; and
  before Save, Save As, New, Open, Export and the close check ask. So Undo with a box open takes back the label (one undo step) and not the step before
  it, a page change sends the label to the page it was typed on, and a close finds the drawing changed. A query (`document.inspect`) and a view command (zoom,
  panels, theme, tool) leave the box open. Ctrl+N, Ctrl+O, Ctrl+S and Ctrl+Shift+S work from the box; the other shortcuts are the box's (Ctrl+Z in it
  undoes typing). The title carries the star while a label has been typed and not sent.
* The window: the Save and Export dialogs check the name they were given, and the window adds `.vsdx` or `.png` to a name with no
  type after that. When the file with the type added is there (a file, a folder or a link), the window asks before it writes: Replace or Cancel. A name
  that already had its type was checked by the dialog and is not asked about again.
* The window: `ChangedOnDisk` from `file.save` or from `file.save_as` onto the drawing's own file is a question, not a failure:
  Overwrite (the same command again with `force`), Save As (the Save dialog, then `file.save_as`), or Cancel. A save that was to be followed by New, Open or
  the close is followed only when the file is written, and Cancel ends it.
* The window: the three questions, and the progress card of a file being read, are modals over a shade (`Tokens::scrim`): nothing under
  them takes the pointer, and the shortcuts and drawing keys are held (with the File view open only the shortcuts of New, Open, Save and Save As run, so
  Delete and Ctrl+Z reach no drawing the person is not looking at). Escape is Cancel. A drag that was on when a card came up is dropped.
* The window; L7 of the M3 review: while a file is being read the window takes no edits from the person, rather than asking at the end
  whether the file may replace what was changed meanwhile (which would hold the file back while a question is answered). A program on the control channel is
  not held: what it changes is replaced when the file arrives, as any `file.open` replaces the document.
* **MOVE-1D**: moving a connector moves its ends that are not glued. A glued end stays with its target, since moving it would break the glue. A
  connector with both ends glued has nothing to move: it is not counted in `moved`, and a command that moves nothing at all leaves the document, its
  history and the drawing's unsaved state as they were. `shape.align` moves by the same rule, and ROTATE-1D-1 turns and flips a connector's ends by it.
* Our rule: a member of a group has its pin in the group's frame, which is the page's only for a group that is not turned
  or flipped. `shape.move` carries the distance it is given (a distance on the page) down through the groups the shape is in, the outermost first
  (`Pose::down_vector`), before it adds it to the member's pin (and to the ends of a connector in a group). `shape.resize` carries the box's corner (a
  point of the page) down the same way, and refuses a member of a turned or flipped group, whose box is not square to the page. A group's children are
  not scaled by the group's cells alone: **a group is sized by `shape.resize` as one shape and its members follow it**. `shape.rotate` and `shape.flip` refuse the member of a turned or flipped group too.
* Replaced by: `shape.resize` refused a turned or flipped shape.
* Our rule (`draw/shapes.rs`): `shape.resize` refuses a connector and a member of a turned or flipped group, takes a group, and
  takes a shape that is turned or flipped. `x` and `y` are the place on the page of **the corner of the box that is at (0, 0) of the shape's own
  frame**, and `width` and `height` are along the shape's own axes; for a shape that is neither turned nor flipped that is the lower-left corner and
  the width and height of the box, as had them. The shape keeps its `Angle`, `FlipX` and `FlipY`. The pin follows so that the corner lands where
  it is asked: the local pin is worked out again for the new size (a pin at the middle stays at the middle), and the pin is the corner plus the local
  pin carried by the shape's turn and flips (`Pose::up_vector`). A window finds the corner with `draw::shape_corners`.
* (ours where no specification says: gives the eight handles round a set of selected 2-D shapes and says a corner handle keeps the proportions, and
  that several shapes turned keep their places in relation to each other "as though the page had been turned"; nothing says how each shape comes out of a stretch, so the set follows what one shape's
  handles do, scaled to the set; `draw/scale.rs`). **`shape.resize` with `ids` (and no `id`) makes the joint box of the shapes the box `x`, `y`, `width`, `height`**: the joint box is the smallest box on the page that holds the boxes of
  the shapes (the four corners of each, carried through its turn, its flips and its groups', and for a connector its two ends and nothing else), `x` and `y` the new box's lower-left corner in inches, and
  **every place is carried by the stretch that takes the old box to the new one** (a stretch along each page axis about the old box's lower-left corner, then the move to `x`, `y`), so the shapes keep their order and their gaps in proportion.
  A shape's centre goes where the stretch puts it and its `Width` and `Height` are scaled by the lengths the stretch gives its own two axes (`sx` and `sy` for a shape not turned, `sy` and `sx` for one turned a quarter, and for a
  shape turned by `a` the square roots of `(sx cos a)^2 + (sy sin a)^2` and `(sx sin a)^2 + (sy cos a)^2`: the same factor on both axes scales a shape by it at any angle). **A shape turned by an angle that is not a quarter keeps its
  angle** (a stretch that is not the same on both axes would shear it, which a box cannot say), its flips are kept, and its pin follows from its centre as for one shape (the local pin is worked out again for the new size).
  A connector carries its ends that are not glued by the stretch (a glued end stays with its shape: the connectors glued to a shape that moved or changed size are routed again, as for any edit). A shape named with a group it is
  in is left to the group and a member of a turned or flipped group is refused. The joint box must have a length along each axis (a line along x has no height to stretch: refused in words), and a resize
  to the box the shapes have is no resize and no undo step. At most 4,096 `ids`. **`snap`** snaps the edges of the joint box that the resize moves, as a shape's box is (`snap::snap_resize_set`; the box of a selection is square to the page
  whatever its shapes are, the shapes resized never targets, at most 512 shapes, `keep: "proportions"` keeps the proportions of the joint box: from a corner the opposite corner stays, from a side the opposite side stays and the other axis grows about its middle),
  and the shapes whose own edge is pulled to a guide are held to it as a move holds them. Each kind of target pulls an edge of the joint box from within its own strength, as it pulls a shape's (the grid from
  5 pixels at first; `snap_resize_set` and `snap_resize_ext` are one function for a box square to the page). The result tells how many shapes changed and which were left alone, and the snap.
* Our rule (`draw/scale.rs`). **A group is one shape**: `shape.resize` of a group, by `id` in its own frame or among `ids`, writes its `Width`, `Height` and pin as it does a
  shape's, and **its members follow by their own formulas** (the ties writes: `Sheet.<group>!Width*<factor>`), which the recalculation after the edit works out. A member whose size or place is a number and not a tie is
  carried by the stretch of the group's own frame (the factors the group's size went by), in the same edit: its `PinX`, `PinY`, `Width`, `Height` (by the lengths the stretch gives its own axes, as above) and a `LocPinX` or `LocPinY` that is a
  number, the ends of a line, and a group among them by its own factors in its turn, once its new size is known; **a cell that holds any formula is data and is left as it is**, and **a line among the members is routed again** with the rest of the edit (its route is rows of its own frame, which the stretch changes). The refit rule then makes the group the box of what its members came to when it was that
  box before. At most 20,000 members are carried in one edit (more are refused in words and nothing changes), and groups nest no deeper than `MAX_GROUP_DEPTH`. **Not done:** a group's own constant `Geometry`, `Connection` and `Controls` rows are not scaled
  (a group `shape.group` makes holds none; one of a file that does keeps its numbers), and `shape.size`, the Size & Position pane's command, still refuses a group.
* (the cells' documented meanings in `sections-protection-events.md`: `LockWidth` "keeps width fixed during sizing", `LockHeight` the same for height, `LockAspect` "allows sizing only in proportion"; no
  specification says what a set does with them, so this is ours). In a `shape.resize` with `ids`, **a shape with `LockWidth` or `LockHeight` set is left alone**: it is neither sized nor moved, and its box still counts in the joint box the others
  are stretched from; it is listed in the result's `locked`. **A shape with `LockAspect` set is stretched by the same factor on both axes**, the geometric mean of the two. When every shape named is locked the resize is refused in words
  ("the shapes are locked against sizing (`LockWidth` or `LockHeight`): shape ...") and nothing changes. The locks are not read by `shape.resize` with `id`, nor by `shape.size`, nor by the window's handles of one shape (a locked shape's grey handle
  bars, R35, are not drawn either).
* Our rule (`LockRotate` "bars turning a flat shape by handle or quarter-turn command"). **A shape with `LockRotate` set is left alone by `shape.rotate`**: it is neither turned nor moved by a turn of a set, and its box still counts in
  the joint box the others turn about (the middle of the box that is drawn); it is listed in the result's `locked`, and when every shape named is locked the turn is refused in words ("the shape is locked against turning (`LockRotate`): shape ...") and nothing changes. A one-dimensional shape is
  held to it as any other. `rotate_shapes` (the function) leaves the locked shapes and says nothing; `turn_shapes` says which.
* Our rule (`draw/arrange.rs`): `shape.rotate` turns a shape by writing `Angle`, counterclockwise
  (positive is leftward, as the Size & Position window has it), the cell in radians and the command in degrees. `angle` sets it, each shape about its **own pin**.
  `by` adds to it: **one shape turns about its pin, several turn as one about the middle of their joint box** (the box on the page that holds all their boxes; **`about`: {x, y} in inches, with `by`, turns each pin about that point instead**, which is how the window turns a selection about the middle of the box it draws; it is refused beside `angle`), so
  their pins move and their angles all grow by `by` ("several shapes turn as if the page turned"). The pin and the size of a shape
  turned about its own pin are not written. The angle written is kept **from just over -180 up to 180 degrees** (-90 from 0 is written -90 and 270 is written -90
  too; the files hold both signs, and counts 34,207 negative `Angle` cells against 323 larger than pi in size), a value within
  a billionth of a radian of a quarter turn is that quarter exactly (and the same angle: a turn of nothing on a shape whose angle it would have snapped writes
  nothing), and it is written with thirteen places, as the files hold one (`1.5707963267949`), not
  the nine of a length. The cell keeps the unit it had (`DEG`, `RAD`); a formula in it (`30 deg`) is replaced by the number, as a drag replaces one, except
  a `BOUND` or `SETATREF` formula, which passes the number on; a `GUARD`ed cell is an error and nothing changes. A turn to the angle a
  shape has, or by a whole turn, writes nothing, and the command is no undo step. A cell that reads the angle (`TxtAngle` as `-Angle`) is worked out
  again with what reads it. Text turns with the shape and is never mirrored.
* Our rule (no source gives what a flip writes): `shape.flip` is a **mirror of the page picture**, across the vertical line
  (`horizontal`: left becomes right) or the horizontal line (`vertical`) through the middle of the joint box of the shapes named, which for one shape is its own box.
  Each shape's pin is mirrored with it, `FlipX` (`FlipY`) is toggled, and `Angle` changes sign, since `M·R(θ)·F = R(-θ)·M·F` for the mirror `M`: the shape is then the
  mirror of the picture, however it was turned. The box of one shape stays where it is (a pin that is not at the middle moves with the mirror, and one that is does not
  move), flipping twice gives the shape back, and several shapes flip as one, keeping their places in the picture. The cells that turn the text are not touched:
  glyphs are never mirrored, and the net turn of the text is `Angle` minus `TxtAngle` under one flip, so the text of a flipped shape is where it was and
  reads the same way. A flip toggles the cell (it writes 0 and 1, and does not take the cell away).
* **ROTATE-1D-1** (ours, after MOVE-1D): a one-dimensional shape (a connector) is turned or flipped by moving **its ends that are not glued**: with no
  end glued, turned about its pin (one shape, or `angle`: the way the line points, from the begin to the end, is set to the angle) or about the joint box (several turned
  `by`); **with one end glued, the free end turns about the glued end**, so the line turns by the angle and `angle` is reached exactly ("a line takes the angle
  from its begin point"; a set turned `by` still turns its free ends about the joint box); mirrored across the same line as any shape.
  It writes no pose cell: `Angle`, `Width` and the pin follow the ends by their formulas, and a route of ours is made again and its label placed. A glued end stays with its
  target and a connector with both ends glued has nothing to turn or flip, is not counted, and is no undo step. A flip of a file's connector whose route is more than a
  straight line and which has no glued end keeps the rows of its route, which are in its own frame.
* Our rule: `shape.order` reorders the list a shape is in: the page's part, or a group's own members (a shape named with others
  from other lists moves in each list). **Bring to Front and Send to Back go to the end of the shape's band**: a
  shape is put just before the first shape after it with a higher `DisplayLevel` (a lower one, for the back), so that it stays behind every higher band and in front of
  every lower one; with the same band throughout that is the end or the start of the list. `forward` and `backward` move a shape one place, past the next shape that is
  not named and not of a higher band (a lower one, for backward); a block of shapes named moves as one, and one at the end stays. Shapes named together keep the order they
  have among themselves, whatever order they are named in. A shape in the reserved band -32768 has none to keep: it goes to the very front or back, and it stops no other shape.
  `DisplayLevel` is read by inheritance (it may come from a master or a style). **No command writes `DisplayLevel`**: the -32768 that writes for a command that carries
  a shape out of its band does not arise, and the guarded cell is never reached. `moved` counts the shapes named that changed place, and an order that changes
  nothing is no undo step. The writer keeps the order of the page part, and the saved file reads back in it.
* Our rule (the others move to the primary shape without changing spacing): `shape.align` moves every shape named after the first, by the distance on the page
  that puts its left, right, top or bottom edge, or its horizontal or vertical middle (`center` is the middle between left and right, `middle` between top and bottom), where
  the first shape's is. The first shape named, not the lowest `ID`, is the primary one and does not move. A shape's edges are those of **its box on the page**: the bounding box of
  its box carried through its turn, flips and groups (the box, not the drawn curve), and for a connector the box of its ends. The move is a move of `shape.move`'s kind
  (MOVE-1D), done once for all, so a member of a turned group aligns too. One shape named, or shapes that are aligned, is no undo step.
* Our rule: a shape named together with a group it is in is left to the group, which moves, turns or flips it (a member would be moved twice), by
  `shape.rotate`, `shape.flip` and `shape.align`. `shape.rotate` and `shape.flip` refuse a member of a turned or flipped group (its cells are in the group's frame, and a
  turn or a flip of the page is another in it, as for a resize and a group's frame); a group itself turns and flips as a shape, its members with it, and a member of a group that
  is neither turned nor flipped turns, flips and aligns with the page. **The group commands keep this true**: a group made inside a turned or flipped group does not lift the rule for its members, and ungrouping
carries the turn and the flips into the members' own cells, after which they turn and flip like any shape of the page.
* Our rule (`draw/clipboard.rs`): `edit.copy` takes the shapes named, each with everything inside it (a shape named with a group it is in is left to the group), in
  the order of the drawing; at most 50,000 shapes, members counted. **The clipboard is the session's, one for a document**: a new or opened drawing starts with none (a paste is within
  one document, M3 second) and an undo leaves it. It also holds the masters the shapes use, so that a paste after an undo that took a master away (a dropped shape's,
  with the drop) puts it back; otherwise **a paste uses the document's masters by `ID` and never makes a second copy of one** (a master of the document with the same `UniqueID` is that
  master, whatever its `ID`; matching a stencil's masters to a document's is `stencil.open`'s). A paste gives every shape in the copy, members included, a new `ID`, in the
  order of the copy (above every `ID` on the page and every one the session has given there); puts the copies **on top** of the drawing order, in their order; and makes a
  `Connect` row between the copies where there was one between the shapes. A connector copied with the shapes it is glued to is glued to the copies, by `PAR(PNT(...))` and by
  `_WALKGLUE` alike; **a connector copied without a shape it is glued to has that end a plain point where it was**, and no row (as the delete rule leaves the end of a connector whose shape is
  deleted). A formula in a copy that names a copied shape names its copy; a name that ends in the shape's `ID` ends in the copy's (`Process.4` copied to 9 is
  `Process.9`; ours: no source read says what a copy is called); `UniqueID` is not copied, since it names one shape. Text, runs, fields, formats, style links, `Data1` to `Data3`, `OriginalID` and every cell copy as they are. A
  **member of a group** is copied alone as a shape of the page: its cells are carried out of its groups when it is pasted, so it draws as it did. A member that takes its
  cells from a master group (`MasterShape` and no `Master`) is refused, since it would lose them, and so is a connector in a flipped group. A picture belongs to its page (the `Rel` in
  `ForeignData` is an id in the page part's relationships, which the writer does not add to): **a copy that holds one is pasted on the page it came from only**. The cells that move a copy
  are written as a drag writes them (a formula in `PinX` becomes the number; a `GUARD`ed one is an error), and none is written for a copy that lands where it was.
* Our rule (the reference app's own offset is not given by any source read): `edit.paste` with no `x` and `y` puts the copies a step (a quarter of an inch, in paper inches
  whatever the page's scale) **right and down** of the originals when the page is the one they were copied from, and one more step for each paste of the same copy; on another page the
  first paste is where the originals were, and each later one a step on. With `x` and `y` the middle of the box that holds the copies lands there. `shape.duplicate` offsets by `dx` and
  `dy` (a quarter of an inch right and down by default) and leaves the clipboard, and the count of pastes, alone, so a window that selects the new shapes steps them on by repeating it.
* Our rule (`draw/remap.rs`): in the formula of a cell of a copy, each `Sheet.<id>!` whose `<id>` is the `ID` of a shape copied becomes
  `Sheet.<new>!`, and the rest of the formula is kept character for character. A reference by name (`Process!Width`, a peer by `NameU`) is kept and goes on naming what it named; a
  `Sheet.<id>` after another qualifier (`Pages[Site-2]!Sheet.9!EndY`) is on another page and is kept; text in a string or a quoted name is not a reference. A reference to a shape not
  copied stays with the original **on the page the shapes were copied from**. **On another page it would reach the shape of the new page with that `ID`** (`Sheet.<id>` reaches a shape of
  the page by its `ID`), so a cell of a copy whose formula names a shape not copied is made the constant of its cached value there (as the end of a connector whose shape was not copied is): the glue formula of
