  a connector with no `Connect` row, a `BegTrigger`, a `Width` of `Sheet.5!Width*0.5`. **What making a cell its value does** (the same for an ungroup): the formula is taken away, and **the cached error (`E`) with it** (a constant reports none; kept, it would be reported for ever and, on the end of a
  connector, would make skip the connector); the value, unit and the rest of the cell are kept; **a `GUARD`ed cell loses its guard with its formula** (it is a plain constant, as after a drag); **a cell with a formula and no cached value** (malformed) is left as it is, formula and all, since an empty cell would read as 0
  and draw differently. A reference by name is kept on another page too, where it names a peer of that name if there is one. **A member copied alone** (copy, cut or duplicate, pasted on any page) **leaves its
  groups: a formula of it, or of anything in it, that names one of them is made the constant of its cached value too, on the same page as well** (the group may be taken apart or deleted, and a name of it
  would be a `#REF!` that is saved).
* Our rule (`draw/group.rs`): `shape.group` makes a shape of `Type` `Group` whose
  `Shapes` hold the shapes named, with an `ID` above every `ID` of the page. Its frame is the box that holds the members **in the frame they were in** (their four corners
  carried through their poses; for a connector its two ends and **nothing else**: a route that leaves the box of its ends is no part of the frame, as the `.odg` reader and writer have it), so that the group frame rule holds: its `PinX` and `PinY` are the middle of the box, its `Width`
  and `Height` the box's size, its `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` `Width*0.5` and `Height*0.5` (formulas, as a file's), and it holds no angle. It is linked to the root style (so it reads
  `DisplayMode` and the other group properties from it) and has no name (`NAME()` of an unnamed shape is `Sheet.<id>`). It stands in the list in the
  place of the topmost of its members, and the members keep the order they had. The shapes named must be in one list (the page's, or one group's members), two at least once a shape named with a
  group it is in is left to the group; a group goes no deeper than 90 levels (`draw::MAX_GROUP_DEPTH`: the model allows 200, but the reader of a file takes XML 200 elements deep and a level of group costs two, so a drawing nested past about 98 levels could be saved and not read again). Members are carried into the group's frame. Moving, turning, flipping and
  deleting a group carry its members (they are cells of its frame); a glue to a member is by `ID` and survives, and a connector inside the group glued to a shape outside it follows that
  shape (the formula carries its point into the group's frame).
  `shape.ungroup` takes apart each group named, **at most 50,000 members in all** (`draw::MAX_UNGROUP_MEMBERS`: the ids a command takes are capped at 4,096, but one id may name a group of any number of members, so the members are counted before
  any is handled, and more are refused in words) (a shape with no members is left alone; a group named with a group it is in is not taken apart in the same call): the members go to the list the group was
  in, in its place and in their order, carried out through the group's pose, which may be turned and flipped. The group's own cells, and its text, go with it. **A connector glued to
  the group itself is left with that end a plain point where it was** (for the group's shape), and no `Connect` row names a group that is gone. **A formula of a member (or of anything inside one) that names a group that is taken apart** (`Sheet.<group>!Width*0.25`) would be worked out against a shape that is not there and saved naming nothing: such a cell is made the constant of its cached value
  before the group goes (a name of a group that stays is kept). A shape outside the group that names it is another matter, as after a delete: it reads a shape that is not there. A group that is an instance of a master
  (`Master` on it), or part of one (`MasterShape` and no `Master`), is refused: its members take their cells from the master through it, and the names in their inherited formulas are bound by the instance, so they would lose them on the page. Copy refuses the same loss.
* Our rule (`draw/refit.rs`, called by `Session::edit_if`). **After an edit of a member, a group that was the box of its members is that box again, in the step of the edit.** The session's group went on being the box of its members as they were: its selection box was the old one, and a new group saved to a `.odg` was refused ("a new group whose frame is not the box of its members"); the saved file of a group the file held was right, since the reader makes that frame from the members. Now every group any of whose members the edit changed (a move, a resize, a turn or a flip, a text that sizes it, a member taken away or added, at any depth, the groups inside first) has `Width` and `Height` the size of the box of its members, its pin at the middle of the box and `LocPinX`, `LocPinY` half the size (a pin given by `Width*0.5` keeps its formula); the members stay where they are on the page, their cells carried into the new frame by a move of minus the box's lower-left corner (the cached values of a glued end are carried and the formulas left, a connector's route is not made again), and a member's tie to the group's size is written again for the new size, so that it keeps its own number (a factor no size can reach holds the number as a value). A turned or flipped group is refitted in its own frame: the new pin is where its pose carries the middle of the new box. Undo and redo hold the group and its members in one step. **A group fits its members, as the reference application's own recalculation of a group's selection rectangle does, and a group whose `LockCalcWH` is set is left as it is** (its documented meaning; no word is said of it): a margin someone gave a group (a frame wider than its members) is lost once the members fill the frame by an edit, which is accepted. **Left alone, with no error:** a group that was **not** the box of its members before the edit (a frame a file chose, or one a person set: a frame is kept up and never imposed); a group with `LockCalcWH` set; a group none of whose members the edit changed (its own cells were the edit: a move, a turn, a size); a group with a formula in `Width`, `Height`, `PinX` or `PinY`, or in `LocPinX` or `LocPinY` that is not half the size; a group with a member whose pin has a formula of its own that is no tie to the group, or a connector with an end that is not glued and has one (a formula is data); a group with no member left; a box that is not a number or is past `MAX_COORD`. A member counts as changed for its group only when a cell that gives its box differs (a pin, a size, a turn, a flip, the ends of a line, the rows of its geometry), so a new colour or a text that leaves the size as it was refits nothing. **One box rule.** A one-dimensional member counts by its two ends and nothing else, in `shape.group`, in the "was the box" gate of the refit, in the refit itself and in the `.odg` writer, so that the refit never disagrees with a reopen of the saved file (a group of two rectangles and the connector between them is the box of the two rectangles, whatever route the connector goes). **The group's own constant points stay where they are on the page:** the constant `X`, `Y` (and `A`, `B`, `C`, `D`) of its `Geometry` rows, its `TxtPinX` and `TxtPinY`, and the `X` and `Y` of its `Connection` and `Controls` rows are moved by the translation the members are, a cell with a formula (it follows `Width` and `Height`) and a cell the group inherits are left (**an assumption to check** against the reference application, in the check list). **The connectors glued to a group whose frame moved are routed again**, with what the commit left of the edit's evaluator budget and **at the most 500 tries for the whole edit** (`draw::MAX_REROUTED`: a try that comes back with no route is a try); the others keep the route they had, their ends follow the frame, and each is said. **Left as they were, and said (revised):** a group the rule asks to refit that cannot be: it has more members than what is left of the 10,000 an edit may read **in all** (`draw::MAX_REFIT_MEMBERS`, the larger of the members a group had and has, counted for each group the edit changed: a file's group of 175,000 members is not rewritten for the edit of one of them, which would cost the memory of a whole write), its own points are more than what is left of the 20,000 rows an edit may carry in all (`draw::MAX_OWN_POINT_ROWS`; a row weighs the points it holds, and a group a `.odg` is read into has four; they are counted before a row is read), its refit failed (said, not swallowed), or the one budget of the edit (`calc::Budget`, shared by the pages) does not cover the commit of the page's refits (the page's groups are then kept whole or not at all). The edit stands, the group keeps the frame it had, **and the answer of the command lists it**: `groups_left`, each `{page, id, kind, why}` (`kind` is `group`, or `connector` for a connector that was not routed again, by a refit or past an edit's tries), the first 100 (`draw::MAX_LEFT_LISTED`) with `groups_left_unlisted` counting the others. **A group the session made and the file has never held is left and listed as any other is, and the edit is made.** (It was refused in words, "group N was not fitted to its members (why), and the file does not hold it", because the `.odg` writer refused such a group at every save while its frame was not its members' box; the writer writes a group whose frame is not the box as its members and says so in a note now, so the refusal's reason is gone and it only stopped the edit.) **There is no cap on the groups an edit changes**; every allowance above is **one for the whole edit**, summed over its groups and its pages (a cap for each group would be that cap times the number of groups), and each group read costs at least one member of the 10,000. **The history is held to the bytes it holds as well as to its steps** (`history.rs`): each step is charged what it keeps that the next version does not (a member moved out of a group of 5,000 leaves a step priced at about 41 MB in the unit of the save's gate; the review measured about 20 MB of address space for each, and 137 of them aborted a process of 3 GB), the price counts **a text part at what a part is** (a `TextPart` of 48 bytes, and its text behind an `Arc`: a text of 100,000 one-letter runs is a vector of 4.8 MB that each edit of it leaves behind, which a price of a byte a part let 560 edits hold 2.7 GB under a history that said 48 MiB; the review's input, 100,000 adjacent runs and 700 range edits, now ends under `ulimit -v 3000000` with 30 steps kept and 180 MB held), and the **sheet of a page, the sheet of the document, the styles, the masters, the themes (their bytes), the colour and font tables, the title and the properties** when a step replaces them (a page's sheet at the structural caps is 0.76 MB that each `page.size` leaves behind), the oldest steps are let go while the steps of a direction hold more than 192 MiB (the newest step is always kept), and an undo past the steps that are left is **refused in words**: "the older steps were let go to keep the history within 192 MiB, and nothing before them can be undone" (the command `edit.undo` is disabled with those words; likewise `edit.redo`). The figure is the 3 GB a drawing is held to, less the model, a save's second model and the render: 2 x 192 MiB is 384 MiB of the 1.96 GiB the process holds in its worst case (module doc of `history.rs`). **Cost** (counted by the unit tests of `draw/refit.rs`; the real time, measured on beast on 2026-11-20 under a load of 31 to 35: **0.5 s** for a member moved out of a group of 10,000 members, the allowance (0.19 to 0.32 s at 5,000, and 1.2 to 1.4 s at 20,000, which is why the allowance is 10,000), and 1.6 s for 3,000 leaves moved in one command across 9,000 groups, of which 5,000 are refitted and the rest said; an `#[ignore]` test of `tests/it/group_frame.rs` prints the times of a size): the shapes of the lists the edit changed are compared by the pointer each is behind, a group costs a pass over its members for the box it had and another for the box it has, **the glued ends of the page are found once for the page** (one pass over its `Connect` rows, whatever the number of groups), and the cells that read what the refits wrote are worked out **in one commit for the page, within one budget for the whole edit**: a member 190 groups deep and a thousand sibling groups with 1,500 connectors glued to their members cost 5,572 steps of work, one pass over the 3,000 `Connect` rows, one commit and about 138 evaluator steps a group beside the 27,000 of the commit itself; the same page in a child under `ulimit -v 3000000` ends as a move (`tests/it/group_frame.rs`), and so do, in children of their own: 40 moves of a member of a group of 5,000 (the history kept to its bytes, the undo past it refused in words), a group with 100,000 rows of its own (left and said before a row is read), 3,000 chains of three groups moved at once (the allowances reached and the rest said), and 5,000 connectors glued to a refitted group (500 tried, the rest said). The tests of `draw/refit.rs` hold the work to a count of each of the three allowances, summed over groups and over pages. **The window** reads the frame (the selection box and the hit test of a group are the cells' `Width`, `Height`, pin and local pin: `ui-egui` `Scene`), so the box shown after a member's edit is the members', asserted without a window in `scene.rs`. **Not changed:** a new group that holds a member turned by another angle than a quarter is still refused by the `.odg` writer, with or without this rule (the file holds an angle to nine places and a cell thirteen, and the writer holds the box to a trillionth of an inch); `shape.place` of a group and of one of its members together puts both pins at the place and then the group is its members' box again, so its pin is the middle of that box. Behaviour of the reference application on a member's edit in a group is **unverified** (no specification says; recorded as a check row for when the reference application is available).
* Our rule (`draw/lift.rs`): to move a shape from one parent to another so that it draws where it did, its cells are carried by the map from the old
  parent's frame to the new one (a turn, perhaps a mirror, and a move): the pin goes through the map; `Angle` becomes the map's angle plus the shape's own, **minus** its own under a mirror (a
  mirror turns the other way round); `FlipX` and `FlipY` are the shape's flips and the map's, each taken as one flip (a map that negates both is a half turn). Group is a move by minus the box's
  corner, ungroup the group's pose, and a member copied alone the poses of its groups. A one-dimensional shape has its ends that are not glued carried through the map, and the cached value of a
  glued end too (under the `Evaluation` origin, so the formula stays), so that an end `calc` cannot work out (`_WALKGLUE`) is where the file had it in the new frame; a map that mirrors is refused for a
  connector (its `Begin` and `End` are fixed by its frame, which a mirror would turn about). The cells written are written as a drag writes them. Text is not mirrored and `TxtAngle` is not touched
  **A cell of a member whose formula names, by `Sheet.<id>!`, the group that is taken apart is made the constant of its cached value** (error cleared, guard lost, a cell with no cached value left as it is) before the group goes (the cell is worked out against nothing otherwise, and saved naming nothing); this reaches the shapes
  inside a member as well, and not the names of a group that stays. The picture of the page is the same after a group, an ungroup and a paste with no offset, which the tests compare by pixels.
* (ours where the samples say otherwise; `draw/local.rs`):
  **a member that is an instance carries its master's `Geometry` itself, and holds its size and pin as formulas of its group.** The two are
  separate and both are written. An instance made by a drop holds only what differs (its pin), which is right on a page. **A reader that does not know the master draws an instance nested in a group
  from the geometry the instance holds, and with none it draws nothing** (LibreOffice, in the black-box checks made with and again: a group of instances saved with no copy shows none of them, with
  the copy it shows them all, and so for the listed members of a dropped group master). The owner chose to keep LibreOffice drawing grouped stencil shapes. So when an instance becomes a member,
  `shape.group` gives each shape it names that is an instance (or stands for a shape of a master by `MasterShape`) a local copy of every `Geometry` section it has once its master is put together with it
  (the sections by `IX`, the rows by `IX` with their `T`, the cells of each), and **the members of a dropped group master are given the same when they are listed**. The instance
  of a master on the page, the group made, a shape that inherits no geometry (the group instance of a master of several shapes) and a group's members that were members already are not written to; an ungroup, a copy
  and a paste leave the cells as they are.
  **Against the samples.** R16 counted what files hold on nested instances whose master shape has a `Geometry` section: those whose `Width` or `Height` differs from the master shape's hold a section of their own in
  14,336 of 14,506 (143 files, 46 sources), and those at the master shape's size in 11 of 114 (3, 3); for members of an instance 37,669 of 50,796 and 1,789 of 69,072. Where a section is held it holds only `Inh` cells in
  13,918 of 14,347 (instances) and 39,277 of 39,458 (members). This reading is the inheritance rule at work: a resized shape's geometry formulas give new results, so their cells are written as `Inh` with the new value,
  and a shape at its master's size has none to write. **So the copy writes sections that 103 of the 114 unresized samples lack, and constants for the master's plain values, and `Inh` cells whose results equal
  the master's, which the project rule never adds; whether the reference app minds is `unknown`.** This is a choice for the readers that are not the reference app, and the check list says so. **Resized** is the definition:
  the instance's (or the member's) `Width` or `Height`, found by inheritance, differs from its master shape's. Two sizes are equal within a millionth of the larger, or within 1E-6 when both are under 1
  (the tolerance the specifications count equal numbers by; R16 gives none of its own). The tests classify by it (`is_resized` in `tests/it/group_geometry.rs`). **That 1E-6 is not the threshold
  of the cells an instance holds.** `calc` adds an `Inh` cell when a result differs from the master's by more than 1E-9 of the larger of 1 and the two (`ORACLE_TOLERANCE`), so an instance made 1E-7 wider
  than its master shape is "at its size" by R24 and holds the cells `calc` gave it (asserted in `an_instance_is_resized_when_its_width_or_its_height_is_not_its_master_shapes`), and an instance at exactly its
  master's size holds none until it is grouped.
  **A cell is held as the files hold one on an instance** (`Inh` on 507,641 cells, a local formula on 34,533, and 1,203,151 left to inheritance): where the master has a formula the cell is
  `F="Inh"` with the result it has on this shape as its `V` (the formula is not copied, R26, and what a formula names by `Sheet.<id>` is bound by the instance), and where the master holds a plain value the cell is a
  local constant with that value and its unit. A cell with no value and no formula is not written. **Only what is missing is written** (a local row may hold fewer cells than the master's, `Del` rows and
  sections hide the master's): a section, a row or a cell the shape holds already (an override, a row it hides, a row of its own, **the `Inh` cells its resize gave it**, a copy a file or an earlier version made) is
  left as it is, a row or section it hides stays hidden, a row it lacks gets the master's `T`, and rows of a section the shape had stay in `IX` order. The effective rows are therefore unchanged and a reader that does not
  know the master has all of them; **the drawing does not change** (tested by pixels, for a group of instances of both built-in stencils, a group nested in a group, a turned and flipped group grouped again, a group of
  instances that hold geometry of their own, one that was resized, a resize afterwards, a connector glued to a member, undo and a save that is read back).
  **What a later edit of the master does:** the shape now holds the rows and the plain values itself, so **a number the master changes in a cell with no formula no longer reaches it**, and a row the master drops stays; the
  formula of a cell it inherits still does (the cell finds it on the master and is worked out again on the shape), and **a row the master gains does reach it**, since the shape's section is merged with the master's by `IX`
  Nothing edits a master yet.
  **The work is bounded**: one command copies at most `draw::MAX_LOCALISED_WORK` = 1,000,000 sections, rows and cells in all (counted, over the master's `Geometry` and what the shapes hold, before anything is written; the
  largest `Geometry` sections of real files and a hundred shapes of 600 rows are well within it), and a `shape.group` that would copy more **is refused in words and changes nothing**; a drop of a group
  master whose members would hold more lists them without the copy and is not refused. Plans are made 50,000 units at a time. One undo step (the copy is made in the group's own step, and a drop's), (no `ID`
  is given or changed), no route is made, no end placed and byte-identical untouched parts on save hold: only the shapes named are written to.
* Our rule where the specification gives no text; `draw/nested.rs`: **a member that is an instance holds
  its `Width`, `Height`, `PinX` and `PinY` as formulas of its group.** R50 counted what nested shapes hold: an instance in a group (`Master`) holds them as local formulas that refer to the enclosing group's own sheet
  by its `ID` in 14,765 (`Width`), 14,596 (`Height`), 14,777 (`PinX`) and 14,728 (`PinY`) of 16,020 shapes (155 to 157 files, 49 sources), and a member of an instance (`MasterShape` alone) in 6,022 to 6,232 of
  141,440 (127 to 145 files, 20 to 29 sources); their `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` are mostly `Inh` or absent. `shape.group` writes the four cells to each shape it makes a member that is an instance, or stands for a
  master shape (`Resolver::master_step` finds one). **The text is not in the specifications** (R50 gives kinds, never text): the form is the one gives for a formula that scales with a shape,
  `Sheet.<id>!Width` or `Sheet.<id>!Height` times a constant. `Width` and `PinX` are `Sheet.<group>!Width*<factor>`, `Height` and `PinY` are `Sheet.<group>!Height*<factor>`, the factor being the cell's number over
  the group's `Width` or `Height` in the shortest decimal that gives the number back (never an exponent, and a pin at the box's edge is `*0`). The group's frame has the box's lower-left corner for its origin
  So a pin's factor is its place in the box. The cell keeps the number it held as its `V`, so nothing that reads it has a new result and **the picture is the same** (tested by pixels), and its unit
  (`IN` when it had none). **A group resized moves and sizes its members by the formulas** (tested: the cells of three members, their inherited local pins, and the pixels equal to those of the same shapes
  resized one by one; a group instance made a member scales its own members, which inherit formulas of it from their master). No command resizes a group (`shape.resize` and `shape.size` refuse one): an edit
  of the group's cells does (a write to its `Width` or `Height`, or a file's own).
  **Where R50 gives no form the value is kept:** (a) a one-dimensional instance: its size and pin are formulas of its ends, and the counts are not split by kind; (b) `Width` and `Height` of
  a member turned by other than a half turn: its width lies along the group's height, or between, and no count says which the files tie it to (its `PinX` and `PinY` are tied). **The samples point the other way:** R50
  counts a plain value for `Width` on 1 and for `Height` on 53 of 16,020 instances in a group, while 997 of them hold an `Angle`, so a turned instance almost always holds a formula of its group for its size too, and
  what is unknown is which axis it follows, not whether it follows; (c) a cell that holds a formula of
  its own, which is data the file holds, **but for a formula of exactly the form written here that names the group the member has left** (a member grouped inside a group: it would go on following a group it is no
  longer in, so it follows the new one); (d) a cell with no number anywhere, a group with no extent (under 1E-9 inch) along the cell's axis, and a factor outside 1E-9 to 1,000,000 (hostile numbers: the formula
  stays short); (e) a guarded cell. A shape with no master is not touched: **a plain group inside a group, and the shapes with no master, do not follow a resize of the group** (R50 counts instances). The group instance of a
  master of several shapes, which inherits from no one shape, is an instance (`Master`) and is tied. **Nothing else is written by this rule**: not `LocPinX` or `LocPinY` (the project rule leaves an inherited cell to
  the evaluation that changes its result), and the geometry is copied by the group geometry rule.
  **The members of a dropped master**. The members of a master of one group shape inherit the formulas of their master shapes and hold no size or pin, as R9 says and the majority
  shows (absent or `Inh` on 94% of the members of an instance); they hold the copy of their master shapes' geometry. The members of a master of several shapes, whose frame is made here, hold the four cells as formulas of the group instance, as the 6,022 to 6,232
  members do (our reading: they are the members of masters of several shapes, whose members' pins cannot be the master shapes' own; no count says so); a member that is a line keeps its end cells as numbers.
  **Ungroup, copy and paste.** An ungroup makes the formulas that name the group the constants of their cached values, **and when the group taken apart was a member of a group, the instances it
  leaves there are tied to that group** as `shape.group` ties the members it makes, with the numbers they have (a group made of shapes with no master leaves no one tied: the case above). A copy of a group renames the
  formulas of its members to the copy. **A member copied, cut or duplicated alone** is a shape of the page when it is pasted, and **a formula of it, or of anything in it, that names a group it leaves
  is made the constant of its cached value**, as an ungroup makes it (`draw/clipboard.rs`: the groups above it are noted when it is copied, so that taking the original group apart, or deleting it, leaves the
  copy with no `#REF!` and no name of a shape that is not its group). The copy then holds numbers for the four cells and the
  geometry the member held, and is tied again when it is grouped. On another page every cell that names a shape not copied is its number, as before.
  **The work**: a fixed number of lookups for each member and each of its four cells, in one write batch (counted by the work counter of `draw/nested.rs`: five for each instance, 500 for 100 and 2,000 for 400, and
  six for each level of a group made sixty levels deep, whatever the depth; the recalculation of grouping 250 and 1,000 instances takes steps in the ratio of the instances, 3.5 to 4.5 for 4, in the same number of commits).
* Our rule (`draw/settle.rs`): after a paste, a group or an ungroup, **no route is made again and no end is placed by the rule for an end glued to a shape**. The shapes were carried together and every
  cell that holds a place with them, so the ends are where they were; the rows of a route are in the connector's own frame, which does not change, so a connector of a file keeps its own route and
  the place its own application chose for a `_WALKGLUE` end. What is worked out afterwards is the cells written, the pose cells of the shapes whose parent changed and the cells of theirs that call
  `PAR`, `LOC` and the like, with what reads them (`calc::commit_changed`); a cell nothing changed keeps the value the file saved. This is also why a copy of a connector of ours that lands in
  another frame (a member copied out of a turned group) keeps the legs it drew with on the page, slanted if its group was turned: it draws where it did.
* A connector glued to a deleted shape stays, with that end a plain point where it was and its `Connect` row gone.
* The window: the Delete key names the connectors glued to the selection in the same `shape.delete`, because
  Delete "removes the selection along with connectors attached to it". The command alone keeps, so a
  program that wants a connector left with a free end still gets one. One undo step brings the shapes and their connectors back. **On a Mac, Backspace
  deletes the selection too** (ours; `ui-egui/src/shortcuts.rs` `MAC_ALSO`): the key a Mac keyboard marks delete sends Backspace, and Delete is
  Fn+Delete there (Forward Delete), which still deletes. It acts only while no text is being edited: with a shape's text or any other text field open,
  Backspace deletes a character. Windows, Linux and FreeBSD keep Delete alone, the key the shortcut list names, and there Backspace does nothing to the
  shapes selected.
* With no `point`, the pair of connection points (one on each shape) with the least distance between them; with one end free,
  the point nearest it; two ends on one shape do not take the same point while there is a choice.
* **A point already held** (`draw/connectors.rs`): a point that is the nearest and that another connector already holds, for an
  end whose `point` was not named, is given up for the free point on the same side of the shape (the same way out, snapped to an axis) that is
  nearest the other end, when there is one. Two connectors between the same shapes then leave by different points and do not share a leg. With
  none free on that side, the nearest stays and the legs are shared; a point that is named is the point. A connector being moved does not count
  its own place as taken. It governs `connector.add` and `connector.reconnect`, and nothing a file holds.
* Replaced by: it said only a connector with `User.DiaCraftRoute` has its rows rewritten, which left the
  connectors of a file with the route they had when a shape moved away.
* `draw/reroute.rs`: a command that moves a connector's end routes it again by our routing rule when the
  connector has a glued end (a `Connect` row on an end cell), whether DiaCraft made it or a file did, and its route is not fixed. A connector of
  ours that the command is about (`connector.add`, `connector.reconnect`) or that follows a shape the command changed is routed again whatever
  moved; a connector of a file only if an end cell now differs from what the command found, so that a file's connector with no glued end, or
  whose glued ends did not move, keeps its rows. A shape inside a group that is moved counts as moved. The rows replace those of `Geometry`
  section 0 (the section's own cells are kept, and rows the connector inherits from its master that the new route does not use are marked `Del`);
  every other cell of the connector stays, but for its label (and, for a connector of ours whose path was edited by hand, `ConFixedCode` 3, which goes back to 0). The route, the label and the ends it follows are one
  undo step with the move. A connector of a file whose end cells hold an error is left as it is, and does not stop its shape moving. **A connector inside a shape the command changed** (a group that is moved, turned or flipped) is a follower too, whatever it is glued to: its
frame changed, and an end glued to a shape outside the group is somewhere else in it; this holds for the ends that rule places as well. **A connector the command names itself is not inside a shape it changed**: a file's connector with one end glued to the whole of a shape (`_WALKGLUE`) and the other free, sent to `shape.rotate`, `shape.flip`, `shape.move`, `shape.align` or `connector.reconnect`, keeps its glued end where the file put it (ROTATE-1D-1), and `angle` is reached exactly; only a command that changes the shape it is glued to, or a group it is inside, places the end.
* A route that is fixed is not written, and only the connector's ends move, by their formulas. A route is fixed when
  (1) the page's `DynamicsOff` is not 0, for a connector that follows a shape (one the command is about is still
  routed, and clearing the cell reroutes nothing); (2) the connector's `ConFixedCode` is 1, 2 or 3 (0 reroutes freely,
  and so do 4, 5 and 6, which files hold for whole-shape glue, and any other number; before a change any number but 0 fixed a route, which kept the connectors of the files that hold 4, 5 or 6 in a cell of their own from being routed again when their shapes moved; the count: counted over every shape that is the `FromSheet` of a `Connect` row, at any depth, in the 2,504 readable sample packages, it is 2,950, which is 2,842 top-level connectors and 108 nested ones); a route fixed by 1 or 3 is made
  again when a leg of it would pass through a shape the connector is glued to, and keeps its setting (a path edited by hand writes 3); (3) for a connector of a file, its effective route style is
  centre to centre (16, which aims along the line between the shapes' centres, where the glue's places do not give one). A straight connector (`RouteStyle` 2) and a curved one
  (`LineRouteExt` 2) are routed again, straight and curved, and a connector DiaCraft made follows its style like any other; before, both were fixed and DiaCraft's own was always
  right-angle. A command *about* a connector can still route it when it is fixed: `connector.style` and `connector.reset` (`settle_forced`) route whatever else holds.
* Our rule (`draw/settle.rs`): **an edit routes at most 10,000 connectors again** (`draw::MAX_ROUTED_PER_EDIT`), in all, however many times it routes and on whatever pages, besides the 500 that a refit of a group routes (`MAX_REROUTED`). A try counts whatever comes of it: a route that comes back as none, and the test of a fixed route for a crossing. The connectors past it are the last in the order of their `ID`s; their ends follow the shapes they are glued to, by their formulas, their routes stay as they were, and **the command's answer lists them** in `groups_left` (`kind` `connector`, "it was not routed again (an edit routes 10,000 connectors again at the most): its ends follow the shapes they are glued to, its route is the one it had"), the first 100 with `groups_left_unlisted` counting the others. A library call that no session makes has the 10,000 for each call and says nothing. The specifications say nothing of what the reference application does with very many connectors, so the number is ours: 10,000 routings cost about what the recalculation of their ends costs ("What it costs"). **What a routing reads:** the glue of the ends of every connector an edit routes, in one pass over the page's `Connect` rows; the connectors and the shapes their ends are glued to, found with the edit's other shapes (no search of the page); the connection points of each glued shape, once for the edit, within `MAX_CROSSING_POINT_ROWS` (65,536) rows for all of them, a shape past it giving its ends no direction of their own; and the routes and labels of all of them are worked out on one reading of the page, then written (a route reads its connector, the page's cells and the shapes its ends are glued to, which no other route or label writes).
* `draw/glue.rs`, the one place: an end glued to the whole of a shape (`ToPart` 3, or a connection point whose
  cells `calc` cannot work out) is put by the engine, when a command changes its shape or the connector's other shape, at the connection point of
  the shape nearest the connector's other end; a shape with no connection point has its outline for the place, and the end goes to the point of it
  nearest the other end. When both ends are glued to the whole of a shape, the pair of places, one on each, with the least distance between them is
  taken (the outline point being the one nearest the middle of the other shape's box). The way the route leaves it is the connection point's, or the
  outward normal of the side of the box it is on, at a corner the side that faces the other end. An end whose cells `calc` can work out, such as
  `PAR(PNT(Sheet.n!PinX,Sheet.n!PinY))`, is `calc`'s, and the engine does nothing to it. The place is written as the cached value of both end
  cells under the `Evaluation` origin; the formula, `BegTrigger` and `EndTrigger` are as the file had them, and what reads the ends is worked out
  after. An end of a connector read from an `.odg` glued to the whole of a shape holds plain values, and is placed although the connector carries `User.DiaCraftRoute` (  it stayed where the file had it),
  by the rule **An `.odg` end glued to a whole shape** below and not by this rule. Ends are placed even while the page's `DynamicsOff` is 1, which only stops reroutes. The connection points of the shapes the ends are glued to are read once for the edit, within `MAX_CROSSING_POINT_ROWS` (65,536) rows for all of them (they were read for each end, and 10,000 ends on a shape of 10,000 points did not finish in a quarter of an hour); a shape past them is placed on as a shape with no connection point is. The places are on the page and the end cells are in the frame of the connector's parent, so a connector in a group has the place carried into the group's frame. A connector is placed when a shape it is glued to is changed or when it is **strictly inside** a shape the command changed, not when the command names the connector itself.
* **An `.odg` end glued to a whole shape** (`draw/odg_glue.rs`; LibreOffice 26.8.2.2 measured as a black box): an end of an `.odg` connector (one the `.odg` reader read, which carries
  `User.DiaCraftRoute`) glued to the whole of a shape goes where LibreOffice puts it, since LibreOffice places such an end itself whenever it loads the file: at one of the
  shape's four standard points (rows 0 to 3 as the reader made them), never at a point the shape lists of its own. A `standard` or `curve` connector takes the pair of points
  whose right-angled route is best: not across a gap narrower than 1.02 cm, then keeping 0.5 cm from both shapes, then the shortest, a route of one bend counted 1.5 cm
  longer and one that leaves a shape by less than 0.51 cm 1.5 cm longer again; a `line` connector the pair with the least of the distance along the axes from the start's
  point to the middle of the end shape and from the end's point to the start's, and a `lines` connector the same from the points 0.51 cm out. With the other end glued to a
  point, the whole end takes the point the same rules pick beside it; with it free, a standard connector takes the nearest point that the free end lies 0.51 cm beyond, and
  a straight one the nearest. Where the reference application's dynamic glue and LibreOffice differ, the `.odg` follows LibreOffice's, since LibreOffice draws the file;
  The rule for an end glued to a shape stays the rule for every other connector.
* Our rule: the outline of a shape, is the boundary of its box (`Width` by `Height` in its own frame, carried to the
  page through its poses and its groups'). `ShapeFixedCode` is documented as a flag that makes a connector glue to a shape's alignment box in place
  of its perimeter, so a box is a place the reference app glues to; the drawn curve of a round or slanted shape is not followed.
* `draw/label.rs`, a project rule: a connector's label stays upright (`TxtAngle` is minus the connector's `Angle`, or
  the angle itself under one flip), is centred on the route's middle leg (of an odd number of legs the middle
  one, of an even number the longer of the two in the middle, the first if they are as long; `TxtPinX` and `TxtPinY` are the middle of that leg in
  the connector's frame, and `TxtLocPinX` and `TxtLocPinY` the middle of the text block), and has a text background that hides the line
  under it (`TextBkgnd` `#FFFFFF`, painted as a rectangle hugging the text; a background the connector already has is left). It is written
  for a connector of ours whenever it is routed, and for a connector of a file only when routes it again and it has text: the text
  cells of any other connector of a file are not touched. A cell that already holds the value is not written, and a guarded cell stays.
* What a command recalculates is what it changed and what reads it, as above; no specification says which cells of
  a drawing from another application a move may rewrite, and rewriting only those that the move can have changed leaves the file's own
  values where nothing changed them. The frame functions' reads (`PAR` and the others) and the `Connect` rows are followed as dependencies
  because that is how a connector follows its shape.
* A route that would double back is replaced by a bridge between the two standoff points, with the shortest
  standoffs that keep it clear of itself; which side a detour goes round is the first of equal cost, and fixed. Ends at the same point are a
  connector of no length.
* **Round the glued shapes** (`draw/route_clear.rs`): **a route leaves each end the way its connection point faces and goes round the shapes it is glued to.** The way a point faces is its `DirX` and
  `DirY` carried through the shape's turns, flips and groups to the page (`draw::connection_points`, which a flip or a turn of the shape changes with the point), snapped to an axis; a point with none leaves
  the way rule 1 gives. Rules 1 to 6 know the ends and not the shapes under them, so a flip that carries a point to the far side of its shape from the other end made them lay a leg across the shape, or hook over
  its top by a standoff, which is across it (the second slice's screenshot 12). Now the two boxes the ends stand on (carried through the shape's turns: a quadrilateral and not its holding
  box) are known. A route of rules 1 to 6 that no leg of enters a box is kept. Otherwise the route is **the shortest way round**: out of each end by a standoff, then three middle legs (across, along, across, or
  the reverse) whose lines are chosen from the middle between the two standoff points, each standoff point's own line, a standoff past each box's edges and past both standoff points; the middle legs keep an eighth of an
  inch from the boxes (the specifications' `LineToNode`), the first and the last leg stand on their boxes; the price is the length and half an inch for each bend (the same as rule 6), and the first of
  equal prices wins, in a fixed order. A way round with a flaw (a leg doubling back, two legs over each other) is not a candidate. An end inside its own box (a point at the middle of a shape), or leaving it inwards,
  has no box to leave and that box is no obstacle; when no way round is clear (the shapes overlap) the route of rules 1 to 6 stands. Only the two boxes are obstacles: **other shapes are still gone through**
  (full routing is later). The curve of a curved connector is drawn through the route, so it follows this too.
* A guarded cell (`GUARD`) that a command would write is an error, and nothing changes (an automation write is
  reported to the caller). The exception is a label cell, which is left as it is.
* The owner's report of 2026-11-21; `diacraft_render::print::PrintColor`, `render/src/gray.rs`, `color::luma`: the Print view's **Black and white** and `file.print {color: grayscale}` draw every colour of the print as **its grey**, the luma `0.299 R + 0.587 G + 0.114 B` (the weights of ITU-R BT.601 on the sRGB-encoded values, the ones the theme transform `Gray` uses), in thousandths and rounded half up, with alpha kept: fills, lines, gradients, patterns, shadows, text, pictures (pixel by pixel) and the colours a theme gives alike. It is a plain grayscale and not pure black on white: names the choice and says nothing of how it is drawn, and nothing in the specs or the engine tells black and white from grayscale (the PDF options' **Color as black** is another setting, not built). The preview draws the sheet again in greys the moment the choice changes, and the PDF a printer is given and the one Save as PDF writes have the same greys; a job to a printer also asks the spooler for monochrome. Black and white is chosen for Save as PDF as for a printer. In the PDF a blurred shadow is drawn sharp, as it always is there.

## Connectors: AutoConnect, hand edits, styles and jumps

The commands `connector.autoconnect`, `connector.style`, `connector.edit_path`, `connector.reset` and `connector.line_jumps`, and the routing fix (above). The reference app was not
inspected, and our output has not been opened in it by the project: every rule below is **ours**, built on the specifications it names,
 and is listed for the check list. The code is `draw/autoconnect.rs`, `style.rs`, `hand.rs`, `jumps.rs`, `jump_settings.rs`, `rows.rs` and `route_clear.rs`; the commands
`cmd/autoconnect.rs` and `cmd/path.rs`.

* **AutoConnect** ("hovering a shape shows four arrows; hovering an arrow offers up to four shapes with a preview; choosing one adds and connects it in that direction"; "a master
  dragged from the Shapes window onto a shape's arrow is placed and connected"). `connector.autoconnect {from, direction, master?, stencil?}` makes the new shape by the functions the
  window's drop and duplicate use (`shape.drop`'s `drop_shape_above` and `stencil::drop_opened` for a `master` of a built-in stencil or of an opened file, `shape.duplicate`'s copy when no `master` is named: the copy
  has the look and the size of the shape and **not its text**, since a shape made by an arrow is made to be written in; a group is copied whole), then moves it beside the shape: **in the direction of the
  arrow, its centre in line with the shape's, a gap between the two boxes** (a shape's box on the page, a turned shape's being the box that holds it) of the page's `AvenueSizeX` (right and left) or
  `AvenueSizeY` (up and down), which a published account has AutoConnect use (`community-report`), and three quarters of an inch where the page holds none. A connector
  is glued from the source's connection point that **faces that way on the page** (its direction through the shape's turns and flips; the nearest to the middle of that side if several face it) to the new
  shape's point that faces back, so a flipped shape's right arrow uses the point that is on its right now; where a shape has no point facing so, the nearest pair is taken. The connector is glued
  by point, as `connector.add` does (`_WALKGLUE` is never written, though the application's AutoConnect makes dynamic glue), takes the page's style, and is routed.
  A shape with no connection point (a group) is refused as `connector.add` refuses it, and nothing is left on the page. A place that another shape of the page already holds (the first
  5,000 shapes are looked at) is slid along to the next free one on the perpendicular, a shape's size and a gap each time, alternately to one side and the other, up to 8 times, so a second arrow on the same side
  makes a second shape beside the first and not on top of it; if all are taken the shape goes to the first place. One undo step (the shape, its move, the connector and, with Auto Size on, the growth of the page); the ids are above every id the session
  has given. `draw::autoconnect_centre` answers where the first place is for a shape of a size (for a window's preview). The window's flyout offers masters; which ones (Quick Shapes) is the window's.
  (ours; no specification says which shape an arrow that is clicked joins: has the arrows, the flyout and the drag, and "a click on an arrow" is
  the task's). **`existing: true`** is a click on an arrow: the shape joined is the **nearest neighbour** of `from` in the arrow's direction (`draw::neighbour`, which `connector.neighbour` answers): a top-level shape of the page,
  not a connector, a guide or `from`, that lies **beyond that side of `from`'s box** (its near side no nearer than the side) and **shares some of its extent across it** (the two boxes' ranges along the other axis overlap by
  more than nothing: a shape that is only diagonal is not "to the right"), the nearest first (a tie goes to the one whose centre is nearer the line through `from`'s middle, then to the lower `ID`), **that has a connection point** (a
  group has none, and is passed over). **When the nearest such shape is joined to `from` already** by a connector, either way round (the `Connect` rows of the page, the first 200,000 looked at), **there is no neighbour**: the click
  makes a copy as before, so a second click on the same arrow makes the next shape beside the first and never a second connector between the same two shapes (the shapes beyond are not looked at). With a neighbour nothing moves, no shape is
  made, and the connector is glued from `from`'s point that faces the arrow to the neighbour's nearest point, by the page's style and routed. **`to`** is a drag from an arrow let go on a shape: that shape, wherever it is,
  is joined the same way, at its `to_point` when one is named (`to_point` needs `to`); a connector, `from` itself, a shape that is not there, a point it has not and a shape with no connection point are refused in words, and `to`
  and `existing` do not go together, nor does either with `master` or `stencil` (they join a shape that is there, which makes none). `created` is false for both. One undo step, `AutoConnect`.
  Our rule: no specification gives the box a place in a file. The View tab's AutoConnect box is the **drawing's** (the support page calls it "a file-level
  option", Learn says the program-wide setting "is on by default", and no page says what a file that says nothing has): **a drawing that holds nothing has it on**, `autoconnect.set {on: false}` writes `<autoconnect on="0"/>` into the
  drawing's extension data (`Document::diacraft`, the namespaced data every format carries: VSDX's solution part, `.odg`'s DiaCraft document file, the model of a `.diacraft` file; `crates/engine/src/view.rs`), and on takes it out again,
  so a drawing whose box is on holds nothing of it and a file that never had it is the file it would have been. **The box is no undo step** (the box is a setting of View > Visual Aids, so the owner's "Unsaved, not undo" of applies to it as it does to Dynamic Grid and the other Snap & Glue options): `autoconnect.set` makes the drawing unsaved (`Session::edit_view`), and undo and redo carry the `autoconnect` child across (`view::carry_settings`), so a box turned off stays off through an undo of an edit. The Snap and Glue switches keep their own child of the data. The command works whatever the box says: the box governs the
  window's arrows only. The **program-wide** setting over it (File, Options, Advanced, Enable AutoConnect) is the window's own, on at first and not saved: see the window's paragraph below.
  **The window's AutoConnect (`crates/ui-egui/src/edit/autoconnect.rs`).** With the box on (and the program's setting), the Pointer Tool in use and nothing being dragged, **the pointer resting on a shape for 250 ms
  shows four arrows round it** (a small pale sky-blue triangle at the middle of each side of the box that holds it, a deeper blue on the one the pointer is on: the theme tokens `auto_arrow` and `auto_arrow_hot`, each 3:1 or more on the page and on the canvas in both themes; a shape that is selected has them a little farther off, clear of its handles and
  its rotation handle), on a shape (not a group or a connector) that has a connection point, and they stay while the pointer crosses from the shape to one of them. **A click on an arrow** (a press and a release within 4 points) is one
  `connector.autoconnect` with `existing: true`: the new shape is selected when one was made, the connector when a neighbour was joined. **A drag from an arrow** shows the rubber line from the middle of the side to the pointer
  (green over what it would join, the connection points marked as the Connector tool marks them) and is `connector.autoconnect` with `to` (and `to_point` when it was let go on a connection point) on release; **let go over bare page, or
  on the shape it began on, it makes nothing**, and the Glue switch does not matter (a command of its own, not a connector end let go); **the drag shows no words and no circle**: the desktop app has no circle handle beside a triangle and no "Glue to Shape" tip, which the connectors page describes under its Web tab for the reference app's web version (replaced took out the handle and the tip that had built from the other reading), and the desktop joins an arrow to a shape already on the page by this drag from the arrow itself. **The pointer resting on an arrow for 400 ms opens the flyout**: up to four masters (**the Quick Shapes of the stencil the Shapes window shows** that are no connector and no part of a structure, the first four of them, and the stencil's first four such masters when it has none: below; which offered the first four masters of the stencil, is replaced by it; and where the stencil feeds the strip nothing, Arrow Shapes or the Cross-Functional Flowchart Shapes, those of the first stencil of the window's list that feeds it: below), as a strip across
  the arrow's direction (a column beside a left or right arrow, a row beyond an up or down one: the preview of the shape stands in the arrow's direction, so a strip across it leaves it in view), each tile drawn as the Shapes window draws
  it; the tile the pointer is on shows **a preview on the page** of the shape where `draw::autoconnect_centre` (the engine's own place) puts it, the master's outline fitted to its size, with the line that would join it; a click on a tile is
  `connector.autoconnect` with its `master` (and `stencil` for a stencil file), the new shape selected. The flyout stays 250 ms after the pointer leaves the arrow and the flyout, goes with Escape and when a master is chosen, and is kept
  inside the drawing area. **A master dragged from the Shapes window onto an arrow** (the arrows show while the pointer is over the shape) is dropped the same way, but **a part of a structure** (a Swimlane, a Separator, a stencil file's container or list) **is dropped as a drop of it is and joined to nothing**. **Keys** (none is published: ours): with one shape
  selected, **Alt with an arrow key is a click on that arrow**, **Alt+Shift with an arrow key opens its flyout** with the first master current, the arrow keys move between the masters (right and down go on, left and up go back, the ends stay),
  Enter drops the current one and Escape closes the flyout; a flyout opened by the keyboard is not taken over by the pointer. **The View box** (Visual Aids, AutoConnect; window command `view.autoConnect {on?}`) shows the drawing's box
  and each press is one `autoconnect.set` (no undo step: the drawing is unsaved, and Ctrl+Z leaves the box as it was turned); it is greyed out, with the reason as its tip, **while the program-wide setting is off** (`window.enableAutoConnect {on?}`: on at first, not saved, the
  window's own; there is no Options page for it yet: the Options dialog has Customize Ribbon alone). `ui.inspect` tells `autoConnect` (`program`, `drawing`, `arrows`: the shape whose arrows show, `flyout`: its shape, side, masters and the
  one current), and `ui.elements` names `autoconnect.arrow.<side>` and `autoconnect.flyout`, `autoconnect.flyout.<n>`. **What is left:** the delays (250 and 400 ms) and the exact blues (the pictures show a pale sky blue and a deeper one, and R11 gives no value) are ours, the strip
  and the keys are ours, reordering stencils (which reorders their Quick Shapes) is not built, the arrows do not show on a touch tap
  And the Options page is not built.
* **Connector styles**. What a connector draws is read from `ShapeRouteStyle` and
  `ConLineRouteExt`, and the page's `RouteStyle` and `LineRouteExt` where the connector's is 0: style 2 is **straight** (the two ends joined by one leg that is level, upright or slanted), 16 is centre to
  centre (a file's connector of that style keeps its route; one DiaCraft made, or one a command is about, is routed straight), and any other style is right angle (flowchart, tree, network and the
  simple styles); appearance 2 on a style that is not straight is **curved**. `connector.style` with `ids` writes, on each, `ShapeRouteStyle` and `ConLineRouteExt` as 1 and 1 (right angle), 2
  and 1 (straight) or 1 and 2 (curved), **the reading of the spec's rule, since its open question says no source gives what the Connectors menu writes**, undoes a hand edit (`ConFixedCode` 3 becomes 0) and routes them again; with
  no `ids` it writes the page's `RouteStyle` and `LineRouteExt` the same way, which new connectors take and existing ones whose own cell is 0 take at their next route (the project rule), and
  **routes nothing again** (asks for a reroute and the window can ask for one by naming the connectors). A connector that has the style already and no hand edit is left alone, and a call that
  changes nothing is no undo step. A shape that is not a connector is refused in words. A curved connector is saved as a `MoveTo` and one `NURBSTo` row:
  the quadratic B-spline whose control polygon is the right-angle route, clamped, so it starts on the begin and ends on the end, tangent to the first and the last leg, and passes the middle of each leg of the
  polygon; the listed knots are the first `n + 1` of 0, 0, 0, 1, 2, ... for `n` control points (implies the rest), the weights 1, `E` is `NURBS(...)` in lengths (types 1, 1) with `U` `NURBS`. Ends
  in line are a `LineTo`. The curve cuts the corners of the polygon inside it; it does not avoid shapes, and the route it is drawn through goes round the glued shapes (rule 7).
* **A path edited by hand**. A right-angle route's legs are its level and upright pieces, counted from the
  begin; `connector.edit_path {id, leg, to}` moves one to the line `to` (inches on the page: a height for a level leg, a place along x for an upright one). A leg that is not at an end takes its two corners
  with it, so the legs before and after stretch; **a leg at an end cannot leave its connection point, so the route gets a jog**: on out along the leg for a standoff (a quarter inch, or a third of the leg where it is
  shorter), across to the line, and on along it (a route of one leg moved across is a Z of five legs). **The glued ends do not move.** Points that repeat or lie between their neighbours are dropped; a
  position that would fold the path back on itself (a leg doubling back, two legs over each other) or make it longer than 16 points is refused in words (`fold back`) and changes nothing; a leg already on the line
  is no edit. The edit writes the route as constants in the connector's own frame with the **last row's `X` as `Width*1`** (as the last row of a file's fixed route has it, so the end of the route stays on the end
  of the connector when its shapes move), **writes `ConFixedCode` 3 locally** ("a path edited by hand writes 3"), places the label and rebuilds the jumps, as one undo step. A route with
  `ConFixedCode` 1, 2 or 3 is not routed again when its shapes move, but for the crossing says: only the ends follow, and the bends stay where they were in the connector's frame (the "end legs stretch along
  their own direction" for 1 and 3 is not done). A straight or curved connector, a connector inside a group, and a route with a slanted leg have no legs to move and are refused in words. **Reset Connector**
  (`connector.reset {ids}`): `ConFixedCode` 0 is written locally (so a master's 3 is overridden) and the connector is routed by its style now, whether or not it had been edited; a connector that
  comes out as it was is no change and no undo step. `connector.reconnect` of a connector whose code is 3 writes 0 with the new glue (the new ends have a route of their own). `draw::connector_path` gives a
  window the corners, the style, whether the path was edited by hand and whether it can be (`editable`).
* **Snapping a path edited by hand** (`draw/hand.rs`, `cmd/path.rs`): `connector.edit_path` takes `snap: true` or `{zoom, view}`, the words of a place (the others are refused in words). **The place the snap pulls is the middle of the leg** (the middle of its two corners), on the line
  `to` asks for, **and it moves along the one axis the leg can move on**: y for a level leg and x for an upright one, which is the lock of a box that keeps to an axis (`lock`) with the library's own rule that a place on the line the leg travels along pulls it too.
  So the grid, the ruler's ticks, the guides and the places of other shapes (connection points, vertices) pull the leg's line as they pull a point put down; the alignment lines of other shapes pulled nothing when it was tried (a shape's edge 0.03 inch from the leg, the alignment box on), so a leg is not lined up with the edge of another shape.
  **The line the snap gives is the line the leg goes to** (`to` and `snap` are in the answer, `snap` as the other commands tell it); a snap that gives the line the leg already lies on is no edit and no undo step. The Snap switch off, or the window's Alt held, makes it do nothing. `draw::leg_subject` makes the place and the axis, so the window's preview and
  the command land alike. **Ours**: no specification says what a hand edit snaps to; Support says only that the midpoint is dragged to a new place and Shift with an arrow key moves it a pixel at a time (the keys are not built).
* **Reroute** (`draw/reroute.rs`, `cmd/path.rs`). `connector.reroute {ids, mode}` writes the Connector tab's Reroute list into `ConFixedCode`, locally (so a master's value is overridden), as one undo step with nothing else: `freely` 0, `as_needed` 1, `never` 2, `on_crossover` 3. A connector that has the setting
  already is not written (a 4, 5 or 6 reads as freely and is left as it is), and the command **routes nothing**: the path a connector has stays until a change of the shapes it is glued to asks for another, as the Behavior dialog's list is a property and not a command (the choice is ours: gives Reset Connector, not the list, a reroute now). **What the settings do when a shape the connector is glued to moves**:
  0 routes it again; 2 never does, even when a leg passes through the shape (the ends follow, the bends stay in the connector's frame); **1 and 3 keep the route unless a leg of it would pass through a shape the connector is glued to, and then make a new route** by the connector's style (rules 1 to 7) and keep their setting (a route made so is not a hand edit undone,
  so the 3 stays). *A leg passes through a shape* when it enters the box of the shape at an end, as `route_clear::crosses` tests it (the route's end legs, which stand on the boxes, are held to the boxes themselves; a box an end is inside of, or leaves inwards, is no obstacle: the test the router's own route is held to). **Not built**: the second cause of 1, a placeable shape that comes to cross a leg (nothing in the engine knows a shape as an obstacle but the two the connector stands on, and
  the route "goes through every other shape", F12), the legs that stretch along their own direction for 1, 2 and 3 where has them (a fixed route keeps its bends in the connector's frame, so a move that turns the connector turns its legs), and the setting of a connector in a group (it is written and read like any other). `DynamicsOff` on the page keeps every row from rerouting, as before. Reset Connector writes 0.
  A straight or curved connector has the setting too: it keeps the route it has under 2, and is made again by its style under 1 and 3 when the route crosses a shape. **What the test costs** (the security scan's findings): each test is a **try**, as a routing is, so a refit of a group tests and routes 500 connectors at the most (`MAX_REROUTED`) and says the others in `groups_left` ("it was not routed again"); the glue of the ends of the connectors to test is read in one pass over the page's `Connect` rows and the shapes it names are found with the rest of the edit's, so no test searches the page; a test reads at most `MAX_CROSSING_ROWS` rows of the route (256, sixteen times the points of a hand path: room for line jumps on every leg), and **a route of more rows is held not to cross and keeps its path, unread** (no route a person or DiaCraft draws has that many); the connection points of a shape an end is glued to are read once for the edit, by the test and the routing alike, and a shape past `MAX_CROSSING_POINT_ROWS` (65,536 rows) for the edit gives its ends no direction of their own, as a point that faces no way does (also gave none to a shape of more than 256 `Connection` rows, which the routing that follows read whole: the two read the same points now). Measured on a crafted page of 10,000 connectors on crossover, each of 4,000 rows and all glued to one moved shape (`tests/it/connect_cost.rs`): the move read 40 million rows and visited 200 million shapes in searches before, and reads none of the rows and visits only the edit's own two walks now.
* **The window's connector path handles (`crates/ui-egui/src/edit/legs.rs`).** A **selected right-angle connector** (the one shape selected, the Pointer Tool in use, a path of level and upright legs that is not in a group: `ConnectorPath::editable`) **shows a blue disc at the middle of each leg** (the selection look's R30, "a bend or middle handle is a filled blue disc about 9 pixels": the theme token `bend_handle`, 9 points across with a white halo, the same at every zoom); **a leg shorter than 24 points on the screen has none**, so that
  the handle does not touch the marks at the ends (a quarter-inch leg has one from 100% on). **A straight or a curved connector has none: no specification says what a handle on one does (Q1 of the report), and a handle that did nothing would only mislead.** **A press on a handle and a drag moves the leg across**, in the one direction across it (the cursor is the arrow that way), from the line it lies on by as far as the pointer goes. **The drag lands where the engine's snap puts it** (the window asks `snap_ext` with the same place and lock, in the
  same words, so the release is what the preview showed; Alt turns the snap off for the drag) and **is drawn at once**: the route the engine will write (`draw::moved_route`), dashed in the edit colour over the picture, the handle where the leg goes and what the snap pulled it to; **where the engine would refuse the place (the path would fold back, or be too long) the route as it is is dashed in the snap's orange**, and the release sends the command anyway, so that the engine's
  words reach the status bar. **The release is one `connector.edit_path`** (with `snap` unless Alt was held or the page was not ready): one undo step, `ConFixedCode` 3, the glued ends where they were; Escape in the middle sends nothing, and a drag that comes back to where it began sends nothing and does not mark the path edited. The connector stays selected, with handles on its new legs.
  **The connector's right-click menu** (`CONNECTOR_MENU` of `ribbon/spec.rs`, when every shape selected is a connector; a menu of the shapes selected otherwise): Cut, Copy, Paste (at the click), Delete (which takes the connectors glued to the selection along, as the key does), Bring to Front and Send to Back (their two rows each), **Reset Connector** (`connector.reset` of the connectors selected: it is on a connector's right-click menu) and **Reroute**, a menu of four check boxes (Reroute Freely, Reroute As Needed, Never Reroute, Reroute on Crossover: the one the connectors selected agree on is ticked), each one `connector.reroute` of the connectors selected, one undo step
  (the reroute setting is an ordinary edit: it is not a View setting). The Reroute rows' place is **ours** (the specifications list the four commands among those that are on no tab and say the Behavior dialog's Connector tab has the list, which this build does not have; Q2 of the report). **Edit Text is not a row**, for want of a command that opens the box (F2 and a double click do). `ui.inspect` tells `legs` (`{connector, handles: [{leg, level, at}], reroute, dragging: {leg, to, raw, snap}}`, `null` with none) and the names `connector.leg.<n>`
  are the handles' press areas; `DiacraftApp::connector_leg_point` is where a client takes hold. The drag is **not built for**: Shift with an arrow key moving the handle by a pixel (Support), a handle on a straight or curved connector, and a leg that is shorter than 24 points.
* **Line jumps**. A jump is rows of the connector that carries it, written
  when connectors are routed, **never at open** and never drawn by the renderer. *Who takes part*: top-level connectors (made by DiaCraft or glued at an end, with `MoveTo`, `LineTo`, `ArcTo`
  and `PolylineTo` rows only, at most 256 legs); a curved connector takes none and gets none, and a line that is not a connector takes no part; at most 2,000 take part and 100,000 points
  between them, past which a page has no jumps made. *What a rebuild reads*: the routes of those connectors and the old routes of the ones routed, within 400,000 rows for the rebuild (`draw::MAX_JUMP_ROWS`), counted before any is merged; a route of more than 4,096 rows takes no part and is not read, and a page whose routes hold more rows than the rebuild may read has no jumps made, as a page of too many connectors has none (the routes were read whole whenever a connector was routed: 16 million rows beside 10,000 routes of 4,000 rows, for the move of one). *When*: after a command routes connectors, the routed ones are rebuilt, and so is every connector whose legs cross their old or their new route **that has a hop to make or take away**: one
  that has no hop and needs none (its rows and its new path are a `MoveTo` and `LineTo` rows only) keeps the rows it has, however they are written; the same after a delete, for the connectors a deleted one
  crossed; no other connector is touched; `connector.line_jumps` rebuilds every connector. Each is rebuilt from the route it has with its jumps
  taken out (the rows' ends with the points on a leg dropped), and a connector whose rows come out as they are is not written. A paste, group or ungroup makes no jumps. *Which connector carries it*:
  the page's `LineJumpCode`, read as 1 where nothing sets it, as the root of every file holds it: 0 none, 1 the flatter leg of the two crossing, 2 the steeper, 3 and 4 the connector
  higher in the page's drawing order (3, the last routed, stands in the order), 5 the lower, 6 and the rest none; equally steep goes to the higher. A connector's own `ConLineJumpCode` overrides
  1 never carries, 2 carries where the page gives the crossing to nobody, 3 never carries and the connector it crosses does instead, 4 neither carries; the page's choice wins a
  conflict. *Size*: a chord of `LineToLineX` times `LineJumpFactorX` on a level or slanted leg and `LineToLineY` times `LineJumpFactorY` on an upright one, in drawing units, 0.125 and 2/3
  where nothing sets them; none for a size of 0. *Room*: at least half a chord from both ends of the leg, and a second jump closer than a chord to the first is not made. *Look*
  (`LineJumpStyle`, `ConLineJumpStyle` when it is not 0): an arc (0, 1, and 5 to 9 by the project rule), a gap (2: a `MoveTo` to the far end of the chord), a square (3: a `PolylineTo` with a control
  point half a chord off each end of the chord), two sides (4: a `PolylineTo` with one control point half a chord off the crossing). *Side*: up on a level or slanted leg and left on an upright one
  by default; `PageLineJumpDirX` or `ConLineJumpDirX` 2 puts it down, `PageLineJumpDirY` or `ConLineJumpDirY` 2 puts it to the right. An arc's `A` is the half chord, **negative to the left of the way the leg
  runs** and carried into the connector's own frame (a mirrored connector has the other sign): a level leg running toward +x bulges up with `-0.0416...`, toward -x with `+`; the cells are plain
  numbers, `U` `DL` on `A`. `connector.line_jumps` takes `show` (the Connectors menu's Show Line Jumps: on makes the page's code 1 where it is 0 and leaves another code alone, off makes it 0 —
  ours, says Support does not say), `code` 0 to 5, `style` 0 to 9, `dir_x` and `dir_y` 0 to 2, `factor_x` and `factor_y` 0 to 10 (the page only); with `ids` it sets the own code, style
  and sides of those connectors (0 gives the page's back). **The work is bounded**: one rebuild tests at most 4,000,000 pairs of legs and writes nothing when that is not enough.

## What it costs

One `shape.move` of a shape with a connector on each side, on a page of 500 shapes and 300 connectors, measured by
`crates/engine/tests/it/edit_cost.rs` in the development profile: **about 4 ms**, where the page-wide recalculation it replaced is about 40 ms
on the same page (the test prints both; they move with the load of the machine, and across runs on a busy one a move was 2 to 4 times cheaper
than the whole page, and 10 times on a quiet one). The cost is mostly listing, for the page, the formulas that read other shapes (the glue
formulas, the frame functions and the `Connect` rows), which is done for each command and not kept. Moving 4,096 shapes in one command on a
page of 4,100 takes about 0.15 s, and deleting them about 0.05 s. The connector work added to a move the list of the connectors with a glued end (once for the
command), the `calc` test of each glued end of a connector of a file that follows (one evaluation), and the route of every connector of a file whose
end moved, which the test page's connectors, all ours, did not need.

**Connectors routed again**, counted by the unit tests of `crates/engine/src/draw/route_cost.rs` and timed by its ignored tests (run by hand, each case against a yardstick of plain content of the same size made just before it, in the development profile on a machine at a load of 37 to 47), on crafted pages of 10,000 copies of one connector, glued to box A and box B, which come after them in the page's order, the copies sharing one route section: a move of A that routes the 10,000 again visited 300 million shapes in searches of the page and passed over 200 million `Connect` rows (6.9 s with routes of 4,000 rows); it visits the edit's own two walks and passes over the rows once, and took 3.7 s against 3.9 s for the same move with the connectors set never to reroute (routes of six rows) and 4.3 s against 2.9 s (routes of 4,000 rows). **The floor of that move** (the connectors set never to reroute) grew with the rows of the route the copies share: the recalculation's walks of the sheets passed over 160 million cells and the pricing of the undo step looked at 80 million rows, 4.3 s against 1.1 s with routes of six rows; `calc` now lists a section's formula cells once for a commit and passes by a section that two versions of a sheet hold behind one pointer, and the step is charged a section once and never one that the next version holds (it was charged the route for each copy): 416,130 cells and 4,001 rows, 2.8 s against 2.9 s with six rows. **10,000 ends on a shape of 10,000 connection points** placed and routed by a move of the shape did not finish in a quarter of an hour (4.7 s with a shape of four points): they read the points once, 4.1 s against 3.9 s (glued to a point) and 3.4 s against 3.2 s (to the whole of the shape). **One connector routed beside 10,000 routes of 4,000 rows** read 16 million rows in the jump pass (19.3 s); it reads 400,008, 4.7 s against 3.0 s beside routes of six rows. **A move of the shape 20,000 connectors are glued to** routes 10,000 and says the others: 6.9 s against 6.1 s for the same move that routes none. The pages have no master, no text and no group; their connectors share one section, which is what a paste or a file's copies hold (connectors that each hold a route of their own of thousands of rows are walked once each, as their content is).

**The arrange commands**, measured by `crates/engine/tests/it/arrange.rs` in the development profile on the same page of 500 rectangles of the built-in stencil and 300 connectors
of ours, each glued at both ends by `PAR` formulas (no `_WALKGLUE`, no group, no text, one page; the numbers move with the load of the machine, and the page-wide recalculation is
about 40 ms on it): turning or flipping one shape that has a connector on each side **about 4 ms**; turning or flipping 100 shapes as one (the connectors between them
follow) **about 20 ms**; aligning 100 shapes, 99 of which move, **about 20 ms**; resizing a turned shape **about 4 ms**; reordering one shape or 100 **under 1 ms** (the order is
a list, and nothing is recalculated). At the most ids a command takes, 4,096 shapes on a page of 4,100 with no connectors: turning them as one 0.23 s, flipping 0.22 s, aligning
0.21 s, reordering 0.06 s. The page has no connector of a file glued to the whole of a shape (places those ends for each connector that follows a shape that was
turned), no group, no text and no master with a long list of connection points, and what they cost was not measured here.
**The format commands**, measured by `crates/engine/tests/it/format_cost.rs` in the development profile on the page of `edit_cost.rs` (500
rectangles of the built-in stencil, each an instance holding its pin, and 300 glued connectors) with a label of one run on each rectangle. Medians of
5 to 7 calls, on a quiet run and on one when the machine's load average was 26, both given since they move together with the page-wide recalculation
that is the yardstick: `shape.format` of one shape with a connector on each side (fill, line, weight and end arrow) **3.7 ms** and 9.7 ms, of all 500
shapes 8 ms and 20 ms; `text.format` of one shape (size, bold, colour) 3.7 ms and 9 to 11 ms, of a range (it splits a run) 3.7 ms and 11 ms, of all 500
labels 8.6 ms and 22 ms; `shape.formatting` of one shape 0.01 ms, of all 500 5 ms and 9 ms; the recalculation of every formula of the page, which a
format would cost if it worked out the page, 42 ms and 86 to 89 ms. So one shape is about a ninth of the page in both runs, and 500 shapes about a fifth.
The cost of one shape is the listing of the formulas of the page that `calc` does for each command, as for a move; the writes are a few cells each.
Formatting 4,096 shapes (the most `ids` takes) on a page of 4,100 labelled rectangles took 0.12 s for `shape.format`, 0.13 s for `text.format` and 0.10 s
for `shape.formatting`, and about twice that when loaded. **The fixture lacks** groups, fields, a second run, a theme part, a style chain beyond the
root and any formula that reads a format cell: a file whose line colour is a shade of its fill colour works out that cell too after a fill, and only
that one; the numbers are not claimed for it.

**A text of many runs and shapes named many times**, measured by `format_scale.rs`: the whole text of a label of 1,500 runs with five cells
changed for each run took 28 ms and of 12,000 runs 270 ms (9.6 times for 8 times the runs; the cells a shape plans are found by address, the rows of a
