section are found once for a command and held by `IX`, and the rows a split names are held in sets, where a scan of the cells planned so far took 10.5 s
at 12,000 runs); a split in the first run of a text of 12,000 runs, which numbers every run again (a row written for each), 90 ms (9 ms at 1,500); 4,096
names of one group of 5,000 members took 76 ms (the group is walked once), and `shape.formatting` of a group of 25,000 named 4,096 times 40 ms. The fixture
is a label of runs of two letters with a row of its own for each run and no master, style row, field or theme; a text whose rows are partly a style's has
the other numbering (next free), which writes fewer rows. A split of a text whose runs name rows far apart (`cp` 0, then `cp` 40,000
and up) takes the rows it leaves behind out in one pass over the section: 3,000 rows took 17 ms and 24,000 rows 186 ms (11 times for 8 times the rows; a
pass for each row took 23 s at 24,000, a ratio of 62), and the unit tests count one pass for 3,000 rows. A text of 320,000 markers that name one row,
formatted over a range of 199,000 of them, plans two rows (a count, in the unit tests). The answer to "does a master or a style hold a row above 0" no
longer copies the document and builds a resolver: it reads the rows the command found already.

**`shape.formatting` over a range**, counted by the unit tests of `formatting/tests.rs` and timed by `format_scale.rs`, on a load average of about 12: a range over
every run of a text of 1,500 runs, each a row of its own that agrees with the others (so nothing ends the reading early), took 4 ms and of 12,000 runs 23 ms (5.6 times for 8
times the runs: a read of each row and a walk of the parts); a range of ten characters at the far end of the same texts 0.05 ms and 0.36 ms (the walk of the parts, which counts
characters). The worst case is a text of many distinct rows that all agree, nothing to stop the reading early: 30,000 rows that nobody holds read in 46 ms and 300,000 in 0.46 s
(1.5 microseconds a row, linear; measured once with a scratch test, not kept). The counts: 200,000 markers that name two rows read two rows, 100,000 runs with a range over the first three read three rows and walk seven parts, the same text
with a point in the middle reads one run and one paragraph, 16,000 rows that agree are read once each, and rows that differ in every character property stop the reading after
two. The fixture is runs of one letter and the page is the engine's alone: the paragraphs of a long text are found by a scan of its characters for line breaks (`paragraph_bounds`,
one test of a character each, as `text.format`'s `align` does), which was not timed apart.

**The clipboard, group and ungroup commands**, measured by `crates/engine/tests/it/edit_cost.rs` (the page above: 500 rectangles of the built-in stencil and 300 connectors of ours, each glued at both ends by `PAR`
formulas; no `_WALKGLUE`, no group to start with, no text, one page) and `clipboard_limits.rs`, in the development profile on a loaded machine (the numbers move with the load; two runs agreed to
within a tenth). On the 500-shape page, one shape with a connector on each side: copy **about 0.03 ms**, paste **3.5 ms**, duplicate **3.5 ms**, cut **4 ms** (the last three timed with the undo that restores the page, a pointer swap).
Four rows of shapes and their 96 connectors, 196 shapes in all: copy **1.4 ms**, paste **14 ms**, duplicate **15 ms**, cut **6 ms**, group **16 ms**, ungroup **15 ms**, a move of the group **19 ms**. At the most ids
a command takes (4,096) on a page of 4,100 boxes with no connector: copy 26 ms, paste 58 ms, duplicate 88 ms, group 102 ms, ungroup 97 ms, cut 337 ms; on a page of **100,000** boxes with 4,096
ids spread over it: copy 30 ms, paste 164 ms, group 214 ms (the page is walked once for all the ids, `draw/scan.rs`; a search for each id from the top of the page would be about 2e8 visits). The same id named 4,096
times costs what the shape costs (the ids are made distinct before anything is walked). A copy of a group of 50,000 shapes is pasted in 74 ms. One id naming a group of many members: an ungroup takes 0.2 s for 10,000 members, 1.3 s for 40,000 and 1.8 s at the cap of 50,000 (four times the members is about six times the time, which is the write of each member's pin, as for `shape.move`; the bookkeeping around it is one pass, and a test counts it). **What these fixtures lack**: no page holds a connector of a file
glued to the whole of a shape for these runs (`_WALKGLUE` ends are carried and not placed, so a paste or a group does not pay for them, but a later move does), no text with fields, no master with a long
list of connection points, and no page of 100,000 shapes with connectors (the 100,000-shape page has none, so the recalculation after the edit had few formulas to follow). A cut calls `delete_shapes`'s
rest unchanged. **Connectors glued to a shape that goes** (an ungroup, a cut, a delete) are found in one walk of the page (it was a search from the top of the page for each `Connect` row, 18.5 s for 100,000 connectors,
now 0.3 s), so an ungroup of a group with 20,000 connectors glued to it takes 0.3 s (4 times the connectors, about 5 times the time). **What a cut or a delete still pays**: `settle` looks each connector it routes up from the top of the page
(`connectors_to_route`, `refresh_route`, `label::place`), not changed here, so a delete of a shape with 5,000 connectors glued to it takes 0.27 s and with 10,000 about 1 s, and with 20,000 3.8 s (the product of the two).

**The connector commands**, counted, not timed (`crates/engine/tests/it/connect_cost.rs`, `diacraft_calc::work`) on the page of `edit_cost.rs` (500 rectangles and 300 connectors of ours, each
glued at both ends, all level, none crossing another; one page), against the **204,347 steps** of the recalculation of the whole page: `connector.autoconnect` of a copy beside a shape with a connector on each
side **3 commits of 88,447 steps** and of a master 2 commits of 88,991 (a drop, a move and a connector; the page is walked once for the place); `connector.style` of 100 connectors **1 commit of 54,006 steps** (53,606
back to right angle); `connector.reset` of 100 **1 commit of 44,201**; `connector.edit_path` of one **no commit** (it writes constants and cells nothing reads); `connector.line_jumps` on the page, which rebuilds
every connector and writes none that comes out as it was, **no commit**. **The jump pass adds no recalculation to the commands that route a connector** (a `shape.move` of a shape with a connector on each side is 1 commit of
about 45,000 steps, as before); it reads each connector's rows once, which a timing on a quiet run put at about 2 ms more for a move on this page (4.4 ms to 6.5 ms, measured once by hand, not held by a test).
**Crafted**, by the unit test of `draw/jumps.rs` that counts the pairs of legs tested (`spend`): 300 connectors of 200 slanted legs that all overlap (3.6e9 pairs) are given up by the budget of
**4,000,000 pairs** with nothing rewritten, and two such connectors test exactly 2 x 200 x 201 pairs; 2,100 connectors (past the 2,000 that take part) and 20 connectors of 1,000 legs (past the 256 of one that takes part)
make no commit and rewrite nothing. The fixture lacks crossing connectors on a large page (a move that rebuilds the jumps of many neighbours is about the jumps written, not the walk), a file's connectors inheriting
rows from a master, and curved connectors in numbers.

**Stencil files**, measured by `crates/engine/tests/it/stencil_hostile.rs` (crafted, timed; the times print with `--nocapture`; the development profile on a loaded machine, two runs each, and
the bound each test holds is 60 s, which asks whether it hangs and not how fast it is). Work is charged to what a file holds (a `Meter` of 3,000,000 shapes, rows, cells and styles for a copy, 32 MiB of icon
text for a listing, 2,048 masters listed), not to calls. **5,000 masters** (a 3.1 MB file): opening **0.23 to 0.33 s**, `stencil.list` 3 ms (a 252,533-byte answer: 2,048 masters listed), dropping the 4,999th master
0.2 to 0.4 ms, and 300 different masters into one drawing 0.11 to 0.14 s; **3,000 masters with one `UniqueID`**: opening 0.16 to 0.18 s; **a master part of 1.3 MB** (70,000 shapes in a group): opening
35 to 43 ms, a drop **0.6 to 0.9 ms** (past the 5,000 members an instance lists); **4,000 members each with four formulas**, listed: a drop **73 to 90 ms** (the recalculation of what reads the pin), 30,000 members left to
the master 15 to 17 ms; **40,000 members (3.9 MB) that all need a style link and a colour rewritten**: a drop **13 to 17 ms**, saving the drawing 0.11 s and reading it back 0.06 s; **a chain of 20,000 styles**: a first drop 8 to 13 ms and
the next 9 to 27 ms (the search of the drawing's styles by name is made again for each master that is not there already); **an icon of 12 MiB of text**: opening 22 to 26 ms, and refusing it 9 microseconds. A drop searches the drawing's masters
once (a few microseconds for each master it holds). What the fixtures lack: **no real stencil** (every package is built in code), formulas that read other members or are long, thousands of icons that each just fit,
styles that are large sheets, and a drawing of tens of thousands of masters. Reading is the reader's (`crates/vsdx`, whose own crafted tests hold it), and was not found slow on these.

The review's quadratic loops and the memory of open stencils; `stencil_hostile.rs`, `stencil_instances.rs`, `bring/tests.rs`. **The work of a copy is now counted by the tests** (`bring.rs` keeps two counters under `cfg(test)`):
a chain of 3,000 styles into a drawing that has all 3,000 of the name compares **3,000 sheets** (one for each), where a search of the drawing's styles of the name was about 4.5 million; a drawing of 6,000 alike-named styles
that differ from a chain of 6,000 compares **none**, and the time for 6,000 and for 12,000 is 10 and 18 ms (a doubling and not a quadrupling); 300,000 colour numbers against two tables of 4,096 examine **8,192 entries** (each table
once) and cost 34 ms. The whole drop of a chain of 20,000 alike-named styles into a drawing of 30,000 (the review's fixture, 6e8 comparisons by the old search) takes **19 to 26 ms**, and the next 20 to 37 ms; 4,000 masters of 64 shapes
open in 0.5 to 0.6 s (the listing sizes at most 8 shapes of a master and 10,000 of a stencil). **Open stencils are charged by their models**: five files of 4.6 MB each, each holding 96 MiB of XML, open and the sixth is refused (the old measure, the size of the file, took all sixteen, 1.5 GiB of XML). The meter now
also charges the hashing of the drawing's styles of a name (once for each name looked up), the two colour tables, and every kept element and attribute it looks at for a relationship.

`stencil_kept_text.rs`, `bring/tests/kept.rs`, `refs.rs`. **Kept text made to cost** (S7): an element of 60 declarations and 50,000 children pushes **60 bindings** (the first round pushed that for each child) in 5 ms; the review's 30,000 declarations and 30,000 children are refused **before anything is pushed** in 0.4 to 0.7 ms (the first round allocated about 29 GB); a shape that keeps 300,000 declarations and 300,000 prefixed attributes is refused in 7 to 13 ms (4.5e10 compares by a scan of the bindings for each attribute), through the reader and from a model built by hand. **The style search** now hashes every field `Sheet ==` compares (kept attributes and elements of the sheet, section, row and cell, a row's local name), charges each comparison, and counts no comparison for 2,000 styles of a name that differ from the chain's only in each of the ten fields; the re-review's fixture (a stencil of 20,000 styles called "Same" reached by 20,000 shapes against a drawing of 45,000 called "Same" that differ only in a kept cell attribute, 9e8 comparisons by the first hash) drops in **40 ms**, and the next drop 3 ms.

`stencil_line_pins.rs`, `bring/tests/kept.rs`, `refs.rs`. **A line's pin**: the dropped line's pin is `begin + (LocPinX / Width) * (end - begin)` and not always the middle of its ends (a line whose `LocPinX` is 0 has it at its begin, and one whose `LocPinX` is a quarter of its width a quarter along), set only where the master holds the pin as a number, then worked out again so that what reads `PinX` follows; tested for a single line and a line among the members of a dropped group, by cells and by pixels. **Kept text**: a declaration is taken back on leaving its element (each of the two `cut_back` calls is covered, for siblings in both orders, at the top and nested); the declarations of the elements above a kept node in its sheet (shape, sheet, section, row, cell) are in scope for it, in nesting order and taken back on leaving, and a sibling's is not seen (ten places for the owner, ten pairs of an enclosing element and a user, three levels of siblings); a node is charged before it is stacked (200,000 children against a budget of 1,000: 1 step stacked, and 996 of 200,000 at the top of a passthrough, where the first round stacked all of them). **The style search** charges a comparison, and the hash that finds it, a unit for 32 bytes of formula or kept text as well as its nodes (a 3.2 MB formula or kept attribute, equal in the drawing and the stencil, is refused by a budget that the nodes alone passed).

**Page setup and Auto Size**, measured by `crates/engine/tests/it/page_setup_cost.rs` (the times print with `--nocapture`; the development profile, run one test at a time on a machine whose load was moderate; two runs in parallel with other
tests were about three times slower). The page setup commands write a handful of cells and work out their readers (`settle_kept`, not the page): they are not timed. **The hook** (`draw::fit_page`) reads two cells when Auto Size is off. With it on it makes **one walk
of the page** for the shapes the command names (`scan`, not a search for each `ID`) and reads their boxes: a drop inside a page of **100,000** top-level shapes takes 0.74 ms with Auto Size off and 1.74 ms with it on, so the walk is about 1 ms for 100,000 shapes; one `shape.move`
on the page of 500 shapes and 300 connectors above takes 4.2 ms with it off and 4.1 ms with it on (no growth; the page was made wide enough to hold the fixture). **A growth** writes `PageWidth` and `PageHeight`: a drop that grew that page on the right (seven sheets) 3.7 ms.
**A growth on the left or the bottom carries every top-level shape** once (linear in the shapes and the `Connect` rows: the writes of the pins, the cached ends of glued connectors, and the recalculation of what reads a pose): 501 shapes and 300 connectors 23 ms, **19,999 shapes 206 ms**
(the most `MAX_SHIFT_SHAPES` carries, the new shape included); a page of 20,000 shapes is not carried (0.8 ms for the drop). What the fixtures lack: shapes with masters whose pin is a formula, groups with many members, and connectors of a file glued by `_WALKGLUE`
(`lift` carries their cached values); a hostile page of that kind was not timed.

**Starting a drawing from a template**, counted by `crates/engine/tests/it/template.rs` (`diacraft_calc::work`, `diacraft_doc::work`), in the development profile. Making a template
(a new drawing, its page size and turn, its theme, the writer, the reader) is **3 commits of the recalculation and 24 steps, with 17 resolvers built**, for Basic Flowchart, and fewer for Basic Diagram (no theme); `file.new {template}`
costs exactly that and **the same on an empty session and on the page of 500 shapes and 300 connectors it replaces** (a test compares the counts: nothing of the old drawing is touched). A start takes about **3 ms** (a hundred
in a row; printed by the test, not asserted). The package is made again each time and not kept: it is small and the cost is constant. A template **file** costs what `file.open` of it does (the reader's, bounded by its limits), plus a read
of its one cell: at most the first 4 KiB of the text and 64 entries (a test hands a cell of 800 KB, the most the reader allows an attribute, and gets at most 64 names counted). What the fixtures lack: a template file of
thousands of pages or masters (the reader's own tests hold that), and a Shapes window with a hundred open stencil files.

**The shapes a command names are found in one walk of the page** (for the format commands and the page a command works on, for the rest), counted, not timed, by the unit tests of `crates/engine/src/draw/loops_cost.rs`: the shapes `Document::find_shape` visited (`diacraft_doc::work`, a search of the page from its top for one `ID`) and the shapes the walks of `draw/scan.rs` visited, for the most ids each command takes spread from the first shape of a page of 30,000 to its last. The commands: `shape.rotate`, `shape.flip`, `shape.order`, `shape.align` and `shape.resize` of several shapes (`draw/arrange.rs::targets`), `shape.move` (`move_shapes_leaving`) and the moves the engine makes (`move_each`), `shape.delete`, `edit.copy`, `edit.cut` and `shape.duplicate` (`delete_shapes`, and `container::with_contents`, which asks whether a shape among them holds members), `shape.formatting` (`look_of_in`), `connector.reset` (each connector compared before and after), the snaps of `shape.move`, `shape.resize` and `shape.place` and `snap.at` with `ids`, on the 512 shapes a snap takes (`snap::bounds_each`, `box_of`, `own_points`, and `own_points_each` for the points of each shape a place moves by its own distance), `shape.change` on its 512 (the shapes named, and each new instance looked for at the end of the page's list, where the drop put it), `callout.insert` for 4,096 shapes on a page with a theme and an active layer (one pass makes the callouts follow the theme, `theme::follow_page_theme_refs`, and one write puts them on the layers, `layers::join_active_at`), and `fields::refresh` over named shapes (the command takes one). Each looked each shape up from the top of the page: **58.7 million** visits for 4,096 names, 117 million for `shape.delete` and `connector.reset` (two searches each), 263 million and 4,096 walks to make the new shapes follow the theme for `callout.insert`, and 7.6 to 23 million for the commands capped at 512. Now no shape is looked up from the top of the page, and each command walks the page **one to five times in all**, whatever the number named (the page the shapes are on, the shapes, and what follows them: an edit's settling, the callouts and members of a move, the containers of a delete). A shape that is not on the page is refused in the words and the order it was ("page 1 has no shape 99999", the first one named that is missing), and a page that is not there too. **Still a walk for each, of another kind:** `shape.change` finds the next free `ID` for each new instance by a walk of the page (`page_stats`, at most 512 of them), and the connector ends it glues again (at most 4,096) are each looked up and each rebuilds the page's `Connect` rows.

## What is left

* **Templates**: the four built-in templates only (Basic Diagram, Basic Flowchart, Cross-Functional Flowchart, Organization Chart); no thumbnail; no choice of metric or US units when starting (the page is Letter; "Choose metric or US units when starting" is P1); no starter
  diagrams or samples (P2); no search (the window filters the list, which has the `category` of each); a template file is started from by its path, and the folders of templates (File Locations) and the recent templates list are
  the window's and the settings'. A built-in stencil cannot be closed (`stencil.close` refuses it, as before). Basic Diagram offers the Basic Shapes and the Arrow Shapes. A template names
  only built-in stencils; the stencil files open at the moment of a Save As are not kept. Opening a *drawing* that came from a template does not open the stencils its list names. A third
  save of a new drawing is gap F14, not this task's.
* **Snap, grid and guides** (gaps F16): writing the share form of glue to a guide, a single guide cell with no row and a line's end on a guide (read and kept), holding a turned shape or a member of a group to a guide, guide points, shape extension lines and isometric angle lines, a guide at an angle, the shapes of a background page and the members of a group as targets, a page's layers' own snap and glue, the window's own `SnapSettings` in the windows part, and the isometric angles in the Snap & Glue dialog (no setting holds them); the View tab's toggles are saved with the drawing (the dialog, the Visual Aids group and the switches are the window's, and so are the drags and the guides on the page). The flag values of `SnapSettings` (but flags 1 and 2), `GlueSettings` and `SnapExtensions` are not in a specification: the options that would need them are kept in DiaCraft's part until a source gives them.
* **Page setup**: Fit to Drawing, resizing the page by dragging its edge, "page size independent of printer paper" as a choice of its own, print margins and centring (the page breaks are drawn on the page), and background pages' own sizes (`page.size` works on any page, a background too, and nothing keeps a
  background the size of the pages that use it) are not here. Auto Size does not grow the page for a connector's route (only for the shapes a command names) or when it is turned on. A page deleted takes its `ID` with it; `Document::add_page`
  may hand the same `ID` out again. The drawing scales and the sizes of the gallery are ours (no specification lists them). A file's `PaperKind` that is not in the table (user-defined papers: 32767 on 34 page sheets of the census) is Letter for the sheets of a growth.
* A text box does not grow with its text (no cell of it reads `TEXTHEIGHT` or `TEXTWIDTH`). There is no tool that draws a vertical text box (Insert, Text Box, Vertical Text Box: a box is made across and Vertical Text turns its text) and no command for the text block's margins or
  background. The Text Block tool's change to a connector's label lasts until the connector is routed again. The net turn of the text of a member of a flipped group is the `Angle` - `TxtAngle` rule applied at every level
  (the specification gives it for a shape's own flip only, so it is ours and unverified).
* **Stencil files** (gaps F7): a master with a picture, an object, an embedded package or another relationship is refused (until the writer can copy related parts), and so is a master of none or of more than 5,000 top-level shapes (a master of several is dropped as a group, whose frame is ours); a stencil's theme part (left it for the writer to be able to add a theme part, which it can: a drop that put the stencil's part on `Document::themes` when no part there carries its number would now be saved, and what a master selects and which part to bring when the target holds another under that number is for a later task), the masters its formulas name by `USE()` and its data
  connections are not copied; a master is dropped at the size it has, whatever the target page's drawing scale; a master the drawing has changed is used as the drawing has it; a copied style keeps its name, so a drawing can hold two
  of one name; a master of more than 5,000 descendants is dropped without its members listed. The pages a stencil may hold (five of 488 in the census) are not offered: only masters are.

* What a function reads of a sheet that no reference and no frame names is not followed: the theme cells `THEMEVAL` reads, the text
  `TEXTWIDTH` reads. The commands change none of those. A formula that reads cells its text does not name (`LISTSHEETREF()!Width`, the container
  and callout functions, `EVALCELL`) is worked out after every command that changes anything, and a formula that names a sheet that is not
  there is worked out after a command that takes a shape out. A command that changed a master, a style or the document's own sheet would be
  worked out as the whole page (`Changes::whole_page`); the theme commands do, and no other.
* The list of what reads a cell **of its own sheet** (`Width` read by `LocPinX`) is found for a sheet when a change first reaches it, again for each command. The list of what reads a cell **through another sheet** (a qualified reference, a function that carries a point between frames, a `Connect` row), the cells that are worked out after every command (they read what their text does not name), and the cells whose reference finds no sheet are kept by the session from one command to the next (`calc::ReaderIndex`, held by the engine's session, `crates/engine/src/calc_index.rs`): a command that replaces a few shapes puts right what it read of those, by a comparison that skips every list and shape it did not write, so it costs what the command touched and not the formulas of the page (a move on a page of 12,000 shapes asked 168,000 formulas whether they read beyond their sheet before). A command that adds, takes out, moves, renames or gives another master to a shape, that changes a master, a style, the name of a page or the `Connect` rows, or that is an undo to a document the list was not made for, **makes the list again whole**, as every command did: it costs what it cost. The list of a page is kept for one page of the drawing at a time, so a drawing whose commands alternate between two pages makes it for each.
* Full routing (avoiding every shape, spreading legs, the other route styles) replaces `draw::route`. A route here goes round the boxes of the two shapes it is glued to and
  **straight through any other shape that is in its way**; a curved connector cuts the corners of its route, so it can clip a shape the route passes close to; the flowchart, tree, network and simple styles are right angle.
* Group resize. An end glued to the whole of a shape goes to the box of the shape and not its drawn curve.
* Rotating and flipping the member of a turned or flipped group, and distributing shapes. A formula that reads the stacking order (`CONTAINERSHEETREF`, `LISTORDER`) is not worked out again after `shape.order`, which changes no cell; a flip of a file's connector with a bent route and no glued end keeps its rows (ROTATE-1D-1).
* `_WALKGLUE` is placed by the engine's rule and not the reference app's, which no source gives, so a file's dynamic glue may pick another
  point than the application that wrote it; `WalkPreference` is not read.
* A connector of a file that is centre to centre is left alone when a shape moves, and so is one with `ConFixedCode` 1, 2 or 3 (a path edited by hand among them), but for a leg that would cross a shape it is glued to under 1 and 3: its
  rows follow only where they have formulas that read `Width`; the bends stay where they were in the connector's frame, and for 1 and 3 is not done. The values are the specification's.
* Line jumps are made when connectors are routed and when the page's jump cells are written; a paste, a group, an ungroup and a file's open make none, so a pasted connector keeps the jumps it had and does not make its
  neighbours jump. A connector in a group takes no part. The rows of 3 to 7 sides are an arc. AutoConnect of a shape with no connection point (a group) is refused: the application's dynamic glue to the whole of a shape is not written.
* The clipboard is within one document: a drawing that is made or opened empties it, so a paste in another drawing is not done (the matching of a stencil's masters to a document's, is there); Paste Special and Paste as Hyperlink are not done, and the system clipboard gets the text of the labels only. A shape with a
  picture is pasted on its own page only (the writer does not add relationships to the page part). A member of a group that takes its cells from a master group is copied with its group only, and a group that is an
  instance of a master is not ungrouped (its members would lose the cells they take from it; making them local is not done). A connector cannot be carried out of a flipped group, copied or ungrouped. A group named with a group
  it is in is not taken apart in the same call. Pages of different scales are not compared on a paste (the cells are copied as they are). A paste does not carry layers, which are the page's.
* A member made from an instance holds `Width`, `Height`, `PinX` and `PinY` as formulas of its group and a local copy of its master's `Geometry` (gaps F8). A one-dimensional instance, the size of a member turned by other than a half turn, a cell with a formula of its own and a group with no extent keep their numbers (no count gives a form), `LocPinX` and `LocPinY` stay inherited, and `shape.resize` sizes a group and the formulas are what its members follow (`shape.size` still refuses a group), and a plain group inside a group and the shapes with no master follow it by their numbers (the stretch of the group's frame) and not by a formula.
* A connector of ours that is carried out of a turned group keeps the legs it was drawn with, slanted on the page (it draws where it did); a move of it, or of a shape it is glued to, routes it again.
* A group is sized by `shape.resize` as one shape and its members follow (`shape.size` still refuses a group, `shape.resize` refuses a member of a turned or flipped group, and `shape.size` does not). `shape_frame` of a
  member gives its box in its group's frame.
* Size & Position: a pin is not dragged (the window has no handle for it yet); the pin of a line or a connector is not set; several shapes are given one X or one Y by `shape.place`
  (the Size & Position pane sends one `shape.place`); a glued end is not typed into (`connector.reconnect` to a place takes the glue off first); a line of no length is not given a length of nothing.
* A text edit is plain characters: no choice of font, size or colour for the characters typed (they take the run they join; `text.format` changes
  them afterwards), no paragraph or tab markers added or taken away by an edit, and a field is not typed: `text.insert_field` puts one in (Insert, Text, Field).
* Formatting does not touch: the transparency of text (`ColorTrans`), `AsianFont` and `ComplexScriptFont`, `Pos`, `FontScale`, `Letterspace`, `DblUnderline`, `IndRight`,
  `IndFirst` but for a bullet's hanging form, line spacing, a bullet of one's own (`text.format`'s bullets are a table of seven marks) and tab stops. A shadow is `shape.shadow`,
  a gradient's stops `shape.gradient`, the fill's and the line's transparency, a line cap and the size of an arrowhead `shape.format`, and the left
  indent, the bullets, the case, the reading direction and vertical text `text.format`. In the case where a master or a style holds a row of `Character` or `Paragraph` at `IX` 1 or above (about 1 text in 100), a split numbers the
  new rows after the rows there are, so a reader that maps runs to rows by position shows a split in a run that has later
  runs wrong there. The rows a split does not write whole (a row left alone or changed in place) are not filled in for such a reader. The theme's marker
  in `Style` that the theme cannot answer reads as no bits.
* `shape.formatting` over a range reads the shape's **own** text, as the range of `text.format` and `shape.set_text` does: a shape that shows its master's
  text and has none of its own has no characters to range over, and the point `0` to `0` there reads row 0, which is the row the first character typed in it
  would take. A range of a group (name a member) and a range of several shapes (the window decides what a selection of several shows) are refused.
* The window's text box shows one face and size for all the characters, not the runs' (the picture of the page shows them), and the fields are shown
  under the characters, not where they stand. With a box open the Font group shows the characters selected, or the run before the caret (gap U19, settled); a caret
  still formats the whole text.
* A file's modified time can be set back to what it was by whatever changed it, and a change that keeps the size and the time (an edit within one
  tick of the file system's clock) is not seen: the check for a file changed on the disk is the size and the time, not the bytes.
